<meta>This is the title page of Emanuel Swedenborg's foundational theological work, originally published in Latin in 1763 as "Sapientia Angelica de Divino Amore et de Divina Sapientia."</meta>

# ANGELIC WISDOM
# CONCERNING THE
# DIVINE LOVE AND
# THE DIVINE
# WISDOM

->By Emanuel Swedenborg<-
<note>original: "Sapientia Angelica de Divino Amore et de Divina Sapientia"</note>

<term>Angelic Wisdom</term> — In Swedenborg’s writings, this refers to spiritual truths and insights as they are understood by those in the celestial and spiritual realms (angels).

<term>Divine Love and Divine Wisdom</term> — These are the two fundamental attributes of God in Swedenborgian theology. Divine Love is considered the "esse" (being or heat) of life, while Divine Wisdom is the "existere" (manifestation or light) through which that love creates and operates.

<term>Emanuel Swedenborg</term> — A prominent Swedish scientist and philosopher (1688–1772) who, in his later life, claimed to have his spiritual sight opened, allowing him to converse with spirits and angels and record their teachings.

<summary>This title page introduces Emanuel Swedenborg’s treatise on the dual nature of the Divine—Love and Wisdom—and how these forces create and sustain the universe.</summary>
<keywords>Emanuel Swedenborg, theology, Divine Love, Divine Wisdom, Angelic Wisdom, New Church, spiritual philosophy</keywords>

## ON DEGREES.

| | No. |
| :--- | :---: |
| That in the spiritual world there are atmospheres, waters, and earths, just as in the natural world; but the former are spiritual, whereas the latter are natural | 173 |

| That degrees are of two kinds: degrees of altitude <note>vertical or discrete degrees, like steps on a ladder</note> and degrees of latitude <note>horizontal or continuous degrees, like the shading of light to dark</note> | 184 |
| That the degrees of altitude are homogeneous <note>of the same kind or nature</note>, and follow one another in a series, like end, cause, and effect | 189 |
| That the first degree is fully present in all the subsequent degrees | 195 |
| That all perfections increase and ascend with these degrees and according to them | 199 |
| That in successive order, the first degree forms the highest and the third the lowest; but in simultaneous order, the first degree forms the inmost and the third the outmost | 205 |

## ON THE CREATION OF THE UNIVERSE.

That the Lord from eternity, who is Jehovah, created the universe and
all things in it from Himself, and not from nothing <note>This refers to the rejection of creation *ex nihilo* (out of nothing), asserting instead that all things proceed from God's own substance.</note> - 282

things in it - 290

of love, the divine of wisdom, and the divine of use; and that these
three appear to come from the sun of the spiritual
world: the divine of love by heat, the divine of wisdom by light, and
the divine of use by the atmosphere, which is the container <note>original: "continent"; meaning the medium that holds or contains these divine qualities.</note> - 296

## PART V.

### ON THE CREATION OF HUMANITY.

That the Lord has created and formed in man two containers <note>original: "receptacles"</note> and dwellings for Himself, called the will and the understanding: the will for His Divine Love, and the understanding for His Divine Wisdom - - - - - - - 358
That the will and understanding, which are the containers of love and wisdom, are located in the brain—in its entirety and in every part—and from there, in the whole body and every part of it - - 362
    I. That love and wisdom, and therefore the will and understanding, make up the very life of man - - - - - 363
    II. That man’s life exists in its primary origins <note>original: "principles"</note> in the brain, and in its derived parts <note>original: "principiates"</note> in the body - - - - - 365
    III. That as life is in its origins, so it is in the whole and in every part - - - - - - 366
    IV. That through these origins, life flows from every part into the whole, and from the whole into every part - - - 367
    V. That the quality of a person's love determines the quality of their wisdom, and thus the quality of the person - - - - - - - - 368
That there is a connection <note>original: "correspondence"</note> between the will and the heart, and between the understanding and the lungs - - - - - - 371
    I. That all aspects of the mind relate to the will and understanding, and all aspects of the body relate to the heart and lungs - - - - - - - - 372
    II. That there is a connection between the will and understanding and the heart and lungs, and consequently a connection between all things of the mind and all things of the body - - - 374
    III. That the will corresponds to the heart - - - - 378
    IV. That the understanding corresponds to the lungs - - - 382
    V. That this connection may be the means of discovering many secrets <note>original: "arcana"</note> concerning the will and understanding, and thus also concerning love and wisdom - - - - - 385
    VI. That a person's mind is their spirit, the spirit is the person, and the body is the outward form through which the mind or spirit feels and acts in the world - - - - - 386
    VII. That the union of a person's spirit with their body occurs through the connection of the will and understanding with the heart and lungs, and their separation occurs when this connection ceases - 390

<meta>This page begins Part I of the treatise, establishing the foundational principle that love is the very essence of human life. It follows the Table of Contents, which outlined the relationship between the heart, lungs, will, and understanding.</meta>

<page-num>1</page-num>
<sig>B</sig>

# ANGELIC WISDOM

### CONCERNING

## THE DIVINE LOVE.

### PART I.

1. **LOVE IS THE LIFE OF MAN.** A person is aware that love exists, but does not understand its nature. They know it exists from how the word is used in common speech, such as when it is said, "Such a person loves me," "the king loves his subjects and subjects love their king," "the husband loves his wife and the mother her children," and the reverse <note>original: "vice versa"</note>. It is also used when it is said that this or that person loves their country, their fellow citizens, or their neighbor; and similarly when it is said of things apart from people that we love this or that thing. Nevertheless, although the word "love" is so commonly used, hardly anyone knows what love actually is. While meditating on it, because one cannot form a clear mental concept of it, he says either that it is nothing real, or that it is merely something that flows in through sight, hearing, touch, and conversation, and thereby affects him. He is completely unaware that love is his very life—not only the general life of his whole body and all his thoughts, but also the life of every specific detail within them. A wise person may perceive this from the following questions: If you remove the affection <note>original: "affection"; in this context, the specific feeling or outward expression that springs from a person's underlying love</note> that comes from love, can you think about anything? Can you do anything? As the affection belonging to love grows cold, do not thought, speech, and action also grow cold? And as it is warmed, are they not also warmed? A wise person perceives this not from a theoretical knowledge that love is the life of man, but from the actual experience of this fact.

2. No one knows what the life of a human being is unless they know it is love. If this is not known, one person may believe that human life consists only in feeling and acting, and another that it consists in thinking; however, thought is actually the first effect of life, and sensation and action are the second. It is said that thought is the first effect of life, but thought exists in different degrees—inner and more

<summary>This page introduces the fundamental concept that love is the essential life-force of a human being, driving all thought and action. It argues that while the word is common, its true nature as the source of vital energy is often misunderstood as a mere sensation.</summary>
<keywords>Divine Love, life of man, affection, Swedenborg, human nature, thought and action</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on how love constitutes the life of man, transitioning from the physical analogy of heat to the theological principle that God alone is Life and Love itself, while angels and humans are recipients of that life.</meta>

<page-num>2—5</page-num>
<header>ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART I.</header>

interior, as well as exterior and even more exterior aspects. Inmost thought, which is a perception of ends <note>original: "ends"; here meaning purposes or goals</note>, is actually the first effect of life. However, these will be discussed later when we address the degrees of life.

3. We can gain some understanding of love as the life of man by looking at the heat of the sun in the natural world. This heat, as we know, is essentially the shared life of all plant life. When heat begins in the springtime, vegetables of all kinds shoot up from the ground, are adorned with leaves, then with flowers, and finally with fruit; in this way, they "live." But when the heat retreats in the autumn and winter seasons, they are stripped of these signs of life and wither. The case is similar with love in man, for love and heat correspond to each other; this is why love is described as being warm.

4. **GOD ALONE, AND THEREFORE THE LORD, IS LOVE ITSELF BECAUSE HE IS LIFE ITSELF; AND ANGELS AND HUMANS ARE RECIPIENTS OF LIFE.** This will be illustrated extensively in the treatises *Divine Providence* and *Life*. Here, we shall only observe that the Lord, who is the God of the universe, is uncreated and infinite, whereas humans and angels are created and finite. Because the Lord is uncreated and infinite, He is Being Itself <note>original: "Esse Itself"</note>, which is called Jehovah, and He is Life Itself, or Life in Himself. No being can be created directly from the uncreated, infinite Being Itself and Life Itself, because the Divine is one and cannot be divided. Instead, beings are created from created and finite substances which are formed so that the Divine may reside within them. Since humans and angels are such beings, they are recipients of life. Therefore, if any person allows themselves to be so misled as to think they are not a recipient of life, but are life itself, they cannot be stopped from thinking they are a god. The feeling that one is life itself, and the belief that follows, is based on a fallacy. This is because in the instrumental cause <note>the tool or means used to perform an action</note>, the principal cause <note>the primary force or agent behind an action</note> is perceived as being one and the same with it. The Lord teaches that He is Life in Himself in the Book of John: "As the Father has life in Himself, so has He given to the Son to have life in Himself" (John 5:26); and "that He is the Life" (John 11:25; 14:6). Now, since life and love are one—as shown in sections 1 and 2—it follows that the Lord, being Life Itself, is Love Itself.

5. In order for this matter to be rightly understood, it is necessary to know that the Lord—being love in its very essence, that is, divine love—appears before the angels in heaven as a sun. Heat and light proceed from that sun. In its essence, the heat proceeding from that sun is love, and the light proceeding from that sun is wisdom. To the degree that angels are recipients of that spiritual heat and light, they are embodiments of love and wisdom; they do not possess love and wisdom from themselves, but from the Lord. This spiritual heat and spiritual light not only flow <note>original: "descend by influx"</note> into the angels and affect them, but also—

...into human beings and affect them in proportion to how they become recipients. They become recipients according to their love for the Lord and their love for their neighbor. This sun, or divine love, cannot create anyone directly from itself through its heat and light; if it did, that person would be love in its very essence—they would be the Lord Himself. Instead, it creates beings from substances and materials formed to receive its heat and light. This is similar to how the sun of our world cannot produce growth in the earth directly with its heat and light; it produces growth from earthly materials where its heat and light can be present to provide life. The fact that the Lord’s divine love appears as a sun in the spiritual world, and that spiritual heat and light proceed from it (giving angels their love and wisdom), can be seen in the work *On Heaven and Hell*, sections 116 to 140.

6. Since a human being is not life itself but only a recipient of life, it follows that a father's role in conception is not the conception of life itself. Instead, it is the conception of the first and purest form capable of receiving life. This serves as a foundation <note>original: "stamen"; Latin for a thread or the warp of a fabric, here referring to a fundamental starting point</note> to which substances and materials are gradually added in the womb. These are organized into forms designed to receive life in their proper order and degree.

7. **That the Divine is not in space.** Although the Divine, or God, is omnipresent—present with every person in the world, every angel in heaven, and every spirit under heaven—this cannot be understood by a purely natural way of thinking. It can, however, be understood by spiritual thought. The reason it cannot be understood by a natural idea is that space is always part of such an idea. Natural thought is formed by things in the material world; in everything we see with our eyes, space is present. Everything in that world, whether large or small, relates to space. Anything with length, breadth, and height has that same connection; in short, space is linked to every measurement, shape, and form in the world of matter. This is why it is said that a purely natural idea cannot grasp that the Divine is not in space, even when we say that the Divine is everywhere. 

Nevertheless, a person can understand this through natural thought if they will only allow some spiritual light into their thinking. Therefore, we should first say something about spiritual ideas and the thinking that comes from them. A spiritual idea does not take anything from space; instead, it draws everything from "state." <note>In this context, "state" refers to the internal quality or condition of a person's mind and heart (their "state of being") rather than a physical location.</note> State is attributed to love, life, wisdom, affections, and the resulting joys—generally speaking, to everything good and true. A truly spiritual idea of these things has nothing in common with space; it is superior to it and sees ideas of space beneath it, just as heaven looks down upon the earth. However, because angels and spirits see with their eyes just as humans do...

<header>7—11 ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART I.</header>
<page-num>4</page-num>

Since physical objects can only be seen within space, the spiritual world—where spirits and angels live—appears to have spaces similar to those on earth. However, these are not actual spaces but *appearances*. They are not *fixed* and *stationary* as they are on earth; they can be lengthened, shortened, changed, and varied. Because they cannot be determined by *measurement*, they cannot be understood in that world through any *natural way of thinking*, but only through a *spiritual way of thinking*. To a spiritual mind, distances of space are nothing other than *distances of goodness* or *distances of truth*, which are the *affinities* <note>meaning: spiritual attractions or connections</note> and *similarities* based on people's inner *states*.

8. From this, it may appear that a person cannot understand how the *Divine* is *everywhere* and yet not in space if they only use a *natural way of thinking*. Yet, angels and spirits clearly understand this. Consequently, a human being can also understand it if they are willing to admit some *spiritual light* into their thoughts. The reason a person can understand it is that the *body* does not think—the *spirit* does. Thus, it is the *spiritual part*, not the *natural part*, that performs the thinking.

9. However, the reason many do not understand this is that they *love what is natural*. Therefore, they will not elevate their *understanding* into *spiritual light*. Those who will not do this cannot think of God except in terms of space; and to think of God in terms of space is to think only of the *physical extension of nature*. It is necessary to establish this point first, because without a knowledge and perception that the Divine is not in space, nothing can be understood about the divine life (which is the *love and wisdom* treated here). Without this, very little—if anything—can be understood concerning *divine providence, omnipresence, omniscience, omnipotence, infinity, and eternity*, which will be discussed in their proper order.

10. It has been said that in the spiritual world, spaces and distances appear just as they do in the natural world. But these are *appearances* according to *spiritual affinities*, which come from *love and wisdom*, or *goodness and truth*. This is why the *Lord*, even though He is everywhere in the heavens with the angels, nevertheless appears high above them as a *sun*. Since the *reception* of love and wisdom creates an *affinity* with Him, those heavens where the angels have a closer affinity through *reception* appear nearer to Him than those where the affinity is more remote. This is also why the *heavens* (of which there are three) are *distinct* from each other, as are the *societies* within each heaven. Likewise, the *hells* beneath them are remote according to how much they *reject* love and wisdom. The same is true for humanity: the Lord is present with every person across the entire globe, and this is solely because the Lord is not limited by space.

The reason for this is that heaven, as a whole and in every part, is in form like a human, and the Divine—

This presence, which exists among the angels, constitutes heaven; and their thoughts proceed according to the form of heaven. Therefore, it is impossible for angels to think of God in any other way. Consequently, everyone in the world who is in connection with heaven thinks of God in this same way when they reflect deep within themselves or in their spirit. Since God is a Man <note>original: "Man." In this context, the author refers to the Divine as the ultimate source of human qualities, form, and personhood, rather than a biological male.</note>, all angels and all spirits are human beings in a perfect form. This is a result of the form of heaven, which is consistent with itself in both its largest and smallest parts. The fact that heaven, as a whole and in every part, is in the form of a man may be seen in the work *Heaven and Hell* (sections 59 to 86); and that thoughts proceed according to the form of heaven is explained in sections 203 and 204. It is known from Genesis 1:26–27 that humans were created in the image and likeness of God; it is also known that God was seen as a Man by Abraham and others. The ancients, from the wisest to the simplest, thought of God only as a Man; and eventually, when they began to worship many gods—as in Athens and Rome—they worshiped them all as humans. What has been said may be illustrated by the following excerpt from a short book published some time ago:

> "The nations outside of Christendom <note>original: "Gentiles"</note>, particularly the Africans, who acknowledge and worship one God as the Creator of the universe, think of God as a Man; they say that no one can have any other idea of God. When they hear that many people imagine God as a small cloud in the middle of the universe, they ask where such people are. When told that there are such people among Christians, they deny that it is even possible. But in response, it is shown that some Christians form such an idea because God is called a 'spirit' in the Word <note>The Bible</note>, and they think of a spirit as nothing more than a thin cloud, not knowing that every spirit and every angel is a human being. Nevertheless, an investigation was made to see if their spiritual concept was similar to their natural concept, and it was found that for those who inwardly acknowledge the Lord as the God of heaven and earth, it was not the same. I heard a certain Christian priest <note>original: "presbyter"</note> say that no one can have any concept of a Divine Humanity. I saw him led to various nations, moving successively to those who were more and more spiritually internal, and from them to their heavens, and finally to the Christian heaven. Everywhere, their inner perception of God was shared with him, and he observed that they had no other idea of God than as a Man, which is the same as the idea of a Divine Humanity."

However, those who consider themselves wiser than the common people declare God to be invisible. They are unable to comprehend how God, as a Man, could have created heaven and earth and filled the universe with His presence. These and other details cannot be correctly understood as long as a person is ignorant of the fact that the Divine is not in space. But those who—

13. The great importance of having a correct idea of God is clear from this consideration: the idea of God forms the innermost thought of everyone who has any religion; for all aspects of religion and divine worship relate to God. Since God is present both generally and specifically in everything belonging to religion and worship, no communication with heaven is possible unless there is a correct idea of God. This is why, in the spiritual world, every nation is assigned its place according to its idea of God as a Man; for it is in this idea, and no other, that the idea of the Lord is truly found. That the state of every person’s life after death depends on the idea of God they have confirmed within themselves is clearly seen from the opposite: the denial of God constitutes hell, and, in the Christian world, the denial of the Lord’s Divinity does the same.

### 14. THAT BEING AND EXISTENCE <note>original: *Esse* and *Existere*</note> IN GOD-MAN ARE DISTINCTLY ONE.

Where there is Being <note>original: *Esse*, the essential "to be"</note>, there is also Existence <note>original: *Existere*, the outward manifestation</note>; one is not possible without the other. Being exists through Existence, and not without it. Reason understands this when it considers whether there can be any Being that does not exist, or whether there can be any Existence except from a Being. Since the one occurs with and not without the other, it follows that they are one, but "distinctly one." They are distinctly one in the same way as love and wisdom; for love is also Being and wisdom is Existence, since love does not exist except in wisdom, nor wisdom except from love. Therefore, when love is in wisdom, it exists. These two are so much a single thing that they may be distinguished in thought, but not in action. Because they can be distinguished in thought but not in action, it is said they are "distinctly one." Being and Existence in God-Man are also distinctly one, like soul and body: the soul does not exist without its body, nor the body without its soul. The divine soul of God-Man is understood as the divine Being, and His divine body as the divine Existence. To think that the soul can exist and exercise thought and wisdom without the body is an error resulting from false appearances; the soul of every person is in a spiritual body after they have cast off the material coverings they carried about with them in the world.

15. A Being is not a Being unless it exists, because it is not in a form; and what is not in a form has no quality, and what has no quality is nothing. Whatever exists from a Being makes one with that Being, because it comes from the Being; this creates a union into one. Therefore, one belongs to the other mutually and reciprocally, and one is "all in all" in the other as if in itself.

16. From this, it may appear that God is a Man, and that by being so...

### 17. THAT WITHIN THE GOD-MAN, INFINITE THINGS ARE DISTINCTLY ONE.

It is well known that God is infinite, for He is called infinite; but He is called so because He truly is infinite. He is not infinite simply because He is essential Being <note>original: *Esse*</note> and Existence <note>original: *Existere*</note> in Himself, but because infinite things are contained within Him. An "infinite" without infinite things within itself is not truly infinite, except in name only. These infinite things within Him cannot be described as "infinitely many" or "infinitely all" because of our natural concept of "many" and "all." The idea of "infinitely many" is limited, and the idea of "infinitely all," while unlimited in theory, is still derived from the limited things we see in the universe. Therefore, since human ideas are natural, a person cannot—even through the highest refinement of thought—reach a true perception of the infinite things in God. An angel, however, whose ideas are spiritual, may through such refinement be elevated above the level of a human, yet even they cannot reach the Divine essence itself.

18. Anyone who believes that God is a Man <note>This refers to the "Divine Humanity," the idea that God possesses the perfect, infinite form of which human beings are a finite reflection.</note> can affirm within themselves that there are infinite things in God. If He is a Man, He has a body and everything belonging to it; thus, He has a face, a chest, an abdomen, loins, and feet, for without these He would not be a man. Having these, He also has eyes, ears, nostrils, a mouth, and a tongue, as well as the internal organs like the heart, the lungs, and their connected systems—all of which, taken together, make a man a man. In a created human being, these components are many and their structures are innumerable; but in the God-Man, they are infinite, with nothing lacking. This is why He possesses infinite perfection. A comparison is made here between the uncreated Man, who is God, and created man, because God is a Man; as it is said in the first chapter of Genesis, man in this world was "created after His image and according to His likeness" (Genesis 1:26–27).

19. That there are infinite things in God is even more apparent to the angels in the heavens where they live. The entire heaven, consisting of millions upon millions of angels, is—in its collective form—like a single man. The same is true for all societies in heaven, whether large or small. Because of this, even an individual angel is a man, for an angel is heaven in its smallest form. That this is the case may be seen in the work ***ON HEAVEN AND HELL*** (sections 51 to 87). The form of heaven—as a whole, in its parts, and in its individuals—exists because of the Divine nature that the angels receive. In the same proportion that an angel receives the Divine, he becomes a man in perfect form. This is why angels are said to be in God, and God in them, and why God is "all in all" for them. It is impossible to describe the innumerable things in heaven; and since the Divine constitutes heaven, those inexpressibly many things...

...are from the Divine, it is evident that there are infinite things in the Essential Man <note>original: "Very Man," meaning the absolute or true prototype of humanity</note>, who is God.

20. The same conclusion may be formed from the created universe, when it is regarded with a view to uses <note>In this context, "uses" refers to the functional purposes or beneficial services that every part of creation performs.</note> and their correspondences. However, before this can be understood, some things must be explained first as an illustration.

21. Since there are infinite things in the God-Man—which appear in the heavens, in angels, and in men as if in a mirror—and since the God-Man is not limited by space (as was shown above, sections 7, 8, 9, 10), it may in some degree be seen and understood how God may be omnipresent, omniscient, and all-providing. It also shows how, as a Man, He could create all things, and can, as a man, preserve the things He created in their proper order for eternity.

22. That infinite things are distinctly one in the God-Man may also appear evident in a human being as if in a mirror. In a person, there are many and countless parts, as was said above, but a person still perceives them as a single whole. From physical sensation, one does not know anything about their brain, heart and lungs, liver, spleen, and pancreas; nor of the countless things in the eyes, ears, tongue, stomach, reproductive organs, and the rest. As a person does not know these things through the senses, they seem to themselves to be a single unit. The reason is that all those things are in such a form that not one of them can be missing; for a human being is a form designed to receive life from the God-Man, as was shown above in sections 4, 5, and 6. The order and connection of all these parts in such a form produces a feeling and an idea as if they were not many and countless things, but a single one. From this, it may be concluded that the many and countless things which constitute a single whole in a person are distinctly—indeed, most distinctly—one in the Essential Man <note>original: "Very Man"</note>, who is God.

### 23. **THAT THERE IS ONE GOD-MAN, FROM WHOM ALL THINGS EXIST.**

All the principles of human reason agree, and as it were converge on this point: that there is one God, the Creator of the universe. Therefore, a rational person, by virtue of the universal capacity of understanding, thinks this way and can think no other way. Tell any person of sound reason that there are two creators of the universe, and you will find a resistance arising within yourself, possibly from the mere sound of the words in your ear. From this, it is evident that all the principles of human reason join and converge in this: **GOD IS ONE**. 

There are two reasons why this is so. *Firstly*, the faculty of thinking rationally, viewed in itself, is not the human being's own, but is God’s presence within them. Human reason depends on this common foundation, and this common foundation causes the mind to see, as if by its own power, that God is one. *Secondly*, a person, by means of that faculty, either exists in the light of heaven or derives the common basis of their thought from it; and it is a universal truth of the light of heaven that God is one. The case is different if a person, by using that faculty, has corrupted the lower parts of their understanding...

[...continuing from the previous page:] such a person indeed possesses the faculty <note>original: "faculty," meaning a mental power or ability.</note>, but by the inward twisting <note>original: "intorsion," referring to a turning away from the truth or toward lower, selfish interests.</note> of their lower nature, they turn it another way, and their reason becomes unsound.

24. Every person, although they are unaware of it, thinks of a collective group of people as if they were a single person. This is why we immediately understand what is meant when it is said that a king is the head and his subjects are the body, or when it is said that this or that person performs such-and-such a role in the public body—that is, in the kingdom. The case is the same with the spiritual body as it is with the civil body: the spiritual body is the church, and its head is the God-Man <note>The author uses "God-Man" (Divine Human) to express that God is the absolute essence and source of all that is truly human.</note>. From this perspective, it is clear how the church would appear as a single person. However, if one thought of several gods instead of one—the Creator and Sustainer of the universe—the church would appear not like a man, but like a monster. It would look like one body with several heads. Even if it were said that those heads have one essence <note>original: "essence," meaning a shared fundamental nature or substance.</note> and together make one head, the only idea that could result is that of one head with several faces, or several heads with one face. Consequently, in such a view, the church would be seen as deformed. In reality, one God is the head and the church is the body, which acts under the control of the head and not on its own, just as it is with a human being. This is also why there is only one king in a kingdom: for more than one would divide and disrupt it, whereas one can preserve it in unity.

25. The situation is similar in the church spread over the whole world, which is called a communion <note>original: "communion," referring to the universal community of believers.</note> because, like one body, it is under one head. It is well known that the head governs and controls the body beneath it; for the understanding and the will reside in the head, and the body is moved by that understanding and will, so much so that the body is nothing but obedience. The body cannot act at all except from the understanding and will in the head; nor can a person of the church act at all except from God. It *appears* as if the body acts on its own—as if the hands and feet move by themselves when they act, and as if the mouth and tongue move by themselves when they speak—when in fact they do not do so at all, but move from the desire <note>original: "affection"</note> of the will and the resulting thought of the understanding in the head. Consider then: if one body had several heads, and each head was free to decide for itself based on its own understanding and will, could the body survive? Unanimity <note>original: "unanimity," meaning complete agreement or being of one mind.</note>, which exists under one head, is impossible in such a case. As it is in the church, so it is in the heavens, which consist of millions upon millions <note>original: "myriads of myriads"</note> of angels. Unless every single one of them looked toward one God, they would fall away from one another and heaven would be dissolved. Therefore, if an angel of heaven even thinks of a plurality of gods, they are immediately separated; they are cast to the furthest boundary of heaven and fall down.

26. Since the universal heaven and everything in it have...

27. What person of sound reason does not perceive that the Divine cannot be divided? Also, that it is impossible for there to be a plurality of Infinites <note>original: "Infinites," referring to beings that are boundless and eternal.</note>, Uncreated beings, Omnipotents, and Gods? If someone without reason should say that several Infinites, Uncreated beings, Omnipotents, and Gods are possible, provided they share the same essence <note>original: "essence," meaning the fundamental nature or substance of a thing.</note>, and that this makes them one infinite, uncreated, and almighty God—is not one and the same essence the same identity? And a single identity cannot be shared among many. If it were said that one comes from the other, then the one who comes from the other is not God in Himself; yet God in Himself is the God from whom all things exist. See above, number 16.

### 28. **THAT THE DIVINE ESSENCE ITSELF IS LOVE AND WISDOM.**

If you gather all the things you know and examine them with your mind, and ask yourself in a moment of spiritual reflection what the universal principle of them all is, you cannot conclude anything else than that it is love and wisdom. These two principles are the essentials of everything in human life. All civil, moral, and spiritual matters belonging to a person depend on these two, and without them, they are nothing. The same is true for human life in its collective state, which—as previously mentioned—is a larger or smaller society, a kingdom or empire, the church, and also the angelic heaven. Take away love and wisdom from them, and consider if they are anything at all; you will discover that without these as the basis of their existence, they are nothing.

29. No one can deny that in God there is love and, at the same time, wisdom in their very essence; for He loves everyone from the love within Himself and guides everyone from the wisdom within Himself. Furthermore, when viewed through the principle of order, the created universe is so full of wisdom grounded in love that it may be said that all things in their entirety are wisdom itself. Uncountable <note>original: "indefinite," here meaning things so numerous they cannot be counted, rather than vague or uncertain.</note> things exist in such order—both one after another and all at once—that together they form a single whole. It is for this reason, and no other, that they can be held together and preserved forever.

30. Because the Divine Essence itself is love and wisdom, a human being has two faculties <note>original: "faculties," meaning inherent mental powers or capacities.</note> of life: one from which they have understanding, and the other from which they have will. The faculty of understanding derives everything it has from the influx <note>original: "influx," the spiritual flow or influence from God into the human mind.</note> of wisdom from God; and the faculty of will derives everything it has from the influx of love from God. The fact that a person may not be truly wise or may not exercise their love rightly does not take away these faculties, but only closes them up within. And so long...

When these faculties are closed up from within, the understanding and will are still called by those names, but in their essence, they are no longer truly such. Therefore, if those faculties were taken away, everything human would vanish, for being human consists of thinking and speaking from thought, and in willing and acting from the will. Thus, it is clear that the Divine resides within a person in these two faculties: the capacity to be wise and the capacity to love (that is, in the potential to do so). I have learned through much experience—which you may read about in detail elsewhere—that humans possess a power of loving even when they are not wise or do not love as they should.

31. Because the Divine Essence itself is love and wisdom, everything in the universe relates to goodness and truth. This is because everything that comes from love is called "good," and everything that comes from wisdom is called "truth." More will be said on this later.

32. Because the Divine Essence is love and wisdom, the universe and everything in it—both living and non-living—exist through heat and light. This is because heat corresponds to love, and light corresponds to wisdom. Therefore, spiritual heat is actually love, and spiritual light is actually wisdom. More will be said on this as well.

33. All human feelings <note>original: "affections"</note> and thoughts come from the Divine Love and the Divine Wisdom; feelings come from Divine Love, and thoughts from Divine Wisdom. Every single thing belonging to a person is nothing more than feeling and thought; these two principles are the sources of everything in their life. All the joys and pleasures of life are derived from them: joys come from the feelings of their love, and pleasures from the thoughts grounded in that love. Since human beings were created to be receivers—and they are receivers only as much as they love God and gain wisdom from that love (that is, as much as they are moved by things from God and think from that feeling)—it follows that the Divine Essence from which everything was created is Divine Love and Divine Wisdom.

### 34. THAT DIVINE LOVE BELONGS TO DIVINE WISDOM, AND DIVINE WISDOM BELONGS TO DIVINE LOVE.

It was shown above (numbers 14 to 16) that the Divine Being <note>original Latin: *esse*</note> and the Divine Manifestation <note>original Latin: *existere*</note> in the God-Man are "distinctly one." Since the Divine Being is Divine Love, and the Divine Manifestation is Divine Wisdom, these two are likewise "distinctly one." They are called "distinctly one" because love and wisdom are two distinct things, yet they are so united that love belongs to wisdom and wisdom belongs to love. Love exists *within* wisdom, and wisdom comes into being *within* love. As it was said earlier (number 14), because wisdom derives its manifestation from love, Divine Wisdom is also a form of "being." It follows from this that love and wisdom, taken together, are the Divine Being <note>original Latin: *esse*</note>; but when viewed separately, love is called the Divine Being, and wisdom the...

# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART I.

Divine Existence <note>original: "divine existere"</note>. Such is the idea the angels have concerning the Divine Love and the Divine Wisdom.

35. Since there is such a union of love and wisdom, and of wisdom and love, in the God-Man, the Divine Essence is one. For the Divine Essence is Divine Love because it is of Divine Wisdom, and it is Divine Wisdom because it is of Divine Love. Since there is such a union of these principles, the Divine Life is also one. Life is the Divine Essence. Divine Love and Divine Wisdom are a single whole because their union is reciprocal, and a reciprocal union creates unity. However, more will be said about reciprocal union elsewhere.

36. There is also a union of love and wisdom in every divine work, by virtue of which it has perpetuity—indeed, eternity. If there were more Divine Love than Divine Wisdom, or more Divine Wisdom than Divine Love, in any created work, it could not continue to exist, except so far as their influence was equal; whatever exceeds such equality passes away.

37. Divine Providence, in the work of reforming, regenerating, and saving people, shares equally in Divine Love and Divine Wisdom. A person cannot be reformed, regenerated, and saved from more Divine Love than Divine Wisdom, or from more Divine Wisdom than Divine Love. Divine Love wills to save all; yet it cannot save them except by Divine Wisdom. All the laws by which salvation is effected belong to Divine Wisdom, and Love cannot go beyond those laws, because Divine Love and Divine Wisdom are one and act in union.

38. In the Word <note>The author uses "the Word" to refer to the Bible</note>, Divine Love and Divine Wisdom are understood by "justice" and "judgment"—Divine Love is represented by justice, and Divine Wisdom by judgment. For this reason, in the Word, justice and judgment are attributed to God; as in the Psalms:

> “Justice and judgment are the support of Your throne,” Psalm 97:2.
> “The Lord shall bring forth your justice as the light, and your judgment as the noon-day,” Psalm 37:6.

The same is understood by "life" and "light" in John:

> “In Him was life, and the life was the light of men,” 1:4.

"Life" in this passage means the Divine Love of the Lord, and "light" means His Divine Wisdom. The same is also meant by "life" and "spirit" in John:

> “Jesus said, the words which I speak to you, they are spirit, and they are life,” 6:63.

<header>PART I. THE DIVINE LOVE. 39—41</header>

39. In a human being, love and wisdom appear to be two separate things, but they are still distinctly one within themselves. This is because a person's wisdom is exactly like their love, and their love is exactly like their wisdom. Wisdom that does not act as one with its love may appear to be wisdom, but it is not; and love that does not act as one with its wisdom may appear to be the love of wisdom, but it is not. This is because each derives its essence and life from the other reciprocally. The reason why wisdom and love in a person appear to be two separate things is that the faculty of understanding can be elevated into the light of heaven, while the faculty of loving cannot—unless the person actually does what they understand. Therefore, that appearance of wisdom which is not united with the love of wisdom eventually collapses back into whatever love it *is* united with—which might be a love of insanity rather than wisdom. For a person may know through wisdom that they ought to do one thing or another, yet still not do it because they do not love it. However, to the extent that a person acts from love upon what wisdom teaches, they are an image of God.

40. **THAT THE DIVINE LOVE AND THE DIVINE WISDOM ARE A SUBSTANCE AND A FORM.** The common idea people have regarding love and wisdom is that they are something volatile and floating in subtle air or ether, or perhaps an exhalation from something of that kind. Hardly anyone thinks that they are really and actually a substance and a form. Even those who do see that they are a substance and a form still perceive love and wisdom as something coming *out* of a subject <note>In philosophy, a "subject" is the underlying entity or thing in which qualities or attributes exist.</note> and issuing from it. What they perceive as issuing from the subject—even if they call it a substance and a form—they still think of as something volatile and floating. They do not realize that love and wisdom are the subject itself, and that what is perceived outside of it as something floating is only an appearance of the state of the subject within itself. There are several reasons why this has not been seen before. One is that appearances are the first things the human mind uses to form its understanding, and it cannot shake them off except by investigating the cause. If the cause lies very deep, the mind cannot investigate it without keeping the understanding in "spiritual light" for some time—something it cannot do for long because "natural life" continually pulls the mind back down. Nevertheless, the truth is that love and wisdom are a real and actual substance and form, and they constitute the subject itself.

41. But since this contradicts appearances, it may not seem to deserve belief unless it is demonstrated. Since it can only be demonstrated through things a person can perceive with their physical senses, it shall be demonstrated through them. A person has five senses, which are touch <note>original: "feeling"</note>, taste, smell, hearing, and sight. The subject of touch is the skin that encompasses a person; the substance and form of the skin cause it to feel...

<meta>This page continues the philosophical argument that love and wisdom are not abstract concepts, but are actual substances and forms, using the physical senses as an analogy for how internal perception works.</meta>

<page-num>41, 42</page-num>

<header>ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART I.</header>

...to which it is applied. The sense of feeling does not exist in the things being felt, but in the substance and form of the skin, which is the subject; the sense is simply an affection <note>original: "affection"; here meaning a state or change produced in the sense organ by an external force</note> of the skin caused by the things applied to it. It is the same with the taste; this sense is only an affection of the substance and form of the tongue; the tongue is the subject. It is the same with the smell; it is well known that odors affect the nose, that they are in the nose, and that the sense is an affection of the nose caused by odor-producing substances touching it. It is the same with the hearing; it appears as if the hearing were in the place where the sound begins, but hearing is actually in the ear and is an affection of its substance and form; the idea that hearing happens at a distance from the ear is only an appearance. 

It is the same with the sight; when a person sees objects at a distance, it appears as if the sight were actually there at the object, but nevertheless, it is in the eye—which is the subject—and is likewise an affection of the eye. Distance is only a conclusion reached by our judgment, which calculates space based on intermediate objects or from the shrinking and consequent blurring of the object, whose image is produced within the eye according to the angle of incidence <note>The angle at which light hits the eye</note>. Therefore, it is clear that sight does not travel from the eye to the object, but rather the image of the object enters the eye and affects its substance and form. It is the same with sight as it is with hearing; hearing does not go out of the ear to catch a sound, but the sound enters the ear and affects it. From this, it appears that the affection of a substance and form, which constitutes a sense, is not something separate from the subject; it only causes a change in that subject, while the subject remains the same before, during, and after. It follows, then, that sight, hearing, smell, taste, and feeling are not some "volatile" <note>original: "volatile"; meaning something fleeting, airy, or unstable</note> thing flowing out of those organs, but are the organs themselves considered in their substance and form; while those organs are affected, the sense is produced.

42. It is the same with love and wisdom, with this only difference: the substances and forms which constitute love and wisdom are not visible to the eyes like the organs of the external senses. Yet, no one can deny that the elements of wisdom and love—which are called thoughts, perceptions, and affections—are substances and forms. They are not fleeting entities emerging from nothing, nor are they abstracted from that real and actual substance and form which is their subject. For there are in the brain innumerable substances and forms in which every interior sense—related to both the understanding and the will—resides. All the affections, perceptions, and thoughts there are not "exhalations" from these substances; they are actually and really the subjects themselves. These subjects do not emit anything from themselves, but only undergo changes according to the influences that affect them, as is clearly shown by what has been said above concerning the physical senses. Of the influences (*alluentia*) <note>original: *alluentia*; a Latin term for things flowing toward or reaching the subject</note> which affect them, more will be said below.

# PART I. THE DIVINE LOVE. 43—46

43. From this, it can first be seen that divine love and divine wisdom are, in themselves, a substance and a form. This is because they are Being <note>original: "Esse"</note> itself and Existence <note>original: "Existere"</note> itself. If they were not such a Being and Existence—that is, if they were not a substance and a form—they would only be an imaginary idea, which is actually nothing in itself.

**44. THAT THE DIVINE LOVE AND THE DIVINE WISDOM ARE SUBSTANCE AND FORM IN THEMSELVES, AND ARE THEREFORE THE SELF-SUBSISTING AND SOLE-SUBSISTING BEING <note>original: *Ipsum et Unicum*</note>.** It has been proven above that divine love and divine wisdom are a substance and a form, and it has also been shown that the Divine Being and Existence <note>original: "Esse" and "Existere"</note> are Being and Existence in themselves. It cannot be said that they are Being and Existence *from* themselves, because that would imply a beginning, or that they originated from something else within them. Rather, the true Being and Existence in itself is from eternity. True Being and Existence in itself is also uncreated, and no created thing can exist except from what is uncreated. Furthermore, what is created is finite, and the finite cannot exist except from the infinite.

45. Anyone who can think deeply enough to conceive of and understand a Being and Existence in itself will perfectly understand that such Being and Existence is the self-subsisting and sole-subsisting Being. That which alone exists is called "self-subsisting" <note>original: *ipsum*</note>, and that from which every other thing originates is called "sole-subsisting" <note>original: *unicum*</note>. Now, since the self-subsisting and sole-subsisting Being is a substance and a form, it follows that it is the self-subsisting and sole-subsisting substance and form. And since this very substance and form is divine love and divine wisdom, it follows that it is the self-subsisting and sole-subsisting love, and the self-subsisting and sole-subsisting wisdom. Consequently, it is the self-subsisting and sole-subsisting essence, and also the self-subsisting and sole-subsisting life; for love and wisdom are life.

46. This shows how shallowly <note>original: "sensually," meaning thinking based only on the physical senses</note> people think about spiritual things when they claim that nature exists by its own power. Such people think only from their bodily senses and the darkness they cause. They think using only their eyes and cannot think using their understanding. Thinking from the eye closes the understanding, but thinking from the understanding opens the eye. They cannot conceive of a Being and Existence in itself that is eternal, uncreated, and infinite. Neither can they think of life as anything other than a fleeting thing that vanishes into nothing; they think the same of love and wisdom. They are completely unable to see that all things in nature derive their existence from these sources. Nor can they see that all things in nature exist from that source unless nature is viewed in terms of "uses" <note>original: "uses"; in this context, the purpose or functional benefit for which a thing is created</note>—its purposes and functions—in their proper sequence and order, rather than just looking at the physical forms that the eye sees. This is because uses come only from life, and their sequence and order come from wisdom and love; forms are simply the containers of those uses. Therefore, if the forms only

46. ...are regarded, nothing of life can be seen in nature, much less anything of love and wisdom, and consequently nothing of God.

47. **THAT DIVINE LOVE AND DIVINE WISDOM MUST NECESSARILY EXIST IN OTHER BEINGS OR EXISTENCES CREATED FROM THEMSELVES.** It is an essential quality of love not to love itself, but to love others and to be joined to them through love. It is also essential to love to be loved by others, because it is through this that a connection <note>original: "conjunction"</note> is formed. The essence of all love consists in this connection—indeed, the very life of love consists in what we call enjoyment, pleasure, delight, sweetness, blessedness <note>original: "beatitude"</note>, happiness, and joy <note>original: "felicity"</note>. Love consists in our wanting what is ours to belong to another, and feeling their delight as if it were our own. This is what it means to love. However, for a person to feel their own delight in another, rather than the other’s delight in themselves, is not love. In the latter case, he loves himself, but in the former, he loves his neighbor. These two types of love are diametrically opposite to each other. Both do create a connection, and it might not seem that a man loving his own interests in another—that is, loving himself—causes a separation. Yet it separates them so thoroughly that the more someone has loved another in this way, the more he later hates them. This connection eventually dissolves on its own, and then such love turns into an equal degree of hatred.

48. Anyone capable of looking into the essence of love can see that this is true. For what is it for a man to love only himself, and not anyone outside of himself who might love him back? This is dissolution rather than connection. The connection of love arises from reciprocation, and reciprocation cannot happen within oneself alone. If it seems to exist there, it is only because of an imaginary reciprocation from others. Therefore, it is clear that divine love must exist in other beings whom it loves and by whom it is loved. Since this quality exists in all love, it must exist in the highest degree—that is, infinitely—in Love itself.

49. Regarding God, it is not possible for Him to love and be loved in return by other beings who possess anything of the infinite, or anything of the essence and life of love in itself—that is, anything of the Divine. If they had anything infinite or Divine in them, then He would not be loving others, but would be loving Himself; for the Infinite, or the Divine, is one. If this existed in others, it would be itself, and God would be practicing self-love, of which not even the smallest amount is possible in Him. This is because self-love is completely opposite to the divine essence. Therefore, this mutual exchange of love must take place between God and other beings in whom there is nothing of the self-existent Divine. That this exchange takes place in beings created from the Divine will be seen below. But for this to exist, there must be infinite wisdom, which must be one with infinite love; that is, there must exist...

50. Understanding and knowing this <term>secret</term> <note>original: "arcanum"</note> is essential for understanding how everything was created and how God continues to sustain it. 51. I urge you, however, not to confuse these ideas with the concepts of time and space. This is because the Divine does not exist within time and space—a point that will be made clear later in this work, particularly when discussing eternity, infinity, and omnipresence.

52. **THAT EVERYTHING IN THE UNIVERSE WAS CREATED FROM THE DIVINE LOVE AND DIVINE WISDOM OF THE GOD-MAN.** The universe, from its largest structures to its smallest particles, and from its initial causes to its final effects, is so filled with divine love and divine wisdom that it can be described as an image of that love and wisdom. This is evident from the "correspondence" <note>Swedenborg uses the term "correspondence" to describe a direct, symbolic, and functional link between the spiritual world and the natural world.</note> of everything in the universe with everything in a human being. Each and every thing in the created universe corresponds so closely to parts of a person that it can be said that a human being is a miniature universe. 

Specifically, there is a correspondence between a person’s emotions <note>original: "affections"</note> (and the resulting thoughts) and everything in the animal kingdom. There is a correspondence between their will (and the resulting understanding) and everything in the vegetable kingdom. Finally, there is a correspondence between the most basic levels of their life and everything in the mineral kingdom. 

This correspondence is not obvious to anyone in the natural world, but it is clear to everyone in the spiritual world who pays attention to it. In that world, all the things found in the three kingdoms of nature exist as well. They are the visible correspondences of the emotions and thoughts—of the will and understanding—of the people who live there. These things appear around them just as the universe appears around us, though on a smaller scale. 

Because of this, it is obvious to the angels that the created universe is an image representing the God-Man, and that His love and wisdom are reflected in the universe. This does not mean the universe *is* the God-Man, but rather that it comes *from* Him. Nothing in the created universe contains substance, form, life, love, or wisdom within itself. Even a human being is not a human being by their own power. Instead, everything comes from God, who is the only being who possesses substance, form, love, wisdom, and life in and of Himself. That which exists in itself is uncreated and infinite. However, whatever comes from that source—having nothing within it that exists purely of itself—is created and finite. This finite creation represents the image of the One from whom it originated and exists.

53. We can say that created and finite things possess being <term>being</term> <note>original: "esse"</note> and existence <term>existence</term> <note>original: "existere"</note>, as well as substance, form, and life—even wisdom and love. However, all these qualities in them are created and finite. This does not mean that created and finite things are divine in themselves; rather, it means they are *in* the Divine and the Divine is *in them*. In and of themselves, all created things are inanimate and dead; they are only animated and brought to life because the Divine is in them and they are in the Divine.

54. The Divine is not different in one subject than it is in another; rather, it is the created subjects themselves that differ. No two things are exactly the same, and therefore each thing serves as a different container <note>original: "continent"</note> through which the Divine appears in various ways according to its own image. His presence in opposite things <note>referring to evil or non-receptive subjects</note> will be discussed in the following sections.

55. ### THAT ALL THINGS IN THE CREATED UNIVERSE ARE RECIPIENTS OF THE DIVINE LOVE AND THE DIVINE WISDOM OF GOD-MAN.

It is well known that every single thing in the universe was created by God; therefore, in the Word <note>The Bible</note>, the universe and everything within it is called "the work of the hands of Jehovah." It is commonly said that the world in its totality was created out of nothing, and people often imagine this "nothing" as absolute non-existence. However, nothing can be made out of absolute nothing. This is a self-evident truth. Therefore, the universe—which is an image of God and is thus full of God—could only be created *in* God and *from* God. Because God is Being <note>original: "Esse"</note> itself, anything that exists must come from that Being. To suggest that something exists which was created from "nothing" (which does not exist) is a complete contradiction.

Nevertheless, what is created in God from God is not a continuous extension of Himself. This is because God is Being in itself, whereas in created things there is nothing that is Being in itself. If there were any "being in itself" within created things, they would be a continuous part of God, and whatever is a continuous part of God *is* God. 

The angels explain this by saying that what is created in God from God is like something a man produces from his own life, but from which the life has been withdrawn. It is shaped by his life, yet it is not the life itself. The angels confirm this through many examples from their heaven, where they say they are in God and God is in them, yet they possess nothing of God—that is, nothing that is actually God—within their own being. More evidence for this will be presented later; for now, it is enough to know this principle.

56. Because of this origin, every created thing is designed to be a recipient of God. This happens not through "continuity" (being an extension of God's substance) but through "contiguity" <note>Contiguity here refers to being distinct from God but in close contact or connection with Him.</note>. Through this contact, rather than extension, a principle of connection is formed. This connection exists because the subject was created in God from God; therefore, there is a shared harmony <note>original: "analogous principle"</note> between them. By means of this connection, the created thing acts like an image of God reflected in a mirror.

57. This is why angels are not angels because of anything in themselves, but because of this connection with the God-Man; and this connection...

[Recep]tion is according to their reception of divine good and divine truth, which are God, and appear to proceed from Him, although they are in Him. Their reception is according to their application of the laws of order (which are divine truths) to themselves, through their free power of thinking and willing according to the reason which they possess from the Lord as if it were their own. By this means, there is a reception of divine good and divine truth as if from themselves, and thereby there is a reciprocation of love; for, as was said above, love does not exist unless it is reciprocal. The same is true for people on earth. From what has been said, it may now first be seen that all things in the created universe are recipients of the divine love and divine wisdom of the God-Man.

58. That the other things in the universe, which are not like angels and men, are also recipients of the divine love and divine wisdom of the God-Man—such as things inferior to man in the animal kingdom, things inferior to these in the vegetable kingdom, and things inferior to these in the mineral kingdom—cannot yet be fully explained to the understanding. First, more must be said concerning the degrees of life and the degrees of the recipients of life. Conjunction with these things is according to their uses; for all good uses derive their origin from no other source than by a similar conjunction with God, though it is dissimilar according to their degrees. In its successive descent, this conjunction becomes such that there is no free will because there is no reason, and hence there is no appearance of life in them; but they are still recipients. Since they are recipients, they are also re-agents; for they are containers <term>containers</term> <note>original: "continents," meaning that which holds or provides a basis for something else</note> because they are re-agents. Conjunction with good uses will be discussed after the origin of evil is shown.

59. From this it may appear that the Divine is in each and every thing of the created universe, and consequently that the created universe is "the work of the hands of Jehovah," as it is called in the Word <note>The Bible</note>—that is, the work of divine love and divine wisdom, for these are meant by the "hands of Jehovah." And although the Divine is in each and every thing of the created universe, there is still nothing of the Divine itself in their being <term>being</term> <note>original: "esse"</note>; for the created universe is not God, but is from God. Being from God, His image is in it, like the image of a man in a mirror: in the mirror the man indeed appears, but still there is nothing of the man actually in it.

60. I heard several people in conversation around me in the spiritual world say that they were willing to acknowledge that the Divine is in each and every thing of the universe, because they see the wonderful works of God there; and the more deeply those works are examined, the more wonderful they appear. Nevertheless, when they heard that the Divine actually *is* in each and every thing of the created universe, they were indignant—a proof that they assert this in words, but do not truly believe it. The same might be seen also from every animal, even the most diminutive, as having organs of the senses, a brain, heart, lungs, and so forth, with arteries, veins, fibers, muscles, and consequent acts, besides the surprising instincts peculiar to each, concerning which whole volumes have been written. ## 61. ALL CREATED THINGS REPRESENT MAN IN A CERTAIN IMAGE. 

This may appear from everything in the animal kingdom, from everything in the vegetable <term>vegetable kingdom</term> <note>In this period, "vegetable" refers to the entire plant kingdom, including trees and flowers.</note> kingdom, and from everything in the mineral kingdom. 

*The relation to man in everything of the animal kingdom* is evident from the following considerations: that animals of all kinds have members by which they move, organs by which they feel, and internal organs by which they operate, which are common to them and to men. They also have appetites and affections similar to the natural appetites and affections in man; and they have innate <note>original: "connate"</note> knowledge corresponding to their affections, in some of which there appears, as it were, something spiritual, which is more or less evident in the beasts of the earth, the birds of heaven, bees, silkworms, ants, and so on. This is why merely natural men liken the living things of that kingdom to themselves, except in regards to speech. 

*The relation to man from everything of the vegetable kingdom* is evident from the following considerations: that they exist from seed, and from there proceed successively through their several stages; that they have something similar to marriage, followed by reproduction <note>original: "prolification"</note>; that their vegetative soul is "use" <term>use</term> <note>In Swedenborg's philosophy, "use" refers to the functional purpose or service a thing provides in the divine order.</note>, of which they are forms, besides many other things which are relations to man, which have also been described by some authors. 

*The relation to man from everything of the mineral kingdom* appears only in the effort to produce forms which represent themselves—which are, as has been said, everything in the vegetable kingdom—and thereby performing uses. For as soon as a seed falls into the bosom of the earth, the earth cherishes it, and provides it supplies from all sides, so that it may germinate and show itself in a form representative of man. That such an effort exists also in its dry parts is evident from corals at the bottom of the sea, and from "flowers" in mines, produced there from minerals and metals. The

PART I. THE DIVINE LOVE.

...effort to grow <note>original: "vegetate"</note>, and thereby to become useful, is the final <term>ultimate</term> <note>In this context, "ultimate" refers to the most basic or final level of physical reality.</note> principle derived from the Divine in created things.

62. Just as there is an effort in the minerals of the earth to grow into plants, so there is an effort in plants to bring themselves to life; from this arise various kinds of insects that correspond to the plants' fragrant vapors. That this is not an effect of the heat of the world's sun alone, but an effect of life acting through that heat according to the nature of those receiving it, will be explained in what follows.

63. That there is a relation to humanity in all things of the created universe may indeed be known from what has been presented, but it can only be seen vaguely; however, in the spiritual world, it is seen clearly. In that world, there are also things from the three kingdoms, in the midst of which is the angel. The angel sees these things all around him and knows that they are representations of himself; indeed, when the deepest level of his understanding is opened, he recognizes himself and sees his own image in them as if in a mirror.

64. From these and many other similar examples, which there is no room to cite here, it may be known for certain that God is a Human <note>original: "Man," referring to the Divine Human or the archetype of humanity.</note>, and that the created universe is an image of Him; for all things have a general relation to Him, just as they have a specific relation to humanity.

65. **THAT THE USES OF ALL CREATED THINGS ASCEND BY DEGREES FROM FINAL THINGS TO MAN, AND THROUGH MAN TO GOD THE CREATOR, FROM WHOM THEY ORIGINATED.**

*Final things* <note>original: "Ultimates"</note>, as has been said above, include everything in the mineral kingdom. These are material substances of various kinds, such as stony, salty, oily, mineral, and metallic substances, covered over with earth that consists of plant and animal matter reduced to the finest powder. Within these materials resides the end goal, and also the beginning, of all the uses that come from life. The end goal of all uses is the effort to produce them, and the beginning is the power acting from that effort. These belong to the mineral kingdom.

*Intermediate things* <note>original: "Mediates (media)"</note> include everything in the vegetable kingdom, such as grasses and herbs, plants and shrubs, and trees of all kinds. These are used by everything in the animal kingdom, both the simple and the complex. They nourish them, delight them, and give them life. They nourish their bodies with their materials, delight their senses with their taste, smell, and beauty, and give life to their emotions. An effort to do these things exists within them from the principle of life.

*Primary things* <note>original: "Primaries (prima)"</note> include everything in the animal kingdom. The lowest in this kingdom are called worms and insects; the middle are birds and beasts; and the highest are humans. In every kingdom there are things that are lowest, middle, and supreme; the lowest exist for the use of the middle, and the middle for the use of the supreme. Thus, the uses of all created things ascend in order from the final things to man, who is the first in order.

->66—68    ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING    PART I.<-

66. There are three degrees of ascent in the natural world, and there are three degrees of ascent in the spiritual world. All animals are recipients of life; the more advanced animals receive the life of the three degrees of the natural world; the less advanced receive the life of two degrees of that world; and the simple <note>original: "imperfect"</note> animals receive only one degree. But a human being alone is a recipient of the life of the three degrees—not only of the natural world, but also of the three degrees of the spiritual world. Because of this, a person may be elevated above nature, which is not the case with any other animal. He has the power of thinking analytically and rationally about social <note>original: "civil"</note> and ethical matters which are within the sphere of nature, and of spiritual and heavenly <note>original: "celestial"</note> things which are above it. Indeed, he may be elevated into wisdom to the point that he may see God. However, the six degrees by which the purposes <note>original: "uses"</note> of all created things ascend in order to God the Creator will be discussed in their proper place. From this summary, it may be seen that there is an ascent of all created things to the First Being, who alone is life, and that the purposes of all things are the actual recipients of life—and are, consequently, the forms of those purposes.

67. It shall also be shown in a few words how a person ascends, or is elevated, from the lowest degree to the first. He is born into the lowest <term>ultimate</term> <note>In this context, "ultimate" refers to the most basic, final, or outermost level of physical reality.</note> degree of the natural world. He is then elevated by knowledge <note>original: "sciences"</note> to the second degree; and as he perfects his understanding by means of this knowledge, he is elevated to the third degree and becomes rational. The three degrees of ascent in the spiritual world are within him above the three natural degrees, but they do not appear before he leaves behind his earthly body. When he leaves it, the first spiritual degree is opened to him, afterwards the second, and lastly the third—but only in those who become angels of the third heaven; these are the ones who see God. Those in whom the second and lowest degrees are capable of being opened become angels of the second and of the lowest heaven. Every spiritual degree in a person is opened according to how they receive divine love and divine wisdom from the Lord. Those who receive some portion of these enter the first or lowest spiritual degree; those who receive more enter the second or middle spiritual degree; and those who receive much enter the third or highest <note>original: "supreme"</note>. But those who receive none of these remain in the natural degrees and gain nothing more from the spiritual degrees than the power of thinking (and thus of speaking) and the power of willing (and thus of acting), but they do not do so with true intelligence.

68. Concerning the elevation of the inner levels <note>original: "interiors"</note> of a person’s mind, this is also to be observed: there is a reaction <note>original: "re-action"</note> from God in every created thing. Life alone has the power of action, and reaction is triggered by the action of life. This reaction appears as if it belonged to the created being because it exists when the being is acted upon. Thus, in a person, it appears as if life were his own, because he perceives it in no other way than as his own, even though a person is only a recipient of life. Because of this, a person...

The balance <note>original: "equilibrium"</note> of all things comes from action and shared reaction, and everything must be in balance. These things are said so that no one might believe they ascend to God by their own power, but rather by the Lord's power.

### 69. THAT THE DIVINE FILLS ALL SPACES OF THE UNIVERSE WITHOUT BEING SUBJECT TO SPACE.

Two things are characteristic <note>original: "proper"</note> of nature: space and time. From these, a person in the natural world forms his ideas and, consequently, his understanding. If he remains within these ideas and does not lift his mind above them, he can never perceive anything spiritual or divine; he wraps those concepts in ideas derived from space and time. To the degree that he does this, the light of his understanding remains merely natural. Reasoning about spiritual and divine matters using only this natural light is like trying to see things in the darkness of night that are only visible in the light of day. This is the origin of <term>naturalism</term> <note>In this context, naturalism refers to the belief that nothing exists beyond the physical, material world.</note>. 

However, the person who knows how to elevate his mind above thoughts involving space and time passes from darkness to light and becomes wise in spiritual and divine matters, eventually seeing the truth within them. Then, by the power of that spiritual light, he shakes off the darkness of natural light and pushes its illusions <note>original: "fallacies"</note> aside. Anyone with understanding can think beyond the limitations of nature—and actually does so—and then affirms and sees that the Divine, being everywhere at once <note>original: "omnipresent"</note>, is not confined by space. He can also see the truth of the points mentioned above. But if he denies the divine presence and attributes everything to nature, then he is unwilling to be elevated, even though he is capable of it.

70. All who die and become angels cast off those two characteristics of nature—space and time. They enter into spiritual light, where the objects of thought are truths and the objects of sight are similar to those in the natural world, but they correspond to their thoughts. The objects of their thoughts (which are truths) take nothing at all from space or time. Although the objects they see appear as if they are in space and time, the angels do not base their thinking on those concepts. This is because space and time in that world are not fixed <note>original: "stated"</note> as they are in the natural world; instead, they change according to the states of their life. Consequently, in their thoughts, "states of life" replace space and time. Instead of space, they think of things related to states of love; instead of time, they think of things related to states of wisdom. This is why spiritual thought—and therefore spiritual speech—differs so much from natural thought and speech.
