### ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING THE DIVINE LOVE AND DIVINE WISDOM

...derived from it. They have nothing in common, except for the internal nature of things, which are all spiritual. This difference will be discussed more elsewhere. Because the thoughts of angels are not based on space and time, but on states of life, it is clear that they do not understand what is meant when it is said that the Divine fills space—for they do not know what space is. However, they understand clearly when it is said, without any thought of space, that the Divine fills all things.

71. We can illustrate how the purely natural person thinks of spiritual and divine things through the lens of space, while the spiritual person does not: the purely natural person thinks using ideas acquired from visible objects. These objects all have shapes based on length, breadth, and height, and forms defined by those dimensions, whether angular or circular. These dimensions are clearly present in his thoughts about physical things on earth. They are also present in his thoughts about invisible things, such as social <note>original: "civil"</note> or moral issues. He does not actually see shapes in these concepts, but the sense of spatial extension remains. 

This is not the case for the spiritual person, especially a heavenly angel. Their thoughts have nothing to do with figures or forms derived from the dimensions of space. Instead, their thoughts are based on the state of a thing as it relates to its state of life. Therefore, instead of "length," an angel thinks of the "good" of a thing based on the goodness of life. Instead of "breadth," they think of the "truth" of a thing based on the truth of life. Instead of "height," they think of the "degrees" of these qualities. In this way, the angel thinks according to the <term>correspondence</term> <note>The spiritual relationship where natural objects or measurements reflect spiritual realities.</note> that exists between spiritual and natural things. Because of this correspondence, in the Word <note>The Bible or Holy Scripture.</note>, "length" signifies the goodness of a thing, "breadth" signifies its truth, and "height" signifies its various degrees. From this, it is clear that when a heavenly angel thinks of the divine omnipresence, they cannot help but think that the Divine fills all things without space. What an angel thinks is truth, because divine wisdom is the light that enlightens their understanding.

72. This way of thinking about God is fundamental. Without it, the things that will be said about the creation of the universe from the **God-Man**, and about His providence, omnipotence, omnipresence, and omniscience, might be understood but will not be remembered. This is because the purely natural person, even when they understand these truths, falls back into the habits of their life, which come from their will. This habitual love scatters these truths and drowns them in ideas of space. The natural person calls this spatial thinking "rational light," not realizing that the more they deny spiritual truths, the more irrational they actually become. 

This can be proven by looking at the idea of the truth **THAT GOD IS A MAN**. I urge you to read carefully what was written above in sections 11 through 13, and what follows after. Then you will understand that this is true. But if you lower your thoughts into natural light—which is limited by space—will this idea not seem like a paradox to you?

<meta>This page continues the explanation of how the Divine exists outside the limitations of space and time, moving from a discussion of spatial dimensions to a discussion of how "time" functions in the spiritual world compared to the natural world.</meta>

...and if you lower your understanding too far into physical concepts, will you not reject the idea? This is the reason why it is said that the Divine fills all space in the universe, rather than saying that the "God-Man" fills it. If we said the "God-Man" fills all space, purely natural reason would not accept it; but when it is said that the Divine fills it, this is accepted because it agrees with the language used by theologians—that God is omnipresent, and hears and knows all things. More can be seen on this subject above in sections 7 to 10.

73. **THAT THE DIVINE IS PRESENT IN ALL TIME WITHOUT BEING SUBJECT TO TIME.** Just as the Divine is in all space without being limited by space, it is also in all time without being limited by time. Nothing that belongs strictly to nature can be attributed <note>original: "predicated"</note> to the Divine, and both space and time belong to nature. In nature, space and time are measurable. Time is measured by days, weeks, months, years, and ages; days are measured by hours; weeks and months by days; years by the four seasons; and ages by years. Nature derives these measurements from the apparent spinning and orbiting of the sun in our world.

But it is not this way in the spiritual world. There, the progressions of life appear to happen in time, because the inhabitants live with one another just as people do in this world—which would be impossible without the appearance of time. However, time there is not divided into seasons as it is in our world, because their sun is constantly in the east and never moves. It is the Lord's divine love that appears to them as a sun; therefore, they do not have days, weeks, months, years, and ages. Instead, they have "states of life" <note>states of life: The inner conditions, moods, or spiritual levels of an individual, which determine how they experience their surroundings.</note> by which distinctions are made. These cannot be called distinctions of time, but rather distinctions of "state."

Because of this, the angels do not know what "time" is, and when the word is mentioned, they perceive a "state" instead. When a state determines time, time is only an appearance; for the joy of a state makes time seem short, and the unpleasantness of a state makes it seem long. From this, it is clear that time in the spiritual world is nothing more than the quality of one's state of being. Consequently, when hours, days, weeks, months, and years are mentioned in the Word <note>the Word: A term used by the author to refer to the Bible or Sacred Scripture.</note>, they signify states and their progression in a series or as a whole. When time periods are attributed to the church, "morning" signifies its first state, "noon" its peak <note>original: "fulness"</note>, "evening" its decline, and "night" its end; the four seasons of the year—spring, summer, autumn, and winter—signify the same things.

74. From these considerations, it appears that time is one and the same with thought based on affection <note>affection: A person's underlying feelings, desires, or loves that drive their thoughts.</note>; for this is where the quality of a person's state comes from. The fact that distances traveled through space in the spiritual world are the same as the progression of time could be illustrated by many examples; in that world, paths are actually shortened according to the desires of a person's thoughts and affections, and conversely <note>original: "vice versa"</note> are lengthened. This is why we also speak of "spaces of time." However, when thought is not joined with a person's own affection, time does not appear at all—as happens in dreams.

<summary>This page explains that God exists outside of time, just as He exists outside of space. In the spiritual world, "time" is actually an appearance created by a person's changing inner "states of life" or emotions.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of time in the spiritual realm, building on the idea that while we perceive time as linear progression, in the Divine and spiritual reality, it is actually a reflection of internal states of being.</meta>

75. Now, since time—which belongs strictly to the natural world—is actually a series of pure internal states in the spiritual world, these states appear to move forward because angels and spirits are finite beings. It can be seen, however, that these states do not "progress" in God because He is infinite. In Him, all infinite things are one (as demonstrated earlier in sections 17 to 22). From this, it follows that the Divine exists in all time without being limited by time.

76. Anyone who does not know how to think of God outside the concept of time, or who lacks the perception to do so, cannot understand eternity as anything other than an "eternity of time." In that case, such a person cannot help but fall into a kind of confusion <note>original: "delirium"</note> when thinking about God from the perspective of eternity. This is because they think from the idea of a "beginning," but beginnings only exist within time. This confusion leads to the idea that God created Himself, which quickly leads to the mistaken belief that nature also created itself. 

One can only escape this line of thinking by adopting the spiritual or angelic idea of the "eternal," which is independent of time. When time is removed from the concept, "eternal" and "the Divine" become the same thing. The Divine is the Divine "in itself," not "from itself." The angels say they can certainly perceive God from eternity, but they cannot perceive nature from eternity, much less nature originating from itself—and certainly not nature as something that exists independently. For that which exists in itself is the *<term>esse</term>* <note>original: "esse," a Latin term meaning "being" or "essence"</note> from which all things originate. This *<term>esse</term>* in itself is life itself, which is the divine love of divine wisdom and the divine wisdom of divine love. To the angels, this is what is eternal; it is therefore separate from time, just as the "uncreated" is separate from the "created," or the infinite from the finite. There is no comparison between the two.

77. **THAT THE DIVINE IS THE SAME IN BOTH THE GREATEST AND THE SMALLEST THINGS.** This follows from the two previous sections: that the Divine is in all space without being limited by space, and in all time without being limited by time. Spaces can be large or the largest, and small or the smallest; and since space and time are inextricably linked, the same applies to time. The Divine is the same in all of them because the Divine does not vary or change like the things of space, time, or nature. Instead, it is unchanging and constant; therefore, it is everywhere and always the same.

78. It often appears as if the Divine is not the same in one person as it is in another—for instance, that it is different in a wise person compared to a simple person, or different in an old man compared to an infant. However, this is a mistake based on how things appear. The person is different, but the Divine within them is not. A human being is a receiver <note>original: "recipient"</note>, and these receivers—or "vessels"—vary. A wise man is a more suitable, and therefore a more complete, receiver of divine love and divine wisdom than a simple man; and an old man who is wise is a more complete receiver than an infant or a young boy. Nevertheless, the Divine is the same in one as it is in the other. 

In the same way, it is a mistake to think that the Divine varies among the angels of heaven or the people of the earth, simply because the angels...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the Divine exists beyond the limitations of human perception, specifically focusing on how God is equally present in both the largest and smallest scales of existence without being divided by space.</meta>

Angels in heaven possess wisdom that is beyond words <note>original: "ineffable"</note>, while humans do not. However, the apparent difference lies in the individuals themselves—based on how well they receive the Divine—rather than in the Lord Himself.

**79.** The fact that the Divine is the same in both the greatest and the most minute things can be illustrated by heaven and its angels. The Divine present in the whole of heaven is the same as the Divine present in a single angel; for this reason, the whole of heaven can appear as one single angel. The same is true of the church and an individual member of the church. The largest body containing the Divine is the entire heaven, as well as the entire church; the smallest is a single angel of heaven or a single person within the church. I have sometimes seen an entire heavenly community appear as one angelic person; I was told that such a community could appear as a great man or a giant, or as a little man or an infant. This is because the Divine is identical in things both great and small.

**80.** The Divine is also the same in both the largest and smallest of all created things that are not alive. In these things, the Divine exists as the "good of their use" <note>In Swedenborg’s philosophy, a "use" is the practical or spiritual purpose a thing serves in the grand design of creation.</note>. The reason they are not alive is that they are not "forms of life," but rather "forms of uses." Their form varies depending on the quality of their use. How the Divine exists within them will be explained later when we discuss creation.

**81.** Set aside the concept of space and completely reject the idea of a vacuum <note>A "vacuum" here refers to the philosophical concept of "nothingness" or empty space.</note>. Then, think of Divine Love and Divine Wisdom as the true essence of reality. Once space is removed and the idea of a vacuum is dismissed, you will perceive that the Divine is the same in both the largest and smallest portions of space. This is because in an essence independent of space, there is no "large" or "small," only identity.

**82.** Now, something must be said regarding a vacuum. I once heard angels talking with Newton <note>Sir Isaac Newton (1643–1727), the famous physicist and mathematician, who in Swedenborg’s accounts is often seen learning spiritual truths in the afterlife.</note> about a vacuum. They told him they could not tolerate the idea of a vacuum as "nothingness." In their world—the spiritual world, which exists within or above the space and time of the natural world—they still feel, think, experience emotion, love, will, breathe, and even speak and act. These things would be impossible in a vacuum of "nothingness," because nothing is nothing, and nothing can be attributed to "nothing." Newton replied that he knew the Divine, which truly exists, fills all things. He said he himself hated the idea of "nothingness" in relation to a vacuum because it destroys the possibility of everything else. He urged those talking with him to beware of the idea of "nothing," calling it a "swoon" <note>original: "swoon," used here to mean a total loss of mental focus or a collapse of thought.</note>, because in "nothingness" there is no actual mental activity.

<summary>This page explains that the Divine is equally present in all levels of creation, from entire heavenly societies to individual angels and even inanimate objects. It also argues that "nothingness" or a vacuum cannot exist because the Divine fills all things with essence and purpose.</summary>

## PART II.

83. **THAT THE DIVINE LOVE AND THE DIVINE WISDOM APPEAR IN THE SPIRITUAL WORLD AS A SUN.** There are two worlds: the spiritual and the natural. The spiritual world does not receive anything from the natural world, nor does the natural world receive anything from the spiritual world. They are completely distinct and communicate only through correspondences <note>original: "correspondencies." In this context, correspondences are the specific relationships between spiritual causes and their natural effects.</note>, the nature of which has been shown in detail elsewhere. To illustrate this, let us take an example: Heat in the natural world corresponds to the good of charity <note>The "good of charity" refers to the practical expression of love and kindness toward others.</note> in the spiritual world, and light in the natural world corresponds to the truth of faith in the spiritual world. Who cannot see that heat and the good of charity, and light and the truth of faith, are totally distinct? At first glance, they appear as two completely different things. They seem so if we ask ourselves, "What does the good of charity have in common with heat, and what does the truth of faith have in common with light?" Nevertheless, spiritual heat is that good, and spiritual light is that truth. Although these principles are so distinct in themselves, they become one through correspondence. For while a person reads about heat and light in the Word <note>The "Word" refers to the Holy Scriptures or the Bible.</note>, the spirits and angels who are with them perceive charity instead of heat, and faith instead of light. This example is brought forward to show that the spiritual and natural worlds are so distinct that they have nothing in common with each other, yet they are created in such a way that they communicate and are joined by correspondences.

84. Since the two worlds are so distinct, it can clearly be seen that the spiritual world is under a different sun than that of the natural world. In the spiritual world, there are heat and light just as there are in the natural world; however, the heat and light there are spiritual. Spiritual heat is the good of charity, and spiritual light is the truth of faith. Now, since heat and light must originate from a sun, it is evident that there is a different sun in the spiritual world than in the natural world. The sun of the spiritual world has an essence that allows spiritual heat and light to exist from it, while the sun of the natural world has an essence that allows natural heat to exist from it. Everything spiritual that relates to good and truth can come from no other source than the Divine Love and Divine Wisdom; for every "good" comes from love, and every "truth" from wisdom. Anyone who is wise can see that they come from no other source.

85. The existence of a sun other than the one in the natural world has been unknown until now. This is because humanity’s spiritual nature <note>original: "spiritual principle"</note> had become so deeply buried in its natural nature that people did not know what "spiritual" meant. Consequently, they did not know that a spiritual world exists—a place where spirits and angels live—that is different and distinct from the natural world. As the spiritual world has been so hidden from those in the natural world, it has pleased the Lord...

...to open my spiritual sight <note>original: "sight of my spirit"</note>, so that I might see the things that are in that world as clearly as I see the things in this one. Afterward, I described that world in the work ***ON HEAVEN AND HELL*** <note>original Latin title: *De Caelo et Ejus Mirabilibus et de Inferno*</note>. In one section of that book, I discuss the sun of the spiritual world; I have seen it myself, and it appeared to be the same size as the sun of the natural world and fiery like it, though more reddish <note>original: "ruddy"</note>. It was revealed to me that the entire angelic heaven is positioned under that sun; the angels of the third heaven see it always, the angels of the second heaven see it very often, and the angels of the first or lowest <note>original: "ultimate"</note> heaven see it occasionally. In the following sections, we will see that all their heat and light, along with everything that appears in the spiritual world, come from that sun.

86. That sun is not the Lord Himself, but emanates from the Lord. The divine love and divine wisdom proceeding from Him appear in that world as a sun. Since love and wisdom are one in the Lord (as shown in Part I), it is stated that the sun is the Divine Love; because divine wisdom belongs to divine love, it is also love itself.

87. The reason that sun appears fiery to the eyes of the angels is that love and fire correspond to each other. Angels cannot see love with their eyes, so instead of love, they see its correspondence <note>In Swedenborg's philosophy, a correspondence is a natural effect that perfectly represents a spiritual cause.</note>. Angels, like humans, have an internal and an external nature: their internal thinks and is wise, wills and loves; their external feels, sees, speaks, and acts. All their external actions and senses are correspondences of their internal states—specifically spiritual correspondences, not natural ones. Divine love is also felt as fire by spiritual beings. This is why "fire" in the Word <note>The "Word" refers to the Holy Scriptures or the Bible.</note> signifies love. The sacred fire in the Israelite <note>original: "Israelitish"</note> church signified the same thing. This is also why it is common in prayers to God to ask that "heavenly fire"—meaning divine love—may ignite the heart.

88. Because there is such a difference between the spiritual and the natural (as seen above in section 83), not even the smallest part of the natural sun’s light or heat, or any physical object from Earth, can pass into the spiritual world. In that world, the light of the natural world is darkness, and its heat is death. Nevertheless, the heat of the natural world can be given life <note>original: "vivified"</note> by the flow <term>influx</term> of heavenly heat, and natural light can be illuminated by the influx of heavenly light. This influx <note>Influx describes the way spiritual energy flows into and sustains the natural world.</note> occurs through correspondences, not through a direct physical connection <note>original: "continuity"</note>.

89. ### THAT HEAT AND LIGHT PROCEED FROM THE SUN THAT EXISTS FROM THE DIVINE LOVE AND THE DIVINE WISDOM.

In the spiritual world where angels and spirits live, there is heat and light just as there is in the natural world where humans live. This heat is felt as heat, and the light is seen as light. And yet, the heat and light of the spiritual and natural worlds differ so much that they have nothing in common, as stated previously. They are as different as the living and the dead. Both the heat and light of the spiritual world are alive in themselves, while both the...

# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART II.

The heat and light of the natural world are dead in themselves. For the heat and light of the spiritual world proceed from a sun which is pure love, and the heat and light of the natural world proceed from a sun which is pure fire. Love is alive, and divine love is life itself; fire is dead, and the fire of the natural sun is death itself—it may be called such because it has nothing of life within it.

90. Angels, being spiritual, cannot live in any other than spiritual heat and light; but humans cannot live in any other heat than natural heat, or in any other light than natural light. This is because spiritual things agree with spiritual things, and natural things with natural things. If an angle were to draw in the smallest portion of natural heat and light, he would perish, for it is entirely incompatible with his life. Every person, as to the inner levels of their mind, is a spirit. When a person dies, they depart entirely from the world of nature; they leave behind everything belonging to it and enter a world in which there is no nature. In that world, they live so separate from nature that they have no direct connection to it by continuity <note>original: "continuity"—meaning a physical progression from dense to refined substance</note>—that is, as if moving from the more coarse to the more refined—but only by correspondences <note>correspondences: the relationship between a spiritual cause and its natural effect</note>; that is, the relationship between cause (the prior) and effect (the posterior). From this, it may appear that spiritual heat is not just a more refined kind of natural heat, nor spiritual light a more refined kind of natural light; rather, they are of a completely different essence. Spiritual heat and light derive their essence from a sun which is pure love—which is life itself—while natural heat and light derive their essence from a sun which is pure fire, in which there is absolutely no life, as was stated above.

91. Since there is such a difference between the heat and light of one world and the other, it is evident why those in one world cannot see those in the other. The eyes of a person who sees by natural light are made of the substance of their world, and the eyes of an angel are made of the substance of their world—each formed so that they may properly receive their own specific light. These considerations show the ignorance of those who refuse to believe that angels and spirits are human beings simply because they cannot see them with their physical eyes.

92. Until now, it has been unknown that angels and spirits live in light and heat entirely different from that of humans. Indeed, it has not even been known that any other light and heat exist than those of this world. No one has ever thought deeper than the inner or more refined parts of nature. For this reason, many have placed the homes of angels and spirits in the ether <note>æther: a substance once thought to fill the upper regions of space beyond the clouds</note> or in the stars—consequently placing them within nature, rather than above or outside it. Nevertheless, angels and spirits are entirely above or outside nature, and in their own world, which is under another sun. And as in that world spaces are only appearances (as mentioned above), it cannot be said that angels and spirits are in the ether or in the stars. Instead, they are present with each person, joined to the affection and thought of that person's spirit. Because a human is a spirit, they think and will from that spirit; thus, the spiritual world is where

PART II. THE DIVINE LOVE. 92—96

...humanity is, and is not at all removed from them. In a word, every person, as to the inner levels of their mind, is in that world in the middle of spirits and angels; they think from its light and love from its heat.

93. **THAT THE SUN IS NOT GOD, BUT IS WHAT PROCEEDS FROM THE DIVINE LOVE AND THE DIVINE WISDOM OF THE GOD-MAN <note>original: "God-Man"—referring to the Lord as the divine source of humanity</note>: SIMILARLY REGARDING THE HEAT AND LIGHT FROM THAT SUN.** 
The sun that is visible to the angels—from which they receive their heat and light—is not the Lord Himself. Instead, it is what first proceeds <note>proceeds: flows out or emanates</note> from Him, which is the highest source of spiritual heat. The highest form of spiritual heat is spiritual fire, which consists of the divine love and the divine wisdom in their first correspondence <note>correspondence: the relationship between a spiritual cause and its natural effect</note>. This is why that sun appears fiery and is like fire to the angels, though it is not so to humans. The fire that is "fire" to humans is not spiritual but natural. There is as much difference between them as there is between what is living and what is dead. Therefore, the spiritual sun, through its heat, gives life to spiritual beings and renews spiritual things. On the other hand, the sun of the natural world does indeed produce similar effects upon natural humans and natural things, but it does not do so from itself. It does so through the influx <note>influx: the flowing in of spiritual energy into the natural world</note> of spiritual heat, to which the natural sun serves as a secondary helper.

94. Spiritual fire, in which light also originates, becomes spiritual heat and light as it flows outward. This heat and light decrease as they move further away, and this decrease happens through "degrees," which we will discuss in the following pages. Ancient people represented this as red circles of fire and shining circles of light around the head of God. This is still common today when God is depicted as a Man in paintings <note>The author is referring to halos or nimbuses used in religious art</note>.

95. That love produces heat and wisdom produces light is proven by real experience. When a person loves, they grow warm; when a person thinks from wisdom, they see things in a kind of light. From this, it is clear that the first thing to proceed from love is heat, and the first thing to proceed from wisdom is light. It is also clear that they are correspondences. This is because heat does not exist *inside* love itself, but originates from it in the will, and from there moves into the body. Similarly, light does not exist *inside* wisdom, but in the thought of the understanding, and from there in speech. Thus, love and wisdom are the essence and life of heat and light. Heat and light are what proceed from them, and because they are such, they are also correspondences.

96. Anyone can know that spiritual light is completely different from natural light if they pay attention to the thoughts of their mind. When the mind thinks, it sees its objects in a certain light. Those who think spiritually see truths, and they see them in the middle of the night just as clearly as in the day. Because of this, "light" is attributed to the understanding, and we say the understanding "sees." When one person speaks, another sometimes says that they "see" it to be so, which means they understand it. Since the understanding is spiritual, it cannot see in this way from natural light; natural light does not stay with the mind but disappears when the sun sets. Therefore, the understanding clearly has a different light than the eye, and that light comes from a different source.

<header>97—101 ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART II.</header>

**97.** But take care not to think that the sun of the spiritual world is God himself. God is a Man. The first thing that proceeds <note>original: "proceeding" — something that flows out or emanates from a source</note> from his love and wisdom is a fiery spiritual principle, which appears to the eyes of the angels as a sun. Therefore, when the Lord manifests himself to the angels in person, he appears as a Man—sometimes within the sun, and sometimes outside of it.

**98.** Because of this correspondence, the Lord is called not only a "sun" in the Word <note>Word: The Bible or Sacred Scripture</note>, but also "fire" and "light." The sun represents him in terms of both his divine love and his divine wisdom together; fire represents him in terms of his divine love, and light represents him in terms of his divine wisdom.

**99. THAT SPIRITUAL HEAT AND SPIRITUAL LIGHT, AS THEY PROCEED FROM THE LORD AS A SUN, FORM A UNITY, JUST AS HIS DIVINE LOVE AND HIS DIVINE WISDOM FORM A UNITY.** How divine love and divine wisdom in the Lord form a unity was shown in the first part. In the same way, heat and light form a unity because they flow out together, and things that flow out together form a unity through correspondence; for heat corresponds to love, and light to wisdom. It follows that just as divine love is the divine *esse* <note>original: "esse" (Latin: being or essence)</note> and divine wisdom is the divine *existere* <note>original: "existere" (Latin: existence or manifestation)</note>—as shown above in sections 14 to 16—so spiritual heat is the Divinity proceeding from the divine *esse*, and spiritual light is the Divinity proceeding from the divine *existere*. Therefore, just as that union means divine love belongs to divine wisdom, and divine wisdom belongs to divine love (as shown in sections 34 to 39), so spiritual heat belongs to spiritual light, and spiritual light belongs to spiritual heat. Because there is such a union, it follows that heat and light, as they proceed from the Lord as a sun, are one. However, the fact that they are not *received* as one by angels and humans will be explained later.

**100.** The heat and light that proceed from the Lord as a sun are called "the spiritual" in a supreme sense. They are referred to as "the spiritual" in the singular because they are one; therefore, in the following pages when "the spiritual" is mentioned, both are understood together. Because of this "spiritual," that whole world is called the spiritual world; everything in that world derives its origin and its name from that spiritual principle. That heat and light are called "the spiritual" because God is called a Spirit, and God, as a Spirit, is that very outflowing. God is called Jehovah because of his essence; but through this outflowing, he gives life <note>original: "vivifies"</note> and enlightenment <note>original: "illustration"</note> to the angels of heaven and the people of the church. This is why giving life and enlightenment are said to be the work of the Spirit of Jehovah.

**101.** The fact that heat and light—or the spiritual proceeding from the Lord as a sun—form a unity can be illustrated by the heat and light that come from the sun of the natural world; these also form a unity as they leave that sun. The fact that they do not seem to be a unity on earth is not because of the sun, but because of the earth. The earth rotates daily on its axis and makes a yearly revolution along the ecliptic <note>ecliptic: the apparent path of the sun across the sky during the year</note>; hence it appears as if heat and light do not form a unity, for at midsummer there is more

**101.** ...heat than light, and in mid-winter there is more light than heat. The case is similar in the spiritual world, except that the earth there has no rotation or revolution; instead, the angels turn more or less toward the Lord. Those who turn to Him most receive more heat and less light, and those who turn to Him less receive more light and less heat. For this reason, the heavens (which consist of angels) are divided into two kingdoms: the celestial kingdom and the spiritual kingdom. The celestial angels receive more heat, while the spiritual angels receive more light. Furthermore, the appearance of the lands where they live depends on how much heat and light the inhabitants receive. There is a complete correspondence <note>correspondence: a direct, symbolic relationship where a spiritual reality is reflected in a physical or outward appearance</note> if you substitute the "change of state" <note>change of state: an internal change in an angel's feelings or thoughts</note> of the angels for the physical motion of the earth.

**102.** In the following pages, we will see that all spiritual things that originate from the heat and light of their sun form a unity when considered in themselves; however, they do not form a unity when considered as they flow from the feelings <note>original: "affections"</note> of the angels. When heat and light form a unity in the heavens, it is like spring for the angels; but when they do not form a unity, it is like either summer or winter—not like winter in the freezing arctic zones, but like winter in warmer climates. This is because the equal reception of love and wisdom constitutes the nature of an angel <note>original: "angelic principle"</note>; therefore, an angel is only an angel of heaven based on the union of love and wisdom within them. It is the same for a person of the church, if love and wisdom—or charity and faith—form a unity within them.

**103. That the Sun of the Spiritual World appears at a middle altitude, as far from the angels as the Sun of the Natural World is from humans.** Most people leave this world with the idea that God is high overhead and that the Lord is in heaven among the angels. The reason for this idea of God is that in the Word <note>Word: The Bible or Sacred Scripture</note>, God is called "the Most High," and He is said to "dwell on high." Because of this, people lift their eyes and hands when they pray and adore, not knowing that "Most High" actually signifies the "inmost" <note>inmost: the core or deepest part of one's being</note>. The reason for their idea of the Lord is that they do not think of Him as anything other than another man, or perhaps as an angel. They do not know that the Lord is the true and only God who governs the universe. If He were in heaven among the angels, He could not have the entire universe under His view, protection, and government. Furthermore, if He did not shine upon those in the spiritual world as a sun, the angels would have no light; for they are spiritual beings, and therefore no light suits their essence except spiritual light. The fact that there is light in heaven that infinitely exceeds the light on earth will be seen later when "degrees" are discussed.

<header>ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART II.</header>

**104.** The sun appears at an angle of about forty-five degrees, or at a middle altitude. It appears as distant from the angels as the sun of our world is distant from men. It also appears constantly at that altitude and distance and does not move. Because of this, the angels have no divisions of time into days and years, nor any progression of the day from morning, through noon, to evening and night. There is also no progression of the year from spring, through summer, to autumn and winter. Instead, there is perpetual light and perpetual spring. Therefore, in heaven, "states" <note>state: an internal condition of the mind or soul, such as a particular mood or level of wisdom, which takes the place of time in the spiritual world</note> exist instead of times, as was mentioned previously.

**105.** The following are the principal reasons why the sun of the spiritual world appears at a middle altitude:

*First*, so that the heat and light proceeding from that sun may be at a middle degree, and therefore equal and in a balanced temperature. If the sun were to appear above its middle altitude, more heat than light would be felt; if it were below it, more light than heat would be felt. This is what happens on earth when the sun is above or below the middle of the sky: when it is higher, the heat increases more than the light, and when it is lower, the light increases more than the heat. The light remains essentially the same in both summer and winter, but the heat increases and decreases according to the sun’s altitude.

*The second reason* why the sun of the spiritual world appears at a middle altitude above the angelic heaven is that this creates a perpetual spring in all the angelic heavens. Because of this, the angels are in a state of peace, as that state corresponds to springtime on earth.

*The third reason* is so that the angels can always turn their faces to the Lord and see Him with their eyes. In the spiritual world, the "East"—and thus the Lord—is always before the faces of the angels, no matter which way they turn their bodies. This is a unique feature of that world. This would not be possible if the sun of that world appeared above or below a middle altitude, and it would be impossible if it appeared directly overhead in the zenith <note>zenith: the highest point in the sky directly above an observer</note>.

**106.** If the sun of the spiritual world did not appear distant from the angels (just as the sun of the natural world appears distant from men), then the entire angelic heaven—along with the hells beneath it and our physical world <note>original: "terraqueous globe," meaning the world of land and water</note> below them—would not be under the view, protection, omnipresence, omniscience, omnipotence, and providence of the Lord. By comparison, if the sun of our world were not at its specific distance from the earth, it could not be present and powerful in all countries through its heat and light, and therefore could not serve as a natural substitute for the sun of the spiritual world.

**107.** It is highly necessary to know that there are two suns: one spiritual and the other natural. The spiritual sun is for those in the spiritual world, and the natural sun is for those in the natural world. Unless this is known, nothing can be accurately understood concerning creation or concerning man (the subjects which are about to be discussed). While the effects of these things may be seen, [the causes remain hidden]...

### 108. THE DISTANCE BETWEEN THE SUN AND THE ANGELS IN THE SPIRITUAL WORLD IS AN APPEARANCE BASED ON THEIR RECEPTION OF DIVINE LOVE AND DIVINE WISDOM.

All the misconceptions <note>original: "fallacies"</note> that take hold of the wicked and the simple-minded arise from treating appearances as absolute facts. As long as appearances are recognized as mere appearances, they are "apparent truths" that a person may use to think and speak. But when they are accepted as literal truths—which happens when someone "confirms" or justifies them in their mind—then these apparent truths become falsehoods and misconceptions. 

For example: it appears that the sun revolves around the earth every day and follows a path along the ecliptic <term>ecliptic: the sun's apparent path across the sky during the year</term> each year. As long as this is not taken as a literal scientific fact, it is an apparent truth that anyone can use in speech. One might say that the sun rises and sets, creating morning, noon, evening, and night, or that at certain times the sun is at a specific height or altitude, bringing spring, summer, autumn, and winter. But if a person insists that this appearance is the absolute truth, they are thinking and speaking a falsehood based on a misconception. This is true for countless other appearances, not only in nature, society, and ethics, but also in spiritual matters.

109. The same principle applies to the distance of the sun in the spiritual world. This sun is the immediate outflow <note>original: "proximately proceeding emanation"</note> of the Lord’s Divine Love and Divine Wisdom. The truth is that there is no physical distance there. Instead, distance is an "appearance" based on the degree to which an angel receives Divine Love and Divine Wisdom. That distances in the spiritual world are appearances is evident from what was said earlier: in sections 7 to 9, it was shown *that the Divine is not in space*, and in sections 69 to 72, *that the Divine fills all space without being limited by space.* If there is no space, there can be no distance; or, to put it another way, if space is an appearance, then distance is also an appearance, since distance is a quality of space.

110. The sun of the spiritual world appears to be at a distance from the angels because they receive Divine Love and Wisdom in degrees of heat and light that are suited to their state. Since an angel is a created and finite being, they cannot receive the Lord in the highest degree of heat and light—the level found directly in the sun—because they would be completely consumed. Therefore, the Lord is received by them in a degree of heat and light that matches their own level of love and wisdom. 

This can be illustrated by the fact that an angel from the lowest heaven cannot ascend to the angels of the third <note>third heaven: the highest or most internal level of heaven in the author's theological framework</note> heaven. If such an angel were to enter that higher heaven, they would fall into something like a faint, and their life would experience a struggle between life and death. This is because their capacity for love and wisdom is of a lower degree, and the heat of love and the...

<summary>This page explains that distance in the spiritual world is an internal appearance based on an angel's capacity to receive Divine Love and Wisdom. It also warns that mistaking "apparent truths" (like the sun revolving around the earth) for literal truths leads to intellectual errors.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how spatial distance in the spiritual world is an appearance based on internal spiritual states. It explores how the Lord's presence is received differently by angels and humans.</meta>

...the light of wisdom within them. What, then, would happen if an angel were to travel toward that sun itself and enter its fire? The differences in how the angels receive the Lord cause the heavens to appear distinct from one another. The highest heaven (called the third heaven) appears to be above the second, and the second above the first. This is not because there is any physical distance between the heavens, but because they *appear* to be distant. In reality, the Lord is just as present with those in the lowest heaven as He is with those in the third heaven. The cause of this appearance of distance lies within the subjects—the angels—and not in the Lord.

111. It is difficult to understand this through a natural mindset <note>original: "natural idea"</note>, because such thinking is based on physical space. However, it can be understood through a spiritual mindset, because in that way of thinking, space does not exist. This is how the angels think. Even so, it can be understood by a natural mindset that love and wisdom—or the Lord, who is Divine Love and Divine Wisdom—cannot move through physical spaces. Instead, He is present with everyone according to how they receive Him. The Lord teaches that He is present with everyone in Matthew 28:20, and that He makes His home with those who love Him in John 14:21.

112. This might seem like a matter of "superior wisdom" because it uses the heavens and angels as proof, but the principle is exactly the same for human beings. In the inner parts of their minds, humans receive heat and enlightenment from that same spiritual sun. Its heat warms them and its light enlightens them in proportion to how much love and wisdom they receive from the Lord. The difference between angels and humans is that angels live only under that spiritual sun. Humans, however, live not only under that sun but also under the sun of this world. Human bodies cannot exist or survive unless they are under both suns; this is not the case for the bodies of angels, which are spiritual.

113. **THE ANGELS ARE IN THE LORD, AND THE LORD IS IN THEM; BECAUSE THE ANGELS ARE RECIPIENTS, THE LORD ALONE IS HEAVEN.**
Heaven is often called "the dwelling place of God" or "the throne of God," which leads people to believe that God is there like a king in his kingdom. However, God (that is, the Lord) exists in the sun above the heavens. Through His presence in the form of spiritual heat and light, He is also within the heavens (as shown in the last two sections). Even though the Lord is present in heaven in this manner, He is still within Himself. As was shown previously (sections 108 to 112), the distance between that sun and heaven is not a physical distance but only an appearance. Since it is only an appearance, it follows that the Lord Himself is in heaven, for He is within the love and wisdom of the angels. And because He is in the love and wisdom of all the angels, and the angels make up heaven, He is within the entire heaven.

114. The Lord is not only *in* heaven but actually *is* heaven. This is because love and wisdom are what make someone an angel, and these two belong to the Lord in...

<summary>This page explains that distance in heaven is a spiritual appearance based on how much an individual receives the Lord's love and wisdom. It concludes that because the Lord is the source of all love and wisdom, He is not just present in heaven but is the very essence of heaven itself.</summary>

<header>PART II. THE DIVINE LOVE. 114—116</header>

the angels; therefore, the Lord is heaven. This is because angels are not angels because of their own self-nature <term>original: "proprium"; the inherent self, ego, or "own-hood" of a person</term>. Their self-nature is exactly like that of a human, which is evil. This is the case because all angels were once humans, and that self-nature is inherent in them from birth. It is only set aside; and in proportion as it is set aside, they receive love and wisdom—which is the Lord—within them. 

Anyone can see, if they elevate their understanding even a little, that the Lord cannot dwell with the angels except in what is His own—that is, in His own love and wisdom—and not at all in the self-nature of the angels, which is evil. Hence, as far as evil is removed, the Lord is in them, and they are angels. The essential angelic principle of heaven is Divine Love and Divine Wisdom; this Divine principle is called "angelic" when it resides in the angels. Thus, it is evident again that angels are angels because of the Lord, and not because of themselves; consequently, heaven exists because of the Lord as well.

115. However, how the Lord is in an angel, and an angel in the Lord, cannot be understood unless the nature of their union <note>original: "conjunction"</note> is known. There is a union of the Lord with the angel, and of the angel with the Lord; therefore, there is a mutual union. On the part of the angel, it works like this: the angel perceives love and wisdom as if they come from themselves, just as it appears to humans. Because of this, it seems to them as if their love and wisdom were their own. 

If they did not perceive the matter this way, there would be no union; therefore, the Lord would not be in them, nor they in the Lord. It is not possible for the Lord to be in any particular angel or human unless the person receiving the Lord’s love and wisdom perceives and feels it as their own. By this means, the Lord is not only received, but once received, is retained and also loved in return. In this way, an angel becomes wise and continues to be wise. Who would be inclined to love the Lord and their neighbor, or to be wise, unless they felt and perceived what they love, learn, and absorb as their own? Who could otherwise retain it? If this were not the case, the love and wisdom received by spiritual influence <note>original: "influx"</note> would have no staying place; it would pass through without affecting the person. An angel would not be an angel, nor a man a man—indeed, he would be merely like a lifeless <note>original: "inanimate"</note> thing. Therefore, it is clear that mutual interaction <note>original: "reciprocation"</note> is necessary for a union to exist.

116. I shall now explain how it happens that an angel perceives and feels <insert>what flows from the Lord</insert> as their own, and so receives and retains it, even though it is not actually theirs. (For it was said above that an angel is not an angel because of what is their own, but because of what is the Lord's in them.) The situation is this: every angel has liberty and rationality. These two principles are in them for the purpose of making them capable of receiving love and wisdom from the Lord. But both liberty and rationality are not the angel's own, but the Lord’s within them. Yet, being so closely joined to the angel's life—so intimately that they may be said to be a built-in part of it—they appear...

to belong to him, or to be his own. These qualities give him the power to think and will, and to speak and act; and what he thinks, wills, speaks, and acts from them appears as if it were from himself. This creates a reciprocal interaction <note>original: "reciprocation"</note>, and through this, a union <note>original: "conjunction"</note>. 

However, to the extent that an angel believes that love and wisdom are within himself—and therefore claims them as his own—the angelic nature is not in him. Consequently, he does not have a union with the Lord because he is not in the truth. Since truth and the light of heaven are one, he cannot be in heaven to that same extent; for by claiming these as his own, he denies that he lives from the Lord and believes instead that he lives from himself—and consequently, that he possesses a divine essence. 

Life, whether called angelic or human, consists of these two principles: **liberty** and **rationality**. From what has been said, it is clear that an angel has this reciprocal interaction for the sake of union with the Lord. But that reciprocity, considered as a capacity, is not the angel's but the Lord's. Therefore, if an angel abuses that reciprocal principle—by which he perceives and feels what is the Lord's as his own—and attributes it to himself, he falls from the angelic state. The Lord Himself teaches that this union is reciprocal in John 14:20–24 and John 15:4–6. He also teaches that the union of the Lord with man, and man with the Lord, is found in those things belonging to the Lord, which are called His words (John 15:7).

**117.** Some imagine that Adam was in such a state of liberty or free will <note>original: "free determination"</note> that he was able to love God and be wise by his own power, and that this free will was lost by his descendants. But this is a mistake. Man is not life itself, but a recipient of life (as seen above in sections <note>original: "n." for "numero"</note> 4 to 6 and 54 to 60). One who is a recipient of life cannot love or be wise from anything of his own. Therefore, when Adam desired to be wise and to love from what was his own, he fell from wisdom and love and was cast out of Paradise.

**118.** What has been said here regarding an angel may also be said of heaven, which consists of angels, because the Divine nature is the same in both the greatest and the least things (as shown above in sections 77 to 82). Likewise, what has been said of an angel and of heaven may be said of a person and the church. This is because an angel of heaven and a person of the church act as one through their union; furthermore, a person of the church is an angel in the inner workings of their mind. By "a person of the church," we mean someone in whom the church truly resides.

### 119. IN THE SPIRITUAL WORLD, THE EAST IS WHERE THE LORD APPEARS AS A SUN, AND THE OTHER DIRECTIONS ARE DETERMINED BY THIS.

We have already discussed the sun of the spiritual world and its essence, its heat and light, and the Lord's presence coming from it. We will now proceed to discuss the directions <term>original: "quarters"; the four cardinal directions (North, South, East, and West)</term> of that world. The sun and that world are discussed because God is discussed, along with love and wisdom; to discuss them in any way other than from their original source would

### PART II. THE DIVINE LOVE. 119—122

**119.** ...would be to treat things from their effects and not from their causes. Yet, effects teach nothing but effects; when they are considered alone, they do not explain a single cause. Conversely, causes explain effects. To know effects by understanding their causes is to be truly wise; but to search for causes by looking only at effects is not wise, because fallacies then present themselves which the researcher mistakenly calls "causes." This only confuses wisdom. This is because causes are prior <note>original: "prior"; meaning primary or coming before</note> and effects are posterior <note>original: "posterior"; meaning secondary or following after</note>. One cannot see earlier things from the perspective of later things, but one can see later things from the perspective of earlier ones: this is the true order of understanding. For this reason, the spiritual world is treated first here, for all causes exist there; we shall afterwards treat the natural world, where everything that appears is an effect.

**120.** We shall begin with the quarters <note>original: "quarters"; referring to the cardinal directions: East, West, South, and North</note> of the spiritual world. There are quarters in that world just as there are in the natural world; however, the quarters of the spiritual world, like that world itself, are spiritual. On the other hand, the quarters of the natural world, like that world itself, are natural. Therefore, they differ so much that they have nothing in common. 

There are four quarters in both worlds: the east, west, south, and north. In the natural world, these four quarters are constant, being determined by the sun in its meridian <note>original: "meridian"; the highest point the sun reaches at noon</note>. Opposite to the meridian is the north; on one side is the east, and on the other side is the west. These quarters are determined by the noon-day position of the sun at each particular place; because the sun’s station at noon is always the same everywhere, it is considered fixed. 

It is different in the spiritual world. There, the quarters are determined by the sun of that world, which constantly appears in its place. Where it appears is the East. Therefore, the determination of the directions in that world does not begin from the south, as in the natural world, but from the east. Opposite to the east is the west; on one side is the south, and on the other is the north. That these directions do not originate from the sun itself, but from the inhabitants of that world—who are angels and spirits—will be explained in the following pages.

**121.** Since these quarters are spiritual because they originate from the Lord as a sun, the homes of angels and spirits are also spiritual, because they are arranged according to these quarters. They are spiritual because the inhabitants live in specific locations based on how they receive love and wisdom from the Lord. 
* Those who exist in a higher degree of love live in the **east**.
* Those who are in a lower degree of love live in the **west**.
* Those who are in a higher degree of wisdom live in the **south**.
* Those who are in a lower degree of wisdom live in the **north**. 

This is why in the Word <note>original: "the Word"; referring to the Bible</note>, the "east," in its highest sense, means the Lord, and in a related sense, love toward Him. The "west" signifies love toward Him that is decreasing. The "south" signifies wisdom in clear light, and the "north" signifies wisdom in shade. Similar meanings are determined based on the specific state of the subjects being discussed.

**122.** Since all the directions in the spiritual world are determined starting from the east, and the "east" in its highest sense means the Lord and Divine Love, it is evident that it is the Lord and His love...

### ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING THE DIVINE LOVE: PART II. 122—125

...to Him, from whom all things exist. In proportion as anyone is not in that love, he is removed from the Lord and dwells either in the west, the south, or the north, at a distance determined by his reception of love.

123. Since the Lord, as a sun, is constantly in the east, the ancients—for whom all the details of worship represented spiritual things—turned their faces toward the east during their adorations. So that they might do the same in all worship, they also turned their temples toward that same direction. Because of this, churches today are still built in the same manner.

124. ## THE QUARTERS IN THE SPIRITUAL WORLD DO NOT ORIGINATE FROM THE LORD AS A SUN, BUT FROM THE ANGELS ACCORDING TO RECEPTION.

It has been shown that the angels dwell in distinct groups: some in the eastern quarter, some in the western, some in the southern, and some in the northern. In the east, they are in a superior degree of love; in the west, in an inferior degree of love; in the south, in the light of wisdom; and in the north, in the shade of wisdom. This diversity of their dwellings appears to originate from the Lord as a sun, when it is actually from the angels themselves. The Lord is not in a greater degree of love and wisdom with one person than another, nor as a sun is He in a greater degree of heat and light with one than with another, for He is everywhere the same. However, He is not received by one in the same degree as by another. This causes them to appear to themselves at a greater or lesser distance from each other, and to live in different quarters. Consequently, the quarters in the spiritual world are nothing more than the various ways love and wisdom—and therefore heat and light—are received <note>original: "receptions"; how the angels take in or respond to the Lord's influence</note> from the Lord as a sun. That this is the case is evident from what was shown above (sections 108 to 112): that distances in the spiritual world are appearances.

125. Since the quarters are simply the various ways angels receive love and wisdom, it is helpful to discuss the variety that creates that appearance. The Lord is in an angel, and an angel is in the Lord, as was shown in the preceding article. However, because it appears as if the Lord as a sun is outside of the angel, it also appears as if the Lord sees him from the sun, and as if the angel sees the Lord in the sun—almost in the same way an image appears in a mirror <note>original: "glass"</note>. Speaking, therefore, according to that appearance, the Lord sees and looks at everyone face-to-face; however, the angels, in their turn, do not see the Lord in that same way. Those who are in love toward the Lord from the Lord see Him directly and are therefore in the east and west. Those who are more focused on wisdom see the Lord at an angle <note>original: "obliquely"</note> to the right, and those who are less in wisdom see the Lord at an angle to the left; the former are therefore in the south, and the latter in the north. The latter see the Lord from an angle because love and wisdom proceed as one from the Lord but are not received as one by the angels, as was said before. The wisdom which...

...is more abundant than love, it might appear to be wisdom, but it actually is not, because it has no life in it from love. These considerations show the origin of that variety of reception, according to which the angels' homes appear in different quarters <note>original: "quarters"; in this context, the four cardinal directions (East, West, South, North) which represent spiritual states.</note> in the spiritual world.

126. The fact that the varied reception of love and wisdom determines one's "quarter" in the spiritual world is clear because an angel's position changes according to the increase or decrease of his love. This shows that the direction does not come from the Lord as the sun, but from the angel according to how he receives the sun’s influence. It is the same for a person’s spirit: through the spirit, a person is in a specific quarter of the spiritual world, regardless of where they are in the natural world. As mentioned before, the directions of the spiritual world have nothing in common with those of the natural world; in the natural world, a person exists through the body, but in the spiritual world, through the spirit.

127. So that love and wisdom can act as one in both angels and humans, many parts of the body come in pairs. The eyes, ears, and nostrils are pairs; the hands, hips <note>original: "loins"</note>, and feet are pairs. The brain is divided into two hemispheres, the heart into two chambers, the lungs into two lobes, and other organs are arranged similarly. Thus, angels and humans have a right and a left side. All parts on the right side relate to love (from which wisdom comes), and all parts on the left side relate to wisdom (derived from love). Or, to put it another way, all parts on the right relate to "good" from which "truth" comes, and all parts on the left relate to the "truth" that comes from "good." <note>In Swedenborg’s thought, "good" refers to the intent or will, and "truth" refers to the thought or understanding.</note> Angels and humans have these pairs so that love and wisdom—or good and truth—can act as a single unit and together look toward the Lord. More will be said on this later.

128. From this we can see the mistake and resulting falsehood of those who imagine that the Lord grants heaven arbitrarily, or that He gives more wisdom and love to one person than another based on a whim. In reality, the Lord desires for everyone to become wise and be saved equally; He provides the means for all. Each person becomes wise and is saved in proportion to how they receive and live according to those means. The Lord is the same toward everyone, but the recipients—angels and humans—are different because of how they receive Him and how they live. This is evident from what has been said about the quarters and the homes of the angels: that diversity comes not from the Lord, but from the recipients.

129. **ANGELS CONSTANTLY TURN THEIR FACES TOWARD THE LORD AS A SUN; THEREFORE, THEY HAVE THE SOUTH TO THEIR RIGHT, THE NORTH TO THEIR LEFT, AND THE WEST BEHIND THEM.** Everything said here about the angels turning toward the Lord as a sun also applies to a human being’s spirit. As far as the mind is concerned, a person is a...

### ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART II.

129. [The human] spirit—if he is in love and wisdom—is an angel. Therefore, after death, when he puts off his outward nature <note>original: "externals"; here referring to the physical body and natural tendencies.</note> which he derived from the natural world, he becomes a spirit or an angel. Because angels constantly turn their faces eastward toward the sun—and therefore toward the Lord—it is also said of a person who is in love and wisdom from the Lord that they "see God," "look to God," and "have God before their eyes." This means that they lead the life of an angel. Such expressions are used in the world because these things actually exist in heaven and within a person’s spirit. In prayer, who does not look before them up toward God, regardless of which direction <note>original: "quarter"</note> their face is turned?

130. Angels constantly turn their faces toward the Lord as a sun because they are in the Lord and the Lord is in them. The Lord interiorly leads their affections and thoughts and constantly turns them toward Himself; consequently, they cannot look any other way than toward the east, where the Lord appears as a sun. From this, it is clear that the angels do not turn themselves toward the Lord, but rather the Lord turns them toward Himself. For when angels think interiorly about the Lord, they think of Him as being within themselves.

Interior thought itself does not create distance. However, exterior thought—which acts in unison with the sight of the eyes—does create distance. This is because exterior thought is bound by space, whereas interior thought is not; and while it is not in space—as is the case in the spiritual world—it still exists in the appearance of space. These concepts, however, can be poorly understood by anyone who thinks of God in terms of physical space. God is everywhere and yet not in space; therefore, He is both within and outside an angel. Thus, an angel can see God—that is, the Lord—both within and outside themselves: within, when they think **from** love and wisdom; outside, when they think **about** love and wisdom.

But we shall speak more particularly of these things when we come to treat of **THE LORD'S OMNIPRESENCE, OMNISCIENCE, and OMNIPOTENCE**. Beware of falling into the detestable <note>original: "execrable"</note> heresy that God has infused Himself into humans and is in them but no longer in Himself. God is everywhere, both within a person and outside them, for He is in all space without being bound by space, as was shown above in sections 7 to 10, and 69 to 72. If He were contained within a person, He would not only be divisible but also restricted to space, and a person might then even think themselves to be God. This heresy is so abominable that in the spiritual world, it stinks like a rotting carcass <note>original: "dead carcase"</note>.

131. The way angels turn toward the Lord is such that, at every turn of their bodies, they look toward the Lord as a sun before them. An angel can turn around and see various things surrounding them, yet the Lord constantly appears before their face as a sun. This may seem miraculous <note>original: "wonderful"; in the 18th-century sense of full of wonder or surprising.</note>, but it is nevertheless the truth. It has also been granted to me to see the Lord as a sun in this way: I see Him before my face, and this has been continuous for many years, regardless of which direction I have turned myself.

It clarifies that God's presence is "without space" and warns against the heresy that God is literally trapped or divided within human beings.</summary>

<header>PART II. THE DIVINE LOVE. 132—136</header>

132. Since the Lord is the sun—and therefore the East—in front of the faces of all the angels of heaven, the South is at their right hand, the North is at their left, and the West is behind them. This is the case no matter how they turn their bodies. As was stated before, all the directions <note>original: "quarters"; in Swedenborg’s theology, these are the four cardinal directions of the spiritual world.</note> of the spiritual world are determined from the East. Therefore, those who have the East before their eyes are in the directions themselves; indeed, they are the very defining points of them. As shown above in sections 124 to 128, these directions do not come from the Lord as the sun, but from the angels according to how they receive Him.

133. Since heaven consists of angels, and angels function in this way, the universal heaven turns toward the Lord. By so turning, it is governed by the Lord as "one man" <note>The concept that all of heaven collectively forms a single, organized human structure, often called the *Grand Man*.</note>, which it actually is in the sight of the Lord. That heaven is like one man in the sight of the Lord may be seen in the work *Heaven and Hell*, sections 59 to 87. This is also the origin of the directions of heaven.

134. Because these directions are, in a sense, inscribed on every angel and on the universal heaven, an angel—unlike a person in the world—knows his house and his dwelling wherever he goes. A person does not know his house and place of residence from a direction within himself because he thinks in terms of space—that is, from the directions of the natural world, which have nothing in common with the directions of the spiritual world. Nevertheless, such knowledge exists in birds and beasts, for they instinctively know their homes and residences, as is well known from much experience. This is proof that such knowledge prevails in the spiritual world; for all things that exist in the natural world are effects, and all things that exist in the spiritual world are the causes of those effects. Everything natural derives its cause from something spiritual.

135. **THAT ALL THE INNER ASPECTS OF BOTH THE MINDS AND THE BODIES OF ANGELS ARE TURNED TOWARD THE LORD AS A SUN.** The angels have understanding and will, a face and a body. They also have the inner aspects <note>original: "interiors"</note> of the understanding and will, as well as those of the face and body. The inner aspects of the understanding and of the will are the things belonging to their interior affection and thought. The inner aspects of the face are the brains, and the inner aspects of the body are the internal organs <note>original: "viscera"</note>, the principal of which are the heart and lungs. In a word, the angels have everything that humans have on earth; because of this, angels are human. An outward form without those internal parts does not make them human; rather, an outward form with them—and indeed, originating from them—makes them human. Otherwise, they would only be images of humans in which there is no life, because they would lack the internal form of life.

136. It is well known that the will and the understanding govern the body at will; for the mouth speaks what the understanding thinks, and the body does what the will desires.

<sig>43</sig>

<header>ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART II.</header>

136. From this, it is evident that the body is a form corresponding to the understanding and the will. Since "form" is also a quality of the understanding and the will, it is clear that the form of the body corresponds to the form of the understanding and the will. To describe the nature of both these forms does not properly fall within the current plan. There are countless things in both; and countless things on both sides act as one because they mutually correspond to each other. Because of this, the mind—or the will and understanding—governs the body at will, acting entirely as itself. From these considerations, it follows that the internal parts <note>original: "interiors"</note> of the mind act as one with the internal parts of the body, and the external parts of the mind act with the external parts of the body. The internal aspects of the mind as well as the internal aspects of the body will be discussed below, after the degrees of life have been addressed.

137. Since the internal parts of the mind are one with the internal parts of the body, it follows that when the mind's internals turn to the Lord as a sun, the body's internals do the same. And since the external parts of both the mind and the body depend on their internal parts, it follows that they also do the same. For whatever the external does, it does from its internal principles; the whole, or general state, derives everything it has from the specific details of which it consists. Hence, it is clear that as an angel turns his face and body to the Lord as a sun, all the internal parts of his mind and body are likewise turned. It is the same with a person, if he always has the Lord before his eyes, which is the case if he lives in love and wisdom. He then looks to the Lord, not only with his eyes and face, but with his whole mind and his whole heart—that is, with every part of his will and understanding—and at the same time with every part of his body.

138. This turning to the Lord is an actual turning and a kind of elevation. There is an elevation into the heat and light of heaven, which is achieved by the opening of the internal parts. When these are open, love and wisdom flow into the internal parts of the mind, and the heat and light of heaven flow into the internal parts of the body. As a result, there is an elevation, as if rising out of mist into air, or out of air into the pure ether <note>original: "æther"; in older science, the rarefied element that fills the upper regions of space.</note>. Love and wisdom, with their heat and light, are the Lord within a person, who, as was said before, turns the person toward Himself. The opposite happens with those who are not in love and wisdom, and even more so with those who set themselves against love and wisdom. Their internal parts, both of the mind and the body, are closed; and when they are closed, their external parts react against the Lord, as that is their nature. Consequently, they turn their backs to the Lord, and turning their backs to Him is turning their faces toward hell.

139. This actual turning toward the Lord is a result of love and wisdom together—not of love alone, or of wisdom alone. Love alone is "being" <note>original: *esse*; the underlying essence or "to be" of a thing.</note> without its "manifesting" <note>original: *existere*; the outward expression or "to exist" of that essence.</note>, for love exists within wisdom; and wisdom without love is like "manifesting" without...

<sig>45</sig>

its essential being <note>original: "esse," a Latin term referring to the core essence or "to be" of a thing</note>, for wisdom comes from love. Love can indeed exist without wisdom, but that is human love and not the Lord’s. Wisdom can also exist without love; however, even though such wisdom comes from the Lord, it does not have the Lord within it. It is like winter light which, though it comes from the sun, lacks the sun's essence, which is heat.

140. **THAT EVERY SPIRIT, REGARDLESS OF THEIR CHARACTER, TURNS IN THE SAME WAY TOWARD THEIR RULING LOVE.** It may be helpful first to explain what a spirit is and what an angel is. After death, every person first enters the "world of spirits," which is positioned in the middle between heaven and hell. There, the person goes through various stages or states and, according to how they lived, is prepared for either heaven or hell. As long as they remain in that world, they are called a **spirit**. One who is raised from that world into heaven is called an **angel**, but one who is cast down into hell is called a **satan** or a **devil**. While these individuals are still in the world of spirits, one preparing for heaven is called an **angelic spirit**, and one preparing for hell is called an **infernal spirit**. During this time, the angelic spirit is connected with heaven, and the infernal spirit with hell. All spirits in the world of spirits are connected to people on earth because people, in the inner workings of their minds, are likewise positioned between heaven and hell. Through these spirits, people communicate with either heaven or hell according to their life. It should be noted that the **WORLD OF SPIRITS** is one thing, and the **SPIRITUAL WORLD** is another. The "world of spirits" is what is being discussed here; however, the "spiritual world," as a whole, includes that world as well as heaven and hell.

141. It may also be useful to say something about the different kinds of love, since we are considering how angels and spirits turn toward the objects of their love. The whole of heaven is divided into societies according to all the different variations of love; hell and the world of spirits are organized in the same way. Heaven is divided into societies according to differences in heavenly <note>original: "celestial"</note> loves, whereas hell is divided according to differences in hellish <note>original: "infernal"</note> loves, and the world of spirits according to differences in both. There are two primary loves to which all others can be traced. The primary love to which all heavenly loves relate is love for the Lord. The primary love to which all hellish loves relate is the love of power <note>original: "love of rule"</note> rooted in the love of self. These two loves are diametrically opposite to each other.

142. Since love for the Lord and the love of power rooted in self-love are completely opposite, and since everyone who is in love for the Lord turns toward the Lord as a sun (as shown in the previous section), it is clear that everyone who is in the love of power rooted in self-love turns away from the Lord. They thus turn their backs on
