[...continued from previous page] the Lord, because those who are in love toward the Lord love nothing more than to be led by the Lord, and desire that the Lord alone should rule. However, those who are in the love of power based on self-love love nothing more than to be led by themselves, and desire that they alone should rule. The "love of power based on self-love" is specified here because there is also a love of power based on a love of performing uses <term>uses</term> <note>original: "performing uses," meaning fulfilling one's duty or being of service to others</note>. This latter love, because it acts as one with love toward the neighbor, is a spiritual love. This spiritual love, however, cannot truly be called a love of power, but rather a love of being useful.

143. Every spirit, regardless of their character, turns toward their ruling love. This is because love is the life of everyone (as shown in Part I, numbers 1, 2, and 3), and life turns its receptacles—which are called the limbs, organs, and viscera <note>original: "viscera," the internal organs of the body</note>, and consequently the whole person—toward that society which shares a similar love. In other words, the person turns toward where their love is.

144. Since the love of power based on self-love is entirely opposite to love toward the Lord, spirits who are in that love of power turn their faces away from the Lord. They look with their eyes toward the West of the spiritual world. Because their bodies are turned this way, the East is behind them, the North is to their right, and the South is to their left. The East is behind them because they hate the Lord; the North is to their right because they love fallacies and the falsehoods derived from them; and the South is to their left because they reject the light of wisdom. They can turn around and around, yet everything they see around them appears in accordance with their love. All such spirits are "sensual-natural" <note>people who think only through their physical senses and ignore spiritual reality</note>; they think that they alone are truly alive and look at others as mere images. They believe themselves to be wiser than everyone else, even though they are in a state of insanity.

145. In the spiritual world, ways appear just like the ways or roads in the natural world. Some lead to heaven and some to hell; but the roads that lead to hell do not appear to those going to heaven, nor do the roads that lead to heaven appear to those going to hell. There are countless such ways, as there are roads leading to every society of heaven and every society of hell. Every spirit enters the path that leads to the society of their own love and does not even see the paths leading anywhere else. Consequently, every spirit moves in the same direction toward which they have turned their ruling love.

146. **THAT THE DIVINE LOVE AND THE DIVINE WISDOM, WHICH RADIATE FROM THE LORD AS A SUN AND CREATE HEAT AND LIGHT IN HEAVEN, IS THE PROCEEDING DIVINE, WHICH IS THE HOLY SPIRIT.** In the work titled ***The Doctrine of the New Jerusalem Concerning the Lord***, it has been shown that God is one in person and in essence, that there is a trinity in Him, and that this God is the Lord. It has also been shown that His trinity is called Father, Son, and Holy Spirit. The "Divine from whom all things originate" <note>original: "Divinum a quo," a Latin theological term for the Divine Source</note> is called the Father; the Divine Human is called the Son; and the Proceeding Divine <note>the Divine power or influence moving outward into the universe</note> is the...

# PART II. THE DIVINE LOVE.

146. **The Holy Spirit.** Although the Holy Spirit is called the "Divine proceeding," no one has known why it is called "proceeding." This has remained unknown because it was also unknown that the Lord appears before the angels as a sun, and that heat (which in its essence is divine love) and light (which in its essence is divine wisdom) proceed from that sun. Because these truths were unknown, it was impossible to realize that the "Divine proceeding" <term>Divine proceeding</term> <note>original: "Divine proceeding," referring to the active operation or "flow" of God's influence into the universe, rather than a separate person in a Trinity.</note> was not a separate divine being. Thus, the Athanasian doctrine <note>The Athanasian Creed is a classic statement of Christian belief that defines the Trinity as three distinct persons—Father, Son, and Holy Spirit—in one Godhead.</note> of the trinity declares that there is one person of the Father, another of the Son, and another of the Holy Spirit. However, when it is understood that the Lord appears as a sun, an accurate idea can be formed of the Divine proceeding, or the Holy Spirit: it is one with the Lord, yet it radiates from Him, just as heat and light radiate from the sun. This is why angels experience divine heat and divine light in the exact same proportion as they possess love and wisdom. No one who is unaware that the Lord appears in the spiritual world as a sun, and that His Divine Spirit radiates from Him in this way, could ever understand what is meant by "proceeding"—whether it merely means communicating things belonging to the Father and the Son, or if it means illuminating and teaching. Even so, there is no reason for enlightened logic to acknowledge the "Divine proceeding" as a separate god, or to make a distinction between them, when it is known that God is one and that He is omnipresent.

147. It was shown earlier that God is not in space, and that He is therefore omnipresent. It was also shown that the Divine is the same everywhere, but that it appears to vary in angels and humans because of their different ways of receiving it. Now, since the Divine—which radiates from the Lord as a sun—consists of light and heat, and since this light and heat flow first into universal recipients (which in the natural world are the atmospheres <term>atmospheres</term> <note>In this context, atmospheres refer to the layers of spiritual and natural air or ether that surround and permeate all things, acting as mediums for light and heat.</note>), and since these are the recipients of clouds, it follows that a person or angel acts as a receptacle for the Divine proceeding based on their "interiors" (the inner states of their understanding). These inner states can be overshadowed by spiritual clouds. By "clouds," I mean spiritual clouds, which are thoughts. If these thoughts are rooted in truths, they harmonize with divine wisdom; but if they are rooted in falsehoods <note>original: "falses," a term used to describe incorrect or deceptive principles.</note>, they disagree with it. Therefore, when thoughts based on truth are seen in the spiritual world, they appear as white clouds; however, thoughts based on false principles appear as black clouds. From this, it is clear that the Divine proceeding is indeed present in every person, but it is veiled differently by each individual.

148. Since the Divine itself is present in angels and humans through spiritual heat and light, it is said of those who possess the truths of divine wisdom and the goodness of divine love—when they are moved by them and think about them with affection—*that they grow warm with God*. This is sometimes even physically felt or perceived, such as when a preacher speaks with great zeal. Of these same people, it is also said *that they are illuminated by God*, because the Lord, through His Divine proceeding...

149. That the Holy Spirit is the same as the Lord, and that it is the essential truth which enlightens humanity, is evident from the following passages of the Word <note>Swedenborg refers to the Bible as "the Word."</note>: "Jesus said, when the *Spirit of truth* is come, he will guide you into all *truth*: he shall not speak on his own; but whatever he hears, that he shall speak." "He shall glorify me; for he shall receive what is mine, and shall show it to you" (John 16:13, 14, 15). "That he shall be with the disciples and in them" (John 17:26). "Jesus said, the words that I speak to you, they are spirit and they are life" (John 6:63). From these passages it is evident that the truth which <term>proceeds</term><note>original: "proceeds," referring to the spiritual radiation or flow from God.</note> from the Lord is called the Holy Spirit; it enlightens because it exists within the divine light.

150. The enlightenment which is attributed to the Holy Spirit is indeed from the Lord acting within a person, but it is nevertheless brought about through the mediation of spirits and angels. The exact nature of this mediation cannot yet be described, except to say that angels and spirits are by no means able to enlighten anyone by their own power, because they—just like human beings—are enlightened by the Lord alone. Because this is the case, all enlightenment comes from the Lord alone. It is communicated through the medium of angels or spirits because a person who is experiencing enlightenment is then placed in the company of such angels and spirits as receive more enlightenment from the Lord than others do.

151. ## THE LORD CREATED THE UNIVERSE AND EVERYTHING IN IT BY MEANS OF THE SUN, WHICH IS THE FIRST EMANATION OF THE DIVINE LOVE AND THE DIVINE WISDOM.

By "the Lord" is meant God from eternity, or Jehovah, who is called the Father and Creator, because He is one with Him, as was shown in the *Doctrine of the New Jerusalem Concerning the Lord*. Therefore, in the following pages, which also deal with creation, He is called "the Lord."

152. That all things in the universe were created from the divine love and the divine wisdom was fully shown in Part I (particularly in sections 52 and 53). We shall show here that this was accomplished by means of the sun, which is the first <term>proceeding</term> <note>original: "proceeding," meaning the first active expression or manifestation.</note> of the divine love and the divine wisdom. No one who is able to understand effects by looking at their causes—and then see how those effects stem from causes in their proper order and sequence—can deny that the sun is the starting point of creation. All things in the natural world <term>subsist</term> <note>original: "subsist," meaning to remain in existence or be sustained.</note> from it; and since they are sustained by it, they also originated from it. The one fact implies and testifies to the other. All things are under the sun's influence because the sun has established them so that they might exist <note>original: *posuit ut sint*</note>; and to keep them under its influence is to continually establish them so that they exist <note>original: *continue ponere*</note>. For this reason, it is also said that "subsistence is perpetual existence" <note>This is a philosophical principle stating that for something to continue existing, it must be continually "re-created" by its source.</note>. If anything were to be entirely withdrawn from the sun's influence acting through the atmospheres, it would...

...would be immediately dissolved. This is because the atmospheres—which become increasingly pure and are energized with power by the sun—hold all things together in their proper connection. Since the continued existence of the universe and everything within it depends on the sun, it is clear that the sun is the primary source of creation and that all other things originate from it. The expression "from the sun" actually means "from the Lord, by means of the sun," because the sun itself was also created by the Lord.

153. There are two suns through which all things were created by the Lord: the sun of the spiritual world and the sun of the natural world. All things were created by the Lord through the spiritual sun, but not through the natural sun. This is because the natural sun is far below the spiritual sun and sits at an intermediate distance; the spiritual world is above it, and the natural world is below it. The natural sun was created to serve as a medium or substitute; its role as a medium will be discussed in what follows.

154. The universe and everything in it were created by the Lord through the spiritual sun because that sun is the <term>proximate proceeding</term><note>The immediate radiance or first manifestation flowing directly from God.</note> of divine love and divine wisdom. As was shown previously (sections 52 to 82), all things originate from divine love and divine wisdom. In every created thing—whether in the largest or the smallest—there are three components: **end**, **cause**, and **effect**. There is no created thing in which these three do not exist. In the largest scale, or the universe, these three exist in the following order: the **end** of all things is in the sun, which is the immediate manifestation of divine love and divine wisdom; the **causes** of all things are in the spiritual world; and the **effects** of all things are in the natural world. How these three exist in the primary and final principles of things will be shown later. Since there is no created thing without these three, it follows that the universe and everything in it was created by the Lord through the sun, which contains the "end" of all things within it.

155. Creation cannot be explained in a way that can be understood unless the concepts of space and time are removed from one's thought; but if these are removed, it can be understood. Remove them if you can (or as much as you can) and keep your mind on an idea free from space and time. You will then perceive that the largest space and the smallest space do not differ at all. At that point, you cannot help but have a concept of the creation of the universe that is similar to the creation of the individual parts within the universe. You will also see that there is a diversity in created things because there are infinite things in the <term>God-Man</term><note>original: "God-Man," Swedenborg’s term for God, emphasizing that the Divine is the essential and infinite Human from whom all human qualities derive.</note>, and consequently countless things in the sun which is the immediate manifestation from Him. These countless things are reflected in the created universe. Therefore, no two things can ever be exactly the same; this explains the variety of all things seen in the natural world (along with space) and the appearance of variety in the spiritual world...

* That in **God-Man** <note>Swedenborg’s term for the Lord, emphasizing that God is the essential and infinite Human.</note>, infinite things are distinctly one (sections 17 to 22).

156. The creation of the universe, and of everything within it, cannot be said to have happened by moving from space to space or from time to time in a progressive or successive way. Instead, it was effected from eternity and infinity. This does not mean an eternity of time—for that does not exist—but an eternity independent of time, which is identical with the Divine. Similarly, it was not from an infinity of space—for that also does not exist—but from an infinity independent of space, which is also identical with the Divine. I know that these ideas transcend the thoughts of those who think only in **natural light** <note>Reasoning based solely on the physical world and the senses.</note>; however, they do not transcend the thoughts of those in **spiritual light**, for in that light, there is no concept of space or time. In fact, these ideas do not entirely exceed the grasp of natural light; for when it is said that there is no such thing as infinite space, everyone agrees with this through common reason. The same is true of eternity, which is the "infinite" of time. The expression "to eternity" is understood in terms of time, but "from eternity" cannot be understood unless the concept of time is removed.

157. **THAT THE SUN OF THE NATURAL WORLD IS PURE FIRE, AND THEREFORE DEAD; AND SINCE NATURE DERIVES ITS ORIGIN FROM THAT SUN, NATURE IS ALSO DEAD.** 
Creation itself cannot be attributed in the least to the sun of the natural world. Instead, it must be attributed entirely to the sun of the spiritual world. This is because the sun of the natural world is completely dead, while the sun of the spiritual world is alive, being the first **proceeding** <note>The immediate radiance or first manifestation flowing directly from God.</note> of divine love and divine wisdom. What is dead does not act on its own, but is acted upon. Therefore, to attribute any part of creation to the natural sun would be like attributing the work of a craftsman to the tool his hand uses. The sun of the natural world is pure fire, from which all life has been removed; but the sun of the spiritual world is fire that contains divine life. 

The angels' concept of the fire of these two suns is this: divine life is *internally* present in the fire of the spiritual sun, but it is only *externally* present in the fire of the natural sun. From this, it can be seen that the activity of the natural sun does not come from itself, but from the living power proceeding from the spiritual sun. Consequently, if the living power of the spiritual sun were withdrawn or taken away, the natural sun would perish. Hence

PART II. THE DIVINE LOVE. 157—162

<page-num>51</page-num>

...it is, that the worship of the sun is the lowest of all kinds of worship of a God; and therefore in **the Word** <note>original: "the Word"; referring to the Bible.</note> it is called an abomination.

158. Since the sun of the natural world is pure fire, and for that reason dead, the heat and light proceeding from it are also dead. By the same logic, the atmospheres—the **ether** <note>original: "æther"; a hypothesized substance once thought to fill upper space and carry light waves.</note> and the air—which receive and communicate the heat and light of that sun, are dead. Because they are dead, each and every thing in the world subject to them—which we call "earth"—is also dead. Nevertheless, each and every one of these things is surrounded by spiritual things that proceed and flow from the sun of the spiritual world. Unless they were surrounded in this way, the earth could not have been set in motion or made capable of producing forms of **uses** <note>In Swedenborg's philosophy, a "use" is the purpose or function for which something is created; it is the physical expression of divine love and wisdom.</note>, such as vegetables, or forms of life, such as animals; nor could the earth provide the materials necessary for the existence and survival of man.

159. Now, since nature begins from that sun, and everything that exists and is sustained by it is called "natural," it follows that nature, along with every single thing belonging to it, is dead. The appearance of nature as being alive in men and animals is due to the life which accompanies and animates nature.

160. Since the lowest substances of nature, which form the earths, are dead—and are not changeable or varied according to the state of one's affections and thoughts as they are in the spiritual world, but are instead fixed and unchanging—spaces and distances exist in nature. These things are the result of creation ending there and remaining in a state of rest. It is therefore evident that space is a property of nature. Since spaces in nature are not just "appearances of space" based on one's state of life (as they are in the spiritual world), they may also be called dead.

161. Since time is also fixed and constant, it is likewise a property of nature. The time of a day is constantly twenty-four hours, and the time of a year is constantly three hundred and sixty-five days and a quarter. Even the states of light and darkness, and of heat and cold, which vary these times, return constantly. The states that return daily are morning, noon, evening, and night; those that return yearly are spring, summer, autumn, and winter. Furthermore, the states of the year constantly modify the states of the days. Since none of these are **states of life** <note>Internal spiritual conditions of the heart and mind, which dictate the "environment" in the spiritual world.</note> as they are in the spiritual world, they are dead. In the spiritual world, there is continual light and continual heat; the light corresponds to the state of wisdom and the heat to the state of love among the angels. Because of this, their states are alive.

162. From this, we can see the foolishness of those who attribute everything to nature. Those who have convinced themselves in favor of nature bring themselves into such a condition that they no longer desire to elevate their minds above nature; for this...

<meta>This page concludes the discussion on how the human mind becomes "sensual-natural" by closing itself to the spiritual world, and begins a new section explaining the necessity of two suns—one living and one dead—for the process of creation.</meta>

<header>162—166 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; PART II.</header>

For this reason, their minds are closed to what is above and opened to what is below. Thus, they become "sensual-natural" <term>sensual-natural: a state where a person only believes and values what can be perceived by the five senses.</term>, or spiritually dead. Because they then think only from things they have learned through their physical senses, or from the world as perceived by those senses, they also deny God in their hearts. In this state, because the person's connection with heaven is broken, they form a connection with hell. They only retain the remaining faculties of thinking and willing: the faculty of thinking, which comes from **rationality**, and the faculty of willing, which comes from **liberty**. Every person receives these two faculties from the Lord, and they are never taken away. They are possessed by devils just as much as by angels; however, devils use them to make themselves insane and to do evil, whereas angels use them to become wise and to do good.

**163. WITHOUT TWO SUNS—ONE LIVING AND THE OTHER DEAD—CREATION CANNOT EXIST.** The universe in general is divided into two worlds: the spiritual and the natural. Angels and spirits live in the spiritual world, and human beings live in the natural world. In their external appearance, these two worlds are entirely alike—so much so that they cannot be distinguished. However, in their internal nature, they are entirely different. The people who are in the spiritual world (the angels and spirits) are spiritual; because they are spiritual, they think and speak spiritually. On the other hand, the people in the natural world are natural, and they think and speak naturally. Spiritual thought and speech have nothing in common with natural thought and speech. From this, it is evident that these two worlds, the spiritual and the natural, are entirely distinct from each other—so much so that they can never occupy the same space.

**164.** Now, because these two worlds are so distinct, there is a necessity for two suns: one from which all spiritual things proceed, and another from which all natural things proceed. Since all spiritual things are inherently alive at their origin, and all natural things are inherently dead at their origin, and since these suns are those respective origins, it follows that one sun is living and the other sun is dead. It also follows that the dead sun was created by the living sun by the power of the Lord.

**165.** A dead sun was created so that all things might be fixed, established, and constant in their **ultimates** <note>original: "ultimates"; in this philosophy, the "ultimates" are the lowest or most external manifestations of spiritual reality, such as physical matter.</note>, and so that there might be permanent and enduring existences. Creation is founded on this basis and no other. The Earth (or the "terraqueous globe" <note>original: "terraqueous globe"; a term for the earth consisting of land and water.</note>), in which, on which, and around which such things exist, serves as a foundation and support. It is the final work in which all things conclude and upon which they rest. It is also, as it were, the **matrix** <note>original: "matrix"; used here in the sense of a womb or a place where something is developed and produced.</note> from which effects—which are the ultimate goals of creation—are produced, as will be shown in the following sections.

**166.** It is evident that the Lord created all things through the living sun and not through the dead sun, because what is living can control and organize what is dead as it pleases, forming it for **uses** <note>In Swedenborg's philosophy, a "use" is the purpose or function for which something is created; it is the physical expression of divine love and wisdom.</note>.

<meta>This page continues the argument that nature is inherently dead and moves into a philosophical explanation of "ends, causes, and effects," establishing that the purpose of creation is a return to the Creator through these three stages.</meta>

<header>PART II. THE DIVINE LOVE.</header>

...which are its goals, but not the other way around <note>original Latin: *vice versa*</note>. No rational person can believe that all things, and even life itself, come from nature. Anyone who thinks this does not understand what life is. Nature cannot give life to anything, as it is completely inert <term>inert: lacking the power to move or act on its own</term> in and of itself. It is entirely contrary to the natural order for what is dead to act upon what is living, or for a dead power to act on a living power—which is the same as saying it is impossible for the natural to act upon the spiritual. Therefore, thinking this way goes against the light of sound reason. What is dead, or natural, may indeed be distorted or changed in many ways by external accidents; however, it still cannot act upon life. Instead, life acts upon it according to the changes made to its form. The same principle applies to **physical influx** <term>physical influx: the theory that the physical body or environment can produce thoughts and feelings in the soul; the author argues the opposite is true</term> into the spiritual operations of the soul. It is well known that this does not happen because it is simply not possible.

**167. THE PURPOSE OF CREATION—WHICH IS FOR ALL THINGS TO RETURN TO THE CREATOR AND FOR A CONNECTION TO BE FORMED—LIVES IN ITS FINAL STAGES.** It is helpful first to discuss "ends" or purposes. There are three things that follow in a specific order: these are called the "first end," the "middle end," and the "ultimate end" <term>ultimate end: the final result or outcome where a purpose is fully realized</term>. They are also known as **purpose**, **cause**, and **effect**. These three must exist in everything for it to be anything at all. A first purpose without a middle and a final purpose is impossible; in other words, a purpose cannot exist without a cause and an effect. Similarly, a cause cannot exist without the purpose from which it starts and the effect in which it is revealed. Nor can an effect exist on its own without a cause and a purpose. This can be understood if we consider that a purpose separated from its effect has no real existence and is merely an empty concept. For a purpose to be real, it must reach its conclusion, and it concludes in its effect. It is only in the effect that it is truly called a "purpose," because that is where it is fulfilled. The acting force <note>original: "agent or efficient"</note> might seem to exist on its own, but this is an appearance caused by the fact that it is active within its effect. If it were separated from the effect, it would vanish in a moment. Therefore, it is clear that these three—purpose, cause, and effect—must exist in everything to make it a reality.

168. Furthermore, it should be noted that the purpose is fully present in the cause and fully present in the effect. This is why purpose, cause, and effect are called the first, middle, and final purposes. But for the purpose to be fully present in the cause, there must be something derived from the purpose for it to inhabit. For it to be fully present in the effect, there must be something derived from the purpose, acting through the cause, for it to inhabit. A purpose cannot exist in itself alone—it must exist in something produced from itself. In that resulting thing, the purpose can exist in its entirety and, by acting, become effective until it reaches a stable state <note>original: "subsists"</note>. It reaches this stable state in the final purpose, which is called the effect.

169. In the created universe, in both its largest and its smallest parts, these three—purpose, cause, and effect—exist because they exist in God the Creator, who is the Lord from eternity. But since He...

<header>ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING THE DIVINE LOVE AND WISDOM: PART II.</header>

He is infinite, and infinite things within the Infinite are distinctly one (as was shown above in numbers 17 to 22). Therefore, these three—purpose, cause, and effect—in Him and in His infinite qualities are distinctly one. Because of this, the universe, which was created from His *esse* <term>esse: being or essence; the core reality of existence</term> and which, when viewed in terms of its uses, is His image, contains these three elements in every single part of it.

170. The universal purpose, which is the goal of all things in creation, is that there may be an eternal connection <note>original: "conjunction"</note> between the Creator and the created universe. This is impossible unless there are subjects in whom His Divine nature can exist as if in Himself, and where it can dwell and remain. For these subjects to be His homes and dwelling places, they must be able to receive His love and wisdom as if from themselves. Consequently, they must be such as to raise themselves toward the Creator as if by their own power and join themselves with Him. Without this back-and-forth interaction <note>original: "reciprocation"</note>, no connection can be achieved. These subjects are human beings, who can elevate and join themselves to God as if of their own accord. It has been shown many times above that humans are such subjects and that they are recipients of the Divine as if from themselves. Through this connection, the Lord is present in every work created by Him, because everything created is ultimately for the sake of humanity. Therefore, the uses of all created things rise by degrees from the lowest levels <note>original: "ultimates"</note> up to humanity, and through humanity back to God the Creator from whom they originated, as was shown above in numbers 65 to 68.

171. Creation is in a constant progression toward this final goal through these three principles: purpose, cause, and effect. This is because these three exist in the Lord the Creator, as was said before; and the Divine is present in all space without being limited by space (numbers 69 to 72), and is the same in both the greatest and the smallest things (numbers 77 to 82). From this, it is evident that the created universe, in its general progression toward its final goal, serves as the intermediate stage. Forms of use are continually raised up from the earth by the Lord the Creator in their proper order toward humanity, who, in terms of the body, is also from the earth. Next, humanity is elevated by receiving love and wisdom from the Lord, and every means is provided so that they may receive them; humans are made in such a way that they can receive these gifts if they choose. From what has been said, it can be seen (though only in a general way for now) that the purpose of creation—which is that all things may return to the Creator and a connection be formed—is realized in its final stages.

172. It is clear that purpose, cause, and effect are in each and every thing in creation when one considers that all effects, or final goals, become new "first" purposes in a continuous chain. This chain runs from the Lord the Creator (who is the first) to the union of humanity with Him (which is the last). The fact that all final goals become new "first" purposes is evident because nothing is so inactive or dead that it lacks any efficiency. Even sand gives off a certain quality <note>original: "principle"</note> that contributes to producing or achieving something.

<header>PART III. THE DIVINE LOVE. 173—176</header>

### PART III.

**173. IN THE SPIRITUAL WORLD, THERE ARE ATMOSPHERES, WATERS, AND EARTHS, JUST AS IN THE NATURAL WORLD; BUT THE FORMER ARE SPIRITUAL, WHILE THE LATTER ARE NATURAL.** It was mentioned in the preceding pages and shown in the work **ON HEAVEN AND HELL** that the spiritual world and the natural world are alike, except that everything in the spiritual world is spiritual and everything in the natural world is natural. Because these two worlds are similar, both contain atmospheres, waters, and earths. These are the general foundations <term>original: "generals"; the broad categories or basic elements of a system</term> through which all individual things exist in infinite variety.

174. The atmospheres—which are called ethers and airs—are similar in both the spiritual and natural worlds, though those in the spiritual world are spiritual and those in the natural world are natural. The spiritual atmospheres are spiritual because they originate from the sun which is the first proceeding <term>proceeding: that which flows out from or is an emitted expression of a source</term> of the Lord’s divine love and divine wisdom. From Him, they receive divine fire (which is love) and divine light (which is wisdom). They carry these two into the heavens where the angels live, making the presence of that sun felt in both the largest and smallest things there. 

The spiritual atmospheres are discrete substances <term>discrete substances: distinct, individual components that are not continuous with each other, like layers or particles</term>, or very tiny forms, coming from the sun. Because they each receive the sun, its fire is divided into many substances or forms. It is, so to speak, covered or enclosed in them and moderated by these coverings. In this way, it becomes heat that is eventually proportioned to the love of angels in heaven and of spirits below heaven; the same process applies to the light of the sun. The natural atmospheres are similar to spiritual ones because they are also discrete substances of a very tiny form, originating from the sun of the natural world. Each of these also receives its sun, stores its fire within, moderates it, and carries it as heat to the earth where humans live; the light is handled the same way.

175. The difference between spiritual and natural atmospheres is that spiritual atmospheres are receptacles of divine fire and divine light—and therefore of love and wisdom—because they contain these qualities within themselves. Natural atmospheres, however, are not receptacles of divine fire and light. Instead, they contain the fire and light of their own sun, which in itself lacks life (as was shown above). Therefore, they do not contain anything from the sun of the spiritual world, although they are still surrounded by the spiritual atmospheres that come from that sun. This difference between the two types of atmospheres is known from the wisdom of the angels.

176. That atmospheres exist in the spiritual world, as well as in the natural, is evident from the fact that angels and spirits breathe, speak, and hear just as humans do in the natural world...

It describes how spiritual atmospheres carry the Lord's love and wisdom (as heat and light) to the angels.</summary>

This is also shown by the fact that angels and spirits see just as clearly as people in the natural world do, and sight is impossible without an atmosphere purer than air. It is further proven by the fact that angels and spirits think and feel emotions just as people in the natural world do, and thought and affection cannot exist without even purer atmospheres. Finally, this is shown by the fact that everything belonging to the bodies of angels and spirits—both internal and external—is held together in its proper connection by atmospheres: their external features by an aerial atmosphere, and their internal features by ethereal <note>original: "æthereal"; referring to the higher, subtler regions of space beyond the clouds</note> atmospheres. If it were not for the surrounding pressure and action of these atmospheres, the interior and exterior forms of the body would clearly fall apart. Since the angels are spiritual, and their bodies as a whole and in every part are held in their connection, form, and order by atmospheres, it follows that those atmospheres are also spiritual. They are spiritual because they originate from the spiritual sun, which is the first proceeding <term>proceeding: that which flows out from or is an emitted expression of a source</term> of the Lord’s divine love and divine wisdom.

177. As mentioned above and shown in the work **ON HEAVEN AND HELL**, there are also waters and earths in the spiritual world just as there are in the natural world. However, the waters and earths of the spiritual world are spiritual. Because they are spiritual, they are moved and modified by the heat and light of the spiritual sun through the atmospheres that come from it—just as the water and earth in the natural world are acted upon and modified by the heat and light of their sun through its atmospheres.

178. Atmospheres, waters, and earths are spoken of here as the general foundations <term>original: "generals"; the broad categories or basic elements of a system</term> through which all individual things exist in infinite variety. The atmospheres are the active powers, the waters are the intermediate powers, and the earths are the passive powers from which all effects exist. These three function as powers in this sequence solely because of the life that flows from the Lord as a sun, which causes them to be active.

179. **THAT THERE ARE DEGREES OF LOVE AND WISDOM, AND CONSEQUENTLY DEGREES OF HEAT AND LIGHT, AND DEGREES OF ATMOSPHERES.** Unless it is known that degrees exist—and specifically what they are and what their nature is—the information that follows cannot be understood. There are degrees in every created thing and, consequently, in every form. Therefore, in this **PART OF THE ANGELIC WISDOM**, we shall discuss degrees. The existence of degrees of love and wisdom is clearly visible in the angels of the three heavens. The angels of the third heaven surpass the angels of the second heaven in love and wisdom, and those of the second surpass the angels of the first heaven. This difference is so great that they cannot live together; the degrees of love and wisdom distinguish and separate them. This is why the angels of the lower heavens cannot ascend to the angels of...

# PART III. THE DIVINE LOVE. 179—182

179. In the higher heavens, if angels from a lower level are allowed to ascend, they do not see the higher angels nor anything that surrounds them. This is because their love and wisdom exist in a higher degree, which transcends the perception of the lower angels. Every angel is their own love and their own wisdom; and love, combined with wisdom, takes the form of a human, because God—who is love itself and wisdom itself—is a Man. I have occasionally been allowed to see the angels of the lowest <term>original: "ultimate"; the final, outermost, or most physical layer in a sequence</term> heaven ascend to the angels of the third <note>The "third heaven" refers to the highest or most internal level of heaven in this theological framework.</note> heaven. When they reached that heaven, I heard them complaining that they saw no one, even though they were in the middle of those angels. Afterward, they were instructed that those higher angels were invisible to them because their love and wisdom were imperceptible to them, and it is love and wisdom that cause an angel to appear as a human.

180. That there are degrees of love and wisdom appears even more clearly when comparing the love and wisdom of angels to that of humans. It is well known that the wisdom of the angels is relatively indescribable <note>original: "ineffable"</note>; that it is also incomprehensible to people when they are focused on natural love will be seen in what follows. It appears indescribable and incomprehensible because it exists in a higher degree.

181. Since there are degrees of love and wisdom, there are also degrees of heat and light. By heat and light, I mean *spiritual* heat and light, such as the angels have in the heavens and such as humans have within the inner parts of their minds; for the heat of human love and the light of human wisdom are similar to the heat and light of the angels. In the heavens, the situation is this: the quality and quantity of love which the angels have determines the quality and quantity of their heat, and their light has the same relationship to their wisdom. This is because there is love within their heat and wisdom within their light, as was shown before. It is the same with people on earth, except that the angels actually feel that heat and see that light, whereas humans do not. This is because humans are currently in natural heat and light; and as long as this is the case, they do not feel spiritual heat except through a certain delight of love, nor do they see spiritual light except through a perception of truth. Now, since a person, while in natural heat and light, knows nothing of the spiritual heat and light within themselves—and since this cannot be known except by experience from the spiritual world—we shall speak here of the heat and light in which the angels and their heavens exist, as this is the only source of enlightenment on the subject.

182. The degrees of spiritual heat cannot, however, be described from experience, because love (to which spiritual heat corresponds) does not fit clearly into the categories of thought. But the degrees of spiritual light *can* be described, because light does fall under the categories of thought—for spiritual light is the essence of thought. From the degrees of light, the degrees of spiritual heat may be understood, for they are similar in degree. Now—

# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART III.

...as for spiritual light, in which the angels live, I have been allowed to see it with my own eyes. The light of the angels of the higher <term>original: "superior"</term> heavens is so bright that it cannot be described, not even by the whiteness of snow, and so glistening that it cannot be described even by the flaming rays of the sun in this world; in a word, it exceeds the sun’s noonday <note>original: "meridian"</note> light on earth a thousand times over. However, the light of the angels of the lower <term>original: "inferior"</term> heavens can be described to some extent by comparisons, yet it still exceeds the brightest light of our world. The light of the angels of the higher heavens cannot be described because their light is one with their wisdom; and as their wisdom is indescribable <note>original: "ineffable"</note> compared to the wisdom of humans, so is their light. From these few points, it may appear that there are degrees of light; and since wisdom and love correspond to each other in degree, it follows that there are similar degrees of heat.

183. Since atmospheres are the receptacles and containers <note>original: "continents"</note> of heat and light, it follows that there are as many degrees of atmospheres as there are degrees of heat and light, and as there are degrees of love and wisdom. The existence of several atmospheres, distinct from each other by degrees, has been revealed to me through much experience in the spiritual world. This is especially clear from the fact that the angels of the lower heavens cannot breathe in the region of the higher angels; they feel as if they are gasping for breath, just as animals do when raised out of air into ether <note>original: "æther"; in older science, the rarefied element filling the upper regions of space</note>, or out of water into air. Also, spirits below the heavens appear to those who are in the heavens as if they are in a mist. That there are several atmospheres, distinct from each other by degrees, can be seen above in section 176.

184. **THAT DEGREES ARE OF TWO KINDS: DEGREES OF ALTITUDE AND DEGREES OF LATITUDE.**

Knowledge of degrees is, as it were, the key to opening the causes of things and entering into them. Without this knowledge, almost nothing about causes can be understood. Without it, the objects and subjects of both worlds appear so uniform <note>original: "univoca"; literally "of one voice," meaning they seem to be of the same nature throughout</note> that they seem to contain nothing more than what the eye sees. However, compared to the things hidden deep within, what is seen is only as one to thousands, or even tens of thousands. These hidden internal things can in no way be discovered unless degrees are understood. This is because exterior things progress to interior things, and these to the innermost things, by degrees—not by continuous degrees, but by discrete degrees. 

Decreases <note>original: "decrements"</note> from thicker to finer, or from denser to rarer—or rather increases from finer to thicker, or from rarer to denser—like the transition from light to shade or heat to cold, are called **continuous degrees**. But **discrete degrees** are entirely different: they exist in the relationship of the first, the subsequent, and the last <note>original: "prior, posterior, and postreme"</note>, or of end, cause, and effect. They are called discrete degrees because the first exists by itself, the second by itself, and the last by...

It introduces the vital distinction between "continuous degrees" (gradual changes) and "discrete degrees" (the layered relationship of end, cause, and effect).</summary>

->### PART III. THE DIVINE LOVE. 184—186<-

...itself; but still, taken together, they make a single whole. The atmospheres which are called ether and air, from the highest to the lowest—or from the sun to the earth—are divided <term>original: "discriminated"</term> into such degrees. They exist as simple elements, then as groupings of these simple elements, and again as groupings of those groupings; taken together, these are called a "composite." These latter degrees are **discrete** because they exist distinctly, and they are understood as **degrees of altitude** (height). However, the former degrees are **continuous** because they increase or decrease gradually; these are understood as **degrees of latitude** (breadth).

185. Every single thing that exists in the spiritual and natural worlds co-exists through both discrete and continuous degrees—that is, from degrees of altitude and degrees of latitude. The dimension consisting of discrete degrees is called "altitude," and the one consisting of continuous degrees is called "latitude." Their position relative to our sight does not change these names. Without a knowledge of these degrees, nothing can be known of the difference between the three heavens, or of the difference between the love and wisdom of the angels there. One could not understand the difference between the heat and light in which they live, or the differences between the atmospheres that surround and contain them. 

Furthermore, without a knowledge of these degrees, nothing can be known of the differences between the interior faculties of the human mind, and therefore nothing of a person's state regarding reformation and regeneration <note>In this context, the process of spiritual rebirth or moral improvement</note>. One would also remain ignorant of the difference between the exterior faculties of the body (in both angels and humans), and would understand nothing at all of the difference between the "spiritual" and the "natural"—and therefore nothing about **correspondence** <note>The relationship between the natural world and the spiritual reality it represents</note>. Indeed, without this knowledge, one cannot see any difference in life between humans and beasts, or the difference between more perfect and less perfect animals, or the differences between the forms of the vegetable kingdom and the materials that make up the mineral kingdom. 

From this, it appears that those who are ignorant of these degrees cannot use their judgment to see **causes**. They only see **effects** and judge the causes based on them. This is usually done by assuming the cause is just a continuation of the effect; however, causes do not produce effects by continuity, but **discretely**. A cause is one thing, and an effect is another; there is a difference between them like that between the "prior" and the "posterior," or between the thing that forms and the thing that is formed.

186. So that the nature and quality of discrete degrees, and the difference between them and continuous degrees, may be better understood, let us take the angelic heavens as an example. There are three heavens, and these are distinct by degrees of altitude, so that one heaven is situated under another. They do not communicate with each other except by **influx** <note>original: "influx"; the spiritual process of life or influence flowing from a higher level to a lower one</note>, which proceeds from the Lord through the heavens in their proper order down to the lowest, and not the other way around <note>original: "vice versa"</note>. 

However, each heaven is also distinct within itself, not by degrees of altitude, but by degrees of latitude. Those who are in the middle, or in the center, are in the full light of wisdom, while those who are in the...

It is the same with people: the inner reaches of their minds are divided into as many levels as the angelic heavens, with one level placed above another. Therefore, the inner reaches of their minds are divided by **discrete degrees**, or **degrees of altitude**. Because of this, a person may be at the lowest level, a higher level, or the highest level, depending on their degree of wisdom. When someone is only at the lowest level, the higher level is closed; this is opened as the person receives wisdom from the Lord. There are also levels of continuity or **degrees of latitude** in a person, just as there are in heaven. A human being is similar to the heavens because, regarding the inner reaches of the mind, a person is a heaven in its smallest form, so long as they are in love and wisdom from the Lord. (That a person is a heaven in its smallest form, regarding the inner reaches of the mind, can be seen in the work *Heaven and Hell*, numbers 51 to 58).

**187.** From these few points, it appears that anyone who knows nothing about discrete degrees or degrees of altitude can know nothing about the state of a person regarding **reformation** and **regeneration**. These processes are brought about by receiving divine love and divine wisdom from the Lord and by the resulting opening of the mind's inner levels in their proper order. Nor can such a person know anything about **influx** <term>The spiritual flow of life and influence from God through the heavens into the world</term> or the order in which humanity was created. If someone thinks about these things only in terms of continuous degrees or degrees of latitude, rather than discrete degrees, they can only see effects and never the underlying causes. Seeing only from effects leads to illusions <note>original: "fallacies"</note>, which cause one error after another. These errors can be so multiplied through faulty reasoning that eventually massive falsehoods might be called truths.

**188.** As far as I am aware, nothing has been understood until now regarding discrete degrees or degrees of altitude; only continuous degrees or degrees of latitude have been known. Yet without a knowledge of both types of degrees, the nature of a "cause" can never be truly known. We will therefore discuss them throughout this entire Part. The purpose of this short work is to uncover causes so that effects can be seen through them. In this way, the darkness in which the person of the church is involved concerning God, the Lord, and spiritual things in general, may be cleared away. I can declare that the angels are saddened by the darkness that prevails on earth. They say that light is hardly visible anywhere, and that people embrace and reinforce illusions, thereby piling falsehood upon falsehood. To justify these errors, they use reasoning based on falsehoods and distorted truths to create fabrications <note>original: "figments"</note> that cannot be easily dismantled—so great is the darkness concerning causes and the ignorance concerning truths. They especially grieve over the arguments used to support "faith separate from charity" and "justification..."

### 189. DEGREES OF ALTITUDE ARE HOMOGENEOUS AND DERIVED FROM ONE ANOTHER IN A SERIES, LIKE END, CAUSE, AND EFFECT.

**189.** Degrees of latitude (or "continuous degrees") are like the gradual transition from light to shade, heat to cold, hard to soft, dense to rare, or coarse to <term>subtle</term> <note>original: "subtile"</note>. These degrees are well known through our senses and physical experience. However, the degrees of altitude—also called <term>discrete degrees</term> <note>Discrete degrees refer to distinct levels of existence that are separate from one another, like the relationship between a thought and the action it produces, rather than a gradual change in intensity.</note>—are not as easily recognized. Therefore, in this Part, we shall treat especially of the latter; for without a knowledge of these degrees, the true causes of things cannot be seen. 

It is indeed well known that end, cause, and effect follow in a specific order, like first, middle, and last. It is also known that the "end" (the purpose) produces the "cause," and through that cause, the "effect" is created so that the purpose may exist. Many other things are also known on this subject. Nevertheless, to know these things only in theory without seeing how they apply to real existence is only to understand abstractions. Such ideas remain in the mind only as long as one is thinking about analytical or metaphysical matters. This is why, although ends, causes, and effects move through discrete degrees, almost nothing is known about those degrees in the world. Bare knowledge of things in the abstract is like something floating in the air, which is soon dispersed; but if abstract concepts are applied to things in the physical world, they become like visible objects and remain in the memory.

**190.** All things that exist in the world which have three dimensions, or which are called "compounds," consist of degrees of altitude or discrete degrees. To illustrate this with an example: it is well known from physical observation that every muscle in the human body consists of very minute fibres. When these are <term>fasciculated</term> <note>original: "fasciculated"; meaning bundled together like a sheaf of grain.</note>, they constitute the larger ones called "moving fibres," and bundles of these produce the compound structure called a muscle. 

It is the same with the nerves: very small nerve fibres are put together into larger ones which appear like filaments, and a collection of such filaments produces the nerve itself. The same logic applies to all other <term>structures</term> <note>original: "compaginations"</note>, <term>groupings</term> <note>original: "confasciculations"</note>, and collections that make up the organs and internal parts; for these are compounds of fibres and vessels fashioned by similar levels. This is also true of every single thing in the vegetable kingdom and the mineral kingdom. In wood, there is a structure of filaments in a three-fold order; in metals and stones, there is a <term>clustering</term> <note>original: "conglobation"</note> of parts, also in a three-fold order. These examples show the nature of discrete degrees: one is formed from another, and by means of the second, a third (or composite) is formed; yet each degree remains distinct from the others.

**191.** Therefore, we may form conclusions about things that are invisible, for the principle is the same. Just as with...

Consequently, since there are degrees of love and wisdom, there must also be degrees of heat and light; for the <term>nature of existence</term> <note>original Latin: "ratio"</note> of the latter does not differ from that of the former.

192. From what has now been said, it is evident that these degrees are <term>homogeneous</term> <note>Of the same kind, nature, or origin.</note>—that is, they share the same essence and nature. The moving fibers of the muscles—from the smallest to the larger and largest—are homogeneous. The nervous fibers—from the smallest to the largest—are homogeneous. The filaments of wood, from the smallest to the combined structure, are homogeneous. So are stony and metallic parts of all kinds. The organic substances that are the receptacles and homes of thoughts and affections—from the most simple to the collective mass which is the brain—are homogeneous. The atmospheres, from pure <term>ether</term> <note>original: "æther"; historically, this referred to a subtle substance thought to fill the upper regions of space beyond the clouds.</note> to air, are homogeneous. The degrees of heat and light in their sequence, following the degrees of the atmospheres, are homogeneous; and therefore, the degrees of love and wisdom are also homogeneous. Those things which do not share the same essence and nature are <term>heterogeneous</term> <note>Of a different kind or nature; not matching.</note> and do not align with those that are homogeneous. Therefore, they cannot form <term>discrete degrees</term> <note>Distinct levels of existence, like the relationship between a thought and the action it produces, rather than a gradual change in intensity.</note> with them, but only with things like themselves that share the same essence and nature.

193. It is evident that these degrees, in their order, act as ends, causes, and effects; for the first (or smallest) produces its cause through the intermediate degree, and its effect through the final degree.

194. It should be observed that every degree is distinguished from another by its own specific coverings, and all the degrees together are distinguished by a common covering. This common covering communicates with the inner and inmost levels in their proper order; because of this, there is a connection and a unified action among all the degrees.

195. ### THAT THE FIRST DEGREE IS ALL IN ALL IN THE SUBSEQUENT DEGREES. 

For the degrees of every subject and every thing are homogeneous, and they are homogeneous because they are produced from the first degree. Their formation is such that the first degree produces the second by <term>bundling together</term> <note>original: "confasciculation"</note> or <term>gathering into a mass</term> <note>original: "conglobation"</note>—in a word, by clustering together—and through the second, it produces the third. It distinguishes each from the other by a layer or covering placed over them. From this, it is clear that the first degree is the principal and sole governing power in the subsequent degrees; consequently, the first degree is "all in all" within the following degrees.

197. In all these things, the first principle is the only governing force in what follows—in fact, it is the only essence within them; and being so, it is everything in them. This is also evident from things that are well known: namely, that the goal (the "end") is the entire essence of the cause, and through the cause, it is the entire essence of the effect. Therefore, end, cause, and effect are called the first, the middle, and the final end. Likewise, the cause of the cause is also the cause of the thing produced; and there is nothing essential in causes except the goal, and nothing essential in motion except effort <note>original: "conatus"; in this context, it refers to a directed force or striving toward an action.</note>. Furthermore, there is only one substance which is substance in and of itself.

198. From these considerations, it may be clearly seen that from the Divine—which is substance in itself, or the only and sole substance—every single created thing exists. Thus, God is all in all in the universe, according to what was demonstrated in Part I: for instance, that divine love and divine wisdom are a substance and a form (numbers 40 to 43); that divine love and divine wisdom are substance and form in themselves, and therefore the self-subsisting and only-subsisting Being (numbers 44 to 46); that all things in the universe were created from divine love and divine wisdom (numbers 52 to 54); that consequently the created universe is a reflection of Him (numbers 61 to 65); and that the Lord alone is heaven, where the angels live (numbers 113 to 118).

199. **THAT ALL PERFECTIONS INCREASE AND ASCEND WITH AND ACCORDING TO DEGREES.** It was shown above (numbers 184 to 188) that there are two kinds of degrees: degrees of latitude <note>Often called "continuous degrees," these represent a gradual change, like a spectrum from light to dark.</note> and degrees of altitude <note>Often called "discrete degrees," these represent distinct levels of existence, like the relationship between a thought and the word spoken.</note>. The degrees of latitude are like light fading into shadow, or wisdom fading into ignorance; but the degrees of altitude are like end, cause, and effect, or like what is prior, posterior, and final <note>original: "postreme"; the very last or ultimate level in a sequence.</note>. These latter degrees are said to "ascend" or "descend" because they are degrees of height (altitude); but the former are said to "increase" or "decrease" because they are degrees of width (latitude). These two types of degrees differ so much that they have nothing in common; therefore, they ought to be understood as distinct and by no means confused with one another.

200. All perfections increase and ascend with and according to these degrees because all qualities <term>predicates</term> <note>In logic, a "predicate" is a quality or property attributed to a subject.</note> follow the nature of the things they describe <term>subjects</term>; and perfection and imperfection are general qualities, for they are attributed to life, to powers, and to forms. *Perfection of life* is the perfection of love and wisdom; and as the will and the understanding are the containers <note>original: "receptacles"</note> of love and wisdom, perfection of life...

200. ...is also the perfection of the will and understanding, and from there, of the affections and thoughts. **Perfection of powers** is the perfection of all things that are put into motion and acted upon by life, without having life themselves. Such powers include the atmospheres in their actual operations; the inner and outer organic substances in humans and in all kinds of animals; and all things in the natural world that receive their activity directly or indirectly from the natural sun.

**Perfection of forms** and **perfection of powers** act as one, for as the powers are, so are the forms. The only difference is that forms are substances, while powers are their activities; therefore, they both share similar degrees of perfection. Even forms that are not simultaneously acting as powers are perfect according to their degrees.

201. I will not speak here of the perfections of life, powers, and forms increasing or decreasing according to **degrees of latitude** or **continuous degrees** <note>Continuous degrees are like the gradual fading of light to shade or heat to cold.</note>, because these degrees are already known in the world. Instead, I will speak of the perfections of life, powers, and forms ascending or descending according to **degrees of altitude** or **discrete degrees** <note>Discrete degrees are distinct levels, like the relationship between cause and effect or the steps of a ladder.</note>, because these degrees are not known in the world.

The way perfections ascend and descend according to these degrees can be understood only slightly from visible things in the natural world, but it can be seen clearly from visible things in the spiritual world. All that is discovered from visible things in the natural world is that the more closely they are examined, the more wonderful are the things that appear—such as in the eyes and ears, the tongue, the muscles, the heart, lungs, liver, pancreas, kidneys, and other internal organs <note>original: "viscera"</note>, as well as in seeds, fruits, flowers, metals, minerals, and stones. It is well known that in all these objects, more wonderful things present themselves to the sight the more deeply they are examined. Nevertheless, it has been little understood from these observations that these things are more perfect on the inside according to **degrees of altitude** or **discrete degrees**; for the ignorance of these degrees has hidden such knowledge. But since these same degrees exist clearly in the spiritual world (the whole of that world, from highest to lowest, being distinctly divided according to them), knowledge of them can be obtained there. Afterward, conclusions may be drawn regarding the perfections of powers and forms that exist in similar degrees in the natural world.

202. In the spiritual world, there are three heavens arranged according to degrees of altitude. In the highest heaven, the angels are superior in every perfection to the angels in the middle heaven; and in the middle heaven, the angels are superior in every perfection to the angels of the lowest heaven. These degrees of perfection are such that the angels of the lowest heaven cannot even ascend to...

...nor do these reach the first limit of the perfections of the angels of the middle heaven, nor do those reach the first limit of the perfections of the angels of the supreme heaven. It has been made known to me by experience that there is such a difference between the affections, thoughts, and consequently the speech of the angels of the superior and inferior heavens that they have nothing in common. Communication between them is achieved only through **correspondences** <note>The relationship between different levels of reality where a higher spiritual cause is reflected in a lower natural effect.</note>, which exist by the Lord’s **immediate influx** <note>The direct spiritual flow of life from God.</note> into all the heavens, and by **mediate influx** through the supreme heaven into the lowest heaven. 

These differences are of such a nature that they cannot be expressed or described in natural language. Because the thoughts of angels are spiritual, they do not fit into natural ideas; they can only be expressed and described by the angels themselves in their own language, words, and writings, and not by human ones. For this reason, it is said that the things heard and seen in heaven are **ineffable** <note>Too great or extreme to be expressed or described in words.</note>. 

These differences may be understood to some extent in this way: the thoughts of the angels of the supreme (or third) heaven are thoughts of **ends**; the thoughts of the angels of the middle (or second) heaven are thoughts of **causes**; and the thoughts of the angels of the lowest (or first) heaven are thoughts of **effects**. It should be observed that it is one thing to think *from* ends, and another to think *of* ends; similarly, it is one thing to think *from* causes, and another to think *of* causes; and it is one thing to think *from* effects, and another to think *of* effects. The angels of the lower heavens think *of* causes and of ends, but the angels of the superior heavens think *from* causes and from ends. Thinking *from* these is a quality of superior wisdom, while thinking *of* them is a quality of inferior wisdom. To think from ends is a quality of **wisdom**; to think from causes is a quality of **intelligence**; and to think from effects is a quality of **knowledge** <note>original: "science"</note>. From this, it is evident that all perfection ascends and descends along with these degrees and according to them.

203. Since a person's inner nature—which belongs to their will and understanding—is similar to the heavens regarding these degrees (for a person’s mind is a heaven in its smallest form), their perfections are also similar. However, these perfections do not appear to anyone during their life in the world, because at that time they are in the lowest degree, and from the lowest degree the higher degrees cannot be known. But after death, they are known. A person then enters the degree that corresponds to their love and wisdom, for they become an angel and think and speak of things that are ineffable to the natural person. There is then an elevation of everything in the mind, not in a simple ratio, but in a **triplicate ratio** <note>A mathematical term here meaning the perfection increases exponentially—like jumping from a flat surface to a 3D volume—rather than just increasing in size.</note>. The degrees of **altitude** follow the triplicate ratio, while the degrees of **latitude** follow the simple ratio. However, no one ascends or is elevated to those degrees except those who, while in the world, were in truths and applied them to their lives.

<meta>This page continues the discussion on degrees of perfection and the structural relationship between the different levels of heaven and the human mind. It explains how the "higher" spiritual levels are actually more perfect and purer than the "lower" material levels.</meta>

204. It appears as if earlier <note>original: "prior"</note> things were less perfect than later <note>original: "posterior"</note> things, or that simple things were less perfect than complex ones. Nevertheless, the earlier things from which later ones are formed—or the simple things from which compounds are made—are actually the more perfect. This is because earlier or simple things are "more naked" (meaning they are purer and less covered by lifeless matter) and are, in a sense, more divine. Therefore, they are closer to the spiritual sun where the Lord is. 

Perfection itself resides in the Lord, and from Him in the sun, which is the first manifestation of His divine love and divine wisdom. From there, perfection flows into those things that immediately follow, and so on in order down to the lowest things, which become more imperfect as their distance from the source increases. If there were not such an extraordinary perfection in things that are earlier and simple, neither man nor any animal could exist or survive from a seed. Nor could the seeds of trees and fruits grow and become productive. Every earlier thing is more protected from harm in proportion to its priority and simplicity, because it is more perfect.

205. **THAT IN SUCCESSIVE ORDER THE FIRST DEGREE CONSTITUTES THE HIGHEST, AND THE THIRD THE LOWEST; BUT THAT IN SIMULTANEOUS ORDER THE FIRST DEGREE CONSTITUTES THE INMOST, AND THE THIRD THE OUTMOST.**

There is successive order and simultaneous order. The successive order of these degrees is from highest to lowest, or from top to bottom. The angelic heavens are arranged in this order: the third heaven is the highest, the second is the middle, and the first is the lowest; such is their relative position. The states of love and wisdom among the angels exist in a similar successive order, as do the heat, light, and spiritual atmospheres. All the perfections of forms and powers there follow this same order. 

When **discrete degrees** <note>Also called degrees of altitude, these are distinct levels of existence—like the difference between a thought and a spoken word—rather than a gradual change in intensity.</note> are in successive order, they may be compared to a column divided into three levels used for ascending and descending. In the upper level are the things that are most perfect and beautiful; in the middle are those that are less perfect and beautiful; and in the lowest are those that are even less so. 

However, "simultaneous order," which consists of similar degrees, has a different appearance. In this arrangement, the things that were "highest" in successive order (which, as mentioned, are the most perfect and beautiful) are in the **inmost** center. The middle things remain in the middle, and the "lowest" things are in the circumference (the outer edge). 

They exist as if in a solid substance consisting of those three degrees: in the center are the most subtle parts; around the center are parts that are less subtle; and in the extremes, which form the outer edge, there are parts composed of the others, which are consequently coarser. It is as if the column mentioned above collapsed into a flat plane: its highest part would become the inmost center, its middle part would stay in the middle, and its lowest part would form the outer edge.

206. Since the highest point of successive order is the inmost part of...

207. In every final form <note>original: "ultimate"</note> there are distinct <note>original: "discrete"</note> degrees in simultaneous order. The moving fibers in every muscle, the fibers in every nerve, and the fibers and vessels in every internal organ <note>original: "viscus"</note> and body part are arranged in this way. In their innermost center are the most simple and perfect elements, from which their outermost layer is composed. A similar order of these degrees exists in every seed, every fruit, and in every metal and stone. The parts that make up the whole are structured this way: the innermost, middle, and outermost principles of these parts exist in those degrees. They are essentially successive compositions—or the bundling together <term>original: confasciculations</term> and gathering into masses <term>original: conglobations</term>—of "simples," which are their first substances or materials.

208. In short, such degrees exist in every final result, and thus in every effect. For every final result consists of prior things, and those consist of their own original sources. Every effect consists of a cause, and every cause consists of an end <note>a goal or purpose</note>. As was demonstrated above, the end is the very essence of the cause, and the cause is the very essence of the effect. The end constitutes the innermost, the cause the middle, and the effect the final result. We will see in the following sections that the same is true for the degrees of love and wisdom, of heat and light, and for the organic forms of human affections and thoughts. The series of these degrees in both successive and simultaneous order is also discussed in ***The Doctrine of the New Jerusalem Concerning the Sacred Scripture***, section 38, and elsewhere, where it is shown that similar degrees exist in every single part of the Word.

209. **THAT THE FINAL DEGREE IS THE CONTAINER, THE ENCLOSURE, AND THE FOUNDATION OF THE PRIOR DEGREES.**
The doctrine of degrees presented in this part has so far been illustrated by various things that exist in both the spiritual and natural worlds. We have looked at the degrees of the heavens where the angels live, the degrees of heat and light there, the degrees of the atmospheres, and various things in the human body and the animal and mineral kingdoms. But this doctrine has a much broader reach; it extends not only to natural things, but to civil, moral, and spiritual things, in their entirety and in every part. There are two reasons why the doctrine of degrees applies to such things. First, because in everything that can be described or defined, there is a "trine" <note>a three-fold structure</note> called end, cause, and effect, and these three relate to each other according to degrees of height <note>original: "altitude"</note>. Second, because anything civil, moral, or spiritual is not just an abstract idea, but is a substance. Just as love and wisdom are not abstract things but are actual substance (as shown above in sections 40 to 43), the same is true for all civil, moral, and spiritual things. These may indeed be thought of as separate from physical substances, but in themselves, they are not abstract; for—
