# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART III.

209. For example, affection and thought, charity and faith, and the will and understanding are not mere abstract ideas. The case with these is the same as with love and wisdom: they do not exist outside of their subjects, which are actual substances. Instead, they are the states of those subjects or substances. By substance, we also mean "form," because no substance can exist without a form.

210. Because the will, understanding, affection, thought, charity, and faith have often been thought of in the abstract—separated from the substances that are their subjects—the correct understanding of them has been lost. That correct idea is that they are states of substances or forms, exactly like sensations and actions, which are not things separate from the organs of sensation and movement. If they were separated from these organs, they would be nothing but mental figments, like sight without an eye, hearing without an ear, or taste without a tongue.

211. All civil, moral, and spiritual matters progress by degrees, just as natural things do. They do this not only by continuous degrees <note>degrees of gradual change, like light fading to dark</note>, but also by discrete degrees <note>distinct levels or layers, like the difference between a purpose and the final action</note>. Since the progression of discrete degrees is like the progression of goals <note>original: "ends"</note> to causes, and of causes to effects, I have chosen to illustrate and confirm this subject (that the final degree is the sum total, container, and foundation of the prior degrees) using the examples mentioned above: love and wisdom, the will and understanding, affection and thought, and charity and faith.

212. It is clearly evident from the progression of goals and causes toward effects that the final <note>original: "ultimate"</note> degree is the sum total <note>original: "complex"</note>, the container <note>original: "continent"</note>, and the foundation <note>original: "basis"</note> of the earlier degrees. Reason, when enlightened, can understand that an effect is the container and foundation of its causes and goals. However, it is not always clearly understood that the goal—along with everything belonging to it—and the cause—with everything belonging to it—actually exist within the effect, and that the effect is their full and complete sum. That this is the case is clear from what has been stated in this Part, particularly that one thing comes from another in a three-part series, and that the effect is nothing other than the cause in its final form. Since the final form is the sum total, it follows that the final form is also the container and the foundation.

213. Regarding love and wisdom: love is the goal <note>the "end" or purpose</note>, wisdom is the instrumental cause, and "use" <note>the practical outcome</note> is the effect. Use is the sum total, container, and foundation of both wisdom and love. Use is such a complete container that it actually contains the entirety of love and the entirety of wisdom, being their simultaneous existence. However, it should be noted that all elements of love and wisdom that are harmonious and in agreement exist within that "use," according to what was shown earlier in articles 189 to 194.

214. Affection, thought, and action follow a similar series of degrees, because all affection relates to love, all thought relates to wisdom, and all action relates to use. Similarly, charity, faith, and good works follow this same series...

<summary>This page explains that spiritual qualities like love and wisdom are not abstract concepts but exist as states of actual substances. It also demonstrates that the "final result" (use) of any process acts as the container and foundation for the goals and causes that produced it.</summary>

# PART III. THE DIVINE LOVE. 214—216

of similar degrees; for <term>charity</term> <note>original: "charity" – here meaning the active love for others</note> belongs to <term>affection</term>, <term>faith</term> belongs to <term>thought</term>, and <term>good works</term> belong to <term>action</term>. The <term>will</term>, <term>understanding</term>, and <term>exercise</term> <note>original: "exercise" – the outward practice of one’s inner state</note> are in a series of similar degrees; for the <term>will</term> comes from <term>love</term> and therefore from <term>affection</term>, the <term>understanding</term> comes from <term>wisdom</term> and therefore from <term>faith</term>, and <term>exercise</term> comes from <term>use</term> <note>original: "use" – the practical benefit or function performed</note> and therefore from <term>work</term>. Therefore, just as the entirety of <term>wisdom</term> and <term>love</term> exists in <term>use</term>, so the entirety of <term>thought</term> and <term>affection</term> exists in <term>action</term>, the entirety of <term>faith</term> and <term>charity</term> exists in <term>good works</term>, and so on; but they must all be homogeneous—that is, in agreement with one another.

**215.** It is not yet known that the final stage of each series—which is <term>use</term>, <term>action</term>, <term>work</term>, and <term>exercise</term>—is the sum and container <note>original: "complex and continent" – the total assembly and the vessel that holds it</note> of all prior principles. It appears as if there were nothing more in <term>use</term>, <term>action</term>, <term>work</term>, and <term>exercise</term> than mere motion. Nevertheless, all the prior principles are actually within them, so fully that nothing is missing; they are included in them like wine in a vessel, or like household goods in a house. They do not appear because they are only viewed externally, and in this view, they seem to be only activities and motions. 

It is like when the arms and hands move: it is not obvious that a thousand moving fibers work together in each motion, or that a thousand elements of thought and affection correspond to these thousand moving fibers, which stimulate them but do not appear to any of the bodily senses because they act internally. It is well known that nothing is done in the body, or by it, except from the will by the thought. As both of these act together, every single thing of the will and thought must necessarily exist in action, for they cannot be separated. This is why judgment is formed regarding a person’s will or intention based on their actions or works. It has been made clear to me that the angels, from a person’s action or work alone, perceive and see everything of the will and thought of the doer. The angels of the third heaven perceive from his will the goal <note>original: "end"</note> for which he acts, and the angels of the second heaven perceive the cause by which that goal operates. This is why, in the Word <note>referring to the Bible</note>, works and actions are so often commanded, and why it is said that a person is known by them.

**216.** It is a tenet of angelic wisdom that unless the will and the understanding (or affection and thought, as also charity and faith) clothe and involve themselves in works or actions whenever possible, they are only like airy things which pass away, or like phantoms <note>original: *imagines* – mental images or appearances</note> in the air which vanish. They only remain with a person and become principles of his life when he practices and does them, because the final stage is the sum, container, and foundation of prior things. Such an airy vapor and phantom is faith separate from good works, and such also are faith and charity without their practice. The only difference is that those who establish faith and charity as principles that must be joined together have the knowledge and may have the will to do good; however, those who remain in faith separate from charity do not.

### 217. THAT THE DEGREES OF HEIGHT ARE IN THEIR FULLNESS AND POWER WHEN IN THEIR FINAL STATE.

It was shown in the previous section that the <term>ultimate</term> <note>original: "ultimate" – the final, lowest, or most external level of a process</note> degree is the assembly and container of the previous degrees. From this, it follows that the prior degrees exist in their fullness within their final state; for they are present in their effect, and every effect is the fullness of its causes.

218. The fact that ascending and descending degrees—which are called "prior" and "posterior," or <term>degrees of altitude</term> <note>original: "degrees of altitude" – also called discrete degrees; these are distinct levels (like end, cause, and effect) rather than a gradual scale</note>—are at their full power in their final state, can be confirmed by all the evidence provided from sensory and observable things in the previous pages. However, here I choose to confirm them only by looking at effort, power, and motion in both non-living and living subjects. 

It is well known that <term>effort</term> <note>original: "endeavor" (Latin: *conatus*) – the internal impulse or tendency to act that precedes movement</note> by itself does nothing, but it acts through powers that correspond to it, and through those powers, it produces motion. Therefore, that effort is the "all-in-all" within the powers, and through those powers, it is the "all-in-all" within the motion. Since motion is the final degree of effort, it is through motion that effort achieves its effectiveness. 

Effort, power, and motion are connected only according to degrees of height. Their conjunction is not through continuity (like a smooth gradient), for they are distinct (discrete) levels; instead, they are joined by <term>correspondences</term> <note>original: "correspondences" – the relationship where a physical action perfectly represents a spiritual cause</note>. Effort is not power, and power is not motion; rather, power is produced by effort (being effort in an active state), and motion is produced by power. Therefore, there is no <term>potency</term> <note>original: "potency" – the actual capacity to produce an effect</note> in effort alone, or in power alone, but only in motion, which is their final product. This might still seem doubtful because it has not yet been illustrated by applying it to things we can see and feel in nature; nevertheless, this is how they progress into active power.

219. Let us apply these principles to living effort, living power, and living motion. In a human being, who is a living subject, "living effort" is the **will** united to the **understanding**. The "living powers" in a person are what constitute the internal structures of the body; in all of these, there are moving fibers woven together in various ways. Finally, "living motion" in a person is **action**, which is produced through those powers by the will united to the understanding. 

The internal workings of the will and understanding constitute the first degree; the internal structures of the body constitute the second; and the whole body, which is their assembly, constitutes the third degree. It is a well-known fact that the internal aspects of the mind have no power except through the powers in the body, and those powers have no strength except through the body's actions. 

These three levels do not act by continuity, but discretely; and to act discretely is to act by correspondences. The internal parts of the mind correspond to the internal parts of the body, and the internal parts of the body correspond to its exterior parts, through which actions exist. Therefore, the higher levels exert their power by means of the body's exterior. *It may seem as if effort and powers in a person have some potency even when there is no action—such as during dreams or while resting—but in these cases, the direction of efforts and*...

<summary>This page explains that "discrete degrees" (effort, power, and motion) only reach their full effectiveness and power in their final, outward expression. In humans, this means that the mind's will and understanding only have actual power when they are expressed through the physical actions of the body.</summary>

219. These powers rely on the shared moving principles of the body, which are the heart and lungs; but when the action of these organs stops, the powers also cease, and the <term>effort</term> <note>original: "endeavor" (Latin: *conatus*) – the internal impulse or intention to act</note> vanishes along with them.

220. Since the whole body has directed its powers mainly toward the arms and the hands—which are the <term>ultimates</term> <note>the final, outermost expression of a process</note>—therefore the arms and hands signify "power" in the Word <note>The "Word" refers to the Sacred Scriptures or the Bible</note>, and the right hand signifies "superior power." Because the unfolding and exercise of these degrees into power happens this way, the angels who are with a person (and who are in <term>correspondence</term> <note>The spiritual law that the physical world mirrors and responds to the spiritual world</note> with everything belonging to that person) can know the person's state of understanding and will from their physical actions alone, performed by the hands. They can also know the person’s state regarding charity and faith, and consequently the internal life of their mind and the external life that flows from it into the body. 

I have often wondered how the angels have such knowledge from the mere physical actions of the hands, but it has occasionally been proven through vivid experience. I was told that this is why <term>inauguration</term> <note>original: "inauguration" – here meaning ordination or appointment to a religious office</note> into the ministry is performed by the laying on of hands, and why touching with the hand signifies communication of spirit, among other similar things. From this, it may be concluded that the whole of charity and faith exists within "works" (actions); and that charity and faith without works are like rainbows around the sun, which quickly vanish and are scattered by a cloud. This is why "works" are so often mentioned in the Word, and why we are told we must do them, and that our salvation depends on them. Furthermore, he who does them is called a wise man, and he who does not is called a foolish man. But it must be observed that by "works" here, I mean <term>uses</term> <note>In this context, "uses" are actions performed for the benefit of others or society</note> which are actually carried out; for the whole of charity and faith exists within and according to those uses. This relationship exists because the connection with uses is spiritual, but it is carried out through physical substances and matters, which are the subjects of those actions.

221. Here, two <term>arcana</term> <note>original: "arcana" – hidden spiritual secrets or mysteries</note>, which can be understood from what has been said above, may be revealed:

**THE FIRST MYSTERY IS**, that the Word, in its literal sense, exists in its full power and fullness. There are three senses within the Word—a celestial sense, a spiritual sense, and a natural sense—which correspond to the three degrees. Because these senses follow the three degrees of height in the Word, and their connection is created through correspondences, the final, natural sense (commonly called the literal sense) is not only the container, foundation, and basis of the inner senses, but is also the Word in its greatest fullness and power. This is shown extensively in **THE DOCTRINE OF THE NEW JERUSALEM CONCERNING THE SACRED SCRIPTURE**, numbers 27 to 36, 37 to 49, 50 to 61, and 62 to 69. 

**THE SECOND MYSTERY IS**, that the Lord came into the world and assumed the <term>Human</term> <note>original: "the Human" – referring to the physical humanity and nature Christ took on during the Incarnation</note> so that He might put Himself in a position of power to conquer the hells and bring all things back into order, both in the heavens and on earth. He took on this Human nature...

221. Over His former <term>Human</term> <note>original: "Human" – here referring to the Divine-Human nature of God before the incarnation</note>. The Human nature that He took on in the world was like the human nature of a man in the world; nevertheless, both were Divine, and therefore infinitely surpassed the limited human nature of angels and men. Since He fully <term>glorified</term> <note>to make Divine or to unite with the Divine</note> His natural Human nature even to its <term>ultimates</term> <note>the final, outermost level of existence; in this case, the physical body</note>, He rose again with His whole body—unlike any other person. By taking on this Human nature, He clothed Himself with Divine <term>omnipotence</term> <note>all-encompassing power</note>, not only to conquer the <term>hells</term> <note>original: "hells" – the various regions of spiritual evil</note> and to bring the heavens into order, but also to keep the hells under control for eternity and to save mankind. 

This power is what is meant by His "sitting at the right hand of the power and might of God." Since the Lord, by taking on the natural Human nature, made Himself Divine Truth in its final forms, He is called "the Word," and it is said that "the Word was made flesh." Divine Truth in its final form is the Word in its literal sense; He made Himself this by fulfilling everything in the Word concerning Himself in the books of Moses and the prophets. Every person is their own good and their own truth, and a person is a person for no other reason; but the Lord, by taking on the natural Human nature, is Divine Good and Divine Truth itself—or, what is the same, He is Divine Love and Divine Wisdom itself, both in first principles and in final forms. For this reason, He appears as a sun in the angelic heavens; after His coming into the world, He shines with more powerful rays and in greater splendor than before His coming. This is an <term>arcanum</term> <note>original: "arcanum" – a hidden or mystical secret</note> that can be understood through the doctrine of degrees. His omnipotence before His coming into the world will be discussed in what follows.

### 222. THAT THERE ARE DEGREES OF BOTH KINDS IN THE GREATEST AND LEAST OF ALL CREATED THINGS.

The fact that the greatest and smallest of all things consist of <term>discrete and continuous degrees</term> <note>Discrete degrees are distinct levels (like end, cause, and effect), while continuous degrees are gradual changes (like light fading to dark)</note>, or degrees of height and breadth, cannot be illustrated by examples from visible things. This is because the smallest things are not visible to the eye, and the largest visible things do not appear to be clearly divided into degrees. Therefore, this subject can only be demonstrated through universal principles. Since the angels possess wisdom from these universal principles, and thus have knowledge regarding specific details, we may state what they affirm on this subject.

223. The angels affirm that there is nothing so minute that it does not contain both kinds of degrees. For example, they say there is not the smallest thing in any animal, vegetable, or mineral, or in ether and air, which does not contain these degrees. Since ether and air are containers for heat and light, there is not the smallest amount of heat and light without them; and as spiritual heat and light are containers for love and wisdom, there is not the smallest amount of these which does not have both kinds of degrees. They also affirm that the smallest affection and the smallest thought—even the smallest part of an idea of thought—consists of both kinds of degrees. They claim that a "smallest part" that does not consist of such degrees is actually nothing; for it has no form, and therefore no quality or...

223. ...a state that can be changed and varied, and thus exist. In these infinitely infinite things, there are degrees of both kinds <note>Referring to discrete degrees (distinct levels of height) and continuous degrees (gradations of breadth).</note>, which are also in Him distinctly one. Since these things are in Him, and all things were created by Him—and because the things created in a certain image represent those things that are in Him—it follows that there is no finite thing, however minute, that does not contain such degrees. These degrees are equally present in the smallest and the largest things, because the Divine is the same in both. That infinite things are distinctly one in the <term>God-Man</term> <note>original: "God-Man" — a term for the Lord, emphasizing that God is essentially Human and the source of all humanity.</note> can be seen above in numbers 17 to 22; and that the Divine is the same in the greatest and smallest things can be seen in numbers 77 to 82; these points are further illustrated in numbers 155, 169, and 171.

224. There is not even the smallest amount of love and wisdom, nor the smallest affection, thought, or mental concept, that does not contain both kinds of degrees. This is because love and wisdom are actual substance and form (as was shown above in numbers 40 to 43), as are affection and thought. Since there is no form in which these degrees do not exist, it follows that similar degrees are in them. To separate love and wisdom, or affection and thought, from substance and form is to destroy them, because they do not exist outside of their subjects; the states of these subjects, as perceived by a person in their variations, are what manifest them.

225. The largest things that contain degrees of both kinds are: the universe in its entire <term>complex</term> <note>original: "complex" — the total structure or whole system of something</note>; the natural world in its totality; the spiritual world in its totality; every empire and every kingdom in its totality; and everything civil, moral, and spiritual belonging to them. This also includes the entire animal, vegetable, and mineral kingdoms, each in its totality; all the atmospheres of both worlds taken together; and their heat and light. This also applies to less general things, such as a human being in their entirety, every animal, every tree and shrub, and every stone and metal. The forms of these things are similar in that they consist of degrees of both kinds, because the Divine, by whom they were created, is the same in the greatest and the smallest things (as was shown above in numbers 77 to 82). The individual parts and specific details of all these things are similar to the general categories and the broader structures above them, in that they are all forms of both kinds of degrees.

226. Yet, not even the smallest thing is exactly the same as another; thus, there is a distinction between every particular and most specific thing. 228. This is because specific examples are characterized by constant variety, and variety can obscure the underlying principle.

229. Some people assert that a substance exists that is so simple it is not a form made from smaller forms, and that from that substance, by <term>coacervation</term> <note>original: "coacervation" — the act of heaping or piling things together; an accumulation of many parts into a mass.</note>, substantial or composite things are created, eventually leading to those substances called matter. Nevertheless, such simple substances do not exist; for what is a substance without a form? It is a thing of which nothing can be defined or described; and from an entity of which nothing can be described, nothing can be built by accumulation. That there are innumerable things in the first of all created substances—which are the smallest and simplest—will be seen in what follows, when forms are discussed.

230. **THAT THERE ARE THREE INFINITE AND UNCREATED DEGREES OF HEIGHT IN THE LORD, AND THREE FINITE AND CREATED DEGREES IN MAN.**

Being love itself and wisdom itself, He is also "use" itself, since love has use for its goal, which it produces through wisdom. Without use, love and wisdom have no boundary or end, and no dwelling place; therefore, it cannot be said that they truly are or exist unless there is a "use" in which they reside. These three—love, wisdom, and use—constitute the three degrees of height in living subjects. They are like the primary purpose (the end), the mediating cause, and the final result (the effect). It was shown and abundantly proved above that end, cause, and effect constitute the three degrees of height.

231. That these three degrees exist in a person may be seen from the elevation of the mind to the degrees of love and wisdom in which the angels of the second and third heavens live. For all angels were born as human beings, and a person, regarding the inner reaches of the mind, is a heaven in its smallest form. Therefore, there are as many degrees of height in a person by creation as there are heavens. A person is also an image and likeness of God; therefore, these three degrees are inscribed in them because they are in the <term>God-Man</term> <note>A term emphasizing that God is essentially Human and the source of all truly human qualities.</note>, that is, in the Lord. 232. These three degrees in the angels are named *celestial, spiritual, and natural*. Their celestial degree is their degree of love, their spiritual degree is their degree of wisdom, and their natural degree is their degree of <term>uses</term> <note>original: "uses" — in this context, the practical application of love and wisdom; the beneficial work or service performed for others.</note>. These degrees are so named because the heavens are divided into two kingdoms: one is named celestial and the other spiritual. The angels who make up the celestial kingdom are founded in love; the angels who make up the spiritual kingdom are founded in wisdom; but people in the world are focused on uses. Therefore, these kingdoms are joined together. How it is to be understood that people are in "uses" will be shown in the next part.

233. It has been told to me from heaven that in the Lord from eternity—who is Jehovah—before He took on the Human form <note>original: "assumption of the Human" — a reference to the Incarnation, or God becoming human in the form of Jesus Christ.</note> in the world, the two prior degrees existed actually, while the third degree existed potentially, just as they are with the angels. However, after His taking on the Human form in the world, He also took on the third or natural degree. By this, He became a man like any man in the world, except that in Him this degree (like the previous ones) is infinite and uncreated, while in angels and humans these degrees are finite and created. For the Divine, which filled all space without being limited by space (see numbers 69 to 72), also reached to the furthest limits of nature. 

Before taking on the Human form, the divine <term>influx</term> <note>original: "influx" — the flowing in of divine life, love, or wisdom into the soul or the natural world.</note> into the natural degree was indirect, passing through the angelic heavens. But after taking on the Human form, it became direct from Himself. This is the reason why all the churches in the world before His coming represented spiritual and celestial things through symbols, but after His coming they became spiritual and "celestial-natural," and symbolic worship was abolished. This is also why the sun of the angelic heaven—which, as was said before, is the immediate outflow of His divine love and divine wisdom—shone with a more remarkable radiance and brilliance after He took on the Human form than before. This is what is meant by the words of Isaiah:

# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART III.

...coming, may be compared to the light of the moon, which is the <term>mediate</term> <note>original: "mediate" — indirect; light that reaches its destination through an intermediary, like the moon reflecting the sun.</note> light of the sun. Because this light was made <term>immediate</term> <note>original: "immediate" — direct; without anything in between.</note> after His coming, it is said in Isaiah, “The light of the moon shall be as the light of the sun” [Isaiah 30:26]; and in the Psalms, “In His days shall the righteous flourish, and abundance of peace until there is no longer any moon,” 72:7. This passage is also speaking about the Lord.

234. The Lord from eternity, or Jehovah, took on this third degree by taking on a *Human* nature in the world. He did this because He could only enter into this degree through a nature similar to human nature; therefore, He did so only by conception from His Divine and by birth from a virgin. In this way, He could cast off that nature which is dead in itself—though still a receptacle of the Divine—and put on the Divine. This is what is meant by the Lord’s two states in the world: <term>exinanition</term> and <term>glorification</term>, which are discussed in *The Doctrine of the New Jerusalem Concerning the Lord*.
<note>original: "exinanition" — from the Latin "exinanitio," meaning "emptying." This refers to the Lord's state of humbling Himself and setting aside His divine power during His life on earth. "Glorification" refers to the process of making His human nature fully Divine.</note>

235. The points above are general statements concerning the triple ascent of the degrees of altitude. However, because these degrees exist in the largest and smallest things (as was stated in the previous article), nothing more can be said of them in detail here, except that these degrees exist in every single part of love, and therefore in every single part of wisdom, and consequently in every single part of <term>uses</term>. In the Lord, all these degrees are infinite, while in an angel or a human being, they are finite. How these degrees function in love, wisdom, and use cannot be described or explained except in a series of lessons.

236. **THAT THESE THREE DEGREES OF ALTITUDE ARE IN EVERY PERSON FROM BIRTH, AND MAY BE OPENED ONE AFTER ANOTHER; AND AS THEY ARE OPENED, A PERSON IS IN THE LORD, AND THE LORD IN THEM.**
That there are three degrees of altitude in every human being has been unknown until now, because these degrees themselves were unknown. As long as they remained unknown, people could only recognize <term>continuous degrees</term>. When only continuous degrees are known, it might be assumed that love and wisdom in a person increase simply by gradual growth. But let it be known that in every person from birth, there are three <term>degrees of altitude</term>, or <term>discrete degrees</term>, one above or within another. Each degree of altitude (discrete degree) also has <term>degrees of latitude</term> (continuous degrees), by which it increases gradually. Degrees of both kinds exist in the largest and smallest of all things, as was shown earlier (numbers 222 to 229), for one kind of degree cannot exist without the other.
<note>original: "discrete degrees" — distinct levels of reality that differ in kind (like soul and body), rather than just "continuous degrees," which are gradual changes in intensity (like light becoming darker).</note>

237. These three degrees of altitude are named <term>natural</term>, <term>spiritual</term>, and <term>celestial</term>, as was stated before in number 232. When a person is born, they first enter the natural degree. This degree increases in them gradually, according to the knowledge and understanding they acquire, until it reaches the highest point of understanding called <term>rationality</term>. Nevertheless, the second (or spiritual) degree...

<summary>This page explains that the Lord took on a third (natural) degree through the Incarnation and that all humans possess three "discrete" degrees—natural, spiritual, and celestial—which are opened progressively as they grow in wisdom and rationality.</summary>

# PART III THE DIVINE LOVE.

...is not opened by this alone. This degree is opened by the love of **uses** <term>uses</term> <note>original: "uses" — in this context, "uses" refers to the beneficial work, service, or function a person performs for the sake of others and society.</note> derived from intellectual things; that is, by the spiritual love of uses, which is love toward the neighbor. This degree may also increase through degrees of *continuity* to its highest point, and it grows through the knowledge of truth and goodness, or through spiritual truths. Nevertheless, the third or celestial degree is not opened by these things, but by the celestial love of use, which is love toward the Lord. And love toward the Lord is nothing else than putting the commandments of the Word into practice in one's life—the summary of which is to flee from evils because they are infernal and diabolical, and to do good because it is heavenly and divine. These three degrees are thus opened one after another in a person.

238. So long as a person is living in the world, they know nothing about the opening of these degrees within them. This is because they are then in the natural or ultimate degree and think, will, speak, and act from it. The spiritual degree, which is more internal, does not communicate with the natural degree by *continuity*, but by **correspondences** <term>correspondences</term> <note>original: "correspondences" — a central concept in the author's work where things in the natural world reflect and act in harmony with spiritual realities, though they are on different levels of existence.</note>, and communication by correspondence is not consciously felt. Nevertheless, when a person sheds the natural degree—which happens when they die—they enter into whichever degree was opened within them while in the world. 

If the spiritual degree was opened, they enter the spiritual degree; if the celestial degree was opened, they enter the celestial degree. If a person enters the spiritual degree after death, they no longer think, will, speak, or act naturally, but spiritually. If they enter the celestial degree, they think, will, speak, and act according to that degree. Because the communication between the three degrees is achieved only through correspondences, the differences in their love, wisdom, and use are so distinct that they have nothing in common through any kind of *continuity*. From this, it is clear that there are three **degrees of altitude** <note>original: "degrees of altitude" — vertical or discrete degrees, which are distinct levels like the layers of an onion, rather than a gradual slope or "continuous" change.</note> in a person, and that they can be opened successively.

239. Since there are three degrees of love and wisdom (and therefore of use) in a person, it follows that there are also three degrees of will and understanding (and therefore of conclusions and the determination to be useful). This is because the will is the **receptacle** <note>original: "receptacle" — a container or vessel that receives and holds something.</note> of love, the understanding is the receptacle of wisdom, and the conclusions are the use derived from them. 

From this, it is clear that in every person there are a natural, a spiritual, and a celestial will and understanding. These exist in **potency** <note>original: "in potency" — having the potential or capacity to exist, even if not yet active.</note> from birth, and in **act** <note>original: "in act" — actually functioning or operating.</note> when they are opened. In short, the human mind—which consists of will and understanding—is by creation and birth made of three degrees. Therefore, a person has a natural mind, a spiritual mind, and a celestial mind. Because of this, a person can be elevated to angelic wisdom and possess it while living in the world; however, they do not truly enter into it until after death. If they then become an angel, they speak of things that are inexpressible and incomprehensible to the natural man. I once knew a man of moderate learning in the world, and after his death, I saw him and spoke with him in heaven, where I clearly...

<summary>This page explains that the spiritual and celestial levels of the human mind are opened through love toward the neighbor and the Lord, respectively. While these levels are hidden during earthly life, they determine a person's level of wisdom and existence in the afterlife.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the discussion of the three degrees of the human mind and introduces the two fundamental human faculties: rationality and liberty. It also explains the relationship between love as an end, wisdom as a cause, and use as an effect.</meta>

...it was perceived that he spoke as an angel, and that what he said was imperceptible to the natural man. This was because, while in the world, he had put the commandments of the Word into practice in his life and had worshiped the Lord; therefore, he was raised by the Lord to the third degree of love and wisdom. It is important that this elevation of the human mind be understood, for on this depends the understanding of what follows.

240. There are two faculties from the Lord in every person by which a human is distinguished from animals: one faculty is that he can understand what is true and what is good; this is called **rationality** <term>rationality</term>, and is the faculty of his understanding. The other is that he can do what is true and good; this faculty is called **liberty** <term>liberty</term>, and is the faculty of his will. By virtue of his rationality, a person can think whatever he pleases, both for God and against God, and for his neighbor or against his neighbor; he can also will and do what he thinks. However, when he sees evil and fears punishment, he can freely refrain from doing it. A person is human, and is distinguished from animals, by these two faculties. Every person has them from the Lord, and this is constant; they are never taken away, for if they were, his humanity would perish. In these two faculties, the Lord resides within every person, whether that person is good or evil, as these are the Lord’s dwellings in the human race; because of this, every person—whether good or evil—lives forever. But the Lord’s dwelling is closer to a person in proportion as the person opens the higher degrees through these faculties. By opening these, he enters higher degrees of love and wisdom and, consequently, comes closer to the Lord. From this, it may be seen that as these degrees are opened, a person is in the Lord, and the Lord is in him.

241. It was stated above that the three **degrees of altitude** <term>degrees of altitude</term> <note>original: "degrees of altitude" — also called "discrete degrees," these are distinct levels of existence (like end, cause, and effect) that do not blend into each other like shades of a color, but exist one within the other.</note> are like end, cause, and effect, and that love, wisdom, and use follow according to these degrees. Therefore, we may say a few words here regarding love as the "end," wisdom as the "cause," and use as the "effect." Anyone who consults their reason while it is enlightened can see that a person's love is the end (the goal) of all things belonging to him; for what he loves, he thinks about, decides upon, and does—consequently, he holds it as his end. Anyone can also see from their reason that wisdom is the cause; for the person (or his love, which is his end) seeks means in his understanding by which he may reach his goal. Thus, he consults his wisdom, and those means constitute the "instrumental cause." That use is the "effect" is evident without any further explanation. But the love in one person is not the same as in another, nor is the wisdom, and consequently, neither is the use. Since these three are **homogeneous** <term>homogeneous</term> <note>original: "homogeneous" — in this context, it means that love, wisdom, and use are of the same spiritual character or quality; they belong together as a single set.</note> (as was shown above in sections 189 to 194), it follows that as the love is in a person, so is the wisdom, and so is the use. We use the term "wisdom" here to mean everything that belongs to a person's understanding.

# PART III. THE DIVINE LOVE. 242–244

242. **SPIRITUAL LIGHT FLOWS INTO A PERSON IN THREE DEGREES, BUT NOT SPIRITUAL HEAT, EXCEPT SO FAR AS THE PERSON AVOIDS EVILS AS SINS AND LOOKS TO THE LORD.** From what was shown previously, it is evident that light and heat proceed from the sun of heaven, which is the first expression of divine love and divine wisdom (see Part II). Light comes from its wisdom and heat from its love. Light is the vessel of wisdom, and heat is the vessel of love. To the extent that a person enters into wisdom, they enter into that divine light; and to the extent that they enter into love, they enter into 그 divine heat. It was also shown above that there are three degrees of light and three degrees of heat (or three degrees of wisdom and three degrees of love). These degrees are formed within a person so that they may be a vessel for divine love and divine wisdom, and thus for the Lord. We shall now show that spiritual light flows into a person through these three degrees, but spiritual heat does not—except to the degree that a person avoids evils as sins and looks to the Lord. In other words, a person may receive wisdom up to the third degree, but not love, unless they avoid evils as sins and look to the Lord. Or, to put it even more simply, a person’s understanding may be raised to wisdom, but their will cannot, except to the degree that they avoid evils as sins.

243. The fact that the understanding can be raised to the light of heaven (angelic wisdom), while the will cannot be raised to the heat of heaven (angelic love) unless a person avoids evils as sins and looks to the Lord, was made very clear to me through experience in the spiritual world. I have several times seen and perceived that simple spirits—who only knew that there is a God and that the Lord was born a Man, and almost nothing else—fully understood the hidden truths <note>original: arcana</note> of angelic wisdom, almost as well as the angels themselves. This was true not only of them but also of many in the diabolical crowd. However, they understood these truths only while they were hearing them, not when they thought by themselves. When they heard them, light entered from above; but when they thought by themselves, no light could enter except what matched their own heat or love. Because of this, after they had heard and perceived those hidden truths, they retained nothing once they turned away their ears. Indeed, those from the diabolical crowd then rejected and entirely denied them. The reason was that the "fire" of their own love and its "light"—which were deceptive—produced a darkness that extinguished the heavenly light entering from above.

244. It is the same in this world. When anyone who is not completely stupid, and who has not confirmed themselves in falsehoods out of the pride of self-intelligence, hears people talking about elevated subjects or reads about them, they can understand, remember, and later confirm those truths—provided they have any affection for learning <note>original: science; here meaning the acquisition of knowledge</note>. This can be done by the wicked as well as the good. Even a wicked person, although in their heart they deny divine things...

<summary>This page explains that while human understanding can be elevated to perceive high spiritual wisdom, the human will (or heart) only advances to higher degrees of love if the person actively avoids sin and turns toward God.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual light, spiritual heat, three degrees, divine wisdom, divine love, understanding vs. will, arcana, spiritual world, rationality</keywords>

[...continued from previous page] of the church can still understand, speak, and preach these truths, and learnedly confirm them through writing. However, when left to himself and his own thoughts, because of his hellish love, he thinks against them and denies them. This makes it clear that the understanding can be in spiritual light even if the will is not in spiritual heat. It also shows that the understanding does not lead the will—or that wisdom does not produce love—but only teaches and shows the way. It teaches how a person ought to live and shows the way in which they ought to walk. It also shows that the will leads the understanding and causes it to act in unity with itself; the love belonging to the will labels whatever agrees with it in the understanding as "wisdom." In what follows, it will be seen that the will does nothing by itself without the understanding, but performs everything it does in conjunction with the understanding. Yet, it is the will that joins the understanding to itself by influx <term>original: "influx", referring to the flowing in of spiritual influence or energy</term>, and not the understanding that joins the will to itself.

245. We shall now proceed to show the nature of the influx of light into the three degrees of life that belong to the human mind. The forms that are the receptacles <note>receptacles: containers or vessels that receive something</note> of heat and light (or love and wisdom) in a person—which, as previously stated, are in a triple order or of three degrees—are transparent from birth. They transmit spiritual light just as crystal glass transmits natural light; because of this, a person can be raised to the third degree in regards to their wisdom. Nevertheless, these forms are not opened until spiritual heat joins itself to spiritual light, or love joins to wisdom. This union opens these transparent forms according to their degrees. This is similar to how the light and heat of the world's sun affect the vegetable kingdom. The light of winter, which is as bright as the light of summer, does not open anything in a seed or a tree; but when the spring heat joins itself to the light, it opens them. The cases are similar because spiritual light corresponds to natural light, and spiritual heat corresponds to natural heat.

246. This spiritual heat is obtained in no other way than by avoiding evils as sins and then looking to the Lord. For as long as a person remains in evils, they are also in the love of evils; they lust after them, and the love of evil and its cravings <term>original: "concupiscences", meaning strong, misplaced, or worldly desires</term> are in a state of love opposed to spiritual love and affection. This love or craving can be removed only by avoiding evils as sins. And since a person cannot avoid them by their own power, but only by the Lord's power, they must look to Him. Therefore, when a person avoids evils by the Lord’s power, the love of evil and its "heat" are removed, and the love of good and its "heat" are introduced in their place. Through this process, a higher degree of the mind is opened. The Lord enters by influx from above, opens that degree, and joins spiritual love (heat) to wisdom (spiritual light). By this union, the person begins to flourish spiritually, like a tree in springtime.

247. The influx of spiritual light into all three degrees of the mind is what distinguishes humans from animals. It enables a person to think analytically—which animals cannot do—and to see not only natural truths...

...to see spiritual truths. When he sees them, he can acknowledge them, and so be reformed and regenerated <note>reformed and regenerated: in this context, the process of spiritual rebirth and the improvement of one's character</note>. The ability to receive spiritual light is what is meant by "rationality" (mentioned earlier), which everyone receives from the Lord and which is never taken away. If it were taken away, a person could not be reformed. Because of this faculty called rationality, humans can not only think but also speak from those thoughts—something animals cannot do. Furthermore, through his other faculty, called "liberty" (also mentioned previously), he can act upon the things he thinks in his understanding. Since these two faculties—rationality and liberty—which are unique to human beings, were discussed above in section 240, no more needs to be said about them here.

248. **IF THE HIGHER OR SPIRITUAL DEGREE IS NOT OPENED IN A PERSON, THEY BECOME NATURAL AND SENSUAL.** It was shown earlier that there are three degrees of the human mind: natural, spiritual, and celestial. These degrees can be opened one after another. First, the natural degree is opened. Afterward, if a person avoids evil actions because they are sins and looks to the Lord, the spiritual degree is opened, and finally, the celestial degree. Since these degrees are opened in sequence based on how a person lives, it follows that the two higher degrees might never be opened. In that case, the person remains in the natural (or lowest) degree. People generally know that there is both a natural and a spiritual man—or an external and internal man. However, it is not well known that the natural man becomes spiritual through the opening of a higher degree within him. This opening is achieved by a spiritual life, which is a life lived according to the divine commandments. Without such a life, a person remains purely natural.

249. There are three types of natural people. The first type consists of those who know nothing of the divine commandments. The second consists of those who know these commandments exist but give no thought to living according to them. The third type consists of those who despise and deny them.

The *first type*—those who know nothing of the divine commandments—inevitably remains natural because they cannot teach themselves. Everyone is taught the divine commandments by others who know them through religion, rather than through direct revelation <note>immediate revelation: a direct, personal message from God without the mediation of scripture or teachers</note>. (See ***The Doctrine of the New Jerusalem Concerning the Sacred Scripture***, sections 114 to 118.)

The *second type*—those who know about the divine commandments but do not think about living by them—also remain natural. They care only for worldly and physical things. After death, they become menial laborers and servants according to the useful tasks they can perform for those who are spiritual. In the spiritual order, the natural man is a servant, while the spiritual man is his master and lord.

# 249—252 ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING THE DIVINE LOVE AND WISDOM: PART III.

249. ...commandments, not only remain natural, but also become sensual, in proportion to how much they despise and deny them. Sensual <note>sensual: in this context, "sensual" does not refer to physical pleasure, but to a state of being limited entirely to the information provided by the physical senses</note> men are the lowest type of natural men; they are unable to think beyond the appearances and illusions of the bodily senses. After death, these individuals are in hell.

250. Since the world generally does not understand what the spiritual man is compared to the natural man, and since purely natural people are often called "spiritual" and vice versa, these two shall be treated distinctly. We shall show:
*   I. What the natural man is, and what the spiritual man is.
*   II. The character of the natural man in whom the spiritual degree is opened.
*   III. The character of the natural man in whom the spiritual degree is not opened, but still not closed.
*   IV. The character of the natural man in whom the spiritual degree is entirely closed.
*   V. Finally, the difference between the life of a merely natural man and the life of an animal.

251. **I. What the natural man is, and what the spiritual man.** A person is not defined as a human being by their face and body, but by their understanding and will. Therefore, when we speak of the "natural man" and the "spiritual man," we are referring to that person's understanding and will, which are either natural or spiritual. 
The natural man, in terms of his understanding and will, is like the natural world; he may also be called a "little world," or a microcosm <note>original: "microcosmus," a term meaning a miniature representation of the universe</note>. Conversely, the spiritual man, in terms of his understanding and will, is like the spiritual world; he may also be called a spiritual world, or heaven. 
From this, it is clear that since the natural man is a reflection of the natural world, he loves the things of the natural world. Because the spiritual man is a reflection of the spiritual world, he loves the things of that world, or of heaven. 
The spiritual man does also love the natural world, but only as a master loves a servant who helps him perform his work. When a natural man finds pleasure in doing "uses" <note>uses: a central concept in this text, meaning useful services or performing one's duties for the benefit of others and the community</note> motivated by the spiritual man, the natural man becomes like the spiritual. This type of person may be called "spiritual-natural." 
The spiritual man loves spiritual truths; he not only loves to know and understand them, but he also wills them—meaning he desires to live by them. Meanwhile, the spiritual-natural man loves to speak those truths and to act upon them. To "do truths" is to perform uses. This hierarchy arises from the connection between the spiritual and natural worlds, because everything that appears and happens in the natural world draws its cause from the spiritual world. It is evident, then, that the spiritual man is completely distinct from the natural man, and the only communication between them is the relationship between cause and effect.

252. **II. The character of the natural man in whom the spiritual degree is opened** is evident from what has been said above. It can be added that the natural man becomes a "full" man when his spiritual degree is opened. He is then in association with both angels in heaven and with people in the world at the same time. In his relationship to both, he lives under the guidance of the Lord; for the...

<meta>This page continues the explanation of the relationship between the spiritual man and the natural man. It describes how the spiritual mind operates through the natural mind, often without the individual's conscious awareness, and begins to define the state of those who have lived good lives but lacked specific spiritual knowledge.</meta>

The spiritual man receives <note>original: "imbibes"</note> instructions through the Word from the Lord and carries them out through the natural man. The natural man, in whom the spiritual level <note>original: "degree"</note> is open, does not realize that he thinks and acts from his spiritual man; it appears to him as if it comes from himself. Nevertheless, it is not from himself, but from the Lord. Nor does he know that, through his spiritual man, he is actually in heaven; in reality, his spiritual man dwells among the angels of heaven. He sometimes even appears to the angels, but he disappears after a short stay because he withdraws back into his natural man.

Furthermore, he does not know that his spiritual mind is filled by the Lord with a thousand secrets <note>original: "arcana," meaning hidden or mysterious truths</note> of wisdom and a thousand delights of love, and that he enters into these after death when he becomes an angel. The natural man does not know these things because the communication between the natural and spiritual man occurs through correspondences <note>correspondences: the systematic relationship between spiritual causes and natural effects</note>. Communication through correspondences is only perceived in the understanding as seeing truths in a clear light, and in the will as performing useful actions out of a sense of affection.

### 253. III. *The character of the natural man in whom the spiritual degree is not opened, but still not closed.*

The spiritual degree is not opened, but is still not closed, in those who have lived a life of charity <note>charity: here meaning selfless love and active goodwill toward others</note> and yet have known very little genuine truth. This is because that degree is opened by the joining together <note>original: "conjunction"</note> of love and wisdom, or of heat and light. Love alone (spiritual heat) does not open it, nor does wisdom alone (spiritual light); they must work together. Therefore, if genuine truths—from which wisdom or light is derived—are not known, love has no power to open that degree. Instead, love simply keeps the degree capable of being opened, which is what is meant by it "not being shut."

The same process happens in the plant kingdom: heat alone does not cause seeds and trees to grow <note>original: "vegetate"</note>; they require heat combined with light. It should be observed that all truths belong to spiritual light, and all goods belong to spiritual heat. Goodness opens the spiritual degree by means of truths; for goodness performs useful actions through truths. These "uses" are the practical expressions of love, which derive their essence from the union of good and truth.

The fate after death for those in whom the spiritual degree is not opened, yet not shut—and who are still natural rather than spiritual—is that they dwell in the lowest parts of heaven. There, they sometimes experience severe sufferings; or they live on the boundaries of a higher heaven, as if in the light of evening. As was said before, in heaven and in every community there, the light decreases from the center toward the boundaries. Those in the center are those who surpass others in divine truths, and those at the boundaries are those who possess few truths. Those who have few truths are those who know no more of religion than simply that there is a God and that the Lord suffered for them; they also know that charity and faith are essential to the church, but they do not know what faith and charity actually are. Nevertheless, faith in its essence is truth, and...

# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART III.

Truth is manifold; and charity consists of every duty of a person’s office or occupation that they perform from the Lord. A person acts "from the Lord" when they avoid evils as sins. It is exactly as we said before: the goal <note>original: "end"</note> is the essence of the cause, and the result <note>original: "effect"</note> is the fulfillment of the goal through that cause. The goal is charity or goodness, the cause is faith or truth, and the results are good works or useful actions. From this, it is evident that charity can only be expressed in works to the extent that charity is joined to the truths of faith; it is through these truths that charity enters into actions and defines their quality.

254. IV. **The nature of the natural man in whom the spiritual level is entirely closed.** The spiritual level <note>original: "degree"</note> is closed in those whose lives are focused on evil, and even more so in those who hold false ideas stemming from those evils. This is like a nerve fiber that contracts at the slightest touch of any foreign <note>original: "heterogeneous"</note> body; or like the muscle fibers—or even the whole muscle or the entire body—reacting when they come into contact with something hard or cold. In the same way, the substances or forms of the spiritual level in a person contract at the approach of evils and their resulting false principles, because these are incompatible. 

Since the spiritual level is structured in the "form of heaven," it admits nothing but what is good and truths derived from goodness, as these are compatible <note>original: "homogeneous"</note> with it; whereas evils and the falsehoods of evil are incompatible. This level is particularly contracted and closed in those who, while in the world, love to rule over others out of self-love, because this love is the opposite of love toward the Lord. It is also closed in those who, out of a love for the world, have an excessive <note>original: "inordinate"</note> lust to possess the goods of others, though not as severely as in the first case. These types of love close the spiritual level because they are the origins of all evils. 

The contraction or closing of this level is like the twisting of a spiral <note>original: "retorsion of a spire"</note> in the opposite direction. Therefore, once it is closed, it reflects the light of heaven back, and instead of heavenly light, there is darkness; so much so that truth, which exists in the light of heaven, becomes repulsive <note>original: "nauseous"</note>. In such people, not only is the spiritual level closed, but the higher region of the natural level—called the **rational** level—is also closed. Eventually, only the lowest region of the natural level, called the **sensual**, remains open. This part is closest to the world and the external senses of the body, and it is from this level that such a person later thinks, speaks, and reasons. 

In the spiritual world, when seen in the light of heaven, a natural person who has become "sensual" through evils and falsehoods does not look like a human being, but like a monster with a drawn-back nose. He appears with a drawn-back nose because the nose corresponds <note>In Swedenborg’s system of "correspondences," the nose and the sense of smell represent the ability to perceive truth.</note> to the perception of truth. He cannot endure a single ray of heavenly light. In their dark caverns, such people have no light other than what looks like torches or a coal fire. From this, it is clear who these people are and what they are like when the spiritual level is closed.

255. V. **The difference between the life of a natural human being and the life of an animal.** We shall speak of this difference more...

particularly in our following works where the subject of life is discussed. We will only observe here that the difference is that a human being has three degrees of mind—three degrees of understanding and will—and that these degrees can be opened one after another. Because these degrees are transparent, the person can be elevated in their understanding toward the light of heaven and see truths—not only those related to civil and moral life, but spiritual truths as well. From many truths seen in this way, they can draw logical conclusions and so perfect their understanding for eternity. 

But animals do not have the two higher degrees; they only have natural degrees. Without the superior degrees, animals have no faculty for thinking about anything civil, moral, or spiritual. Since their natural degrees are not capable of being opened and thereby elevated into a higher light, they cannot think in a "successive" or logical order. They only think in "simultaneous" order, which is not true thinking, but merely acting from the knowledge that corresponds to their natural instincts <note>original: "love"</note>. Because they cannot think analytically or observe their lower thoughts from the perspective of a higher thought, they cannot speak; they can only utter sounds that agree with the knowledge proper to their instincts. 

Nevertheless, the "sensual" person—the person at the lowest natural level—differs from an animal only in this: he is able to fill his memory with <term>scientifics</term> <note>original: "scientifics"; in this context, it refers to facts, data, or pieces of information learned through the senses.</note> and to think and speak from them. He derives this ability from a faculty every person has: the power to understand truth if he wishes to. This is his distinguishing human faculty, although many people, by abusing this power, have made themselves lower than animals.

### 256. THE NATURAL DEGREE OF THE HUMAN MIND, CONSIDERED IN ITSELF, IS CONTINUOUS; BUT THROUGH ITS CONNECTION WITH THE TWO HIGHER DEGREES, IT APPEARS AS IF IT WERE DISCRETE WHEN IT IS ELEVATED.

Although this is difficult to understand for those who have no knowledge of <term>degrees of altitude</term> <note>original: "degrees of altitude"; also called "discrete degrees," these are distinct levels of existence, like the difference between a thought and the action that results from it.</note>, it must nevertheless be revealed because it is a principle of angelic wisdom. This wisdom, while it cannot be grasped by the natural person in the same way the angels grasp it, can still be understood when the mind is elevated to the level of light in which the angels exist; for the human understanding is capable of being elevated that far and being enlightened according to its elevation. 

The enlightenment of the natural mind, however, does not ascend by <term>discrete degrees</term> <note>original: "discrete degrees"; distinct levels that do not blend into one another, like steps on a ladder.</note>; instead, it increases by a <term>continuous degree</term> <note>original: "continuous degree"; a gradual increase or decrease, like the way light fades into shadow or heat turns to cold.</note>. As it increases, it is illuminated from within by the light of the two higher degrees. 

How this happens can be understood by perceiving degrees of altitude: one is above the other, and the natural (or lowest) degree acts as a common covering for the two higher degrees. As the natural degree is elevated toward the level of the higher degrees, those higher degrees act from within upon the outer natural degree and illuminate it. This illumination is indeed produced from within by the light of the superior degrees; but that light is received by the natural degree (which enfolds and surrounds them) through a continuous process—and therefore, the more it is elevated, the more clearly and purely it is received.

...according to its ascent. That is, the natural degree is enlightened from within by the light of the higher degrees in distinct steps <note>original: "discretely"; refers to levels that are separate and distinct, like the floors of a building, rather than a gradual slope.</note>, but within itself, it is enlightened in a gradual way <note>original: "continuously"; refers to a smooth transition, like the fading of light into shade.</note>. Because of this, it is evident that as long as a person lives in the world and remains in the natural degree, they cannot be raised into a wisdom like that of the angels. They can only be raised into a higher light—even to the level of the angels—and receive enlightenment from their light, which flows in from within and provides illumination. However, these things cannot be described more clearly; they are better understood through their effects. Effects make causes visible in their own light, provided those causes are first somewhat understood.

257. The effects are:
1. The natural mind can be raised to the light of heaven, where the angels are, and perceive naturally what the angels perceive spiritually (and therefore, not as fully). However, a person's natural mind still cannot be raised into true angelic light.
2. Through their natural mind, when raised to the light of heaven, a person can think and speak with angels. But when this happens, the thought and speech of the angels flow into the natural thought and speech of the person, and *not the other way around*. Therefore, angels speak with people in natural language, or in the person's native tongue. 
3. This is achieved by spiritual influence flowing into the natural realm, and not by natural influence flowing into the spiritual.
4. Human wisdom, which is natural for as long as a person lives in the world, cannot possibly be raised to the level of angelic wisdom, but only to a certain image of it. This is because the elevation of the human mind happens gradually <note>original: "by continuity"</note>—like moving from shade to light, or from something coarse to something pure. Nevertheless, the person whose spiritual degree is open enters into that wisdom when they die. They can also enter into it by putting the bodily senses to sleep while receiving an influence from above into the spiritual aspects of their mind.
5. A person's natural mind consists of both spiritual and natural substances. Thought is produced from its spiritual substances, but not from its natural ones. The natural substances fall away <note>original: "recede"</note> when a person dies, but the spiritual substances do not. Consequently, the same mind remains in a form similar to what it had in the world after death, when the person becomes a spirit or angel.
6. The natural substances of that mind which (as previously mentioned) fall away at death, form the skin-like covering <note>original: "cutaneous covering"</note> of the spiritual body of spirits and angels. Their spiritual bodies are maintained by means of this covering, which is taken from the natural world; for the natural realm is the final container <note>original: "ultimate continent"</note>. For this reason, there is no spirit or angel who was not first born as a human being. These mysteries <note>original: "arcana"</note> of angelic wisdom are presented here so that the nature of the natural mind in humans may be known; this will be discussed further in the following sections.

258. Every person is born with the capacity to understand truths to the highest degree, just like the angels of the third heaven. This is because the human understanding, rising gradually above the two higher degrees, receives the light of the wisdom belonging to those degrees...

<header>PART III. THE DIVINE LOVE.</header>

As previously shown in section 256, a person can become rational in proportion to this elevation. If the mind is elevated to the third degree, he becomes rational from the third degree; if to the second, from the second; and if it is not elevated, he is rational in the first degree. We say that he becomes rational *from* those degrees because the natural degree is the common container <note>original: "receptacle"</note> for their light. A person does not become as rational as they potentially could because love (which belongs to the will) cannot be elevated in the same way as wisdom (which belongs to the understanding). Love, which belongs to the will, is elevated only by avoiding evils as sins and by the good deeds of charity—meaning "uses" <note>original: "uses"; in this context, the practical application of one's talents for the benefit of others</note>—which a person later performs from the Lord. Consequently, if love, which belongs to the will, is not elevated at the same time, then wisdom, which belongs to the understanding, will still fall back to its own level of love, no matter how high it may have ascended. Therefore, if a person's love is not elevated to the spiritual degree at the same time, they remain rational only in the lowest <note>original: "ultimate"</note> degree. From these considerations, it appears that a person's rational faculty seems to exist on three levels: from a heavenly <note>original: "celestial"</note> basis, from a spiritual basis, and from a natural basis. Furthermore, rationality—which is the capacity for being elevated—remains in a person whether it is actually elevated or not.

259. We stated that every person is born with that capacity, or rationality, but we mean every person whose physical body <note>original: "externals"</note> has not been damaged by accident—whether in the womb or after birth—by disease, by head injuries, or by an uncontrollable <note>original: "insane"</note> love that breaks out and removes all self-restraint. In such individuals, the rational principle cannot be elevated; for life, which belongs to the will and understanding, has no boundaries or limits <note>original: "closing terms"</note> in such people that are arranged to allow it to perform physical actions in an orderly way. The mind in these cases acts according to physical impulses, but not from an internal direction. That rationality cannot exist in infants and children may be seen below at the end of section 266.

### 260. THE NATURAL MIND, BEING THE COVERING AND CONTAINER OF THE HIGHER DEGREES OF THE HUMAN MIND, IS REACTIVE; IF THE HIGHER DEGREES ARE NOT OPENED, IT ACTS AGAINST THEM, BUT IF THEY ARE OPENED, IT ACTS WITH THEM.

In the preceding article, it was shown that the natural mind, being in the lowest <note>original: "ultimate"</note> degree, surrounds and includes the spiritual and heavenly minds, which are higher in degree. It now remains to be shown that the natural mind reacts against these higher or internal minds. It reacts because it covers, includes, and contains them, and this cannot happen without a reactive force. If it did not react, the internal parts <note>original: "interiors"</note> would become loose, escape, and be scattered. It is just as if the coverings of the human body did not react: in that case, the internal organs <note>original: "viscera"</note> would fall out and be dispersed. It is also like the membrane that covers the moving fibers of a muscle; if it did not react against the power of those fibers, they could not function.

## PART III.

...in action; in that case, not only would action cease, but the internal structures <note>original: "interior textures"</note> would all be dissolved. It is the same with every lowest level <note>original: "ultimate degree"</note> of the degrees of height <note>original: "altitude"</note>; consequently, this applies to the natural mind in respect to the higher degrees. As was said above, there are three degrees of the human mind: natural, spiritual, and heavenly <note>original: "celestial"</note>, and the natural mind is in the lowest degree. 

Again, the natural mind reacts against the spiritual mind because the natural mind consists of substances not only from the spiritual world, but also from the natural world (as was said above in section 257). By their very nature, the substances of the natural world react against the substances of the spiritual world. Natural substances are dead in themselves and are acted upon from the outside by spiritual substances; things that are dead and acted upon from the outside naturally resist and, therefore, react by their very nature. This shows that the natural man reacts against the spiritual man, and that there is a conflict <note>original: "combat"</note>. It is the same thing whether you say the natural and spiritual "man," or the natural and spiritual "mind."

261. From this, it appears that if the spiritual mind is closed, the natural mind continually acts against the things of the spiritual mind and is afraid that anything might flow in <note>original: "inflow"</note> from there to disturb its own state. Everything that flows in through the spiritual mind comes from heaven, because the spiritual mind, in its form, is a heaven; and everything that flows into the natural mind comes from the world, because the natural mind, in its form, is a world. 

Consequently, when the spiritual mind is closed, the natural mind reacts against everything of heaven and does not admit it, except so far as those things serve as a means to acquire and possess the things of the world. When heavenly things serve the natural man for his own selfish purposes <note>original: "ends"</note>, then those means—though they may appear heavenly—actually become natural, and the person’s goal defines them. They become like mere facts <note>original: "scientifics"; in this context, external knowledge or information</note> of the natural man that have no internal life. But since heavenly things cannot be joined to natural ones so as closely as to act as one, they separate themselves. In purely natural men, heavenly things take their place on the outside, surrounding the natural things which are within. This is why a purely natural man can talk about and preach heavenly things, and even imitate them in his actions, even though inwardly he thinks against them; he does the latter when alone, and the former when in company. More will be said regarding these things in what follows.

262. The natural mind or man, through an inborn <note>original: "connate"</note> reaction, acts against the things of the spiritual mind or man when a person loves himself and the world above all things. In that case, he also feels pleasure in all kinds of evils, such as adultery, fraud, revenge, verbal abuse <note>original: "reviling"</note>, and the like. He acknowledges nature as the creator of the universe and justifies all these things through his power of reasoning <note>original: "rational principle"</note>. After he has convinced himself <note>original: "after confirmation"</note>, he either distorts <note>original: "perverts"</note>, stifles, or repels <note>original: "reflects"</note> the goodness and truths of heaven and the church, and at length either avoids...
