<header>PART III. THE DIVINE LOVE. 262—264</header>

...or holds them in aversion, or even hates them. He does this in his spirit, and also in his body as far as he dares to speak from his spirit to others without fear of losing his reputation—which he values for the sake of honor and profit. When a person is like this, he gradually shuts his spiritual mind tighter and tighter. Confirming <note>Here, "confirming" means to provide intellectual justifications or rationalizations for one's actions.</note> evils through falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note> especially shuts the mind; therefore, once evil and false principles are confirmed, they cannot be rooted out after death. They can only be rooted out in this world through repentance.

263. However, the state of the natural mind is completely different when the spiritual mind is opened. In this case, the natural mind is organized to be obedient to the spiritual mind and is held in a subordinate position. The spiritual mind acts from above and from within upon the natural mind; it removes the things that resist there and adapts the natural mind to act in harmony with itself. Because of this, the excessive resistance is gradually removed. 

It should be noted that there is action and reaction in everything in the universe, both living and dead, from the greatest to the smallest. This creates the equilibrium of all things, which is lost when action overcomes reaction, or vice versa. It is the same with the natural mind and the spiritual mind. When the natural mind acts from the delights of its own loves and the pleasures of its own thoughts—which are essentially evils and falsehoods—then the reaction of the natural mind pushes away the things of the spiritual mind. It shuts the door against them and causes all action to come from things that agree with its own resistance. Thus, there is an action and reaction of the natural mind that is opposite to the action and reaction of the spiritual mind; this results in a closing of the spiritual mind, similar to the backward twisting of a spiral <note>original: "retorsion of a spire"</note>. 

But if the spiritual mind is opened, then the action and reaction of the natural mind is reversed. The spiritual mind acts from above or from within, and at the same time—through the things in the natural mind that have been made obedient to it—it acts from below or from without. This untwists the spiral <note>original: "retwists the spire"</note> that contains the action and reaction of the natural mind. By birth, the natural mind is in opposition to the things of the spiritual mind, an opposition it derives hereditarily from parents, as is well known. Such is the change of state called reformation and regeneration <note>In this context, "reformation" is the intellectual shift toward truth, and "regeneration" is the spiritual rebirth of the heart and will.</note>. The state of the natural mind before reformation may be compared to a spiral winding or twisting downward; but after reformation, it is like a spiral winding or twisting upward. Therefore, before reformation, a person looks downward toward hell, but after reformation, he looks upward toward heaven.

264. ### **THAT THE ABUSE OF THE FACULTIES PROPER TO HUMANS, CALLED RATIONALITY AND FREEDOM <note>original: "liberty"</note>, IS THE ORIGIN OF EVIL.**

By "rationality" is meant the faculty of understanding truths (and therefore falsehoods), and goods (and therefore evils). By "freedom" <note>original: "liberty"</note> is meant the faculty of thinking, willing, and acting freely. From what has been said before, and from what follows, it will appear...

It will further appear that every person has these two faculties <note>Swedenborg identifies these as "rationality" (the ability to understand truth) and "liberty" (the ability to choose and act freely).</note> by creation and therefore by birth, and that they come from the Lord. These faculties are never taken away. Because of them, it appears as though a person thinks, speaks, wills, and acts on their own. The Lord lives within these faculties in every person; as a result of this connection, a person lives forever. Furthermore, a person can be reformed and regenerated through these faculties—and cannot be without them. These are also what distinguish human beings from animals.

265. The abuse of these faculties is the source of evil, which will be demonstrated in this order:
I. A bad person enjoys these two faculties just as much as a good person.
II. A bad person abuses them to justify <note>original: "confirm"</note> evils and falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note>, while a good person uses them to justify goods and truths.
III. Once evils and falsehoods are justified, they remain and become the principles of a person’s love and, consequently, their life.
IV. Those things that become the principles of love and life are passed down as hereditary traits to a person’s offspring.
V. All evils, both inherited and acquired, reside in the natural mind.

266. I. **A bad person enjoys these two faculties as well as a good person.**
The previous section showed that the natural mind’s understanding can be raised to the light in which the angels of the third heaven live. In that light, a person can see truths, acknowledge them, and then speak them. From this, it is evident that because the natural mind can be elevated this way, a bad person enjoys the faculty of rationality just as much as a good person. Since the natural mind can be elevated so far, it follows that a bad person can also think and speak truths.

Both reason and experience testify that a bad person *could* choose to will and do these truths, even if they do not. **Reason** tells us: who is unable to will and do what they think? If they do not will or do it, it is because they do not *love* to do it. The power to will and act is "liberty," which every person receives from the Lord. However, the reason a person does not do good when it is in their power is their love of evil, which opposes the good. Nevertheless, they are capable of resisting that evil, and many people do resist it.

**Experience** in the spiritual world has occasionally confirmed this same point. I have observed there that evil spirits—who were inwardly devils and had rejected the truths of heaven and the church while in the world—could still understand the deep secrets <note>original: "arcana"</note> of angelic wisdom. This happened while their desire for knowledge <note>original: "affection of science"</note> (which every person has from childhood) was sparked by a certain "glory" or excitement that surrounds every love like the glow of a fire. Indeed, they could understand these secrets just as well as the good spirits who were inwardly angels. These evil spirits were even heard to declare that they could indeed will and act according to such wisdom, but they *would* not. When they were told that they could choose to do so if they would only shun evils as sins, they said they could do that too, but they *would* not. Thus, it appeared very clearly that the wicked have the faculty of liberty just as much as the good. Anyone may consult their own conscience and perceive that this is true: a person has the power to will,

...because the Lord, from whom that faculty comes, continually gives him power. As was said above, the Lord dwells in these two faculties in every person; consequently, He dwells in the faculty or power of being able to will. The faculty of understanding called rationality does not fully exist in a person until their natural mind reaches the age of maturity. In the meantime, it is like a seed in unripe fruit, which cannot be opened in the ground to grow into a shrub. This faculty also does not exist in those mentioned previously in section 259 <note>referring to those who lack the use of reason, such as the severely mentally disabled or those who have not reached the age of discretion</note>.

267. II. **That a bad person abuses these faculties to justify <note>original: "confirm"</note> evils and falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note>, and a good person uses them to justify goods and truths.** 

The intellectual faculty called rationality, and the voluntary faculty called liberty, enable a person to justify whatever they wish. The natural person may raise their understanding to a higher light as far as they desire it; but one who lives in evils and their resulting falsehoods does not raise it higher than the upper region of their natural mind, and rarely to the region of their spiritual mind. This is because they find pleasure in the delights of the love of their natural mind, and if they raise their understanding above that, the delight of their love is lost. If the understanding is raised higher and they see truths that are opposite to the delights of their life—or to the principles of their own intelligence—then they either distort <note>original: "falsify"</note> them, ignore them with contempt, or keep them in their memory only to serve their selfish desires or their intellectual pride.

The fact that the natural person can justify whatever they wish is obvious from the many heresies in the Christian world, each of which is justified by its followers. Who does not know that evils and falsehoods of every kind can be justified? It is possible to justify—and the wicked do justify—that there is no God, that nature is everything and created itself, and that religion is only a means to keep simple minds under control. They justify the idea that human prudence does everything and divine providence does nothing, except perhaps maintaining the universe in the order in which it was first created. They also justify murder, adultery, theft, fraud, and revenge as being allowable, according to Machiavelli <note>original: "Machiavel"; referring to Niccolò Machiavelli, whose political theories were often associated with ruthless pragmatism</note> and his followers. 

The natural person can justify these and similar things; indeed, they can fill books with such justifications. When those falsehoods are justified, they then appear in a deceptive <note>original: "infatuating"</note> light, while truths appear in such obscurity that they can only be seen as ghosts <note>original: "phantoms"</note> in the night. In short, take the most false thing imaginable, turn it into a proposition, and ask an ingenious person to justify it; they will justify it until the light of truth is completely extinguished. But if you set aside their justification and look at the proposition itself from your own rationality, you will see its falsehood in all its ugliness. 

From this, it is evident that a person may abuse these two faculties implanted in them by the Lord to justify evils and falsehoods of every kind. Animals <note>original: "beasts"</note> cannot do this because they do not possess these two faculties. Therefore, animals are born into the natural order of their lives and the knowledge of their natural instincts <note>original: "loves"</note>, quite unlike human beings.

<header>268, 269 ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART III.</header>

268. III. *That evils and falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note>, when justified <note>original: "confirmed"</note>, remain and become parts of a person's love and life.* The justification of evil and falsehood is nothing other than the removal of goodness and truth; if these justifications increase, they become total rejections. This is because evil removes and rejects what is good, and falsehood rejects what is true. Therefore, the justification of evil and falsehood also closes off heaven, for every good and truth enters through a spiritual influence <note>original: "influx"</note> from the Lord through heaven. When heaven is shut, a person is in hell, joined to a society where similar evils and falsehoods rule, and from which they cannot afterwards be withdrawn. 

I have been permitted to speak with some who justified the falsehoods of their religion within themselves ages ago, and I saw that they continue in those same beliefs just as they did when they were in the world; for everything that a person justifies becomes part of their love and life. It becomes part of their love because it becomes part of their will and understanding, and the will and understanding make up the life of everyone. When it becomes a part of a person's life, it becomes a part not only of their entire mind but also of their entire body. From this, it is clear that a person who has justified themselves in evils and falsehoods is defined by them from head to foot. They cannot then be brought back to an opposite state or drawn out of hell by any turning around or reversal <note>original: "retorsion"</note>. These points, and those previously discussed, show the origin of evil.

269. IV. *That the things which become parts of a person's love, and consequently of their life, are passed down hereditarily to their offspring.* It is well known that a person is born into evil and inherits it from their parents. Some believe this inheritance does not come from their parents, but through their parents from Adam; however, this is a mistake. A person inherits it from their father, from whom they receive their soul; this soul is then clothed with a body within the mother. The seed from the father is the first vessel <note>original: "receptacle"</note> of life, but it is a vessel shaped exactly as it was in the father. This is because it is in the form of his love, and a person's love is consistent with itself in both the greatest and smallest details, always striving toward the human form, which it gradually assumes. 

Therefore, what are called hereditary evils are passed down from fathers, grandfathers, and great-grandfathers successively to their descendants <note>original: "posterity"</note>. Experience teaches the same thing; all nations share similarities in their dispositions <note>original: "affections"</note> with their first ancestor, and families show even greater similarity, and individual households greater still. Indeed, this similarity is so strong that generations are distinguished from each other not only by their characters <note>original: "minds"</note> but by their faces. 

More will be said later regarding the inheritance or transmission of the love of evil from parents to children, when we further discuss the correspondence <note>the spiritual relationship and mapping</note> of the mind—the will and understanding—with the body and its members and organs. The few observations provided here are only intended to show that evils are derived successively from parents and that they increase by building up over generations until a person, from birth, is nothing but evil. The malice <note>original: "malignity"</note> of evil increases in proportion as the spiritual mind is closed, for—

<header>PART III. THE DIVINE LOVE.</header>

...thus the natural mind is also closed from above. This state is not restored in descendants <note>original: "posterity"</note> except by shunning evils as sins from the Lord. By this means alone is the spiritual mind opened, and the natural mind is thus brought back into a corresponding form.

270. V. *That all evils and resulting falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note>, both inherited and acquired, reside in the natural mind.* Evils and resulting falsehoods reside in the natural mind because that mind, in its form or image, is a "world," whereas the spiritual mind, in its form or image, is a "heaven." Evil cannot find a home in heaven; therefore, the spiritual mind is not opened from birth but only exists with the capacity to be opened. The natural mind also derives its form partly from the substances of the natural world, but the spiritual mind is formed only from substances of the spiritual world. These are preserved in their purity by the Lord so that a person may have the power to be made truly human: one is born an animal, but one is made a human being. The natural mind, with everything belonging to it, turns in spiral movements <term>original: "circumvolutions"; winding or coiled movements</term> from right to left, but the spiritual mind turns from left to right. Thus, these minds turn in opposite directions to each other—a sign that evil resides in the natural mind, and that of itself it acts against the spiritual mind. The rotation <term>original: "circumgyration"</term> from right to left turns downward, and consequently toward hell, but the rotation from left to right tends upward, and consequently toward heaven. That this is the case was made evident to me by the fact that an evil spirit cannot rotate his body from left to right, but only from right to left; whereas a good spirit finds it difficult to rotate his body from right to left, but easy from left to right. This rotation follows the flow of the inner workings of the mind.

271. **THAT EVILS AND FALSEHOODS ARE ENTIRELY OPPOSED TO GOODNESS AND TRUTHS, BECAUSE EVILS AND FALSEHOODS ARE DIABOLICAL AND INFERNAL, AND GOODNESS AND TRUTHS ARE DIVINE AND HEAVENLY.**

That evil and good are opposites, as are the falsehood of evil and the truth of good, is acknowledged by everyone who hears it. However, those who are in evil do not feel it as such; they perceive evil as good because it delights their senses—especially sight and hearing—and consequently delights their thoughts and perceptions. Therefore, they may acknowledge intellectually that evil and good are opposites, but when they are actually in evil, its delight causes them to say that evil is good and good is evil. For example: someone who abuses their freedom to think and do evil calls that abuse "liberty," and its opposite—thinking what is good (which is good in itself)—he calls "slavery." Nevertheless, the latter is true liberty, while the former is actually bondage. Someone who loves adultery calls the act of adultery "liberty," but calls it "slavery" to be forbidden from it; for he feels a delight in lust <note>original: "lasciviousness"</note> and a dislike for chastity. Someone who is in the love of ruling based on self-love feels a delight of life in that power that exceeds all other delights;

<header>ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING THE DIVINE LOVE: PART III.</header>

...hence everything belonging to that love he calls good, and everything which opposes it, evil—even though the reverse is true. It is the same with every other evil. Although, therefore, everyone acknowledges that evil and good are opposites, those who are immersed in evil have a distorted idea of that opposition; only those who are in good have a correct idea. No one can see what is good while they are in evil, but someone who is in good can see evil. Evil is below, as if in a cave; good is above, as if on a mountain.

272. Now, as many do not know what evil is, or that it is altogether opposite to good—and since it is important that this should be known—the subject shall be considered in the following order:
I. That the natural mind, which is in evils and resulting falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note>, is a form and image of hell. 
II. That the natural mind, which is a form and image of hell, descends by three degrees. 
III. That the three degrees of the natural mind, which is a form and image of hell, are opposite to the three degrees of the spiritual mind, which is a form and image of heaven. 
IV. That the natural mind which is hell, is entirely opposed to the spiritual mind which is heaven.

273. I. *That the natural mind, which is in evils and resulting falsehoods, is a form and image of hell.* It is not possible to describe here exactly what a person's natural mind is in its substantial form—that is, its physical structure consisting of a texture of substances from both the natural and spiritual worlds within the brain, where the mind resides in its primary sources <note>original: "principles"; refers to the biological and spiritual origins of thought in the brain</note>. A general idea of that form will be given later, where the correspondence between the mind and body is discussed. Here we shall only speak briefly of its form in terms of its states and their changes, which produce perception, thought, intention, will, and related things. 

For the natural mind that is in evils and resulting falsehoods is, in regard to them, a form and image of hell. This form requires a substantial foundation; for changes of state cannot exist without a physical substance <note>original: "substantial form"</note> as their subject, just as sight cannot exist without an eye, and hearing cannot exist without an ear. With respect, therefore, to the form or image by which the natural mind resembles hell: that form is such that the dominant love and its lusts <term>original: "concupiscences"; intense, disordered, or selfish desires</term>—which make up the general state of this mind—act like the devil in hell. The false thoughts arising from that love are like the diabolical multitude <note>original: "crew"</note>. 

This is what "the devil and his crew" refers to in the Word <note>The Bible</note>. The situation is similar: in hell, the love of ruling based on the love of self is the dominant love; this is called "the devil" there. The false affections and the thoughts arising from that love are called his "crew." It is the same in every community in hell, with specific differences. The natural mind that is in evil is organized in a similar form. Therefore, such a person enters an infernal society like themselves after death, and then in every single thing they act as one with it; for he enters into his own...

<header>PART III. THE DIVINE LOVE.</header>
<sig>95</sig>

form—that is, into the state of his own mind. There is also another love, called "Satan," which is subordinate to the former love called "the Devil." This is the love of possessing the goods of others by any evil artifice; clever malicious acts and deceptions are his followers. Those who are in this hell are generally called "satans," while those in the first-mentioned hell are generally called "devils." Those among them who do not act in secret do not refuse these names. This is why the hells, when viewed collectively <note>original: "in the complex"</note>, are called "the Devil and Satan."

There are two hells, generally distinguished according to these two loves, because all the heavens are distinguished into two kingdoms: the celestial and the spiritual. These are organized according to two types of love. The diabolical hell corresponds in opposition to the celestial kingdom, and the satanic hell corresponds in opposition to the spiritual kingdom. That the heavens are divided into these two kingdoms, the celestial and the spiritual, may be seen in the work *Heaven and Hell* <note>original: *ON HEAVEN AND HELL*; the author's 1758 work *De Coelo et ejus Mirabilibus et de Inferno*.</note>, sections 20 to 28. The natural mind, when it is in this state, is a hell in its very form, because every spiritual form is consistent with itself in both its largest and smallest parts. Therefore, every angel is a "heaven" in a smaller form (as shown in the work *Heaven and Hell*, sections 51 to 58), and every man or spirit who is a devil or a satan is a "hell" in a smaller form.

274. II. *That the natural mind, which is a form or image of hell, descends by three degrees.* We have seen previously (sections 222 to 229) that degrees of two kinds—degrees of altitude <term>vertical or discrete levels</term> and degrees of latitude <term>horizontal or continuous expansion</term>—exist in the greatest and least of all things. This is also true of the natural mind in its greatest and smallest parts; here, we are discussing degrees of altitude.

The natural mind, by virtue of its two faculties called rationality and liberty <note>Rationality is the capacity to understand truth, while liberty is the freedom to choose it.</note>, is in a state where it can either ascend through three degrees or descend through three degrees. It ascends by means of goods and truths, and it descends by means of evils and falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note>. When it ascends, the lower degrees that lead toward hell are closed; when it descends, the higher degrees that lead toward heaven are closed. This is because these degrees act in opposition to one another. 

These three higher and lower degrees are not opened or shut immediately when a person is born, for a newborn is in a state of ignorance regarding good and truth, as well as evil and falsehood. But as a person adopts these principles, the degrees are opened or shut on one side or the other. When they are opened toward hell, the highest or most internal place is occupied by the ruling love (which belongs to the will). The second or middle place is occupied by thoughts of falsehood, which the understanding produces from that love. The lowest place is occupied by the final results of that love through thought, or of the will through the understanding. This follows the same order as the degrees of altitude discussed earlier: they move from end to cause to effect—or from the primary goal, through the intermediate means, to the final outcome. As these degrees descend toward the body, they grow denser and more material.

# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING THE DIVINE LOVE
### PART III.

...and corporeal. If truths from the Word <note>The Word refers to the Sacred Scripture or the Bible.</note> are applied to the second degree to form it, then those truths are falsified by the first degree—which is the love of evil—and made into servants and slaves. From this, it is clear what happens to the truths of the church taken from the Word when they are held by those who are in the love of evil, or whose natural mind is a form of hell: by being made subservient to the devil as tools, they are profaned. This is because the love of evil ruling in the natural mind (which is hell) is "the devil," as was said before.

275. **III. That the three degrees of the natural mind, which is a form and image of hell, are opposite to the three degrees of the spiritual mind, which is a form and image of heaven.**

It was shown above that there are three degrees of the mind—natural, spiritual, and celestial—and that the human mind, consisting of these degrees, looks toward heaven and turns spirally in that direction. From this, it can be seen that when the natural mind looks downward and turns <note>original: "circumgyrates"</note> toward hell, it also consists of three degrees, each of which is opposite to a degree of the mind that is heaven.

That this is the case was made evident to me from what I have seen in the spiritual world: namely, that there are three heavens, and these are distinct according to three degrees of height <note>original: "altitude"</note>; and that there are three hells, and these are also distinct according to three degrees of height or depth <note>original: "profundity"</note>. The hells, in every detail, are opposite to the heavens. Specifically, the lowest hell is opposite to the highest heaven, the middle hell to the middle heaven, and the highest hell to the lowest <note>original: "ultimate"; in this context, the first or entry-level heaven</note> heaven. It is the same with the natural mind that is in the form of hell, for spiritual forms are consistent with themselves in both the greatest and the least things.

The heavens and hells are in this state of opposition because their loves are in such opposition. Love for the Lord, and the resulting love for the neighbor, constitute the inmost degree in the heavens; however, the love of self and the love of the world constitute the inmost degree in the hells. Wisdom and intelligence rooted in their respective loves constitute the middle degree in the heavens, but folly and insanity (which appear as wisdom and intelligence) rooted in their loves constitute the middle degree in the hells. Lastly, the conclusions from these two degrees—which are either stored in the memory as knowledge or expressed in the body as actions—constitute the lowest <note>original: "ultimate"</note> degree in the heavens. Likewise, the conclusions from their two degrees—which either become knowledge or action—form the outermost degree in the hells.

How the goods and truths of heaven are turned into evils and falsities in the hells—and thus into opposites—may be seen from the following experience. I heard that a certain divine truth from heaven descended by flow <note>original: "influx"</note> into hell, and I was told that as it descended, it was turned by degrees into what was false, until in the lowest hell it became something altogether opposite. From this it was evident...

...that the hells are in a step-by-step opposition to the heavens regarding goodness and truths. Goodness and truths become evils and falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note> through influx <note>The flow of spiritual influence or life from God into the mind.</note> into forms that have been turned in the opposite direction. It is well known that anything entering by influx is perceived and felt according to the forms receiving it and their current state. 

I was also shown that they are turned into their opposite through the following experience: I was allowed to see the hells in their position relative to the heavens, and the inhabitants appeared upside down, with their heads pointing downward and their feet upward. However, I was told that they still seem upright to themselves. This situation can be compared to that of the **antipodes** <note>People living on the opposite side of the globe.</note>. These experiences show that the three degrees of the natural mind—which in its form and image is a hell—are opposite to the three degrees of the spiritual mind, which in its form and image is a heaven.

276. **IV. That the natural mind, which is a hell, is in complete opposition to the spiritual mind, which is a heaven.** 

When loves are opposite, then everything related to perception becomes opposite as well. This is because all things flow from love—which constitutes the very life of a person—like rivers from their source. Those things that are not based in love separate themselves in the natural mind from those that are. The things based in love stay in the center, while the rest move to the sides. These side elements, if they are truths of the church from the Word <note>The Word refers to the Sacred Scripture or the Bible.</note>, are pushed further and further away and eventually expelled. 

In this state, the person (or the natural mind) perceives evil as good and sees falsehood as truth, and vice versa. Consequently, he thinks malice is ***wisdom***, insanity is ***intelligence***, cunning is ***prudence***, and evil schemes are ***ingenuity***. He also ignores the divine and heavenly aspects of the church and worship, while exalting physical and worldly things to the highest place. Thus, he inverts his state of life, so that what belongs at the head he places at the soles of the feet and tramples upon; and what belongs at the soles of the feet he places at the head. In this way, a person changes from being alive to being dead; one whose mind is a heaven is said to be alive, and one whose mind is a hell is said to be dead.

277. **THAT ALL THINGS OF THE THREE DEGREES OF THE NATURAL MIND ARE INCLUDED IN WORKS, WHICH ARE PERFORMED BY ACTS OF THE BODY.** 

The <term>science of degrees</term> <note>The study of the distinct levels of existence—natural, spiritual, and celestial—and how they relate to one another.</note> delivered in this part reveals the following <term>arcanum</term> <note>original: Latin for "secret" or "mystery"; a profound hidden truth.</note>: all things of a person’s mind, including their will and understanding, are contained in their actions or works, just as visible and invisible things are contained in a seed, fruit, or egg. On the outside, actions or works appear to be nothing more than physical movements; yet internally, there are countless components. These include the combined powers of the muscle fibers of the whole body and everything in the mind that triggers and directs those powers—which exist in three degrees, as was shown above.

277—280 ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART III.

...and as there are all things of a man's mind, there are all things of his **will**, or all the affections of his love, which constitute the first degree; there are all things of his **understanding**, or all the thoughts of his perception, which constitute the second degree; and there are all things of his **memory**, or all the ideas of the thought closest to speech, which compose the third degree. From these, when directed toward action, physical works exist. In these works, when seen in their external form, the prior things <note>The internal causes or higher degrees that exist before the physical act.</note> which they actually contain are not visible. That the ultimate <note>The final, outward expression or "bottom" level of a process.</note> is the sum, container, and basis of prior things, may be seen above in numbers 209 to 216; and that the degrees of altitude <note>Discrete levels of reality (like end, cause, and effect) rather than gradual changes in intensity.</note> are in their fullness in their ultimate expression, in numbers 217 to 221.

278. Acts of the body, seen by the eye, appear simple and uniform—much like seeds, fruits, and eggs in their external form, or like nuts and almonds in their shells. And yet, they contain all the prior principles from which they exist, because every ultimate has a covering and is thereby distinct from the things that came before it. Each degree is enclosed in a covering and is thereby distinct from the others; therefore, the things of the first degree are not known by the second degree, nor the things of the second by the third. 

For example: the love of the will (which is the first degree of the mind) is not known in the wisdom of the understanding (the second degree), except by a certain delight that accompanies a thought about a thing. Nor is the first degree—the love of the will—known in the knowledge of the memory (the third degree), except by a certain pleasure in knowing and speaking. From this it follows that a "work" (which is an action of the body) includes all these things, even though in its external form it appears simple and as a single whole.

279. This is proved by the following fact: the angels who are present with a person perceive individually the things of the mind that are within an action. The **spiritual angels** perceive the things within it that come from the understanding, and the **celestial angels** <note>In Swedenborg's theology, celestial angels relate to the heart and will (love), while spiritual angels relate to the lungs and understanding (wisdom).</note> perceive the things within it that come from the will. This seems like a paradox, but it is nonetheless true. 

It should be noted, however, that the things of the mind belonging to the specific object being considered are in the center, while the rest are arranged around them according to how closely they are related. The angels say that a person’s character is perceived from every work, and that each act is a unique likeness of their love, depending on how that love is directed toward certain affections and thoughts. In short, to the angels, every act or work of a spiritual person is like a delicious, useful, and beautiful fruit which, when opened and eaten, provides flavor, use, and delight. That the angels have such a perception of the actions and works of men may also be seen above, in number 220.

280. It is the same with human speech. The angels know a person’s love from the sound of their voice, their wisdom from the articulation of that sound, and their knowledge from the meaning of the words. They say that these three are present in every expression.

Furthermore, every expression in the Word <note>The Bible, or Sacred Scripture.</note> contains a spiritual meaning related to divine wisdom and a celestial meaning related to divine love. These are perceived by the angels whenever a person reads the Word devoutly, as has been shown extensively in *The Doctrine of the New Jerusalem Concerning the Sacred Scripture*.

281. It follows, then, that in the works of a person whose natural mind descends through three degrees toward hell, all of their evils and false beliefs are present. Conversely, in the works of a person whose natural mind ascends toward heaven, all of their goodness and truths are present. Both are perceived by the angels simply from the person's speech and actions. This is why it is said in the Word that a person is to be judged according to their works, and that they must give an account of their words.

->PART IV.<-

282. **THAT THE LORD FROM ETERNITY, WHO IS JEHOVAH, CREATED THE UNIVERSE AND EVERYTHING WITHIN IT FROM HIMSELF, AND NOT FROM NOTHING.** It is known throughout the world and acknowledged by every wise person through inner perception that there is one God, the Creator of the universe. We know from the Word that God the Creator is called JEHOVAH, a name derived from *Being* <note>original: "ESSE," the Latin verb for "to be." It refers to the core of God's existence.</note>, because He alone IS. The fact that the Lord from eternity is that Jehovah is shown at length from the Word in the *Doctrine of the New Jerusalem Concerning the Lord*. Jehovah is called the Lord from eternity because He took on human form <note>original: "the human"</note> so that He might save people from hell; He then commanded His disciples to call Him "Lord." Therefore, in the New Testament, Jehovah is called the Lord, as can be seen from these passages: “You shall love *Jehovah your God* with all your heart and with all your soul” (Deuteronomy 6:5); which in the New Testament is expressed as, “You shall love *the Lord your God* with all your heart and with all your soul” (Matthew 22:37). The same is true in other passages taken from the Old Testament in the Gospels.

283. Anyone who thinks with clear reason sees that the universe was not created out of nothing, because they see that it is impossible for anything to be made from nothing. Nothing is nothing, and to make something out of nothing is a contradiction. A contradiction is contrary to the light of truth, which comes from divine wisdom; and whatever does not come from divine wisdom does not come from divine omnipotence <note>The quality of having unlimited power.</note>. Anyone who thinks with...

<summary>This page argues that human speech and actions contain the full state of a person's mind and that the universe was created by Jehovah from His own essence rather than from nothing.</summary>
<keywords>Third Heaven, The Word, Sacred Scripture, Jehovah, Esse, Creation, New Jerusalem, Divine Wisdom, Divine Omnipotence, Creatio ex nihilo</keywords>

--- Page 111 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the origin of the universe, focusing on the idea that God created all things from His own substance rather than from nothing. It explains why humans often struggle to understand this concept because they try to view God through the limitations of time and space.</meta>

283. A person with clear reason also sees that all things were created out of a substance that is "substance in itself." This is the essential Being <note>original: "esse," the Latin verb "to be," referring here to the fundamental essence of existence.</note> from which everything that exists originates. Since God alone is substance in itself and the essential Being, it is obvious that the existence of things comes from no other source. Many people have realized this because reason allows them to see it; however, they did not dare to confirm it. They feared that if they did, they might come to think that the created universe is God because it comes from God, or that nature exists by its own power, and thus that the innermost part of nature is what we call God.

Consequently, although many have seen that the existence of all things comes from no source other than God and His Being, they did not dare to think further than that first thought. They were afraid of tangling their understanding in a "Gordian knot" <note>An ancient Greek metaphor for a complex problem that is nearly impossible to untie or solve.</note> from which they might never escape. The reason they might not have been able to free their understanding is that they thought about God and the creation of the universe through the lenses of time and space, which belong only to the natural world. No one can perceive God and the creation of the universe from a natural perspective; however, anyone whose understanding has even a small amount of inner light can perceive nature and its creation from God’s perspective, because God is not bound by time and space. 

It was shown earlier that the Divine is not in space (see sections 7–10), that the Divine fills all spaces of the universe without being limited by space (sections 69–72), and that the Divine is in all time without being limited by time (sections 73–76). In the following pages, we will see that although God created the universe and everything within it from Himself, there is still nothing at all in the created universe that *is* God. Further points will also be shown that will clarify this matter.

284. *The first part of this work discussed God: that He is Divine Love and Divine Wisdom, and that He is Life; also that He is a substance and form which is the essential and sole Being <note>original: "esse."</note>. The second part discussed the spiritual sun and its world, and the natural sun and its world, and how the universe with everything in it was created from God through these two suns. The third part discussed the degrees <note>In this context, "degrees" refers to the levels of reality or tiers of existence.</note> in which everything was created. This fourth part will now discuss the creation of the universe by God. These subjects are discussed because the angels have expressed sorrow before the Lord: when they look down into the world, they see nothing but darkness. They see that among humans there is no knowledge of God, of heaven, or of the creation of nature—knowledge upon which their angelic wisdom might find a solid foundation.*

285. **THAT THE LORD FROM ETERNITY, OR JEHOVAH, COULD NOT HAVE CREATED THE UNIVERSE AND EVERYTHING WITHIN IT IF HE WERE NOT A MAN.** Those who have a physical and bodily <note>original: "natural corporeal"</note> idea of God as a man cannot understand at all how God, as a man, could create the universe and everything within it. They think to themselves: how could God, as a man, travel through the universe from one space to another to...

### PART IV. THE DIVINE LOVE.

...space, and create? Or how can He, from His place, speak the word and have creation follow immediately once it is spoken? These difficulties arise in the mind when it is said that God is a man, especially among those who think of the **God-Man** <term>God-Man: The concept that God is the essential "Divine Human," the source of all human qualities like love and wisdom, rather than a limited physical person.</term> as if He were just a person in this world, and who think about God based on nature and the things belonging to nature, which are time and space.

But those who think of the **God-Man** without focusing on their ideas of a person in this world—and not from the perspective of nature and its space and time—clearly perceive that the universe could not have been created if God were not a man. Direct your thoughts toward the angelic idea of God as a human being; remove the idea of space as much as you can, and in your thoughts, you will come near to the truth. 

Some scholars even realize that spirits and angels are not in space, because they perceive what is spiritual as being without space. It is like thought: although thought is within a person, that person can use it to be present elsewhere, even in the most remote place. This is the state of spirits and angels, who are human beings even in regard to their bodies. They appear in the place where their thought is, because spaces and distances in the spiritual world are appearances that act as one with their thoughts and their feelings <note>original: "affection." In this context, it refers to the underlying desire or emotion that drives thought.</note>. 

From this, it may be evident that God—who appears far above the spiritual world as a sun, and to whom there can be no appearance of space—is not to be thought of in terms of space. In this way, it can be understood that He created the universe not out of nothing, but out of Himself. Additionally, His human body cannot be thought of as either great or small, or of any particular height, because these are qualities of space. Therefore, in both the first principles and the final results—in the greatest things and the smallest—He is the same. Furthermore, the human essence is the innermost part of everything created, yet it exists without the limitations of space. That the Divine is the same in both the greatest and the smallest things can be seen above in sections 77 to 82; and that the Divine fills all spaces without being limited by space can be seen in sections 69 to 72. Because the Divine is not in space, it is also not a continuous physical substance, as the innermost part of nature is.

286. That God could not have created the universe and everything in it if He were not a man can be very clearly understood by a rational person from this consideration: he cannot deny within himself that in God there are love and wisdom, mercy and kindness, and goodness and truth themselves, because these come from Him. And as he cannot deny that these things are in God, neither can he deny that God is a man; for none of these things can exist in the abstract, apart from a person. A human being is their "subject" <note>In philosophy, a "subject" is the underlying entity or being in which qualities (like wisdom or love) exist. Without a subject, the qualities have nowhere to "live."</note>, and to separate them from their subject is to say they do not exist. 

Think of wisdom, and try to imagine it existing outside of a person. Is it anything? Can you imagine it as something airy <note>original: "æthereal."</note> or like a flame? You cannot—unless perhaps you imagine it within those very elements. But if it were in them, it would have to be wisdom in a form, such as a human has; it would have to be in every part of that form. Not one thing can be missing for wisdom to reside in it. In a word, the form of wisdom is a man; and since a man is the form of wisdom, he is also the form of love, mercy,

mercy, goodness, and truth, because these are one with wisdom. That love and wisdom cannot exist except in a form may be seen above in paragraphs 40 to 43.

287. That love and wisdom are human may also be seen from the angels of heaven. To the extent that they are in love and the resulting wisdom from the Lord, they are, in that same proportion, beautiful human beings. The same is shown by what is said of Adam in the Word <note>The "Word" refers to the Sacred Scripture or the Bible.</note>, "that he was created into the likeness and image of God" (Genesis 1:26, 27), because he was created into the form of love and wisdom. Every person on earth is born in a human form regarding their physical body because their spirit—also called the soul—is a human being. It is human because it is capable of receiving love and wisdom from the Lord. To the degree that a person's spirit or soul receives these, they become a human being after the death of the physical body that once surrounded them. To the extent that they do not receive them, they become a monster, which only retains a shadow of humanity because of the underlying capacity to receive.

288. Because God is human, the entire angelic heaven in its totality resembles one human being; it is divided into regions and provinces according to the members, internal organs, and sensory organs of a human. There are communities in heaven that form the "province" of every part of the brain and every organ of the face, as well as every internal organ of the body. These provinces are distinct from each other, just as those organs are in a person; the angels also know which province of the human form they occupy. The entire heaven exists in this form because God is human. Furthermore, God is heaven because the angels who make up heaven are recipients of love and wisdom from the Lord, and those who receive Him are His images. That heaven is in the form of all the parts of a human being is shown in *Heavenly Secrets* <note>original: *Arcana Cœlestia*, Swedenborg's multi-volume commentary on Genesis and Exodus.</note> at the end of several chapters.

289. These points show how empty the ideas are of those who think of God as anything other than a human being, or who think of divine qualities as being anything other than qualities in God as a human being. When separated from the human form, these attributes are just mental fantasies. That God is the essential Human, and that every person is human only to the extent they receive love and wisdom from God, may be seen above in paragraphs 11, 12, and 13. This is repeated here because of what follows: so that the creation of the universe may be understood as being produced by God precisely because He is human.

### 290. THAT THE LORD FROM ETERNITY, OR JEHOVAH, PRODUCED THE SUN OF THE SPIRITUAL WORLD FROM HIMSELF, AND THROUGH IT CREATED THE UNIVERSE AND EVERYTHING WITHIN IT.

The sun of the spiritual world was discussed in Part II. It was shown there that divine love and wisdom appear in the spiritual world as a sun (paragraphs 83 to 88); that spiritual heat and light flow from that sun (paragraphs 89 to 92); and that the sun itself is not God, but is an outpouring from the divine love and divine wisdom of the **God-Man** <term>God-Man: The Divine Human; the concept that God is the source and essence of all human qualities, such as love and wisdom.</term>.

...so likewise the heat and light of that sun (see paragraphs 93 to 98). It was shown that the sun of the spiritual world is at a middle height and appears as distant from the angels as the sun of the natural world does from people (paragraphs 103 to 107). In the spiritual world, the east is where the Lord appears as a sun, and the other directions are determined by it (paragraphs 119 to 123, 124 to 128). The angels constantly turn their faces toward the Lord as a sun (paragraphs 129 to 134, 135 to 139). The Lord created the universe and everything in it through that sun, which is the immediate emanation <note>original: "proximate proceeding"</note> of divine love and divine wisdom (paragraphs 151 to 156). The sun of the natural world is pure fire, and therefore nature—which originates from that sun—is "dead" <note>meaning inert or lacking inherent life compared to the spiritual realm</note>. This natural sun was created so that the work of creation could be completed and contained (paragraphs 157 to 162). Without two suns—one living and the other dead—there could be no creation (paragraphs 163 to 166).

291. It was further shown in Part II that the spiritual sun is not the Lord Himself, but is an emanation from His divine love and divine wisdom. It is called an emanation because that sun is produced from divine love and divine wisdom, which are in themselves actual substance and form; through this sun, the Divine flows out. However, human reason is such that it is not satisfied unless it understands a thing through its cause—unless it perceives how things are brought about. Therefore, something should be said regarding how the sun of the spiritual world (which is not the Lord, but an emanation from Him) is produced. 

I have had many conversations with the angels about this. They said that they perceive it clearly in their spiritual light, but that it was difficult for them to explain it to a person in their natural light because of the great difference between these two types of light and the thoughts that come from them. They said, however, that the spiritual sun is like the sphere of affections and thoughts that surrounds every angel, which makes their presence felt both near and far. This surrounding sphere <note>original: "ambient sphere"</note> is not the angel itself, but comes from every part of their body; substances continually emanate <note>original: "emane"</note> from them like a flowing stream. What emanates surrounds them; these substances remain connected to the body and are constantly moved by the two sources of life—the heart and the lungs. This movement activates the surrounding atmospheres and creates a perception of the angel's presence in others. 

Therefore, there isn't a *separate* sphere of affections and thoughts that leaves the angel and continues onward, though it is often called that; rather, the affections are simply the states of the mental structures within them. They said, moreover, that such a sphere exists around every angel because it exists around the Lord. That sphere around the Lord is likewise from Him, and that sphere is their sun, or the sun of the spiritual world.

### 292—295 ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART IV.

292. I have often been allowed to perceive that there is such a sphere surrounding every angel and spirit, as well as a general sphere surrounding several individuals in a community. I have also been shown this through various appearances. In heaven, it sometimes appears as a thin flame; in hell, it appears as a thick, heavy fire. Sometimes in heaven it appears as a thin, white cloud <note>original Latin: *nubis*</note>, and in hell as a thick, black storm cloud <note>original Latin: *nimbi*</note>. I have also been allowed to perceive these spheres through various types of scents and stenches. This proved to me that a sphere of substances—dissolved and released from their bodies—surrounds everyone in both heaven and hell.

293. It was also perceived that a sphere flows out not only from angels and spirits, but also from each and every thing that appears in that world: from trees and fruits, shrubs and flowers, herbs and grasses, and even from the soil and all its parts. From this it was evident that it is a universal law, in both living and dead things, that everything is surrounded by something similar to what is inside it, and that this substance is constantly exhaling from it. That the same is true in the natural world is known from the experience of many learned men; for example, they know that a stream of particles <note>original: "effluvia"</note> constantly flows from humans, from all animals, and from trees, fruits, shrubs, flowers, and even from metals and stones. The natural world derives this quality from the spiritual world, and the spiritual world derives it from the Divine.

294. Since the things that make up the sun of the spiritual world are from the Lord, but are not the Lord Himself, they are not "life in itself," but lack life in themselves. This is just like the things that flow from an angel or a human and create the spheres around them; these emanations are not the angel or the person, but are from them and lack their life. These spheres are united with the angel or person only in that they harmonize with them, being derived from the forms of their bodies, which were the forms of the life within them. This is a spiritual secret <term>arcanum</term> <note>a hidden or profound truth known to those with specialized knowledge</note> that the angels can see in their thoughts and express in their speech through their spiritual ideas. However, humans cannot do this with their natural ideas, because a thousand spiritual ideas make up a single natural idea. One natural idea cannot be broken down by a human into any spiritual idea, much less into so many; for the two differ according to <term>degrees of altitude</term> <note>distinct levels of reality, such as the relationship between cause and effect, which cannot be measured on a continuous scale</note>, which were discussed in Part III.

295. The fact that there is such a difference between the thoughts of angels and humans was revealed to me through this experience: angels were told to think of something spiritually and then to tell me what they thought of. When they tried to tell me, they could not, saying they were unable to express it. It was the same with their spiritual speech and spiritual writing. There was not a single word of spiritual speech that was like a word of natural speech, nor was there anything in spiritual writing like natural writing—except for the letters, each of which contained a distinct meaning. But what is wonderful, they said, that they seemed—

## 296. THAT IN THE LORD THERE ARE THREE THINGS WHICH ARE THE LORD: THE DIVINE OF LOVE, THE DIVINE OF WISDOM, AND THE DIVINE OF USE; AND THAT THESE THREE ARE PRESENTED IN APPEARANCE OUT OF THE SUN OF THE SPIRITUAL WORLD: THE DIVINE OF LOVE BY HEAT, THE DIVINE OF WISDOM BY LIGHT, AND THE DIVINE OF USE BY THE ATMOSPHERE, WHICH IS THE CONTAINER <note>original: "continent"; used here to mean that which contains or holds something together</note>.

It has been shown above (numbers 89 to 92, 99 to 102, 146 to 150) that heat and light proceed from the sun of the spiritual world, and that the heat proceeds from the Lord's divine love while the light proceeds from His divine wisdom. It shall now be shown that the third thing proceeding from the sun in that world is the atmosphere, which is the container of heat and light, and that this atmosphere proceeds from the Lord’s Divine essence, which is called use.

297. Anyone who thinks with any enlightenment can see that love has use as its goal and intention, and produces use by means of wisdom. Love by itself cannot produce any use except through wisdom. Indeed, what is love unless there is something that is loved? This "something" is use; and since use is what is loved and it is produced by wisdom, it follows that use is the container of wisdom and love. That these three—love, wisdom, and use—follow in order according to degrees of altitude, and that the final <note>original: "ultimate"</note> degree is the sum, the container, and the basis of the previous degrees, was shown in numbers 209 to 216 and elsewhere. From this, it may appear that these three—the Divine of love, the Divine of wisdom, and the Divine of use—are in the Lord, and that in their essence, they are the Lord.

298. It will be fully shown in what follows that a human being, considered in both their outer and inner aspects, is a form of all uses, and that all uses in the created universe correspond to those uses. This is mentioned here merely to show that God, as a Man, is the essential form of all uses—the form from which all uses in the created universe originate; and that the created universe, viewed in terms of its uses, is an image of God. Those things that are from God-Man (that is, from the Lord) by the order of creation are called uses; but those that are from a person’s *self-ego* <note>original Latin: *proprium*; a term for the self or what is "one's own," usually implying the fallen or selfish nature of man</note> are not, for that *self-ego* is hell, and the things that come from it are contrary to order.

299. Now, since these three—love, wisdom, and use—are in the Lord and *are* the Lord, and since the Lord is everywhere (being omnipresent), and since He cannot present Himself to any angel or human exactly as He is in Himself and in His own sun, He therefore presents Himself through such things as can be received: He presents love by heat, wisdom by light, and use by the atmosphere. The Lord presents Himself as use by the atmosphere, because the...

The atmosphere is the container <note>original: "continent"; that which holds or carries something</note> of heat and light, just as use is the container of love and wisdom. The light and heat that proceed from the Divine Sun cannot travel through nothing—and consequently not through a vacuum—but must move through some container which is their subject. We call this container the atmosphere, which surrounds the sun, receives it into itself, and conveys it to the heavens where angels dwell, and from there to the world where men dwell, thus making the Lord present everywhere.

300. It was shown above (sections 173 to 178, and 179 to 183) that there are atmospheres in the spiritual world as well as in the natural world. It was also stated that the atmospheres of the spiritual world are spiritual, while those of the natural world are natural. Now, from the origin of the spiritual atmosphere which closely surrounds the spiritual sun, it can be seen that every part of it, in its essence, is exactly like the sun in its essence. The angels prove this through their spiritual ideas, which are not limited by physical space. They consider that there is only one single substance which is the source of all things, and that the sun of the spiritual world is that substance. Since the Divine is not limited by space and is the same in both the greatest and the smallest things, the same is true of that sun, which is the first proceeding of the God-Man <note>original: "God-Man"; Swedenborg’s term for the Lord as the source of all human qualities</note>. Furthermore, that unique substance—the sun—proceeds through atmospheres by degrees of continuity <note>original: "degrees of latitude"; gradual changes like thinning or thickening</note> and, at the same time, through discrete degrees <note>original: "degrees of altitude"; distinct levels or tiers, like the difference between a thought and a word</note>, producing the variety of all things in the created universe. The angels said that these concepts can never be understood unless ideas of physical space are removed from the mind. If space is not removed, it is impossible for appearances not to lead to misconceptions; however, such misconceptions cannot take root as long as one remembers that God is the real Being <note>original: "esse"; the fundamental essence or "to-be-ness" of God</note> from which all things originate.

301. Moreover, from angelic ideas which are free from the concept of space, it is clear that in the created universe nothing has life except the God-Man alone (that is, the Lord), and nothing moves except by life from Him; and that nothing exists except by the sun from Him. Thus, it is a truth that in God "we live, move, and have our being." <note>This is a reference to Acts 17:28 in the New Testament.</note>

### 302. THAT THE ATMOSPHERES, WHICH ARE THREE IN BOTH THE SPIRITUAL AND NATURAL WORLDS, END IN THEIR FINAL FORMS <note>original: "ultimates"</note> AS SUBSTANCES AND MATTERS LIKE THOSE ON THE EARTH.

It was shown in Part III (sections 173 to 176) that there are three atmospheres in both the spiritual and natural worlds, which are distinct from each other according to vertical degrees <note>original: "degrees of altitude"</note>, and which decrease as they descend according to horizontal degrees <note>original: "degrees of latitude"</note>. Since the atmospheres decrease as they descend, it follows that they become continually more compressed and inactive <note>original: "inert"</note>. At length, in their final forms, they become so compressed and inactive that they are no longer atmospheres but substances at rest—and in the natural world, they become fixed, like the substances on earth called matter. This origin of substances and matters shows, first, that these substances and matters are also of three de—

# PART IV. THE DIVINE LOVE.

...degrees. Second, that they are held together in mutual connection by the surrounding atmospheres. Third, that they are adapted to produce all "uses" <note>original: "uses"; in this context, "uses" refers to the beneficial functions or purposes for which something is created</note> in their proper forms.

303. Everyone who believes that there is a continuous chain of intermediate stages from the first to the last agrees that substances or matter, like those found on earth, were produced from the sun through its atmospheres. They understand that nothing can exist except from something prior to itself, and ultimately from the First Cause. This First Cause is the sun of the spiritual world, and at the heart of that sun is the God-Man <note>original: "God-Man"; Swedenborg’s term for the Lord as the source of all human qualities</note>, or the Lord. Since the atmospheres are the prior things through which that sun presents itself in "ultimates" <term>ultimates: the final, most physical, or outward stage of a process</term>, and since these prior things continually decrease in activity and expansion as they reach these final stages, it follows that when their activity and expansion cease in the ultimates, they become substances and matters like those on earth. These substances retain from the atmospheres they originated from a drive and an effort to produce uses. Those who do not understand that the universe and everything in it were created through continuous steps from the First Cause cannot help but build disconnected theories that are separated from their causes. When these are examined by a mind that looks deeply into the inner nature of things, they do not appear like well-built houses, but like piles of debris.

304. Because of this universal origin of everything in the created universe, there is a similarity in every part of it: they all proceed from their first stage to their last. These last stages are in a state of rest so that the process may conclude and remain stable <note>original: "subsist"</note>. For example, in the human body, fibers proceed from their initial forms until they become tendons; fibers and vessels proceed from their beginnings until they become cartilage and bone, upon which they can rest and remain firm. Just as there is such a progression of fibers and vessels in a person from first to last, there is a similar progression of their "states"—which are sensations, thoughts, and affections. These must also pass from their first stage, where they are in light, to their last stage, where they are in shade; or from their first, where they are in heat, to their last, where they are not. In short, there is such a progression for love and all its attributes, and for wisdom and all its attributes—indeed, for everything in the created universe. This is the same principle shown earlier (sections 222 to 229): that there are degrees of both kinds <note>original: "degrees of both kinds"; referring to "discrete" degrees (distinct levels) and "continuous" degrees (gradual scales)</note> in the greatest and smallest of all created things. There are also degrees of both kinds in the very smallest things because the spiritual sun is the only true substance and the source of all things, as understood by the angels in their spiritual ideas (section 300).

305. **THAT THERE IS NOTHING OF THE DIVINE ITSELF WITHIN THE SUBSTANCES AND MATTERS THAT MAKE UP THE EARTHS, YET THEY STILL ORIGINATE FROM THE DIVINE ITSELF.**

The origin of the earths, discussed in the previous section, shows that there is nothing of the Divine itself within the substances and matters that compose them. Instead, they are deprived of everything Divine in

305. ...itself. As previously stated, these substances are the ends and terminations of the atmospheres, where their heat has ended in cold, their light in darkness, and their activity in stillness <note>original: "inertness"</note>. Yet, through their continuity from the spiritual sun, they have brought with them what was present there from the Divine. As mentioned above (sections 291 to 293), this was a sphere surrounding the God-Man <note>original: "God-Man"; Swedenborg’s term for the Lord as the source of all human qualities</note>, or the Lord. From this sphere—proceeding from the sun by means of the atmospheres—the substances and matters that make up the earths were formed.

306. The origin of earths from the spiritual sun through the atmospheres cannot be described using words based on natural ideas. It can only be described through spiritual ideas, because the latter are independent of space; therefore, they do not easily translate into the words of a natural language. As shown in section 295, spiritual thought, speech, and writing differ so much from their natural counterparts that they have nothing in common and communicate only through "correspondences" <term>correspondences: the symbolic or functional relationship between spiritual causes and natural effects</term>. It is sufficient, therefore, for the origin of earths to be understood to some degree from a natural perspective.

### 307. ALL USES, WHICH ARE THE GOALS OF CREATION, ARE IN FORMS, AND THEY RECEIVE THESE FORMS FROM SUBSTANCES AND MATTERS LIKE THOSE ON EARTH.

All things discussed so far—the sun, the atmospheres, and the earths—are only means to an end. The goals <note>original: "ends"</note> of creation are the things produced by the Lord as a sun, through the atmospheres and from the earths. These goals are called **uses**. They include everything in the vegetable kingdom and the animal kingdom, and ultimately the human race, through which the angelic heaven exists. These are called uses because they are recipients of divine love and divine wisdom. Furthermore, they look toward God, their Creator, and thereby unite Him to His great work; through this connection, they continue to exist <note>original: "subsist"</note> by Him, just as they were first created by Him. 

We say that they "look toward God" and "unite Him to His work," but this is described from how it appears. The reality is that God the Creator causes them to look toward Him and unite themselves to Him as if by their own power. How they look toward Him and connect will be explained in the following sections. This was touched upon earlier, where it was shown:
* That divine love and divine wisdom must exist in other things created from them (sections 47 to 51).
* That everything in the created universe receives divine love and wisdom (sections 54 to 60).
* That the uses of all created things ascend through levels to humanity, and through humanity to God the Creator from whom they originate (sections 65 to 68).

308. Anyone can see clearly that the goals of creation are uses when they consider that nothing else can exist—and therefore nothing else could be created—from God the Creator but use. And in...

...in order for something to be a "use" <term>use: a functional service or benefit that fulfills a divine purpose</term>, it must be for others. Even a use performed for one’s self is actually for others, since a person cares for themselves so that they may be in the right state to be of use to others. Anyone who thinks this way may also realize that "use"—in its true sense—cannot come from man alone; it exists with man but comes from Him who is the source of everything that exists as a use—that is, from the Lord.

### 309.
Since we are now considering the forms of these uses, they shall be treated in the following order:
I. That there is an effort <note>original: "endeavor"</note> within the earth to produce uses in specific forms, or "forms of uses."
II. That there is a certain image of the entire creation within all forms of uses.
III. That there is a certain image of man within all forms of uses.
IV. That there is a certain image of the infinite and eternal within all forms of uses.

### 310. I. THAT WITHIN THE EARTH THERE IS AN EFFORT TO PRODUCE USES IN FORMS, OR FORMS OF USES.
It is evident that such an effort exists within the earth because of its origin. The substances and materials that make up the earth are the endpoints and boundaries <note>original: "ends and terminations"</note> of the atmospheres that proceed as uses from the spiritual sun (as seen above in sections 305 and 306). Because the materials of the earth come from that origin, and because their physical masses are held together by the surrounding pressure of the atmospheres, it follows that they possess a perpetual effort to produce forms of uses. They derive this ability to produce from their origin, as they are the final products <note>original: "ultimates"</note> of the atmospheres and are therefore in harmony with them. 

When we say this effort and quality are "in the earth," we mean they are in the substances and materials of the earth, whether they are in the ground itself or have evaporated from the earth into the atmospheres; it is well known that the atmospheres are full of such substances. This effort and quality in earthly matter are clearly seen in the fact that seeds of all kinds, when opened to their innermost core by heat, are impregnated by very subtle <note>original: "subtile"</note> substances. These substances, being from a spiritual origin, have the power to connect themselves to a "use"—which gives them their reproductive <note>original: "prolific"</note> quality. Then, by joining with matter from a natural origin, they cannot help but produce forms of uses. Afterward, they send them forth as if from a womb, so that they may come into the light, sprout <note>original: "germinate"</note>, and grow. 

This effort continues from the earth through the root to the final parts, and from the final parts back to the primary parts <note>original: "primaries"</note> where the use itself originates. In this way, uses pass into forms. These forms are derived from a "use," which acts like a soul as they progress from primary to final stages and back again. Because of this, they receive the property that every single part of them serves some purpose. We say that the use is like the soul because its physical form is like the body. There is a still more interior effort: to produce uses through sprouting <note>original: "egermination"</note> for the animal...

I. They are the ultimate forms <term>ultimates: the final, most physical manifestation of divine energy in the material world</term>, and ultimate forms contain all prior things together in their proper order, as was shown in various places above.

II. There are degrees of both kinds (discrete and continuous) in the greatest and least of all things, as was shown in sections 222 to 229; this is also true regarding the effort mentioned above.

III. All uses are produced by the Lord from ultimate forms; therefore, the effort toward those uses must exist within those ultimate forms.

### 311.
However, all these efforts are not "living" in themselves. They are the efforts of the final powers of life. In these powers—by virtue of the life from which they originate—there is eventually a drive to return to their source through the means provided. In their final state, the atmospheres become these powers, through which substances and matter (like those in the earth) are moved into specific forms and held in those forms from both within and without. There is not enough time to explain this matter in greater detail here, as the subject is so extensive.

### 312.
The first production from these earths, when they were still new and in their simplest state, was the production of seeds; the first effort within them could not have been anything else.

### 313. II. THAT THERE IS A CERTAIN IMAGE OF CREATION IN ALL FORMS OF USES.
Forms of uses are of three kinds: forms of uses of the mineral kingdom, the vegetable kingdom, and the animal kingdom. 

The forms of uses of the mineral kingdom cannot be described because they are invisible. The first forms are the substances and materials of the earth in their smallest parts. The second forms are the compositions made from them, which have infinite variety. The third forms come from vegetables that have turned to dust and from dead animals, as well as from their constant evaporations and exhalations; when these are added to the earth, they constitute the soil <note>original: "ground"</note>. 

The forms of the three degrees of the mineral kingdom exhibit an image of creation because, when acted upon by the atmospheres and their heat and light, they produce uses in specific forms—which were the goals <note>original: "ends"</note> of creation. This image of creation lies hidden in their inherent efforts, as mentioned above in section 310.

### 314.
In the forms of uses of the vegetable kingdom, an image of creation appears in the way they proceed from their primary forms to their ultimate forms, and then from those ultimate forms back to the primary ones. Their primary forms are seeds, and their ultimate forms are the stems covered with bark. Through the bark, which is the final part of the stem, they tend back toward seeds, which, as was said, are their primary forms. Their bark-covered stems represent the globe covered with earth, from which the creation and formation of all uses exist. This vegetation is produced by means of the outer and inner bark and the protective coat, which make an effort through the coverings of the roots and continue up the stems and...
