...branches to the parts <note>original: "instruments"</note> of the fruit, and likewise through the fruit to the seed, is well known. An image of creation in the forms of uses is visible in their gradual development from first principles <term>primaries: the initial spiritual causes or origins</term> to final effects <term>ultimates: the physical conclusion or outward manifestation of a spiritual process</term>, and back from final effects to first principles. This specifically shows that all their progress involves the goal of producing fruits and seeds, which are of use. From what has been said above, it is clear that the progression of the creation of the universe moved from its first principle—which is the Lord clothed with the spiritual sun—to its final effects, which are earths. From these earths, it returns through uses to their first principle, the Lord; it is also clear that the purpose of the whole of creation was uses.

### 315.
It should be noted that the heat, light, and atmospheres of the natural world do not contribute anything to this image of creation. Only the heat, light, and atmospheres of the sun of the spiritual world do; these carry that image with them and clothe it in the forms of uses found in the vegetable kingdom. The heat, light, and atmospheres of the natural world only serve to open seeds, keep their growth expanded, and provide the physical substance <note>original: "induce on them matters"</note> that makes them solid. However, they do this not by any power derived from their own sun—which, considered in itself, has no power—but by power from the spiritual sun, which constantly drives them toward those tasks. They contribute nothing at all toward producing the image of creation within those things, for the image of creation is spiritual. Nevertheless, so that it may appear and perform a use in the natural world, and remain fixed and enduring, it must be *materialized*—that is, filled with the physical matter of this world.

### 316.
The forms of uses in the animal kingdom involve a similar image of creation. From seed placed in the womb or egg, a body is formed, which is its final physical state <note>original: "ultimate"</note>. When this body grows up, it produces new seed. This progression is similar to that of the vegetable kingdom: seeds are the beginnings; the womb or egg is like the earth; the state before birth is like the state of a seed in the ground while it develops a root; and the state after birth until the time of reproduction <note>original: "prolification"</note> is like the growth of a tree until it bears fruit. 

From this comparison, it is evident that just as there is a resemblance to creation in plants, there is also one in animals, because there is a progression from first principles to final effects, and back again. A similar image of creation exists in everything within a human being. There is a similar progression from love, through wisdom, to use; therefore, there is a similar progression from the will, through the understanding, to action, and from charity, through faith, to good works. The will and understanding (as well as charity and faith) are the first principles from which everything else proceeds; actions and works are the final effects. From these, through the delight found in being useful, there is a return to their first principles—which, as stated, are the will and understanding, or charity and faith. That this return is achieved through the delight of being useful is evident from the pleasure felt in actions and works.

A similar progression from first principles to final effects <term>ultimates: the physical conclusion or outward manifestation of a spiritual process</term>, and back again, exists in the most purely organic forms of human affection and thought. In the brain, there are star-like forms called gray matter <note>original: "cineritious substances"</note>; fibers extend from these through the white matter <note>original: "medullary substance"</note> and the neck <note>original: "cervix"</note> into the rest of the body. They reach the final physical structures <note>original: "ultimates"</note> and then return to their origins. This return of the fibers to their origins is carried out by the blood vessels. 

All affections and thoughts follow a similar progression; these are simply changes and variations in the state of those brain forms and substances. The fibers extending from these forms are comparable to the atmospheres from the spiritual sun, which contain heat and light. Physical actions are like the things produced from the earth by those atmospheres, and the joy derived from their use returns back to its source. 

However, fully understanding that such a progression exists—and that it contains an "image of creation"—is difficult for the human mind. This is because thousands and millions of powers working together in a single act appear as one single thing, and because the joy of being useful does not present itself as a clear idea in our thoughts, but rather affects us without us perceiving it distinctly. On this subject, refer to what was shown earlier: specifically, that the uses of all created things ascend through "degrees of altitude" <term>degrees of altitude: levels of reality or perfection, like the steps from spiritual to physical</term> to humanity, and through humanity to God the Creator from whom they originate (see sections 65 to 68). Furthermore, the purpose of creation is found in the final physical results <note>original: "ultimates"</note>, which is for all things to return to the Creator so that a connection <note>original: "conjunction"</note> can be established (see sections 167 to 172). These points will be even clearer in the following Part, where the correspondence <term>correspondence: a symbolic and functional relationship between spiritual and natural things</term> of the will and understanding with the heart and lungs will be discussed.

### 317. 
III. ***That there is a certain image of humanity in all forms of uses*** was shown earlier (sections 61 to 64). The next article will explain how all uses—from first principles to final effects and back—relate to and correspond with everything in a human being. Because of this, a human being is, in a sense, an image of the universe, and conversely, the universe, viewed in terms of its use, is an image of a human being.

### 318. 
IV. ***That there is a certain image of the infinite and eternal in all forms of uses.*** The image of the infinite in these forms is visible in their drive and power to fill the spaces of the entire world—and even many worlds—forever <note>original: "ad infinitum"</note>. For example, one seed produces a tree, shrub, or plant that occupies its own space. Each of those plants then produces seeds, sometimes thousands of them. When these are sown and grow, they fill their own spaces. If every one of those seeds were to produce new plants again and again, within a few years the entire world would be filled; and if their offspring...

Consider that a thousand seeds can come from one seed, then multiply those thousands into tens of thousands, twenties of thousands, and hundreds of thousands, and you will see the scale. The image of the eternal is also visible in these forms, as seeds are propagated from year to year, and their reproduction never ceases. They have not ceased since the creation of the world, nor will they cease for all eternity. These two facts are manifest proofs and signs that all things in the universe were created by an infinite and eternal God. 

Besides these images of the infinite and eternal, there is also an image of the infinite and eternal in the diversity of things. No substance, state, or thing in the created universe can ever be exactly the same as another—not in the atmospheres, nor in the earth, nor in the forms produced from them. Consequently, among all the things that fill the universe, nothing can be produced that is identical to another for all eternity. This is clearly seen in the variety of human faces; no two are the same in the whole world, nor can they be to all eternity. Consequently, no two minds are exactly the same, since the face is the outward representation <term>type: an external form or symbol representing an internal reality</term> of the mind.

319. **THAT ALL THINGS OF THE CREATED UNIVERSE, VIEWED IN TERMS OF THEIR USES, REPRESENT MAN IN AN IMAGE; AND THAT THIS TESTIFIES THAT GOD IS A MAN.** 

In ancient times, a human being was called a microcosm <term>microcosm: literally "little world," the idea that a human is a miniature version of the entire universe</term> because he resembles the macrocosm <term>macrocosm: the "great world" or the universe as a whole</term>. However, today it is not known why the ancients used this term. From a modern perspective, a person appears to be part of the universe only because he is nourished by the animal and vegetable kingdoms, stays alive through the world's heat, sees by its light, and hears and breathes by its atmospheres. These physical circumstances, however, do not actually make a person a "little universe" in the way that the universe itself is a "great universe." The ancients called a person a microcosm from their knowledge of correspondences <term>correspondences: the spiritual relationship and symbolic link between natural things and divine realities</term>, a science in which the most ancient people were experts and which they used in their communication with the angels of heaven. For the angels of heaven know, from the visible things around them, that everything in the universe, when seen through the lens of its purpose or use, reflects the image of a human being.

320. But the idea that a person is a microcosm because the created universe, viewed in terms of its uses, is human-like in image, cannot be understood or known by anyone except through the perspective of the universe as seen in the spiritual world. Therefore, this cannot be proven except by an angel in the spiritual world, or by someone to whom it has been granted to be in that world and see the things there. As this has been granted to me, I am able to reveal this hidden truth <note>original: *arcanum*</note> based on what I have seen there.

321. Let it be known that the spiritual world, in its external appearance, is completely similar to the natural world. Lands, mountains, hills, valleys, plains, fields, lakes, rivers, and fountains all appear there,

Animals, birds, and fish of every kind—meaning everything from the animal kingdom—likewise appear there. In that world, a person is an angel and a spirit. This is explained beforehand so that it may be known that the universe of the spiritual world is entirely similar to the universe of the natural world, with the only difference being that things there are not fixed and permanent like those in the natural world. This is because nothing is *natural* in the spiritual world; instead, everything is *spiritual*.

322. They stay around them and do not leave. The proof that they are produced or created from the angels is that when an angel goes away, or a society moves to another place, these things no longer appear. Also, when other angels take their place, the appearance of everything around them changes: the paradises change their trees and fruits, the gardens change their roses and seeds, the fields change their herbs and grasses, and the types of animals and birds change as well. These things exist and change in this way because they all come into being according to the affections <term>affection: a person's underlying feelings, desires, or loves</term> and resulting thoughts of the angels. They are *correspondences*; and because things that correspond act as one with the person they correspond to, they are a *representative image* of that person. This image does not actually appear when these things are seen in their physical forms, but only when they are seen in their uses. I have been allowed to see that when the Lord opened the eyes of the angels and they saw these things based on the correspondence of uses, they recognized and saw themselves reflected in them.

323. Now, since the things that exist around the angels according to their feelings and thoughts resemble a kind of universe—containing lands, plants, and animals—and since these make up a *representative image* of the angel, it is clear why the ancients called a human a *microcosm* <term>microcosm: "a little world"; the idea that a human being reflects the entire universe in miniature</term>.

324. This reality is shown in great detail in *Heavenly Secrets* <note>original: *Arcana Cœlestia*</note>, as well as in the work *Heaven and Hell*, and in many places in the preceding pages where correspondence was discussed. It is also shown there that there is nothing in the created universe that does not have a correspondence with something in a human being—not only with our feelings and thoughts, but also with the organs and internal parts <term>viscera: the internal organs of the body, especially those in the abdomen and chest</term> of the body. This correspondence is not with them as physical substances, but with them in terms of their uses. This is why in the *Word* <note>the Word: a term for the Bible or Divine Scripture</note>, when the church and its members are discussed, there is such frequent mention of trees (like olives, vines, and cedars), gardens, groves, and forests, as well as the beasts of the field, birds of the air, and fish of the sea.

324. They are mentioned there because, as previously stated, they correspond and, through that correspondence, they act as one. 325. Since everything in the universe represents a human in an image, Adam is described—in terms of wisdom and intelligence—by the Garden of Eden. For the things mentioned above do not exist around a human-angel because of the angel himself, but from the Lord through the angel. It is as if a creation of a universe is produced before the angel's eyes; from this, those in heaven know that God is a Man and that the created universe, viewed in terms of use, is an image of Him.

**327. THAT ALL THINGS CREATED BY THE LORD ARE USES; AND THAT THEY ARE USES IN THE ORDER, DEGREE, AND RESPECT IN WHICH THEY HAVE RELATION TO MAN, AND BY MAN TO THE LORD THEIR CREATOR.** 

On this subject, it was said above:
* That nothing but use can exist from God the Creator (paragraph 308); 
* That the uses of all created things ascend by degrees from ultimates <term>ultimates: the lowest or most material level of creation</term> to man, and through man to God their Creator (paragraphs 65 to 68); 
* That the end <term>end: the final purpose or goal</term> of creation exists in ultimates—which is that all things may return to God the Creator and that there may be conjunction <note>conjunction: a spiritual union or connection between God and humanity</note> (paragraphs 167 to 172); 
* That they are uses so far as they respect the Creator (paragraph 307); 
* That the Divine must be and exist in others created from itself (paragraphs 47 to 51); 
* That all things of the universe are recipients according to uses, and this according to degrees (paragraph 58); 
* That the universe, viewed from uses, is an image of God (paragraph 59). 

In addition to these details, this truth is manifest: all things created by the Lord are uses, and they exist in the order, degree, and respect in which they relate to man, and through man to the Lord their Creator. It remains for some specific details to be mentioned here concerning uses.

328. By "man," to whom uses relate, we mean not merely an individual person, but a collection of people and society, both small and large—such as a community, kingdom, or empire—and even the largest society, which is the universal world. Both the individual and the collective are "a man"; just as in the heavens, the universal angelic heaven, before

[the Lord] is like a single man; in the same way, each society of heaven is also like a man, and thus every angel is a man. That this is the case may be seen in the work **HEAVEN AND HELL** <note>original: *De Coelo et Ejus Mirabilibus et de Inferno*, Swedenborg’s 1758 treatise on the afterlife</note>, sections 68 to 103. 329. From the goal <note>original: "end" (referring to the final purpose or objective)</note> of the creation of the universe, the meaning of "use" can be seen. The goal of the creation of the universe is the existence of the angelic heaven. And because the angelic heaven is the goal, so too is the human race, since heaven is composed of the human race. Therefore, all things that are created are intermediate goals <note>original: "mediate ends"</note> and uses, in the specific order, degree, and way that they relate to humanity, and through humanity, to the Lord.

330. Since the goal of creation is the angelic heaven from the human race—and consequently the human race itself—all other created things are intermediate goals. As these relate to humanity, they concern three things: the body, the rational principle <term>rational principle: the human capacity for reason and logical thought</term>, and the spiritual principle, for the sake of connection <note>original: "conjunction"</note> with the Lord. A person cannot be connected to the Lord unless they are spiritual; they cannot be spiritual unless they are rational; and they cannot be rational unless their body is in a healthy state. These things are like a house: the body is like the foundation, the rational principle is like the structure built upon it, the spiritual principle is like the things inside the house, and connection with the Lord is like living in the house. Thus, it is clear in what order, degree, and way uses (which are the intermediate goals of creation) relate to humanity: specifically, for sustaining the body, perfecting the rational principle, and receiving a spiritual principle from the Lord.

331. ***Uses for sustaining the body*** involve its nourishment, clothing, housing, recreation and enjoyment, protection, and preservation of its condition. Uses created for the nourishment of the body include everything from the vegetable kingdom intended for food and drink, such as fruits, berries, seeds, legumes <note>original: "pulse"</note>, and herbs; and everything from the animal kingdom that is eaten, such as oxen, cows, calves, deer, sheep, kids, goats, lambs, and their milk; as well as many kinds of birds and fish. Uses created for the clothing of the body also include many things from these two kingdoms. The same is true for housing, recreation, enjoyment, protection, and preservation of health. These are not all listed here because they are well known, and listing them would be a waste of paper. There are, indeed, many things that humans do not use; however, surplus does not cancel out use, but ensures that uses continue to exist. There is also such a thing as the abuse of uses, but abuse does not take away the use itself, just as the falsification of truth does not take away the truth, except for those who are guilty of the falsification.

332. ***Uses for perfecting the rational principle*** include everything that teaches the subjects just mentioned. These are called sciences and vocations <note>original: "pursuits"</note>, which relate to natural, economic, civil, and moral matters. These are learned from parents or teachers, from books, from communication with others, or by reflecting on what...

334. It is clear that such things are given to humans to use and are free gifts <note>original: "gratuitous gifts"</note> when we look at the state of the angels in the heavens. Angels have a body and a rational and spiritual nature just like people on earth. They are nourished for free, for they are given food every day; they are clothed for free because garments are given to them; they live for free because houses are given to them. They have no worry about these things, and as long as they are rational and spiritual, they enjoy delight, protection, and the preservation of their state of being. The difference is that the angels see clearly that these things come from the Lord, because they are created according to their state of love and wisdom (as was shown in the preceding article, number 322). People on earth do not see this as clearly because their resources return yearly and do not appear to exist according to the state of their love and wisdom, but according to their own care and labor.

335. Although it is said that these are "uses" because they relate to the Lord through humanity, it cannot be said that they are uses performed by humans for the Lord's sake. All uses are infinitely one in the Lord, and none exist in a person except from the Lord; a person cannot do good on their own, but only from the Lord—and "good" is the same as "use." The essence of spiritual love is to do good to others, not for one’s own sake, but for the sake of others. This is true of the Divine Love to an infinite degree. This is like the love of parents toward their children, who do good to them out of love—not for their own sake, but for the sake of their

Many believe that because the Lord is to be adored, worshiped, and glorified, He desires this adoration, worship, and glory for His own sake. In reality, He loves these things for our sake, because worship brings us into a state where the Divine can flow in and be felt. Through worship, we set aside our self-centeredness <note>original: "proprium"</note>, which blocks this flow and reception. This self-centeredness, which is essentially the love of self, hardens and closes the heart. It is removed by acknowledging that on our own we produce nothing but evil, and from the Lord comes nothing but good. This realization leads to a softening of the heart and a sense of humility, from which true adoration and worship flow.

Therefore, it follows that the "use" or purpose the Lord fulfills through us is that, out of love, He may be able to do good for us. Because this is His very nature, our reception of His love is the delight of His own love. No one should believe, therefore, that the Lord is present with those who only adore Him; rather, He is with those who follow His commandments—consequently, those who perform "uses" or acts of service. He makes His home with the latter, but not with the former. (See what was said earlier on this subject in sections 47, 48, and 49.)

### 336. **THAT EVIL USES WERE NOT CREATED BY THE LORD, BUT THAT THEY ORIGINATED ALONG WITH HELL.**

All good things put into action are called "uses," and all evil things put into action are also called "uses." However, the latter are called "evil uses" and the former "good uses." Since everything good comes from the Lord and everything evil comes from hell, it follows that only good uses were created by the Lord, and that evil uses originated from hell. 

By "uses," which are specifically discussed in this article, we mean everything visible on earth, such as all kinds of animals and plants. Among these, those that provide a benefit to people are from the Lord, and those that cause harm to people are from hell. Similarly, by "uses from the Lord," we mean everything that refines our ability to reason <note>original: "rational principle"</note> and helps us receive spiritual life <note>original: "spiritual principle"</note> from the Lord. By "evil uses," we mean everything that destroys our reason and prevents us from becoming spiritual. 

Harmful things are still called "uses" because they are used by the wicked to do evil, and because they help absorb harmful influences <note>original: "malignities"</note>, thus acting as a kind of remedy. The word "use" is applied to both good and bad, just like the word "love." We speak of both "good love" and "evil love," and whatever love puts into action, it calls a "use."

337. The fact that good uses are from the Lord and evil uses are from hell will be demonstrated in the following order:
I. What is meant by evil uses on earth.
II. That everything that constitutes an evil use is in hell, and everything that is a good use is in heaven.
III. That there is a continual flow <note>original: "influx"</note> from the spiritual world into the natural world.
IV. That this flow from hell produces evil uses in places where there is a "correspondence" <note>the spiritual relationship between a cause in the spiritual world and an effect in the physical world</note>.
V. That the spiritual "ultimate" <note>the lowest or most external level of spiritual energy</note>, when separated from its higher source, produces this.

### 338. I. *What is meant by "evil uses" on earth.* 

"Evil uses" on earth refer to all harmful things in both the animal and vegetable kingdoms, as well as in the mineral kingdom. It would be tedious to list every harmful thing in these kingdoms; to do so would merely be to heap up names. Without explaining the harmful effect each kind produces, such a list would not serve the purpose intended for this work. For the sake of science <note>original: "science"; in this context, it refers to systematic knowledge or learning</note>, it is enough to name a few specific examples here. 

In the animal kingdom, these include poisonous serpents, scorpions, crocodiles, dragons <note>original: "dragons"; likely referring to large reptiles or perhaps personifications of spiritual evils</note>, horned owls, screech owls, mice, locusts, frogs, and spiders. It also includes flies, drones, moths, lice, and mites—in short, all those that consume grass, leaves, fruits, seeds, meat, and drink, and are harmful to both beasts and men. In the vegetable kingdom, "evil uses" include all malignant, virulent, and poisonous herbs, as well as legumes <note>original: "pulse"</note> and shrubs of the same kind. In the mineral kingdom, they include all poisonous earths. These few examples show what is meant by "evil uses" on earth; "evil uses" are everything opposite to "good uses," which were discussed in the preceding section.

### 339. II. *That all things that are "evil uses" are in hell, and all that are "good uses" are in heaven.* 

Before it can be understood that all "evil uses" existing on earth come from hell and not from the Lord, we must first establish certain facts concerning heaven and hell. Unless this is known, "evil uses" might be attributed to the Lord alongside "good uses," and people might suppose they have both existed together since the creation. Alternatively, people might attribute them to nature, or believe they originate from the physical sun. 

A person cannot be freed from these two errors unless he knows that nothing whatever exists in the natural world that does not derive its cause and origin from the spiritual world. He must also know that good is from the Lord, and evil is from the devil (that is, from hell). By the "spiritual world," we mean both heaven and hell. 

In heaven, all those things that are "good uses" appear (as mentioned in the previous section). In hell, all things that are "evil uses" appear (as listed above in section 338). These include wild beasts of all kinds, such as serpents, scorpions, dragons, crocodiles, tigers, wolves, foxes, swine, various owls, bats, rats, mice, frogs, locusts, spiders, and many kinds of harmful insects. They also include hemlock and aconite, and all kinds of poison, whether in herbs or in the soil. In short, everything that hurts or kills humans is found there. Such things appear in the hells vividly, just as they do on and in the earth. 

It is said that they "appear" there, yet they do not exist there in the same physical way as they do on earth. Instead, they are *correspondences* <note>In this context, "correspondences" are outward, physical representations of internal, spiritual states or desires.</note> of the lusts that spring from evil loves, and they present themselves to others in these forms. Because such things exist in the hells, those places also abound in foul smells—scents of corpses, dung <note>original: "stercoraceous"</note>, urine, and rot—in which the diabolical spirits there take delight, just as certain animals do...

That they are not from the Lord is also evident because hell is not from Him; therefore, nothing exists in hell except what corresponds to the evils of its inhabitants.

### 340. III. *There is a continual influx from the spiritual world into the natural world.*

Anyone who does not know that there is a spiritual world—and that it is as distinct from the natural world as a cause is from its effect <note>original: "as prior from posterior, or the cause from the thing caused"</note>—cannot understand anything about this influx. This is why those who have written about the origin of plants <note>original: "vegetables"</note> and animals could not help but attribute it to nature. Even if they attributed it to God, they supposed that God endowed nature from the beginning with the power to produce such things. 

In this, they did not realize that nature is not endowed with any power of its own; in herself, nature is dead. She contributes no more to the production of these things than a tool contributes to the work of an artist, which must be constantly moved in order to act. The spiritual principle <note>term: "spiritual principle"; the living force or energy originating from the spiritual sun</note>, which originates from the sun where the Lord is and proceeds to the furthest limits of nature, produces the forms of plants and animals. It provides the wonders found in both and gives them stability by using materials from the earth, so that these forms may be fixed and permanent. 

Now that it is known that there is a spiritual world, that the spiritual principle comes from the sun where the Lord is, and that this principle drives nature to act (just as a living thing drives a dead thing), it can be seen that plants and animals exist only through that world from the Lord, and that they continue to exist through it perpetually. Therefore, there is a continual influx from the spiritual world into the natural. This will be confirmed by many points in the next section. That harmful things are produced on earth by influx from hell follows the same "law of permission" <note>term: "law of permission"; the theological idea that God allows evil to exist to preserve human free will</note> by which evils themselves flow from hell into humans. This law will be discussed in the *Angelic Wisdom concerning Divine Providence*.

### 341. IV. *Influx from hell produces things that are evil uses in places where corresponding materials exist.*

The things that correspond to "evil uses"—meaning malignant herbs and harmful animals—are dead, decaying, excrement-filled, dung-like <note>original: "stercoraceous"</note>, rancid, and urine-soaked matters. Therefore, in places where these materials exist, the herbs and tiny creatures mentioned above appear. In tropical regions <note>original: "torrid zones"</note>, similar things of a larger size exist, such as serpents, basilisks, crocodiles, scorpions, mice, and others.

### 341.
Everyone knows that marshes, stagnant ponds, dung, and foul-smelling earth are full of such things. One also sees that harmful insects fill the atmosphere like clouds, and harmful worms fill the earth like armies, consuming plants down to their very roots. I once observed in my garden that in a space of about an ell <term>ell: a historical unit of measurement, roughly 45 inches</term>, almost all the dust turned into tiny insects; when stirred with a stick, they rose up like clouds. 

That corpse-like and foul-smelling substances harmonize with those harmful and useless tiny creatures <note>original: "animalcules"</note>, and that they are of the same nature, is evident from experience alone. This can be clearly seen from the cause: there are similar stenches and vapors <note>original: "effluvia"</note> in the various hells, where such tiny creatures also appear. Because of this, those hells are named after them; some are called "corpse-like" <note>original: "cadaverous"</note>, some "dung-like" <note>original: "stercoraceous"</note>, some "urine-like" <note>original: "urinous"</note>, and so on. However, these hells are all covered over to prevent those odors from escaping <note>original: "transpire"</note>. When they are opened even a little—as when newly arrived <note>original: "novitiate"</note> devils enter—they cause vomiting and headaches, and those that are also poisonous can cause fainting. Even the dust there is of such a nature, which is why it is called "damned dust." Thus, it is clear that wherever there are such stenches, these harmful things exist because they correspond to one another.

### 342. 
We shall now investigate whether such things exist because eggs were carried there—by the air, by rain, or by moving water—or whether they arise from the dampness and stenches themselves in those places. The idea that such harmful tiny creatures and insects are produced only from eggs carried there, or hidden in the earth since the creation, is not supported by general experience. This is because worms appear inside seeds, nuts, wood, and stones, and even from leaves. Similarly, lice and moths appear on and inside plants. Flies also appear in houses, fields, and woods during the summer, produced in great abundance without any visible egg-like matter. 

The same is true of the tiny creatures that devour meadows and lawns, and those that fill and infest the air in some hot climates, as well as those that swim and fly invisibly in foul water, sour wines, and diseased <note>original: "pestilential"</note> air. These facts support the opinion of those who say that smells, vapors, and exhalations rising from plants, soil, and ponds themselves give rise to such tiny creatures. The fact that they later propagate through eggs or spawn after they are created does not disprove their immediate origin <note>This refers to "spontaneous generation," the historical belief that life could arise directly from non-living matter under certain conditions.</note>. This is because every animal, along with its internal organs, also receives organs for reproduction and means of propagation (discussed below in section 347). This is further proven by the previously unknown fact that similar things exist in hell.

Rather, they are all around them—indeed, they are inside those who are wicked. Thus, they are directly touching <note>original: "contiguous"</note> the earth. ### 343.
Every person—in regards to their desires <note>original: "lusts"</note> and the thoughts derived from them, as well as their actions (which are either good or evil uses derived from both)—is in the midst of either the angels of heaven or the spirits of hell. Since the same things that exist on earth also exist in the heavens and the hells, it follows that the spiritual influence <note>original: "influx"</note> from those realms immediately produces such things when the conditions <note>original: "temperature"</note> are favorable. All things that appear in the spiritual world, both in heaven and hell, are "correspondences" of affections and desires, for they exist there according to those internal states. Therefore, when affections and desires (which are spiritual in themselves) encounter similar or corresponding materials on earth, there is a spiritual essence <note>original: "principle"</note> that provides a soul, and a material substance that provides a body. Every spiritual thing also possesses a striving <note>original: "endeavour"</note> to clothe itself with a physical body. The hells are all around humanity and are therefore right next to the earth, because the spiritual world does not exist in physical space, but exists wherever there is a corresponding affection.

### 344.
I heard two presidents of the English Royal Society, Sir Hans Sloane and Martin Folkes, conversing together in the spiritual world about the existence of seeds and eggs and their development on earth. Sir Hans Sloane <note>1660–1753; a physician and collector whose massive collection became the foundation of the British Museum</note> attributed <note>original: "ascribed"</note> them to nature, insisting that nature was endowed from creation with the power to produce such things by means of the sun's heat. Martin Folkes <note>1690–1754; an English antiquary and scientist who succeeded Sloane as President of the Royal Society</note> said that this power in nature comes continually from God the Creator. 

To settle the dispute, a beautiful bird was shown to Sir Hans Sloane, and he was told to examine whether it differed in even the smallest detail from a similar bird on earth. He held it in his hand, examined it, and said there was no difference. Yet, he knew that this bird was nothing more than an expression of a certain angel’s affection, manifested outside of that angel as a bird, and that it would vanish or cease to exist along with that affection—which is exactly what happened. 

Sir Hans Sloane was convinced by this experiment that nature does not contribute at all to the production of plants and animals, but only that which flows from the spiritual world into the natural world. He also said that if that bird were to be filled in its smallest parts with corresponding matter from the earth and thus made permanent <note>original: "fixed"</note>, it would be a durable bird, just like birds on earth; and that the same applies to things that come from hell. He added further that if he had known then what he now knew about the spiritual world, he would have attributed no more to nature than that it served the spiritual essence from God by giving physical form to the things that continually flow into nature.

In a human being, by creation and therefore by birth, these three degrees exist and are opened according to how the person lives. If the celestial (the highest and innermost) degree is opened, the person becomes celestial; if the spiritual...

When the children of Israel lived according to the commandments, the earth yielded its harvest <note>original: "increase"</note>, as did the flocks and herds. The same conclusion can be drawn from the plagues of locusts, frogs, and lice in Egypt.

346. VI. *There are two forms upon which spiritual influence <note>original: "influx"</note> operates: the vegetable and the animal form.* 

It is well known that there are only two universal forms produced from the earth, categorized by the two kingdoms of nature: the animal and the vegetable. All subjects within a single kingdom have many things in common. In the animal kingdom, subjects have organs of sense and motion, as well as limbs and internal organs <note>original: "viscera"</note> which are controlled by their brains, hearts, and lungs. In the vegetable kingdom, subjects send roots into the earth and produce stems, branches, leaves, flowers, fruits, and seeds. 

Both the animal and vegetable kingdoms derive their origin and development into these forms from spiritual influence <note>original: "influx"</note> and the operation of the sun of heaven (where the Lord is), rather than from the influence of physical nature and its sun—except for their physical solidification <note>original: "fixation"</note>, as mentioned before. 

All animals, great and small, originate from the spiritual principle in the final or natural degree. Man alone originates from all three degrees: the heavenly <note>original: "celestial"</note>, the spiritual, and the natural. Since each "degree of height" <note>original: "degree of altitude," referring to discrete levels of existence rather than a continuous scale</note> decreases from perfection toward imperfection (like light fading into shade) through a continuous process, animals also vary in this way. There are, therefore, perfect, less perfect, and imperfect animals. 

The perfect animals include elephants, camels, horses, mules, oxen, sheep, goats, and others from the herd or flock. The less perfect are birds. The imperfect are fish and shellfish which, being at the lowest point of that degree, are as if in the shade while others are in the light. Still, because they live only from the final spiritual or natural degree, they cannot look otherwise

...than toward the earth, their food, and their mates for the sake of reproduction <note>original: "propagation"</note>. The soul of all these creatures is natural affection and appetite. It is the same with the members of the plant <note>original: "vegetable"</note> kingdom, which are also classified as perfect, less perfect, and imperfect. The perfect are fruit trees, the less perfect are vines and shrubs, and the imperfect are grasses. However, plants—by virtue of their spiritual origin—are "uses," while animals—by virtue of theirs—are affections and appetites, as mentioned before. <note>In this context, "uses" refers to the beneficial or functional purpose an object serves in the divine order.</note>

347. VII. *That both forms, as long as they exist, receive the means of reproduction.* It was shown above (sections 313 to 318) that in all the earth's products, whether they belong to the plant or animal kingdom, there is an image of creation, an image of man, and an image of the infinite and eternal. This image of the infinite and eternal is seen in the fact that they can be reproduced forever. Therefore, they all receive the means of reproduction—animals by seeds in an egg, in the womb, or by spawning; and plants by seeds in the earth. From this, it appears that although imperfect and harmful <note>original: "noxious"</note> animals and plants originate from a direct influx <note>original: "immediate influx"; the direct flow of spiritual energy</note> from hell, they are still subsequently reproduced through seeds, eggs, or grafts <note>original: "graffs"</note>. The fact that they have a hellish origin does not disprove that they reproduce naturally.

348. That all uses, both good and evil, have a spiritual origin—and thus come from the sun where the Lord is—can be illustrated by this experience. I heard that certain "goods" and "truths" were sent down through the heavens from the Lord to the hells. As they were received, they were gradually changed during their descent into the evils and falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note> opposite to the goods and truths that were sent. This happened because the subjects receiving them turn everything that flows into them into whatever matches their own nature <note>original: "forms"</note>. For example, objects whose internal substances are shaped to suffocate and extinguish light will turn the sun's white light into blackness and ugly colors. Similarly, stagnant water, manure, and dead carcasses turn the heat of the sun into foul smells. From this, it appears that even evil uses come from the spiritual sun, but that good uses are converted into evil uses in hell. This makes it evident that the Lord does not, and never did, create anything but good uses; it is hell that produces evil uses.

### 349. THAT THE VISIBLE THINGS IN THE CREATED UNIVERSE EVIDENCE THAT NATURE HAS PRODUCED NOTHING AND DOES PRODUCE NOTHING, BUT THAT THE DIVINE HAS PRODUCED AND DOES PRODUCE ALL THINGS FROM HIMSELF, AND THROUGH THE SPIRITUAL WORLD.

Most people in the world speak based on appearances. They say that the sun, through heat and light, produces what we see in plains, fields, gardens, and woods. They also say the sun's heat brings worms out of eggs and causes the animals of the earth and the birds of heaven to be fertile; they even say it gives life to <note>original: "vivifies"</note> man.

<summary>This page explains that all living things are spiritual "uses" or "affections" that possess the ability to reproduce. It argues that while everything originates from the Divine Sun, hellish subjects distort this good influence into evil and harmful forms.</summary>

<keywords>spiritual origin, influx, uses, plant kingdom, animal kingdom, reproduction, hell, spiritual sun, divine creation, correspondence</keywords>

--- Page 136 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on how people perceive the relationship between God and nature, specifically addressing why some fall into atheism while others are excused for their misunderstanding due to ignorance of the spiritual world.</meta>

<header>PART IV. THE DIVINE LOVE.</header>

Those who speak this way only based on appearances may do so without ascribing everything to nature, because they are not actively thinking about the subject. It is just like people who speak of the sun "rising" and "setting," or causing days and years, or being at a certain altitude. Such people speak from how things look; they can do so without actually attributing these powers to the sun, for they are not thinking about the sun’s fixed position and the earth's revolution. 

However, those who convince themselves <note>original: "confirm themselves"</note> that the sun, through its heat and light, produces everything that appears on earth eventually credit all things to nature—even the creation of the universe. They become naturalists and, ultimately, atheists. These individuals might later say that God created nature and gave her the power to produce such things; they say this out of fear of losing their reputation. But in their hearts, by "God the Creator" they really mean nature—specifically its innermost workings—and in this state, they think very little of the divine truths taught by the church.

350. Some people are indeed to be excused for attributing certain visible things to nature, for two reasons: 

*Firstly*, because they knew nothing of the "sun of heaven" where the Lord is, or of the influx <note>original: "influx"; the flow of spiritual energy and life into the natural world</note> from that sun. They knew nothing of the spiritual world and its state, or of its presence within man. Therefore, they could not think otherwise than that the "spiritual" was simply a purer form of the "natural." Consequently, they thought that angels were located either in the atmosphere <note>original: "ether"</note> or in the stars. Regarding the devil, they thought he was either man's own evil or, if he actually existed, was located in the air or the deep ocean. They also believed that the souls of men after death were either in the center of the earth or in some abstract place or space until the Day of Judgment. These and other such ideas are fantasies suggested by ignorance of the spiritual world and its sun. 

*Secondly*, they are excused because they could not understand how the Divine could produce all the things that appear on earth, where there are both good and evil things. They feared to believe God created everything lest they should also attribute evil things to God, or conceive of God as a material being, or make God and nature one and the same. 

These are the two reasons why those who believed that nature produces visible things by an inherent power are to be excused. Yet, those who have made themselves atheists by actively convincing themselves in favor of nature are not excused, because they could have instead convinced themselves in favor of the Divine. While ignorance serves as an excuse, it does not remove a "confirmed" falsehood <note>An error that someone has deeply analyzed and chosen to believe as truth</note>. A confirmed falsehood is linked with evil and, consequently, with hell. Therefore, those who have so thoroughly convinced themselves in favor of nature that they separate the Divine from it consider nothing to be a sin; for all sin is against the Divine, which they have rejected. After death, when they become spirits, those who in their spirit consider nothing a sin are allied to hell. They rush into wickedness according to the cravings <note>original: "lusts"</note> to which they have surrendered themselves.

351. Those who believe in the divine operation in all things in nature may confirm their faith in the Divine by many...

...that they see there. Those who convince themselves <note>original: "confirm themselves"</note> of this pay attention to the wonders seen in the growth of plants. *In the growth of plants*: they observe that from a tiny seed cast into the earth comes a root, and from the root a stem, and then branches, leaves, flowers, and fruits, eventually ending in new seeds. It is as though the seed knew the order of succession, or the process by which it was to renew itself. What rational person can think that the sun—which is pure fire—knows this? Who could believe the sun can endow its heat and light with the power to achieve such things, or can create these wonders and intend them for a specific use? No person of high reasoning ability, who sees and weighs these things, can think otherwise than that they come from Him who has infinite wisdom—that is, from God. 

Those who acknowledge the Divine see and think this, but those who do not, neither see nor think it, because they choose not to. Thus, they lower their reasoning power into their physical senses <note>original: "sensuality"; here meaning a state of mind that relies only on what can be physically seen or felt</note>, which derive all their ideas from the body's senses and reinforce their delusions. They ask, "Do you not see the sun producing these things by its heat and light? Can you see anything else? Is there anything else?" 

Those who convince themselves in favor of the Divine also pay attention to the wonderful things seen in the *reproduction of animals*. To mention only eggs: within them lies a chicken in its seed or initial state <note>original: "initiament"</note>, containing every requirement until it leaves the shell, and every stage of growth afterwards until it becomes a bird of the same form as its parent. If one examines its form, it is truly astonishing. If he thinks deeply, he sees that in both the smallest and largest of such animals—both the microscopic and the visible—there are organs of the senses: sight, smell, taste, and touch. There are organs of motion, or muscles, for they fly and walk; and there are internal organs surrounding the heart and lungs that are set in action by the brain. That even tiny insects have such features is known from their anatomy as described by writers, especially by Swammerdam in *The Bible of Nature* <note>original: "BIBLIA NATURÆ"</note>. 

Those who attribute all things to nature do indeed see these things, but they only think that they exist, and say that "nature" produces them. They say this because they have turned their minds away from thinking of the Divine. Those who have done this, when they see the wonders of nature, cannot think rationally, much less spiritually. Instead, they think through their physical senses and material terms. They think from within nature about nature, rather than thinking above her. They are like those who are in hell, differing from animals only in having the faculty of reason—that is, in being able to understand and think differently, if they only had the will to do so.

The eye sees many little insects as a single blurry speck; and yet, every one of these is organized to feel and move, and is thus endowed with fibers and vessels, with little...

# PART IV. THE DIVINE LOVE.

352. ...hearts, pulmonary pipes <note>original: "pulmonary pipes"; likely referring to the bronchial tubes or the structures of the lungs</note>, internal organs <note>original: "viscera"</note>, and brains. These are woven from the purest substances in nature, and their tissues correspond to a spark of life that causes their smallest parts to function distinctly. Because physical sight is so limited <note>original: "gross"</note> that many such parts—each containing countless details—appear as a single blurry object, it is clear how limited the minds of those who judge only by their physical senses <note>original: "sensual"</note> have become. They live in darkness regarding spiritual matters.

353. Anyone can choose to find evidence of the Divine in the visible things of nature, and whoever thinks of God while observing life does so. For example, when observing the birds of the sky, one sees that every species knows its own food and where to find it. They recognize their companions by sound and sight, knowing who are friends and who are enemies. They pair up in marriage-like <note>original: "connubial"</note> unions, know how to mate, and skillfully build nests. They lay eggs, wait through the period of incubation <note>the time spent sitting on eggs to hatch them</note>, and when the time is right, they hatch their young. They love them most tenderly, protect them under their wings, and provide food until the young can care for themselves and repeat the cycle to perpetuate their kind. Anyone willing to consider the Divine influence <note>original: "influx"</note> flowing from the spiritual world into the natural world can see it in these behaviors. They may say in their hearts that such knowledge cannot come to birds from the sun's rays; the sun, which is the source of nature's physical essence, is pure fire and its rays are entirely lifeless. Thus, one may conclude that these instincts come from the flow of Divine wisdom into the lowest levels <note>original: "ultimates"</note> of nature.

354. Anyone can find evidence of the Divine in nature when they observe worms <note>here referring specifically to larvae or caterpillars</note>. Driven by a specific desire, these creatures long to change their earthly state into a kind of heavenly one. To do this, they crawl into specific places and tuck themselves away as if in a womb to be born again. There they become chrysalises, pupae <note>original: "aureliæ"</note>, caterpillars, nymphs, and finally butterflies. Having undergone this transformation <note>original: "metamorphosis"</note> and being clothed with beautiful wings according to their kind, they fly into the air as if it were their heaven. There they play with delight, mate, lay eggs, and ensure a new generation, all while nourishing themselves with the sweet and pleasant food of flowers. Who that looks for the Divine in nature does not see in these creatures an image of man's earthly state in the worm, and his heavenly state in the butterfly? Yet those who focus only on nature see these things and, having rejected the idea of a heavenly state for man, dismiss them as nothing more than the "instincts of nature."

# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART IV.

...the wonders known about bees. They have the skill to collect wax and suck honey from herbs and flowers, and to build cells like small houses. They arrange these in the form of a city, with streets through which they may come in and go out. They can smell flowers and herbs from a distance, from which they collect wax for their houses and honey for their food. When they are laden with these materials, they fly back in the correct direction to the hive. In this way, they provide themselves with food and shelter for the winter, as if they foresaw and knew it was coming. 

They also appoint over themselves a mistress or queen, from whom a new generation may be reproduced <note>original: "propagated"</note>. They build a palace for her above them, with guards around it. When the time for giving birth is near, she goes, attended by her guards, from cell to cell and lays her eggs, which her followers coat over <note>original: "smear over"</note> so that they are not harmed by the air; from these, a new generation is born. Afterward, when this offspring reaches maturity and can do the same, they are driven from the home. The swarm thus expelled first gathers together and then, to prevent being scattered, flies forth as a group to seek a new home. 

Around autumn, the useless drones are also led out and their wings are removed <note>original: "deprived of their wings"</note>, so they cannot return and consume food for which they have not worked. Many other details could be mentioned. From these things, it is clear that because of their usefulness to the human race, they have—through influence <note>original: "influx"; the spiritual flow of life from God through the spiritual world into nature</note> from the spiritual world—a form of government similar to what men have on earth, and even similar to what the angels have in heaven. 

What person of sound mind does not see that such instincts in these animals are not from the natural world? What does the sun, from which nature originates, have in common with a government that imitates and is similar to that of heaven? From these and other similar things in the animal kingdom <note>original: "brute creation"</note>, the person who acknowledges and worships nature uses them to confirm his belief in nature, while the person who acknowledges and worships God uses them to confirm his belief in the Divine. The spiritual person sees spiritual things in them, and the natural person sees only natural things. 

As for myself, such things were proofs of an influence <note>original: "influx"</note> from the spiritual into the natural—that is, from the spiritual world into the natural world—and consequently from the Divine wisdom of the Lord. Consider this: can you think analytically about any form of government, any civil law, moral virtue, or spiritual truth, unless the Divine flows in with His wisdom through the spiritual world? As for me, I never could, nor can I now; for I have distinctly and sensibly observed that influence <note>original: "influx"</note> now for nineteen years continually. Therefore, I say this from personal experience.

356. Can anything in nature have a purpose <note>original: "use for an end"</note>, and arrange those purposes into organized systems and forms? None but a wise being can do this; and none but God, who has infinite wisdom, can so order and form the universe. Who, or what else, can foresee and provide all the things needed for human food and clothing—food from the fruits of the earth and animals, and clothing from the same? It is one among many wonders that a lowly <note>original: "mean"</note> creature like the silkworm...

In spiritual light, their understanding appeared open at the bottom but closed at the top, because in their thoughts they looked downward toward the earth and not upward toward heaven. Above the sensory level—which is the lowest part of the understanding—there appeared a kind of veil. In some, it flashed with the fire of hell; in others, it was black as soot; and in others, it was pale and grey like a corpse. Everyone should be careful, then, about how they convince themselves in favor of nature. Instead, find evidence for the Divine; there is no shortage of material to do so.

# PART V.

358. **THAT THE LORD HAS CREATED AND FORMED TWO RECEPTACLES AND DWELLINGS FOR HIMSELF IN MAN, CALLED THE WILL AND THE UNDERSTANDING; THE WILL FOR HIS DIVINE LOVE, AND THE UNDERSTANDING FOR HIS DIVINE WISDOM.** 

We have already discussed the Divine Love and Divine Wisdom of God the Creator (who is the Lord from eternity) and the creation of the universe; now we will speak about the creation of human beings. We read that man was created in the image of God, according to His likeness (Genesis 1:26). In this context, the "image of God" refers to Divine Wisdom, and the "likeness of God" refers to Divine Love. Wisdom is simply the image of love, because love makes itself seen and known through wisdom; therefore, wisdom is its image. Love is the very being <note>original: "esse"</note> of life, and wisdom is the manifestation <note>original: "existere"</note> of life that comes from it. The likeness and image of God are clearly visible in the angels: love shines from within their faces, and wisdom shows in their beauty—and beauty is the outward form of their love. I have seen this and know it to be true.

359. A person cannot be an image of God according to His likeness unless God is within them and serves as their life from their innermost core. That God is in man and is his life from his inmost part follows from what was shown earlier (sections 4 to 6): that God alone is life, and that humans and angels are merely recipients of life from Him. It is also known from the Word <note>A term often used for the Bible</note> that God is in man and makes His home with him. For this reason, it is common for preachers to urge their listeners to prepare themselves to receive God, so that He may...

That the Holy Spirit is the Lord, and not a God who is a separate person, is shown in *The Doctrine of the New Jerusalem Concerning the Lord*, numbers 51, 52, 53; for the Lord says, "In that day you shall know that you are in me, and I in you" (John 14:20; see also chapter 15:4 and chapter 17:23).

360. Now, as the Lord is Divine Love and Divine Wisdom, and these two are essentially Himself, in order that He may dwell in man and give life to man, it is necessary that He should have created and formed in man receptacles and habitations for Himself—one for love and another for wisdom. The will and understanding are such receptacles and habitations: the will for love, and the understanding for wisdom. That these two belong to the Lord within man, and that all of a person's life comes from them, will be seen in what follows.

361. That every person has these two—will and understanding—and that they are distinct from each other, just like love and wisdom, is both known and not known in the world. It is known from common perception, but it is not known from abstract thought, and still less when that thought is put into writing. Who does not know from common perception that the will and understanding are two distinct things in a person? Everyone perceives this when he hears it, and moreover can say to others, "This person’s will is good, but his understanding is not; and that person’s understanding is good, but his will is not. I love the person who has a good understanding and a good will, but not the one who has a good understanding and a bad will." 

But when the same person thinks about the will and understanding, he does not treat them as two distinct things but confuses them, because his thought is limited by his physical senses <note>original: "communicates with the sight of his body"</note>. He comprehends even less that the will and understanding are two distinct things when he writes, because then his thought connects with the sensory level, which is his <term>proprium</term> <note>original: "proprium" — a Latin term Swedenborg uses to describe the "self" or the ego; the part of a person that feels as if it acts independently of God.</note>. This is why some people can think and speak well, but cannot write well; this was a common observation regarding the education of women <note>The author is reflecting an 18th-century social observation regarding gender and formal writing.</note>. 

It is the same with many other things. Who does not know, from common perception, that a person who leads a good life is saved, and that one who leads a wicked life is condemned? Also, that a person who leads a good life enters the society of angels, and there sees, hears, and speaks like a man? Also, that he who does what is just because it is just, and what is upright because it is upright, has a conscience? But if he moves away from common perception and submits these things to abstract thought, then he no longer knows what conscience is, or that the soul can see, hear, and speak like a man, or that a "good life" is anything more than giving to the poor. And if you write these things from such thought, you con—

For this reason, simple people often see what is good and true more clearly than those who consider themselves wiser. This common perception comes by <term>influx</term> <note>the spiritual process of life and thought flowing from God into the human mind</note> from heaven and flows into our thoughts and our sight; however, thought that is separated from common perception falls into mere imagination, driven by sensory sight and the <term>proprium</term> <note>the "ego" or the sense of self that feels independent of God</note>. Consider, then, which of the two is truly wiser.

362. **THAT THE WILL AND UNDERSTANDING, WHICH ARE THE RECEPTACLES OF LOVE AND WISDOM, ARE LOCATED IN THE BRAIN—IN THE WHOLE AND IN EVERY PART THEREOF—AND FROM THERE THEY ARE IN THE BODY, IN THE WHOLE AND IN EVERY PART THEREOF.** We shall prove these points in the following order:
I. That love and wisdom, and consequently the will and understanding, constitute a person’s very life.
II. That a person’s life is present in its <term>principles</term> <note>origins or primary sources</note> in the brain, and in its <term>principiates</term> <note>derivatives or extensions of those origins</note> in the body.
III. That as the life is in its origins, so it is in the whole and in every part.
IV. That life, through these origins, is present from every part in the whole, and from the whole in every part.
V. That as the love is, so is the wisdom, and consequently, such is the man.

363. I. *That love and wisdom, and consequently the will and understanding, constitute a person’s very life.* Hardly anyone knows what life truly is. When a person thinks about it, it appears as something fleeting or ethereal, of which they have no clear idea. This is because it is not known that God alone is life, and that His life is Divine Love and Divine Wisdom. From this, it is evident that life in a human being is nothing else, and that a person possesses life in the same degree that they receive these two from God. It is well known that heat and light radiate from the sun, and that all things in the universe are recipients of them; to the degree that they receive them, they grow warm and shine. So it is also with the sun where the Lord is: the heat proceeding from it is love, and the light proceeding from it is wisdom (as shown in Part II). Therefore, life comes from these two which proceed from the Lord as a sun. That love and wisdom from the Lord are truly life can also be seen from this: that...

<header>363—365 ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART V.</header>

363. A person grows sluggish as love recedes from them, and grows foolish as wisdom recedes from them; if both were to recede entirely, the person would be annihilated. There are several attributes of love that have been given distinct names as they branch out—such as affections, desires, appetites, and their various pleasures and delights. There are also several attributes of wisdom, such as perception, reflection, memory, thought, and attention. Additionally, there are several attributes belonging to both love and wisdom together, such as consent, conclusion, and the determination to act. All of these belong to both principles, but they are named after whichever one is most powerful or most directly involved. 

From these two principles, the senses are ultimately derived: sight, hearing, smell, taste, and touch, along with their pleasures and delights. It appears as though the eye sees, but it is actually the understanding that sees through the eye; this is why we use the word "seeing" to describe understanding. It appears as though the ear hears, but the understanding hears through the ear; thus, hearing is often equated with attention and listening—two acts of the understanding. It appears as though the nose smells and the tongue tastes, but the understanding, through its perception, smells and tastes; therefore, smelling and tasting are often used to describe perception. This is true in all other cases as well. The sources of all these functions, both the mental and the physical, are love and wisdom. From this, it is clear that these two constitute the entire life of human beings.

364. Everyone can see that the understanding is the vessel for wisdom, but few see that the will is the vessel for love. This is because the will does nothing by itself but acts through the understanding. It is also because the love within the will, in order to pass into the wisdom of the understanding, first enters into an affection and travels that path. Affection is only felt as a certain pleasure in thinking, speaking, and acting, which we usually do not notice. Nevertheless, it is clear that it comes from the will because everyone "wills" what they love and does not "will" what they do not love.

365. II. *That a person’s life exists in its "principles" within the brain, and in its "principiates" within the body.* 
The <term>principles</term> <note>original: "principles"; refers to the primary or governing origins of a system</note> are the primary forms, and the <term>principiates</term> <note>original: "principiates"; refers to the secondary parts or structures produced by and derived from the primary principles</note> are the parts produced and formed from them. By "life in its principles," we mean the will and the understanding. These two are in the brain in their primary forms, and in the body in their derived parts. That the primary forms of life are in the brain is obvious from the following:

I. From sensation itself: when a person focuses their mind and thinks, they feel that they are thinking in the brain. They draw in their eyesight, as it were, and keep their forehead tensed, perceiving an inner contemplation <note>original: "speculation"</note> taking place mainly within the forehead and slightly above it.

II. From the formation of a human in the womb: the brain or head is formed first and remains larger than the rest of the body for a long time afterward.

III. From the fact that the head is above and the body is below: it is the natural order for superior things to act upon inferior things, and not the other way around.

IV. From the fact that when the brain is injured—whether in the womb...

# PART V. THE DIVINE LOVE. 365, 366

5. All the external senses of the body—sight, hearing, smell, and taste, as well as the general sense of touch—along with speech, are located in the front part of the head, called the face. They communicate directly with the brain through fibers and receive their sensitive and active life from it.

6. Because of this, the affections of a person's love are reflected in their face, and the thoughts of their wisdom appear as a certain light in their eyes.

7. Anatomy shows that all the fibers from the brain descend into the body through the neck, and none ascend from the body through the neck into the brain. Life is present in its primary forms where the fibers begin. Can anyone deny that the origin of life is in the same place as the origin of these fibers?

8. Ask any person of common sense where their thought is, or where they think it happens, and they will answer, "In my head." But then take someone who has assigned a specific location to the soul—either in a particular gland <note>original: "some particular gland". This is likely a reference to the pineal gland, which philosopher René Descartes famously identified as the "seat of the soul."</note>, in the heart, or elsewhere—and ask them if affection and thought are not in the brain in their primary forms. They will either answer "No" or say they do not know. The reason for this lack of knowledge was explained earlier in section 361.

### 366. III. Since life exists in its foundational principles, it is also present in the whole and in every part.

To understand this, we will now describe where these principles <term>The word "principles" here refers to the "beginnings" or "origins" of things, from the Latin word *principia*.</term> are located in the brain and how they are distributed. Anatomy shows that there are two brains <note>The cerebrum and the cerebellum.</note> that continue from the head down into the spine. They consist of two substances: a **cortical substance** <term>The gray matter of the brain, consisting of nerve cell bodies.</term> and a **medullary substance** <term>The white matter of the brain, consisting of nerve fibers that carry signals.</term>. 

The cortical substance is composed of what look like countless tiny glands, and the medullary substance is composed of what look like countless fibers. Since these glands are the "heads" of the tiny fibers, they are also their starting points. The fibers begin there, proceed outward, and eventually bundle together into nerves. These bundles or nerves descend to the organs of sense in the face and the organs of motion in the body, forming them. Any anatomist can confirm this. 

This cortical or glandular substance forms the surface of the **cerebrum** and the **corpora striata** <term>Subcortical structures in the brain involved in coordinating movement.</term>, which gives rise to the **medulla oblongata** <term>The lower part of the brainstem that connects to the spinal cord.</term>. It also forms the middle of the **cerebellum** and the spinal cord. The medullary or fiber-based substance begins and proceeds from there, forming the nerves that build every part of the body. We can see this through direct observation. 

Anyone who knows these facts—either from studying anatomy or from the accounts of anatomists—can see that the origins of life are in the same place as the beginnings of the fibers, and that fibers cannot arise on their own but must come from those origins. These principles, or beginnings, appear as nearly countless glands; the multi-

...the vast number of them can be compared to the multitude of stars in the universe. The tiny fibers <note>original: "fibrillæ"</note> proceeding from them are like the multitude of rays coming from the stars, which carry their heat and light to the earth. The vast number of these glands can also be compared to the countless societies of angels in the heavens, which (I was told) are arranged in the same order as these glands. The many tiny fibers coming from these glands are like the spiritual truths and forms of goodness that flow from those angelic societies like rays. 

Because of this, a human being is like a universe or a heaven in miniature, as has been stated and shown in various places above. From this, it is clear that just as life is in its origins <term>original: "principles"; the starting points or foundations of things</term>, it is also in the things derived from those origins <term>original: "principiates"; things that arise or are formed from a primary source</term>. In other words, just as life exists in its primary forms within the brains, it exists in the same way in the body parts that arise from them.

### 367. IV. Life, through these primary origins, exists from every part in the whole, and from the whole in every part.

The "whole"—which refers to the brain and the body together—originally consists of nothing but fibers that start from their beginnings in the brains. They have no other origin, as was shown above in section 366; therefore, the whole is made up of every part. Life is also present in every part because of the whole, since the whole provides every part with its specific task and requirements, thus causing the part to be integrated into the whole. In short, the whole exists from its parts, and the parts depend on the whole for their existence. That there is such a reciprocal connection and union is evident from many things in the body. It works much like a city, a state, or a kingdom, where the community exists because of the people (its parts), and the parts or people depend on the community for their survival. This is true for anything that has a structure, and especially so in human beings.

### 368. V. As a person’s love is, so is their wisdom, and therefore, so is the person themselves.

As the love and wisdom are, so are the will and the understanding. The will is the container of love, and the understanding is the container of wisdom—as shown above—and these two define the person and their character. Love is diverse, so much so that its varieties are limitless. This is evident from the human race on earth and in the heavens, where no person or angel is so much like another that there is no difference between them. 

It is love that distinguishes us, for every person is defined by their own love. It is often supposed that wisdom is what makes people different, but wisdom actually comes from love, being the form that love takes. Love is the essence <note>original Latin: "esse"; the underlying being or "is-ness" of a thing</note> of life, and wisdom is the manifestation <note>original Latin: "existere"; the way that being appears, exists, or comes forth</note> of life from that essence. In the world, people believe the understanding defines the person, but this is because the understanding can be raised into the light of heaven, as shown before, making a person appear wise. However, the part of the understanding that goes beyond—meaning the part that does not belong to the person's actual love—only appears to belong to the person. Even though it might make someone seem wise, it is only an appearance. The part of the understanding that goes beyond a person's character actually belongs to the love of...
