368. A person can be knowledgeable and wise without simultaneously loving to apply what they know to their life. Because of this, while they are in the world, that wisdom either eventually fades away or stays only on the surface of the memory as something ready to fall off. Consequently, after death, this wisdom is separated from them, and only as much remains as matches the true love of their spirit. Since love makes a person's life—and therefore the person themselves—all the communities in heaven and all the angels within them are arranged according to their types of love <note>original: "affections of love"</note>. No community or angel is organized based on any intellectual understanding that is separate from their love. The same is true in the hells and their communities, but according to loves that are opposite to heavenly ones. From this, it is clear that as the love is, so is the wisdom, and therefore so is the person.

369. It is commonly recognized that a person is defined by their ruling love <note>the primary motivation or passion that drives a person's life</note>, but people usually think this only applies to the mind and character, not the body—thus not to the whole person. However, through much experience in the spiritual world, it has been revealed to me that a person—from head to foot, or from the first things in the head to the last things in the body—is exactly like their love. In that world, everyone is a physical form of their own love: angels are forms of heavenly love, and devils are forms of hellish <note>original: "infernal"</note> love. The devils are deformed in face and body, while the angels are beautiful. When their love is attacked, their faces change, and if it is attacked severely, they disappear entirely. This is unique to that world and happens because their bodies are at one with their minds. 

This explains why all parts of the body are derived forms <term>original: "principiates"; things formed from a primary source</term>—meaning they are compositions of fibers starting from primary origins <term>original: "principles"</term>, which are the receptacles of love and wisdom. These derived forms must be like their origins. Therefore, wherever the origins lead, the derived forms follow; the two cannot be separated. This is why anyone who lifts their mind toward the Lord is completely lifted toward the Lord, and anyone who sinks their mind toward hell is completely sunk toward it. In short, the whole person, according to their life's love, goes either to heaven or to hell. It is a core belief of angelic wisdom that a person’s mind *is* the person, because God is a Man; the body is simply the external part of the mind that feels and acts. Thus, they are one thing, not two.

370. It should be noted that the forms of a person’s limbs, organs, and internal parts <note>original: "viscera"</note> are composed of fibers that begin in the brain. However, they are made solid <note>original: "fixed"</note> by material substances and matter found on earth, and from the earth in the air and ether. This solidification is done by means of the blood. Therefore, in order for the body to keep its shape and continue functioning, a person must be nourished by material food and constantly renewed.

371. **THAT THERE IS A CORRESPONDENCE <term>a spiritual relationship where a natural thing reflects a spiritual reality</term> OF THE WILL WITH THE HEART, AND OF THE UNDERSTANDING WITH THE LUNGS.** This shall be proved in the following series:—I. That all things of the mind are...

That the joining of a person’s spirit and body is brought about by the correspondence of their will and understanding with their heart and lungs, and their separation is caused by a lack of this correspondence.

372. I. *That all things of the mind can be traced back to the will and understanding, and all things of the body to the heart and lungs.* By the mind, we mean nothing more than the will and the understanding. Together, these make up everything that affects a person and everything they think—thus, all things of their affection and thought. The things that affect them belong to their will, and the things they think belong to their understanding. It is well known that all of a person’s thoughts belong to the understanding, because a person thinks from the understanding. However, it is not as well known that all aspects of a person’s affection belong to the will. This is because when a person thinks, they do not pay attention to the affection, but only to the thought itself. It is like when someone hears another person speaking: they do not focus on the tone of the voice, but on the words. Yet, affection is related to thought in the same way that tone is related to speech. Therefore, a speaker’s tone shows their affection, and their speech shows their thought. 

Affection belongs to the will because all affection comes from love, and the will is the vessel for love, as was shown previously. Someone who does not know that affection belongs to the will confuses affection with understanding; they say it is the same as thought, when in reality they are not the same, though they act as one. That the two are confused is clear from the common saying, "I think to do this," when the person really means, "I *will* to do it." But that they are two distinct things is also evident from another common saying, "I will think about this." When the person then thinks about it, the affection of the will is present in the thought of the understanding, just as the tone of voice is present in speech, as has been said. It is well known that all things of the body can be traced back to the heart and lungs; however, the fact that there is a correspondence between the heart and lungs and the will and understanding is unknown. Therefore, this matter will be discussed in the following sections.

373. Since the will and understanding are vessels for love and wisdom, they are two physical structures <note>original: "organic forms"</note> organized from the purest substances. They must be such structures in order to serve as containers. It is no objection that their physical organization is not visible to the eye, as it is beyond the reach of sight, even when that sight is enhanced by...

Very small insects are also beyond the range of our sight <note>original: "interior to sight"</note>, and yet they have organs of sense and motion, for they feel, walk, and fly; they also have brains, hearts, airways <note>original: "pulmonary pipes"</note>, and internal organs <note>original: "viscera"</note>, as skilled anatomists have discovered by using the microscope. Since the insects themselves are invisible, and their component organs are even more so, and yet it is not denied that they are organized in every detail, how can it be said that the two receptacles of love and wisdom—the will and understanding—are not organic forms? 

How can love and wisdom, which are life from the Lord, act upon something that is not a concrete subject or a substantial existence? Is not the brain, where thought exists, completely full, and is not every thing within it organized? The organic forms there appear to the naked eye; and in the outer layer of the brain <note>original: "cortical substance"</note>, the foundations <note>original: "principles"</note> of the receptacles of the will and understanding are located, where we see those small glands mentioned above in section 366. I beg you, do not think of these things in terms of a vacuum: a vacuum is nothing, and in nothingness, nothing exists, and from nothingness, nothing is created. Concerning the idea of a vacuum, see section 82 above.

374. II. *That there is a correspondence <note>a direct spiritual and functional connection</note> of the will and understanding with the heart and lungs, and therefore a correspondence of all things of the mind with all things of the body.* 

This concept is new and has been unknown until now, because it has not been understood what the spiritual is, how it differs from the natural, and therefore what correspondence is (there being a correspondence between spiritual and natural things, which allows them to be joined together). It is said that it has been unknown until now what the spiritual is and what its correspondence with the natural is—and consequently what correspondence itself is—but both still could have been known. 

Who does not know that affection and thought are spiritual, and therefore that all things involving affection and thought are spiritual? Who does not know that action and speech are natural, and therefore all things involving action and speech are natural? Who does not know that affection and thought, which are spiritual, cause a person to act and speak? From this, who might not recognize the correspondence between spiritual and natural things? Does not thought cause the tongue to speak, and does affection combined with thought not cause the body to act? 

They are two distinct things. I can think and not speak, and I can will and not act; and it is well known that the body does not think or will on its own, but that thought results in speech, and will results in action. Does not affection shine through the face and present an image <note>original: "type"</note> of itself there? Everyone knows this. Is not affection, considered in itself, spiritual, while the changes of the face or the looks are natural? Who might not have concluded from this that there is a correspondence? And therefore, that there is a correspondence of all things of the mind with all things of the body? And as all things of the mind relate to affection and thought—or, what is the same thing, to the will and understanding—and all things of the body relate to the heart and lungs—who...

374—376 ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART V.

Might they not have concluded from this that there is a correspondence <note>a direct spiritual and functional connection</note> of the will with the heart and of the understanding with the lungs? These things have not been known—even though they could have been—because humanity has become so focused on the external that people are unwilling to acknowledge anything except what is natural <note>original: "natural"; meaning physical or material</note>. This focus is the delight of man's love, and consequently of his understanding. Therefore, to lift his thoughts above the natural world to anything spiritual that is separate from the natural is unpleasant to him. Because of this, he cannot think any other way from his natural love and its delight than to assume that the "spiritual" is simply a more refined version of the "natural," and that correspondence is something that flows through a simple physical continuity. Indeed, the purely natural person cannot think of anything separate from the natural world; to him, such a thing is nothing.

Furthermore, these things have been unseen and unknown until now because all religious or spiritual matters have been removed from sight by the dogma <note>a set of principles laid down by an authority as incontrovertibly true</note> accepted throughout the whole of Christendom. This doctrine states that theological or spiritual matters determined by councils and certain leaders are to be believed blindly because, they say, these things transcend human understanding. Because of this, some have imagined that what is spiritual is like a bird flying high above the air in the ether, where the eyes cannot reach. Yet, in reality, it is like a bird of paradise that flies near the eye, touching the pupil with its beautiful wings and wishing to be seen. By "sight," we mean the sight of the intellect.

**375.** The correspondence of the will and understanding with the heart and lungs cannot be proved abstractly, or by logic alone, but it can be proven by effects. This is the same as with the causes of things: causes can be seen through logic, but they are only seen clearly through their effects. Causes exist within the effects and are visible through them, and the mind is never fully convinced of a cause until it sees the effect. The effects of this correspondence shall be shown in the sections that follow. 

However, so that no one falls into misconceptions based on common hypotheses about the soul, let him first read what was shown in the preceding article:
* That love and wisdom (and therefore the will and understanding) constitute a person’s very life (n. 363, 364).
* That a person's life exists in its <term>principles <note>the primary or governing foundations of life, located in the brain</note></term> in the brain, and in its <term>principiates <note>original: "principiates"; the structures of the body that are derived from and governed by the primary principles</note></term> in the body (n. 365).
* That the life in the principles is the same as the life in the whole and in every part (n. 366).
* That the life, through those principles, exists from every part in the whole, and from the whole in every part (n. 367).
* That as the love is, so is the wisdom, and therefore so is the person (n. 368).

Through a marvelous <term>fluxion into gyres <note>original: "fluxion into gyres"; a flowing movement into circular or spiraling patterns</note></term> that no words can express, the angels formed the likeness of a heart and lungs, including all their internal structures. In doing this, they followed the "flow" of heaven; for heaven naturally tends toward such forms because of the <term>influx <note>the spiritual "in-flowing" of life and order from God</note></term> of

The correspondence of the literal and spiritual senses of the Word <note>The Bible</note> is treated of throughout *Heavenly Secrets* <note>original: *Arcana Cœlestia*</note>, regarding which see also the *Doctrine of the New Jerusalem concerning the Lord*, n. 5 to 26, 27 to 65.

378. III. **That the will corresponds to the heart** may not be as immediately obvious on its own as when the will is viewed through its effects, as we mentioned above. However, it can be seen on its own by the fact that all the emotions <note>original: "affections"</note> of love change the movements of the heart, as is clear from the pulsing of the arteries, which act in sync with the heart. Its changes and movements according to the emotions of love are countless. Those felt by the finger are few—such as whether it beats slow or fast, high or low, soft or hard, steady or uneven, and so on. Therefore, it beats differently in joy and sadness, in peace of mind and in anger, in fearlessness and in fear, in feverish illnesses and in chills, and so forth. Since the movements of the heart—its *systole and diastole* <note>the contraction and expansion of the heart</note>—change and vary in this way according to the emotions of a person’s love, many of the ancients, and some modern thinkers following them, have attributed the emotions to the heart and assigned their home there. 

Hence, in conversation, we speak of a brave and a timid heart, a joyful and a sad heart, a soft and a hard heart, a great and a little heart, a whole and a broken heart, a fleshy and a stony heart. We also speak of being "fat," soft, and meek in heart, and of giving the heart to a thing, of giving a "single" heart, of giving a new heart, of laying up in the heart, of receiving in the heart, of something "not coming upon the heart," of hardening the heart, and of being a friend "at heart." From this also come terms like *concord* <note>literally "hearts together"</note>, *discord* <note>literally "hearts apart"</note>, *vecord* <note>original: *vecordia*, meaning senseless or mad; literally "without heart"</note>, and other similar expressions that are used to describe love and its emotions. The Word speaks in the same way because the Word is written through correspondences. Whether you say "love" or "the will," it is the same because, as was said above, the will is the vessel that receives love.

379. It is well known that there is vital heat in humans and in every animal, but its origin is unknown. Everyone speaks of it based on guesswork. Therefore, those who have no knowledge of the correspondence of natural things with spiritual things have attributed it either to the heat of the sun, to the activity of particles, or to life itself. But because they did not know what life is, they went no further than merely saying the word. However, anyone who knows that there is a correspondence between love (with its emotions) and the heart (with its branching vessels) can know that love is the origin of vital heat. Love proceeds from the spiritual sun, where the Lord is, as heat, and

379. Love is also felt by the angels as heat. This spiritual heat, which in its essence is love, flows by correspondence into the heart and the blood, giving it heat and, at the same time, giving it life <note>original: "vivifies it"</note>. It is well known—because it can be felt and seen—that a person becomes heated, and almost "fired up," according to their love and its intensity, and grows sluggish <note>original: "torpid"</note> and cold as that love decreases. This is felt from the heat of the whole body and seen from the redness of the face; conversely, the fading away <note>original: "extinction"</note> of love is felt from the coldness of the body and seen from the paleness of the face. Since love is the life of man, the heart is the beginning and end of his life. And because love is the life of man, and the soul carries out its life in the body through the blood, the blood is called the "soul" <note>In many older translations of the Bible, the word for "life" is translated as "soul."</note> in the Word <note>The Bible</note>, specifically in Genesis 9:4 and Leviticus 17:14. What is meant by "soul" in various senses will be discussed in what follows.

380. The blood is red because of the correspondence of the heart and the blood with love and its emotions <note>original: "affections"</note>. In the spiritual world, there are colors of all kinds. Red and white are the fundamental colors; all others derive their varieties from these and their opposites—the latter being a dark-fire <note>original: "dusky-fiery"</note> color and black. In that world, red corresponds to love, and white corresponds to wisdom. Red corresponds to love because it originates from the fire of the sun of that world, and white corresponds to wisdom because it originates from the light of that same sun. Since love corresponds to the heart, the blood must necessarily be red and reflect its origin. Because of this, in the heavens where love for the Lord is the most important quality <note>original: "predominant"</note>, the light is flame-colored, and the angels are dressed in purple garments. In the heavens where wisdom is most important, the light is white, and the angels are dressed in white linen garments.

381. The heavens are divided into two kingdoms: the celestial kingdom and the spiritual kingdom. Love for the Lord is most important in the celestial kingdom, and wisdom coming from that love is most important in the spiritual kingdom. The kingdom where love is most important is called the cardiac <term>heart-related</term> kingdom of heaven, and the kingdom where wisdom is most important is called the pulmonic <term>lung-related</term> kingdom of heaven. It should be noted that the entire angelic heaven, in its total structure <note>original: "complex"</note>, resembles a single person and appears as one person before the Lord. Therefore, its heart constitutes one kingdom, and its lungs another. There is a heart-like and lung-like <note>original: "cardiac and pulmonic"</note> motion shared by the whole heaven, and consequently by each individual angel. This shared motion of the heart and lungs comes from the Lord alone, because love and wisdom are from Him alone. In the sun where the Lord is—and which originates from the Lord—these two motions exist, and therefore they exist in the angelic heaven and throughout the universe. Set aside the idea of physical space <note>original: "abstract space"</note> and think instead of God's presence everywhere <note>omnipresence</note>, and you will be convinced that this is true. Regarding the division of the heavens into two kingdoms, the celestial and the spiritual, see the work on **HEAVEN AND HELL**, sections 20 to 28; and regarding how the entire angelic heaven resembles one person, sections 59 to 87.

# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART V.

### 382. IV. The understanding corresponds to the lungs.

This follows from what we said regarding the correspondence of the will with the heart. There are two things that rule in the spiritual person, or the mind—the will and the understanding. Likewise, there are two things that rule in the natural person, or the body—the heart and the lungs. Because there is a correspondence between all things of the mind and all things of the body (as was stated above), it follows that while the will corresponds to the heart, the understanding corresponds to the lungs.

Anyone may also perceive within themselves that the understanding corresponds to the lungs, both through their thought and their speech. 

*Through thought:* No one can think unless their breathing works together and agrees with it. Therefore, when a person thinks quietly, they breathe quietly; if they think deeply, they breathe deeply. They contract and relax, compress and elevate the lungs according to the influence of emotion <note>original: "affection"</note> from love—whether slowly, hastily, eagerly, mildly, or attentively. Indeed, if a person holds their breath entirely, they cannot think, except within their spirit through its own respiration, which is not clearly felt. 

*Through speech:* Not even the smallest expression can come from the mouth without the assistance of the lungs. All articulate sound is generated by the lungs through the trachea and epiglottis. Therefore, speech may be raised to a shout <note>original: "clamor"</note> depending on how those "bellows" are inflated and how their passage is opened; it is diminished according to their contraction. If the passage is closed, speech and thought cease.

### 383. Since the understanding corresponds to the lungs, and therefore thought corresponds to respiration, the "soul" and "spirit" in the Word <note>The Bible</note> signify the understanding. 

This is seen where it is said, "You shall love the Lord your God with all your heart, and with all your soul" (Matthew 22:37); and "That God will give a new heart and a new spirit" (Ezekiel 36:26; Psalm 51:10). We showed above that the heart signifies the love of the will; therefore, soul and spirit signify the wisdom of the understanding. 

That the "Spirit of God," or the "Holy Spirit," means divine wisdom and therefore divine truth, which enlighten people, may be seen in <term>The Doctrine of the New Jerusalem concerning the Lord</term>, sections 50 and 51. For this reason, "The Lord breathed on His disciples, and said, Receive the Holy Spirit" (John 20:22). Similarly, it is said that "Jehovah God breathed into the nostrils of Adam the breath of lives, and he became a living soul" (Genesis 2:7); and He said to the prophet, "Prophesy to the wind, and say to the wind, Come from the four winds, O breath [spirit], and breathe upon these slain, that they may live" (Ezekiel 37:9). There are other similar examples; because of this, the Lord is called the "breath [spirit] of the nostrils," and the "breath of life" <note>original: spiraculum</note>. 

Since the breath passes through the nostrils, the nostrils signify perception. Thus, an intelligent person is said to be "sharp-nosed" <note>original Latin: acutæ naris; meaning keen-witted</note>, and one who is not intelligent is said to be "dull-nosed" <note>original Latin: obesæ naris; meaning slow or thick-witted</note>. For this reason also...

PART V. THE DIVINE LOVE. 383—385

...spirit (or breath) and wind are expressed by the same word in Hebrew and some other languages. This is because the word "spirit" derives its origin from breathing <gloss>original: *animatio*, meaning the act of giving life or breath</gloss>. For this reason, when a person dies, it is said that they "give up the ghost" <gloss>original: *emittat animam*, meaning to release the soul or breath</gloss>. Consequently, people often believe that a spirit is merely wind or something airy, like the breath from the lungs, and they think the same of the soul.

From this, it may appear that loving God with "all the heart and all the soul" means with all the love and all the understanding. Likewise, the promise of "giving a new heart and a new spirit" means giving a new will and a new understanding. Because "spirit" signifies the understanding, it is said of Bezaleel that he was "filled with the spirit of wisdom, of intelligence, and of knowledge" (Exodus 31:3). It is said of Joshua that he was "filled with the spirit of wisdom" (Deuteronomy 34:9). Nebuchadnezzar says of Daniel that "the excellent spirit of knowledge, of intelligence, and of wisdom was in him" (Daniel 6:3). And in Isaiah, it is said, "Those who erred in spirit shall have intelligence" (Isaiah 29:24). This same usage appears in many other places.

384. Since everything in the mind relates to the will and the understanding, and everything in the body relates to the heart and the lungs, the head contains two brains that are distinct from each other, just like the will and the understanding. These are the cerebellum, which relates particularly to the will, and the cerebrum, which relates particularly to the understanding. Similarly, the heart and lungs in the body are distinct from the other internal organs <term>original: "viscera"</term>; they are separated by the diaphragm and enclosed in their own protective covering, the pleura, forming the part of the body called the chest <note>original: "breast"</note>. 

In other parts of the body—the members, organs, and internal systems—the will and understanding are joined together, which is why they often appear in pairs: the arms and hands, the loins and feet, the eyes, and the nostrils. Inside the body, the kidneys, ureters, and testicles are pairs; even the organs that are not in pairs are divided into right and left sides. The brain is divided into two hemispheres, the heart into two ventricles, and the lungs into two lobes. In each case, the right side relates to the "good of truth" and the left to the "truth of good." In other words, the right side relates to the "good of love" (from which the truth of wisdom comes), and the left relates to the "truth of wisdom" (which comes from the good of love). Because the union of good and truth is reciprocal—and that union makes them act as one—these pairs work together and in unison in all their functions, movements, and senses.

385. **V. This correspondence can be used to discover many hidden secrets <term>original: "arcana"</term> concerning the will and understanding, and thus also concerning love and wisdom.** 

In the world, people hardly know what the will and love actually are. This is because a person cannot love—and therefore cannot use their will—entirely on their own, even though they can understand and think as if by their own power. It is just like how a person cannot consciously force their heart to beat, even though they can consciously act on their lungs to make themselves breathe. Now, because it is barely known in the world what the will and love truly are...

...are, and yet it is known what the heart and lungs are. [The heart and lungs are objects of sight and can be seen; they are also observed and described by anatomists. In contrast, the will and the understanding are not objects of sight and cannot be seen.] Therefore, when it is known that they correspond and thereby act as one, many mysteries <note>original: "arcana," meaning hidden spiritual secrets</note> may be discovered concerning the will and understanding that cannot be discovered otherwise. 

These include the union of the will with the understanding and the mutual union of the understanding with the will; the union of love with wisdom and the mutual union of wisdom with love; the way love branches into affections, the association of those affections, and their flow into perceptions and thoughts; and finally, according to their correspondence, how they flow into the acts and senses of the body. These and many more mysteries may be both discovered and demonstrated by the union of the heart and lungs, and by the flow of blood from the heart into the lungs and its mutual flow from the lungs back into the heart, and from there through the arteries into all the members, organs, and internal organs of the body.

386. VI. **That a person's mind is their spirit, and that the spirit is a person, and that the body is the external part by which the mind or spirit feels and acts in the world.** 

That a person’s mind is their spirit, and that the spirit is a person, is difficult to believe for those who think that the spirit is merely wind, and the soul is something ethereal, like the breath from the lungs. They ask: how can a spirit be a person when it is a spirit? And how can the soul be a person when it is the soul? They think similarly of God because He is called a Spirit. 

They have derived this idea of the spirit and the soul from the fact that "spirit" and "wind" are expressed by the same word in some languages. Also, when a person dies, they are said to "give up the ghost," and life is said to return when the breath of the lungs returns in people who have been suffocated or have fainted. Because people do not then perceive anything but wind and air, they judge—based only on their eyes and bodily senses—that a person's spirit and soul after death is not a human being. 

This physical viewpoint of the spirit and soul has given birth to various hypotheses, and these have led to a belief that a person does not truly become a person until the Day of Judgment, and that in the meantime, they stay in some place or other and wait for a reunion with the body, according to what we said in the **Continuation of the Last Judgment**, numbers 32 to 38. Because a person's mind is their spirit, the angels (who are also spirits) are called "minds."

387. A person's mind is their spirit, and the spirit is a person, because the "mind" refers to everything involving a person's will and understanding. These exist in their primary sources in the brains and in their <term>principiates</term> <note>original: "principiates," referring to the parts of the body that are derived from and governed by the primary principles in the brain</note> in the body. Therefore, they constitute the whole person in terms of their form. This being the case, the mind—or the will and understanding—activates the body and all its parts at will.

The body does whatever the mind thinks and wills. The mind directs the ear to hear and positions the eye to see; the mind moves the tongue and lips to speak; it activates the hands and fingers to do whatever it pleases, and the feet to walk wherever it wants. Is the body, then, anything but the obedience of the mind? Can the body be this way unless the mind, in its derivative structures <note>original: "principiates," referring to the parts of the body that originate from and are governed by the brain</note>, is in the body? Is it reasonable to think that the body acts in obedience simply because the mind wills it? At this rate, they would be two separate things—one above and the other below—where one would command and the other obey. Since this is not reasonable, it follows that a person's life begins in its first principles <note>original: "principles," the primary sources or starting points of life</note> in the brain, and extends into its derivatives throughout the body, according to what we said above in section 365. It also follows that as the life is in its principles, so it is in the whole and in every part (section 366); and that life, through these principles, is present from every part in the whole, and from the whole in every part (section 367). That everything in the mind relates to the will and understanding, that the will and understanding are the vessels <note>original: "receptacles"</note> of love and wisdom from the Lord, and that these two constitute a person’s life, was shown in the preceding pages.

388. From what has now been said, it may also be seen that a person’s mind is the person himself. The first initial stage of the human form—or the human form itself, with each and every one of its parts—is derived from principles continued from the brain through the nerves, as was also shown above. After death, a person enters into this form, which is then called a spirit and an angel; this spirit is a person in all perfection, but a spiritual one. The material form, added and placed over the spirit in the world, is not a human form by itself, but receives its form from the spiritual form mentioned above. This material body is added to enable a person to perform useful functions in the natural world and to carry with them, from the purer substances of the world, a fixed container <note>original: "continent," meaning a vessel that holds or contains something</note> for spiritual things, and so to continue and perpetuate their life. It is a tenet of angelic wisdom that the mind of a person, not only in general but in every particular, is in a perpetual effort toward the human form, because God is a Man.

389. For a person to be a person, no part that exists in a perfect human—either in the head or the body—must be missing. There is nothing there that does not enter into that form and constitute it. It is the form of love and wisdom, which, viewed in itself, is divine. It involves all the expressions <note>original: "determinations"</note> of love and wisdom, which are infinite in the God-Man but finite in His images—in men, angels, and spirits. If any part that exists in a person were missing, there would be something missing from the expression of love and wisdom corresponding to it. This correspondence is the means through which the Lord might be in man from the highest things to the lowest <note>original: "from primaries in ultimates"</note>, and from His divine love through His divine wisdom provide uses in the created world.

*...with his heart and lungs, and their separation when they no longer correspond. Because it has not been known until now that a person’s mind—meaning their will and understanding—is actually their spirit, and that the spirit is the person, and that the spirit has a pulse and breathing just like the physical body does, it could not be known that the pulse and breathing of a person’s spirit flow into the pulse and breathing of the body and produce them. Since a person’s spirit has a pulse and breathing just like the body, it follows that there is a similar correspondence <note>original: "correspondence"; in this context, it refers to a functional and spiritual connection where the physical action mirrors a spiritual one</note> between them. As previously stated, the mind is the spirit; therefore, when the connection between these two movements stops, they separate, which results in death. Separation or death occurs when the body, due to disease or accident, reaches a state where it can no longer act as one with its spirit. Thus, their correspondence perishes, and with it, their connection. This happens not simply when breathing stops, but when the heart stops beating. As long as the heart moves, love and its vital heat <note>original: "vital heat"; an old scientific concept suggesting that a specific type of heat was the source of life</note> remain and preserve life. This is evident in cases of fainting and suffocation, as well as the state of fetal life in the womb. In short, the life of a person’s body depends on its pulse and breathing matching the pulse and breathing of their spirit. When that match ends, the body’s life ends; the spirit departs and continues its life in the spiritual world. That life is so similar to life in the natural world that the person often does not even know they have passed away. Generally, people are fully conscious in the spiritual world two days after leaving the body; I have personally spoken with some only two days after their death.*

391. The fact that spirits have a pulse and breathing just like people in the body can only be proven by spirits and angels themselves, when permission is given to talk with them. This permission has been granted to me. When I questioned them about this matter, they said they are just as human as people in the world and that they also have a body—though theirs is a spiritual one. They also feel their heart beating in their chest and their pulse at the wrist, just like people in the physical world; I have asked many about this, and they all said the same thing. I have also learned from my own experience that a person’s spirit breathes within their body. Once, the angels were allowed to guide my breathing, slowing it down as they pleased and eventually stopping it, until only the breathing of my spirit remained, which I then clearly felt. A similar experience happened when I was being taught about the state of dying persons, which can be read in the work *Heaven and Hell*, section 449. Sometimes I have also been brought to a state where I breathed only with my spirit; I could then clearly feel that this breathing was in harmony with the collective breathing of heaven. Many times I have shared this state with the angels and have been raised up to them into heaven, existing there in the spirit...

The breathing of the spirit is so subtle <note>original: "secret"</note> that a person does not perceive it; it flows into the obvious breathing of the physical body as a cause flows into an effect—just as a thought flows into the lungs and, through the lungs, into speech. From this it is also clear that the connection <note>original: "conjunction"</note> between the spirit and the body in a person occurs through the correspondence <note>a spiritual connection where the physical action mirrors a spiritual one</note> of the heart <note>original: "cardiac"</note> and lung <note>original: "pulmonary"</note> motions in both.

392. The heart and lung motions exist and continue because the entire angelic heaven, both as a whole and in every part, is within them. The angelic heaven is in them because the Lord—acting from the spiritual sun where He resides and which originates from Him—pours these motions into them. That sun carries out these two motions from the Lord. Since everything in heaven and the world depends on the Lord through that sun in such a way that they form a single interconnected work from beginning to end, and since the life of love and wisdom comes from Him, and all powers in the universe come from life, it is clear that they have no other source. It follows that any variation in these motions depends on how much love and wisdom a person receives.

393. More will be said about the correspondence of these motions in the following sections. This includes how it works with those who breathe in harmony with heaven and those who breathe in harmony with hell; it also includes those who speak with heaven but think with hell—such as hypocrites, flatterers, deceivers, and others.

394. **THAT EVERYTHING THAT CAN BE KNOWN ABOUT THE WILL AND THE UNDERSTANDING (OR ABOUT LOVE AND WISDOM), AND CONSEQUENTLY EVERYTHING THAT CAN BE KNOWN ABOUT THE HUMAN SOUL, CAN BE UNDERSTOOD THROUGH THE CORRESPONDENCE OF THE HEART WITH THE WILL AND THE LUNGS WITH THE UNDERSTANDING.** Many in the academic world have worked hard to investigate the soul. However, because they knew nothing of the spiritual world or the state of humans after death, they could only create theories <note>original: "hypotheses"</note>. These theories did not focus on the soul’s true nature, but rather on how it operates within the body. They could imagine the soul only as something extremely pure within the "ether," and they saw the soul's container as being made of ether itself <note>original: "ether"; in older science, ether was thought to be a weightless fluid that filled the universe</note>. They did not dare to publish much on this subject, fearing they might accidentally describe the soul as something natural or physical, when they knew the soul is spiritual. Because they had this concept of the soul, yet knew it acted on the body to produce every sensation and movement, they struggled (as mentioned before) to investigate how the soul operates on the body. Some said this happened by "influx" <note>the theory that the soul flows into the body</note> and others by "harmony" <note>the theory that the soul and body are pre-synchronized like two clocks</note>. But because these methods revealed nothing that could satisfy a mind seeking the true basis of things, I have been allowed to speak with angels...

<header>394—396 ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING THE DIVINE LOVE AND WISDOM. PART V.</header>
<page-num>148</page-num>

...and to be enlightened by their wisdom on this subject. A fundamental principle of this wisdom is that the human soul, which lives after death, is the person's spirit. This spirit is a person in a perfect human form. The soul of this form is the will and the understanding, and the soul of these two is love and wisdom from the Lord. These two—love and wisdom—constitute human life, which comes from the Lord alone. For the sake of being received by humanity, the Lord causes life to appear as if it belonged to the person themselves. However, lest anyone should claim life as their own and thus distance themselves from receiving the Lord, He has also taught that everything of love (which is called "good") and everything of wisdom (which is called "truth") comes from Him, and nothing comes from the person. Since these two make up life, it follows that the entirety of life comes from Him.

395. Because the soul, in its very essence <note>original Latin: *esse*</note>, is love and wisdom, and because these two come from the Lord within a person, two receptacles <note>containers or vessels</note> are created in the person. These are the Lord's dwellings: one for love and the other for wisdom. The dwelling for love is the **will**, and the dwelling for wisdom is the **understanding**. 

Now, just as love and wisdom in the Lord are "distinctly one" (as seen above in sections 17 to 22), and His Divine Love belongs to His Divine Wisdom, and His Divine Wisdom belongs to His Divine Love (sections 34 to 39), they proceed from the God-Man (that is, the Lord) in such a way that these two receptacles and dwellings—the will and understanding—are created to be distinctly two, yet still act as one in every operation and sensation. In these actions, the will and understanding cannot be separated. 

However, to enable a person to become a dwelling for the Lord, it was necessary that the human understanding be able to be raised above its own love into a certain light of wisdom that the person does not yet love. By this light, a person can see and be taught how they ought to live so that they may eventually enter into that love and enjoy eternal happiness <note>original: "beatitude"</note>. But because humans have abused this ability to raise their understanding above their love, they have destroyed the potential dwelling place for the Lord—the home of love and wisdom. They did this by making their **will** the dwelling of self-love and love of the world, and making their **understanding** the dwelling of justifications <note>original: "confirmations"</note> for those loves. This is the original cause of how these two dwellings, the will and understanding, became homes for hellish <note>original: "infernal"</note> love and, through rationalizing them, homes for hellish thought, which those in hell consider to be wisdom.

396. The reason that the loves of self and the world are hellish loves—and the reason a person could fall into them and destroy their own will and understanding—is that the loves of self and the world are actually heavenly by their original creation. They were intended to be loves of the natural man that serve spiritual loves, just as foundations serve as the base for houses. Out of the loves of self and the world, a person wishes well to their own body; they desire to be fed, clothed, and to have a home.

<summary>This page explains that the human spirit is a perfect form whose life consists of will and understanding, which are meant to be dwellings for the Lord’s love and wisdom. It also describes how humanity fell into "infernal love" by using the understanding to justify selfish desires rather than to reach higher spiritual truths.</summary>

<keywords>soul, spirit, will, understanding, love, wisdom, essence, God-Man, infernal love, self-love, natural man, spiritual growth</keywords>

<header>PART V. THE DIVINE LOVE. 396—398</header>

...to care for the well-being of his household, to seek employment for the sake of being useful, and even to be honored according to the importance of the tasks he manages for the sake of duty. He may also be delighted and refreshed by the pleasures of the world; but all these must be for a specific purpose, which should be usefulness. By these means, he is in a position to serve the Lord and to serve his neighbor. But when there is no love for serving the Lord and the neighbor, but only a love for serving oneself based on worldly desires, then that love ceases to be heavenly and becomes hellish. It causes a man to sink his mind and soul into his <term>proprium</term> <note>original Latin: *proprium*; refers to the self, or what is "one's own," which in this context is the self-centered nature of a person apart from God</note>, which in itself is nothing but evil.

397. Now, so that a person’s understanding is not in heaven while his will is in hell—which would give him a divided mind—all of the understanding that is higher than his own love is removed after death. Consequently, the will and understanding in everyone eventually act as one. In those who are in heaven, the will loves what is good and the understanding thinks what is true; but in those in hell, the will loves what is evil and the understanding thinks what is false. A person does the same in the world when he thinks from his spirit, which occurs when he is alone, although many think differently when they are in the body, which happens when they are with others. In the latter case, they think differently because they raise their understanding above the <term>proprium</term> of their will, or the love of their spirit. These observations are intended to show that the will and understanding are two distinct things, yet they were created to act as one, and they are forced to act as one after death, if not before.

398. Since love and wisdom, and consequently the will and understanding, are the soul, and since in what follows it will be shown how the soul acts on the body and operates everything belonging to it—and since this may be known by the <term>correspondence</term> <note>the spiritual relationship and alignment between a spiritual cause and a natural effect</note> of the heart with the will and of the lungs with the understanding—that <term>correspondence</term> reveals the following points:

I. That love or the will is the very essence of a person's life.
II. That love or the will continually strives toward the human form and everything related to that form.
III. That love or the will cannot do anything through its human form without a marriage with wisdom or the understanding.
IV. That love or the will prepares a house or bridal chamber for a future spouse, which is wisdom or the understanding.
V. That love or the will also prepares everything in its human form so that it may act together with wisdom or the understanding.
VI. That after this "marriage," the first union is through the affection for knowing, which gives rise to the affection for truth.
VII. That the second union is through the affection for understanding, which gives rise to the perception of truth.
VIII. That the third union is through the affection for seeing truth, which gives rise to thought.
IX. That love or the will, through these three unions, enters into its sensory and active life.
X. That love or the will introduces wisdom or the understanding to everything in its...

...household. 
XI. That love (or the will) does nothing except in conjunction with wisdom (or the understanding). 
XII. That love or the will joins itself to wisdom or the understanding, and causes wisdom or the understanding to be reciprocally joined to it. 
XIII. That wisdom or the understanding, by virtue of the power given to it by love or the will, may be raised up and receive the things of the light of heaven, and perceive them. 
XIV. That love or the will can in like manner be raised and perceive the things of the heat of heaven, if it loves its <term>spouse</term> <note>original: "spouse"; the author uses the metaphor of marriage to describe the union between the will and the understanding</note> to that degree. 
XV. That otherwise love or the will pulls down wisdom or the understanding from its height, so that they act as one. 
XVI. That love or the will is purified by wisdom in the understanding, if they are raised together. 
XVII. That love or the will is defiled in the understanding, and by it, if they are not raised together. 
XVIII. That love purified by wisdom in the understanding becomes spiritual and <term>celestial</term> <note>original: "celestial"; pertaining to the highest heaven or the highest state of love and goodness</note>. 
XIX. That love defiled in and by the understanding becomes natural and <term>sensual</term> <note>original: "sensual"; here meaning focused entirely on the physical senses and worldly desires</note>. 
XX. That the faculty of understanding, called rationality, and the faculty of acting, called liberty, still remain. 
XXI. That spiritual and celestial love is love for the neighbor and love for the Lord; and that natural and sensual love is love for the world and love for self. 
XXII. That it is the same with <term>charity and faith</term> and their union, as it is with the will and understanding and their union.

399. I. ***That love or the will is a man's essential life*** follows from the <term>correspondence</term> <note>the relationship between the spiritual and physical worlds where physical organs like the heart mirror spiritual functions like the will</note> of the heart with the will (as discussed above in sections 378 to 381). This is because the will acts in the mind as the heart acts in the body; and just as the existence and movement of all things in the body depend on the heart, so the existence and life of all things in the mind depend on the will. In using the expression "on the will," we mean on the love, because the will is the vessel for love, and love is life itself (see above, sections 1, 2, and 3); and love, which is life itself, comes from the Lord alone. 

From the heart and its expansion throughout the body by the arteries and veins, it may be known that love or the will is the life of man. This is because things that "correspond" act in the same manner, with the difference being that one is natural and the other is spiritual. How the heart acts in the body is shown by anatomy—for instance, that everything lives (or is in obedience to life) wherever the heart acts through the vessels it sends out, and that nothing lives where the heart does not act through its vessels. Moreover, the heart is the first and last organ that acts in the body. That it is the first is evident from the <term>fetus</term> <note>original Latin: *foetus*</note>, and that it is the last is evident from dying persons. That it acts without the cooperation of the lungs is evident from people who have been suffocated and from those in <term>swoons</term> <note>original: "swoons"; fainting spells or states of unconsciousness</note>. 

From this, it may be seen that just as the <term>substituted</term> <note>original: "substituted"; here meaning the physical life that represents or stands in for the spiritual life</note> life of the body depends on the heart alone, so the life of the mind depends on the will alone; and that the will lives when the thought ceases, just as the heart lives when the respiration...

It then establishes the heart as the physical counterpart to the spiritual will, arguing that love is the primary source of human life.</summary>

# PART V. THE DIVINE LOVE.

...stops, as is also evident in fetuses, dying or suffocating people, and those in a faint. From this, it follows that love, or the will, is the essential life of a human being.

400. **II. That love or the will constantly strives toward the human form, and toward everything belonging to the human form.**
This is evident from the <term>correspondence</term> <note>original: "correspondence"; the philosophical concept that the physical body is a direct reflection or map of spiritual realities</note> of the heart with the will. It is well known that all parts of the body are formed in the womb, and that they are formed by fibers from the brain and by blood vessels from the heart. The tissues of all the organs and <term>viscera</term> <note>original: "viscera"; the internal organs in the main cavities of the body, such as the heart, lungs, and intestines</note> are formed by these two. From this, it is clear that everything in a person exists from the life of the will, which is love, proceeding from its origins in the brain through the fibers; and that everything in the body exists from the heart through the arteries and veins. Thus, it is obvious that life, or love—and consequently the will—is in a constant effort toward the human form. Because the human form consists of every single thing within a person, it follows that love or the will is in a continual endeavor and effort to form all those things. There is an endeavor and effort toward the human form because God is a Man, and divine love and divine wisdom are His life, from which everything in life originates. Anyone can see that unless life (which is truly "the Man") acted on what is not in itself life, nothing like a human could be formed. Within a person, there are millions of things that act as one and unanimously work together to reflect the image of the life from which they come, so that man may be able to be a vessel and a dwelling place for that life. Hence, it can be seen that love—and consequently the will and the heart—are in a constant effort toward the human form.

401. **III. That love or the will cannot do anything through its human form without a marriage with wisdom or the understanding.**
This is also evident from the correspondence of the heart with the will. A human fetus lives through its heart, but not its lungs. In this state, the blood does not flow from the heart into the lungs to give it the ability to breathe; instead, it flows through the <term>foramen ovale</term> <note>original: "foramen ovale"; a natural opening in a fetus's heart that allows blood to bypass the lungs before birth</note> into the left ventricle of the heart. Consequently, the fetus cannot move any part of its body but remains confined, nor can it feel, because its sensory organs are closed. It is the same with love or the will; the person still lives from it, but in "obscurity"—that is, without sensation or action. But as soon as the lungs are opened, which happens after birth, the person begins to feel and act, and in the same way, begins to will and to think. Thus, it appears that love or the will, without a marriage with wisdom or the understanding, cannot do anything through its human form.

402. **IV. That love or the will prepares a house or bridal apartment for a future spouse, which is wisdom or the understanding.**
In the created universe and all its parts, there is a "marriage" of good and truth. This is because good belongs to love and truth belongs to wisdom, and these two are in the Lord, by whom all things were created. How this marriage exists in man may be...

# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING THE DIVINE LOVE AND WISDOM: PART V

402. This relationship can be seen, as in a mirror, in the union of the heart with the lungs; for the heart <term>corresponds</term> <note>original: "corresponds"; the spiritual principle that physical organs reflect higher spiritual functions</note> to love or good, and the lungs to wisdom or truth, as shown above in numbers 378 to 384. From that union, it may be seen how love (the will) becomes engaged to wisdom (the understanding), and afterwards takes it as a wife or marries it. Love becomes engaged to wisdom in that it prepares a house or bridal apartment for wisdom; and it takes wisdom as a wife in that it unites wisdom to itself through affections, and then practices wisdom with it in that house. 

This process cannot be fully described except in spiritual language, because love and wisdom—and the will and understanding—are spiritual things. While they can indeed be described in natural language, they can only be unclearly perceived because of a lack of knowledge regarding the nature of love and wisdom, as well as the affections for "good" and the affections for wisdom (which are affections for "truth"). 

Nevertheless, the nature of this engagement and marriage between love and wisdom—or the will and understanding—may be seen through the parallel that exists because of their correspondence with the heart and lungs. It is the same with the latter as with the former; so much so that there is no difference, except that one is spiritual and the other natural. From the heart and the lungs, therefore, it is evident that the heart first forms the lungs and afterwards unites itself to them. It forms the lungs in the <term>fetus</term> <note>original: "fœtus"</note> and joins with them after birth. The heart does this in its "house," the chest, where their dwelling place is located, separated from the rest of the body by a divider called the **diaphragm** and by a membrane enclosing them called the **pleura**. It is the same with love and wisdom, or with the will and understanding.

403. **V. That love or the will also prepares everything in its human form so that it may act together with wisdom or the understanding.** 
We use the words "will" and "understanding," but let it be remembered that the will is the whole person. The will and understanding are in their <term>principles</term> <note>original: "principles"; here meaning the primary origins or nerve centers</note> in the brain, and in their <term>principiates</term> <note>original: "principiates"; the parts of the body that are derived from and controlled by those primary origins</note> in the body, and from there they are in the whole and in every part, as was shown above in numbers 365, 366, and 367. 

From this, it may appear that the will is the whole person in terms of form—both regarding the general form and the form of every specific part—and that the understanding is its companion, just as the lungs are to the heart. Be careful not to think of the will as something separate from the human form, for they are one and the same. 

From this, it can be seen not only how the will prepares a bridal apartment for the understanding, but also how it prepares everything in its "house" (which is the entire body) so that it may act in union with the understanding. It prepares these things in such a way that each and every part of the body may be joined to the understanding just as they are to the will; or, that each and every part of the body may be in obedience to the understanding just as they are to the will. How each and every part of the body is prepared for union...

He argues that the will is the primary substance of the entire human form, preparing the body to act in total harmony with the understanding.</summary>

# PART V. THE DIVINE LOVE.

...conjunction with the understanding as with the will, cannot be seen, except in a kind of mirror or image through the science of anatomy in the body. This shows how all things in the body are so connected that when the lungs breathe (<term>respire</term>), every single thing is influenced by their respiration, at the same time that they are also influenced by the beating (<term>pulsation</term>) of the heart. Anatomy shows that the heart is joined to the lungs by the <term>auricles</term> <note>original: "auricles"; the upper chambers or appendages of the heart</note>, and that these continue into the interior of the lungs. It also shows that all the <term>viscera</term> <note>original: "viscera"; the internal organs in the main cavities of the body, such as the abdomen</note> of the body are joined by ligaments to the cavity of the chest. This is done in such a manner that when the lungs breathe, every thing in general and each detail in particular receives something of that respiratory motion. 

When the lungs are inflated, the ribs expand the chest (<term>thorax</term>), the <term>pleura</term> <note>original: "pleura"; the membrane surrounding the lungs</note> is dilated, and the diaphragm is pressed downward. Along with these, all the lower parts of the body, which are connected to them by ligaments, receive some action from the action of the lungs. I will not mention other details, lest those who have no knowledge of anatomy should be confused by their ignorance of scientific terms. Simply consult those who have skill and knowledge in anatomy to see whether all things in the entire body, from the breast to the lowest part, are not so connected that when the lungs are inflated during breathing, they are all moved to an action that is <term>synchronous</term> <note>original: "synchronous"; happening at the same time</note> with that of the lungs. 

From this, the nature of the union prepared for the understanding with every particular of the human form through the will is evident. Simply examine their connection and survey them with an anatomical eye; then, according to their connection, consider their cooperation with the lungs in breathing and with the heart. Then, instead of the lungs, think of the understanding, and instead of the heart, think of the will, and you will see the truth of it.

404. VI. *That after the marriage, the first conjunction is by the affection of knowing, from which comes the affection of truth.* By <term>nuptials</term> <note>original: "nuptials"; here referring to the developmental stages of the union between the will and the understanding</note> we mean a person’s state after birth, moving from a state of ignorance to a state of intelligence, and from there to a state of wisdom. The first state, or that of mere ignorance, is not what is meant here by nuptials, because at that time there is no thought in the understanding, but only an obscure <term>affection</term> <note>original: "affection"; a fundamental drive or inclination belonging to the will</note>, which belongs to love or the will. This state is the introduction to the nuptials. 

It is well known that in the second state, which is childhood, there is an "affection of knowing." Through this, the infant child learns to speak and to read, and successively learns things that belong to the understanding. It cannot be doubted that love, which belongs to the will, drives this process; for if love or the will did not do so, it would not happen. Everyone who consults experience through reason acknowledges that every person, after birth, has the affection of knowing and thereby learns things by which the understanding is gradually formed, increased, and perfected. It is also evident that the affection for truth comes from this; for when a person is driven by the affection of knowing...

# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING THE DIVINE LOVE
## PART V.
-> 154 <-

...becomes intelligent, he is led not so much by the desire <note>original: "affection"; in this context, the emotional drive or love that directs the will</note> to know, as by the desire to reason and to draw conclusions about things he loves, whether they relate to household management <note>original: "economical"</note>, civic life, or morality. When this desire is raised to spiritual matters, it becomes the desire for spiritual truth. That its starting point <note>original: "initiament"</note> was the desire to know can be seen from this: the desire for truth is simply an elevated form of the desire to know. To be moved by truths is to want to know them out of desire, and once they are found, to absorb them out of the delight that desire brings.

VII. *That the second union <note>original: "conjunction"</note> is through the desire to understand, which results in the perception of truth*, is obvious to anyone willing to look at this through rational intuition. From rational intuition, it is clear that the desire for truth and the perception of truth are two powers of the understanding. In some people, these two meet as one, but in others they do not. They meet as one in those who want to perceive truths in their understanding, but not in those who only want to know truths. It is clear that everyone experiences the perception of truth in proportion to their desire to understand. Take away the desire to understand truth, and there will be no perception of truth; but provide the desire to understand truth, and perception will follow according to the degree of that desire. A person with a sound mind is never without the perception of truth, provided they have the desire to understand it. It was shown above that every human being has the capacity to understand truth, which is called rationality.

VIII. *That the third union is through the desire to see truth, which results in thought.* The desire to know truth is one thing, the desire to understand it is another, and the desire to see it is yet another. Or, to put it another way, the desire for truth, the perception of truth, and thought are three different things. This is only dimly understood by those who cannot clearly see the workings of their own minds, but it is very clear to those who can. 

It is dim to those who do not distinguish the mind's operations because these three stages happen together in the thoughts of those who desire and perceive truth; when they occur simultaneously, they cannot be easily separated. A person is in "obvious thought" when their spirit thinks while in the body, which is especially the case when in the company of others. However, when a person is in the desire to understand and thereby gains the perception of truth, they are in the "thought of their spirit," or in meditation. This meditation does eventually descend into the thoughts of the body, but as a silent <note>original: "tacit"</note> thought; for it exists above the body and looks down upon the things in the memory as if they were below it, using them to draw conclusions or confirm ideas. But the desire for truth is only felt as an effort of the will arising from a certain pleasure—this pleasure is the inner life of meditation and is usually given very little attention. From this, it appears that these three—the desire for truth, the perception of truth, and thought—

# PART V. THE DIVINE LOVE.
-> 404 <-

...follow in order from love, and they exist nowhere but in the understanding. When love enters the understanding—which occurs when a union between them is established—it first produces the desire <note>original: "affection"; in this context, the emotional drive or attraction of the will toward an object</note> for truth. This is followed by the desire to understand what it knows, and finally the desire to see what it understands in physical thought; for thought is essentially internal sight. 

Thought does indeed exist first in the sequence of time, because it belongs to the natural mind. However, the specific kind of thought that arises from the perception of truth (which itself stems from the desire for truth) exists last in the spiritual sequence. This latter form of thought belongs to wisdom, whereas the former is simply thought drawn from the memory through the vision of the natural mind. None of the operations of love or the will that occur outside of the understanding relate to the desire for truth; they relate only to the desire for good.

-> 405 <-

405. A rational person can comprehend that these three stages—proceeding from the love in the will—follow in order within the understanding, yet they may not see it clearly enough to be firmly convinced. However, love (which belongs to the will) acts as one with the heart through correspondence <term>correspondence</term> <note>A central concept in Swedenborg's work, meaning the functional and symbolic relationship between the spiritual and physical worlds</note>, and wisdom (which belongs to the understanding) acts as one with the lungs. Therefore, what was stated above in section 404 regarding the desire for truth, the perception of truth, and thought cannot be more clearly seen or confirmed than by looking at the lungs and their structure. 

For this reason, the lungs shall be briefly described. After birth, the heart sends blood from its right ventricle into the lungs; once the blood has circulated through them, the heart receives it back into its left ventricle. In this way, the heart "opens" the lungs. The heart performs this through the pulmonary arteries and veins. The lungs are also equipped with *bronchi* <note>original: "bronchia"</note> or air-pipes that branch out and eventually end in air-cells, through which the lungs take in air and breathe <note>original: "respire"</note>. 

Around the bronchi and their branches, there are also arteries and veins called *bronchial* vessels, which arise from the *azygos vein* <note>original: "vena azygos"; a vein running up the right side of the thoracic vertebral column</note> or *vena cava* <note>original: "vena cava"; the large vein carrying deoxygenated blood into the heart</note>, and from the *aorta*. These bronchial arteries and veins are distinct from the pulmonary arteries and veins. 

From this, it is clear that blood flows into the lungs through two different paths and returns from them through two different paths. This explains why the lungs can breathe at a different rhythm than the heart; it is well known that the rhythmic movements of the heart and the lungs do not always act as one. Since there is a correspondence of the heart and lungs with the will and understanding (as shown before), and since their union by correspondence is so close that one acts as the other does, we can see from the flow of blood from the heart into the lungs how the will flows into the understanding. 

This physical process produces those spiritual results shown in section 404—namely, the desire for truth, the perception of truth, and thought. This correspondence revealed these things to me, along with many other details about them that cannot be described in just a few words. Since love (the will) corresponds to the heart, and wisdom (the understanding) corresponds to the lungs, it follows that the blood vessels of the heart within the lungs correspond to the desires...

# ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART V.

...of truth, and the branching of the bronchi <note>original: "bronchia"; the main air passages of the lungs</note> in the lungs corresponds to the perceptions and thoughts arising from those desires. Anyone who examines all the tissues of the lungs from this perspective, and compares them with the love of the will and the wisdom of the understanding, may see a visual representation of the principles discussed above in section 404, and thus be confirmed in their belief. However, since the anatomical details of the heart and lungs are known to only a few, and trying to prove a point using unfamiliar facts only leads to confusion, I will refrain from describing this resemblance in any greater length.

406. IX. **Through these three connections, love (or the will) exists in its sensory and active life.** Love without understanding—or the desire belonging to love without the thought belonging to the understanding—cannot feel or act in the body. This is because love without understanding is essentially blind; desire without thought is like being in the dark, as the understanding provides the light by which love sees. The wisdom of the understanding also originates from the light that radiates from the Lord as a sun. Since the love of the will cannot see without the light of the understanding and is therefore blind, it follows that without the light of the understanding, the physical senses would also be blind and dull <gloss>dullness; original Latin: obesitas</gloss>. This applies not only to sight and hearing but to all the other senses as well.

The other senses would be affected because every perception of truth comes from love acting within the understanding, as shown previously, and all physical senses derive their ability to perceive from the perception of the mind. The same is true for every physical action: an act motivated by love without understanding is like a person acting in the middle of the night when they do not know what they are doing. Consequently, such an act would contain nothing of intelligence or wisdom and could not be called a "living" act; for an action derives its essence from love, but its specific quality from intelligence. Furthermore, all the power of "good" is exercised through "truth." Therefore, good acts within and through truth; "good" belongs to love, and "truth" belongs to the understanding. From this, it is clear that love (the will) exists in its sensory and active life through these three connections (see section 404).

407. This principle can be vividly confirmed by the connection between the heart and the lungs. Because there is such a correspondence <term>correspondence</term> <note>the functional and symbolic relationship between the spiritual and physical worlds</note> between the will and the heart, and between the understanding and the lungs, love acts with the understanding spiritually just as the heart acts with the lungs naturally. Therefore, what has been said may be seen as a visual image presented to the eye. It is evident that a person has no sensory or active life when the heart and lungs do not work together; this can be seen in the state of a fetus <note>original: fœtus</note> or infant in the womb, and its state after birth. As long as a human is an embryo in the womb, the lungs are closed; therefore, the child has no sensation or movement, as the sense organs are shut and the hands and feet are restricted. But after birth, the lungs are opened, and in proportion as this takes place the...

This spiritual union is mirrored physically by the cooperation between the heart and lungs, most notably during the transition from the womb to birth.</summary>

how the infant feels and acts: the lungs are opened by the blood sent into them from the heart. It is also evident from fainting spells <note>original: "swoons"</note> that a person possesses no sensory or active life without the cooperation of the heart and lungs. In a faint, the heart still beats but the lungs do not, because breathing has stopped; it is well known that in such a state there is no sensation or movement. The same is true for a person who is suffocated, whether by water or by anything that blocks the larynx and closes the passage for breathing. It is well known that such a person appears dead, feeling and doing nothing, yet is still alive at heart; he returns to both sensory and active life as soon as the obstructions to the lungs are removed. In the meantime, the blood does indeed circulate through the lungs, but through the pulmonary arteries and veins rather than the bronchial arteries and veins; it is these bronchial vessels that provide the ability to breathe. This process mirrors the way love flows into the understanding.

408. X. **That love (or the will) introduces wisdom (or the understanding) to everything in its house.**

By the "house" of love or the will, we mean the whole person in relation to everything in their mind. Since the mind corresponds to every part of the body (as shown above), by "house" we also mean the whole person in relation to all their bodily parts, members, organs, and internal organs <note>original: "viscera"</note>. Just as the understanding is introduced to everything in the mind, the lungs are introduced to all these bodily parts. This is evident from what was shown previously: that love (the will) prepares a house or "bridal suite" for its future spouse, which is wisdom (the understanding) (see section 402); and that love prepares everything in its human form, or its "house," so that it may act together with wisdom (see section 403). 

From those sections, it is clear that each and every thing in the body is connected by ligaments <note>strong bands of connective tissue</note> extending from the ribs, vertebrae, breastbone <note>original: "sternum"</note>, and diaphragm, and from the peritoneum <note>the lining of the abdominal cavity</note> which depends on them. Because of this connection, when the lungs breathe, each and every part is drawn into a rhythmic, alternating motion with them. Anatomy shows that this rhythmic breathing also enters the internal organs, even to their innermost recesses. The ligaments mentioned above are joined to the outer coverings <note>original: "sheaths"</note> of the organs, and these coverings extend into the organs' innermost textures through physical extensions <note>original: "exsertions"</note>, just as arteries and veins do through their branching <note>original: "ramifications"</note>. 

Therefore, it appears that the breathing of the lungs is perfectly joined with the heart throughout every part of the body. For this union to be complete, the heart itself follows the motion of the lungs: it lies within the "bosom" of the lungs, is attached to them by its upper chambers <note>original: "auricles"; now commonly called atria</note>, and rests on the diaphragm, which allows its arteries to share in the lungs' motion. Furthermore, the stomach is joined in a similar way through the connection between the esophagus <note>original: "œsophagus"</note> and the windpipe <note>original: "trachæa"</note>. These anatomical facts are adduced to show what kind of conjunction—
