409. XI. **That love or the will does nothing except in conjunction with wisdom or the understanding.**
Since love has no sensory or active life without the understanding, and since love introduces the understanding into all things of the mind (as was shown above in sections 407 and 408), it follows that love or the will does nothing except in conjunction with the understanding. After all, what is it to act from love without understanding? This can only be called irrational, for the understanding instructs what is to be done and how it is to be done. Love does not know this without the understanding; therefore, there is such a marriage between love and the understanding that, although they are two, they still act as one. There is a similar marriage between good and truth, for good belongs to love and truth belongs to the understanding. 

Such a marriage takes place in each and every thing in the universe created by the Lord; their use relates to good, and the form of that use relates to truth. It is because of this marriage that in every single part of the body there is a right and a left side. The right side relates to good, from which truth proceeds, and the left side relates to truth from good—and thus to their union <note>original: "conjunction"</note>. This is why there are pairs in the human body: there are two brains, two hemispheres of the brain, two ventricles of the heart, two lobes of the lungs, two eyes, two ears, two nostrils, two arms, hands, loins, feet, kidneys, testicles, etc. Even where there are not pairs, there is a right and a left side. All this is because good seeks truth so that it may exist, and truth seeks good so that it may be. It is the same in the angelic heavens and in every heavenly society. More on this subject may be seen above in section 401, where it is shown that love or the will cannot do anything in its human form without a marriage with wisdom or the understanding. The union of evil and the false, which is the opposite of the union of good and truth, shall be spoken of elsewhere.

410. XII. **That love or the will joins itself to wisdom or the understanding, and causes wisdom or the understanding to be reciprocally joined to it.** 
That love or the will joins itself to wisdom or the understanding is evident from their correspondence with the heart and lungs. Anatomy teaches that the heart experiences vital motion before the lungs do. Experience teaches this from cases of fainting <note>original: "swooning"</note> and of suffocated persons, as well as from the fetus in the womb and the chick in the egg. Anatomy also teaches that the heart, while it acts alone, forms the lungs and adapts them so that it may perform respiration within them. It also forms the other internal organs <note>original: "viscera"</note> and bodily structures so that it may perform various functions <note>original: "uses"</note> through them: the organs of the face so that it may have senses, the organs of motion so that it may act, and the rest of the body so that it may produce functions corresponding to the—

It highlights how the heart (will) initiates life and forms the lungs (understanding) to facilitate its functions.</summary>

<header>PART V. THE DIVINE LOVE.</header>

...affections of love. From this, it is evident that just as the heart produces these structures for the sake of the various functions it performs in the body, love does the same in its own vessel, which is called the will, for the sake of the various affections that make up its form. That its form is the human form was shown previously. 

Since the first and closest affections of love are the desire to know, the desire to understand, and the desire to see what it knows and understands, it follows that love creates the understanding for these purposes. Love actually enters into these affections when it begins to feel and act, and when it begins to think. It is clear from the correspondence between the heart and lungs (mentioned above) that the understanding contributes nothing to this process. 

From these points, we can see that love—or the will—joins itself to wisdom—or the understanding—rather than the other way around. We can also see that the knowledge acquired by the desire to know, the perception of truth acquired by the desire to understand, and the thought acquired by the desire to see what is known are not products of the understanding, but of love. Thoughts, perceptions, and the knowledge derived from them do indeed flow from the spiritual world; however, they are not received by the understanding, but by love according to its specific affections within the understanding. 

It seems as if the understanding receives them instead of the will, but this is an illusion. It also seems as if the understanding joins itself to the will, but this is also an illusion. Love, or the will, joins itself to the understanding and causes the understanding to be joined back to it in return. This mutual connection occurs because of love’s "marriage" with the understanding; it creates a reciprocal bond stemming from the life and power of love. 

The same may be said of the marriage of good and truth; for "good" belongs to love, and "truth" belongs to the understanding. Good performs all actions and welcomes truth into its "house," joining with truth as much as they agree. Good can even admit truths that do not agree, but it does so out of a desire to know, understand, and think about its own concerns—before it has committed itself to "uses" <note>original: "uses" — In this context, "uses" refers to productive, charitable actions that serve as the ultimate goal of spiritual life.</note>, which are its ultimate goals and are called its "goods." 

Therefore, the Lord views every person, spirit, and angel according to their love or "good," rather than their intellect or "truth" when separated from that love. As shown before, a person's life is their love. Their life is shaped by how they have elevated their affections through truths—that is, how they have perfected their affections through wisdom. The affections of love are elevated and perfected by truths, and therefore by wisdom. In these cases, love acts together with wisdom as if it were acting *from* wisdom. In reality, love acts from itself *through* wisdom as its form. This form derives nothing from the understanding itself, but everything from a specific purpose or affection of love.

<summary>This page argues that the will (love) is the primary driver of the human spirit, creating the understanding (wisdom) as a tool to fulfill its desires and purposes. It emphasizes that a person's spiritual quality is defined by their love and the "good" they do, rather than their intellectual knowledge alone.</summary>

411. Love calls everything that supports it its "goods," and it calls the means that lead to those goods its "truths." Because they are means, they are loved and used as instruments of its affection. In this way, they become affections in a specific form. Therefore, truth is nothing other than the form that the affection of love takes. The human form is nothing other than the form of all the affections of love. Beauty is the intelligence that love gains through truths, which it receives through either outer or inner sight, or through hearing. Love arranges these truths into the forms of its affections, and these forms are very diverse; however, they all share a likeness to their common form, which is the human form. All these forms appear beautiful and lovable to love, while others are the opposite. From this, it is also clear that love joins itself to the understanding, and not the other way around <note>original: "vice versa"</note>, and that their mutual connection also comes from love. This is what is meant when we say that love—or the will—causes wisdom—or the understanding—to be joined to it in return.

412. What has been said can be seen through a specific image and confirmed by the **correspondence** <term>In this context, "correspondence" refers to the spiritual law where physical organs and processes reflect and act in harmony with spiritual realities.</term> of the heart with love and the lungs with the understanding, which was discussed above. Since the heart corresponds to love, its pathways—the arteries and veins—correspond to affections; in the lungs, they correspond to the affections for truth. Since there are also other vessels in the lungs called air vessels, which carry out breathing, these vessels correspond to perceptions. It is important to remember that the arteries and veins in the lungs are not themselves affections, and breathing is not literally perception and thought; rather, they are correspondences, for they act in harmony or at the same time. Similarly, it should be noted that the heart and lungs are not literally love and the understanding, but are their correspondences. Because they are correspondences, one may be seen reflected in the other. 

Anyone who knows the anatomical structure of the lungs and compares them with the understanding can clearly see that the understanding does nothing by itself. It does not perceive or think on its own; instead, it does everything based on the affections of love. Within the understanding, these are called the affections for knowing, understanding, and seeing, which were discussed above. All states of the lungs depend on blood from the heart, the *vena cava* <note>original: "vena cava"; the large vein that carries blood from the body to the heart.</note>, and the *aorta* <note>original: "aorta"; the main artery that carries blood from the heart to the rest of the body.</note>. Respiration in the bronchial branches exists according to the state of the blood; for when the flow of blood stops, breathing stops. Much more could be discovered by comparing the structure of the lungs with the understanding to which they correspond, but since anatomy is known only to a few, and explaining something using an unknown subject makes it confusing, it is not best to say more about these matters. By studying the structure of the lungs, I was fully convinced that love joins itself to the understanding through its affections, and that the understanding does not join itself to any affection of love, but is reciprocally joined by love to

...itself, so that love may have a sensitive and active life. However, it is especially important to note that a person has a twofold respiration—one of the spirit and the other of the body. The respiration of the spirit depends on the fibers from the brains, while the respiration of the body depends on the blood vessels from the heart, the *vena cava* <note>The large vein carrying deoxygenated blood into the heart.</note>, and the aorta. Furthermore, it is evident that thought produces respiration, and also that the affection of love produces thought. Thought without affection would be like respiration without a heart, which is impossible. From this, it is clear that the affection of love joins itself to the thought of the understanding (as we said above) in the same way that the heart joins itself to the lungs.

413. XIII. **That wisdom or the understanding, by virtue of the power given it by love or the will, may be elevated to receive and perceive things that belong to the light of heaven.** It was shown in many places above that people can perceive the mysteries <note>original: "arcana"</note> of wisdom when they hear them. This faculty is the "rationality" which every person possesses by creation. It is this faculty—the ability to understand things deeply and to decide what is just, fair, good, and true—that distinguishes humans from animals. Therefore, this is what is meant when it is said that the understanding can be elevated, and can receive and perceive things that belong to the light of heaven. 

This process can also be seen in a physical image in the lungs, because the lungs correspond to the understanding. It can be seen in their cellular substance, which consists of the bronchi <note>original: "bronchiæ"</note> that continue down to the smallest follicles <note>Small sac-like structures, now known as alveoli, where gas exchange occurs.</note>, which receive the air during breathing. These are the physical substances that act as one with thoughts through **correspondence** <term>A spiritual law where physical organs reflect and act in harmony with spiritual realities.</term>. This follicular substance is designed so that it can expand and contract in two different states: one in coordination with the heart, and the other almost separate from the heart. 

In the state coordinated with the heart, the lungs expand and contract through the pulmonary arteries and veins, which come only from the heart. In the state that is almost separate from the heart, they function through the bronchial arteries and veins, which come from the *vena cava* and aorta; these last vessels are outside the heart itself. This occurs in the physical lungs because the understanding can be elevated above a person's own self-love (which corresponds to the heart) and receive light from heaven. 

Yet, even when the understanding is elevated above a person’s own love, it does not fully break away from it. Instead, it draws from that love what is called the "affection of knowing and understanding," usually for the sake of some honor, glory, or profit in the world. This motivation clings to every love like a surface layer; because of this, the love appears bright at the surface, though with the wise, it is translucent. These observations about the lungs are provided to prove that the understanding can be elevated to receive and perceive the light of heaven, for there is a complete correspondence. Through this correspondence, the inner workings of the understanding can be seen by looking at the lungs.

414. XIV. **That love (the will) can be elevated in the same way, perceiving things that belong to the heat of heaven, if it loves its spouse <note>In Swedenborg’s thought, the Will and the Understanding are seen as a married couple; love is the "husband" or "wife" to wisdom.</note> to that degree.** It was shown in the preceding article and in many places above that the understanding may be elevated into the light of heaven to absorb wisdom. It was also shown that love (the will) can be equally elevated if it loves things belonging to the light of heaven, or wisdom. However, love—or the will—cannot be elevated by any desire for honor, glory, or financial gain as its end goal. It can only be elevated by the love of use <note>original: "love of use"; meaning the desire to be of service or perform a useful function for its own sake.</note>—not for the sake of oneself, but for the sake of one's neighbor. Since this love is given only from heaven by the Lord, and only when people shun evils as sins, love or the will can be elevated by these means, but not without them. 

Love (the will) is elevated into the heat of heaven, while the understanding is elevated into the light of heaven. If both are elevated, a marriage is formed there, which is called the "heavenly marriage" because it consists of heavenly love and wisdom. Therefore, it is said that love is also elevated if it loves wisdom, its partner, to that degree. Love toward the neighbor, granted by the Lord, is the love of wisdom, or the genuine love of the human understanding. This is similar to the relationship between light and heat in the physical world. Light exists both without heat and with heat: it exists without heat in winter, and with heat in summer. When there is heat with light, then all things flourish. The light in a person that corresponds to winter light is wisdom without its love; the light in a person that corresponds to summer light is wisdom with its love.

415. This union and separation of wisdom and love can be seen reflected in the connection between the lungs and the heart. The heart, through the blood it sends out, may be joined to the branches of the bronchial vesicles <note>The small air sacs in the lungs where oxygen enters the blood.</note>. It can also be joined by blood not sent out by itself, but through the *vena cava* <note>The large vein carrying blood into the heart.</note> and the aorta <note>The main artery carrying blood away from the heart.</note>. Thus, the respiration of the body may be separated from the respiration of the spirit; but when only the blood from the heart acts, then the respirations cannot be separated. 

Now, because the thoughts are one with the respirations through correspondence, the twofold state of the lungs regarding breathing clearly proves that a person may think one thing and yet speak and act differently in the company of others. When a person is in company, they act based on their thoughts while fearing a loss of reputation. In private, however, when they are not afraid of such loss, they may think, speak, and act quite differently. In those private moments, they may think and speak against God, their neighbor, the spiritual matters of the church, and moral or civil laws. They may even act against them by stealing, seeking revenge, blaspheming, and committing adultery. But in public, where they fear losing their reputation, they may speak, preach, and act exactly like a spiritual, moral, and civil person. From this, it is evident that love or the will, like the...

Swedenborg uses the dual functions of the heart and lungs to explain how a person can maintain a public persona of morality while harboring private thoughts of a different nature.</summary>

...understanding, can be elevated and receive things belonging to the heat or the love of heaven, if it loves wisdom to that degree; and if it does not love wisdom, the understanding may, as it were, be separated from it.

416. XV. **That otherwise, love (the will) pulls wisdom (the understanding) down from its elevation so that they act as one.** There is natural love and there is spiritual love. He who possesses both natural and spiritual love is truly rational. But he who possesses only natural love may be able to think rationally, just like a spiritual person, yet he is still not truly rational. Although he lifts his understanding up to the light of heaven—and consequently to wisdom—the things of wisdom (the light of heaven) are not part of his love. His love performs this elevation, but only out of a desire for honor, glory, and personal gain. 

When, however, he realizes that he does not receive any such rewards from that elevation—which happens when he thinks to himself from his natural love alone—he then fails to love the things belonging to the light of heaven or wisdom. For this reason, he then pulls his understanding down from its elevation so that it acts as one with himself. 

For example: when the understanding is elevated into wisdom, the love sees the true nature of justice, sincerity, chastity, and genuine love. The natural love can see these through its ability to understand and view things in the light of heaven; it can even speak about, preach, and describe them as both moral and spiritual virtues. But when the understanding is not elevated, the love—if it is merely natural—does not see these virtues. Instead, it sees injustice in place of justice, fraud in place of sincerity, and lust <note>original: "lasciviousness"</note> in place of chastity, and so forth. If he then thinks about the things he spoke of while his understanding was elevated, he might laugh at them, believing they only serve as tools to deceive others. This shows how it is to be understood that love, unless it loves wisdom its spouse <note>original: "consort"; Swedenborg describes the Will and Understanding as a married pair.</note> to that degree, pulls her down from her elevation to act as one with itself. That love can be elevated if it loves wisdom to that degree can be seen above in section 414.

417. Since love corresponds to the heart and the understanding to the lungs, what has been said above can be proven through their correspondence <note>The spiritual symbolic link between physical organs and spiritual functions.</note>. Specifically, it proves how the understanding can be lifted to a wisdom above a person's own love, and how the understanding is pulled down from that elevation by that love if it is merely natural. 

A person has a twofold respiration <note>Breathing.</note>: one of the body and the other of the spirit. These two respirations can be either separated or joined together. In merely natural people, especially in hypocrites, they are separated, but in spiritual and sincere people, this is rarely the case. Therefore, the merely natural person and the hypocrite—whose understanding is elevated and whose memory contains many things belonging to wisdom—can speak wisely in the company of others based on thoughts drawn from memory. However, when he is not in company, he does not think from the memory, but from his spirit, and consequently from his love. He acts in the same manner...

418. It is a common opinion that wisdom is what makes a person who they are; thus, when people hear someone speak and teach wisely, they believe that person is wise. Indeed, the speaker thinks so of himself at that time. When he speaks and teaches in public, he thinks from his memory; if he is merely worldly <term>natural: focused only on the physical world rather than spiritual reality</term>, he thinks from the surface level of his love, which is the desire for honor, glory, or profit. But when he is alone, he thinks from the deeper love of his spirit, and then he sometimes thinks not wisely, but insanely. From this, it is clear that no one should be judged by the wisdom of their speech, but by their life—that is, not by wise speech separated from life, but by wisdom joined to life. By "life," we mean love. It was shown above that love is life.

419. XVI. **That love (the will) is purified by wisdom in the understanding, if they are raised up together.** From birth, a person loves nothing but themselves and the world; nothing else appears before their eyes, and therefore they think of nothing else. This love is physically-oriented <note>original: "corporeal-natural"</note> and may be called material; furthermore, it has become impure because heavenly love was separated from it in our ancestors. This love cannot be cleansed of its impurity unless a person has the ability to lift their understanding into the light of heaven to see how they ought to live. By doing so, their love may be raised up along with their understanding into wisdom. 

Through the understanding, the love—that is, the person—sees those evils that pollute and defile it. The person also sees that if they avoid and turn away from those evils as sins, they will love the things opposite to them, which are all heavenly. They also see the methods by which those evils may be avoided and turned away from as sins. The person sees this by using their ability to lift the understanding into the light of heaven, which is the source of wisdom. 

In this case, to the extent that the love puts heaven in the first place and the world in the second—and to the extent that it puts the Lord in the first place and self in the second—the love is purged of its uncleanness and purified. That is, it is raised into the warmth of heaven and joined to the light of heaven in which the understanding resides. A "marriage" is then achieved: the marriage of good and truth, which is the union of love and wisdom. 

Anyone can grasp this in their understanding and see rationally that to the extent they avoid and turn away from theft and fraud, they love sincerity, uprightness, and justice. Also, to the extent they avoid revenge and hatred, they love their neighbor. To the extent they avoid adultery, they love chastity, and so on. Indeed, hardly anyone knows what there is of heaven...

<summary>This page explains that true wisdom is measured by how a person lives and loves, rather than how they speak. It also describes how the understanding can lead the will to be purified by identifying sins and choosing heavenly virtues like justice and sincerity.</summary>
<keywords>will, understanding, purification, wisdom, love, respiration, sincerity, justice, marriage of good and truth</keywords>

<sig>165</sig>

...and love of the Lord in sincerity, integrity, justice, love toward the neighbor, chastity, and all the other affections <note>original: "affections" — in this context, specific spiritual desires or emotional states</note> of heavenly love, before he has removed their opposites. When he has removed those opposites, then he truly lives within those heavenly affections and can recognize and see them from within. 

In the meantime, a kind of veil is placed in between. This veil does allow the light of heaven to reach his love, but because he does not yet love wisdom (his "spouse") to that same degree, he does not fully receive it. Indeed, perhaps he even reprimands and scolds her when she returns from her elevated state. He is only satisfied by this: *that the wisdom of his understanding may serve his pursuit of honor, glory, or profit as a tool.* In this state, he puts himself and the world in the first place, and the Lord and heaven in the second place. Anything put in the second place is loved only as long as it is useful; if it is no longer useful, it is renounced and rejected—certainly after death, if not before. Thus, this truth is clear: the love (or the will) is purified in the understanding, provided they are both raised up together.

420. This same process is mirrored in the lungs. There, the arteries and veins correspond to the affections of love, and the acts of breathing correspond to the perceptions and thoughts of the understanding, as we mentioned earlier. *It is evident from much experience that the blood of the heart purifies itself of waste <note>original: "crude matters"</note> in the lungs and also nourishes itself with suitable substances from the air that is breathed in.* 

The fact *that the blood purifies itself of waste in the lungs* is clear not only from the incoming blood—which is venous and therefore full of the chyle <term>chyle: a milky fluid containing fats and lymph, taken up from the intestines during digestion</term> collected from food and drink—but also from the moisture and smell of the air we exhale. It is also shown by the reduced amount of blood that returns into the *left ventricle* of the heart. 

*That the blood nourishes itself with suitable substances from the inhaled air* is evident from the massive amount of scents and vapors continually rising from shrubberies, gardens, and forests. It is also clear from the huge quantity of various salts rising with water from the land, rivers, and lakes, and from the vast amount of human and animal exhalations and vapors with which the air is filled. It cannot be denied that these enter the lungs with the air. Since this is the case, it also cannot be denied that the blood attracts from the air whatever is useful to it; these useful things are those that correspond to the affections of its love. 

Consequently, in the air-cells or the innermost parts of the lungs, there are countless tiny veins with small openings that absorb these substances. Because of this, the blood returned to the left ventricle of the heart is transformed into bright red <note>original: "florid"</note> arterial blood. These facts prove that the blood purifies itself of dissimilar <note>original: "heterogeneous"</note> things and nourishes itself with harmonious <note>original: "homogeneous"</note> ones. While it is not yet known on earth that the blood in the lungs purifies and nourishes itself in a way that corresponds to the mind’s affections, this is very well known in the spiritual world. For the angels in the heavens are...

...delighted only by fragrances that correspond to the love of their wisdom; whereas the spirits in hell are delighted only by odors that correspond to some love that opposes wisdom. These latter odors are foul-smelling, while the former are fragrant. It follows, therefore, that people in the world saturate their blood with similar things based on their correspondence <note>original: "correspondence" — the spiritual connection between physical things and internal states</note> with the desires <note>original: "affections"</note> of their love. For what a person's spirit loves, their blood craves and draws in through breathing, according to that correspondence. From this, it follows that a person is purified in their love if they love wisdom, and they are defiled if they do not love her. All of a person’s purification is achieved through the truths of wisdom, and all of their defilement is caused by the falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note> that are opposed to them.

421. XVII. ***That love or the will is defiled in the understanding, and by it, if they are not elevated together:*** because, if love is not elevated, it remains impure, as we said above in sections 419 and 420. When it remains impure, it loves impure things, such as revenge, hatred, fraud, blasphemy, and adultery. These then become its desires <note>original: "affections"</note>, which are lusts, and it rejects the qualities of charity, justice, sincerity, truth, and chastity. We say that love is defiled *in* the understanding and *by* it; it is defiled *in* the understanding when love is influenced by those impure things, and *by* the understanding when love forces the things of wisdom to serve its own ends—and even more so when it perverts, falsifies, and corrupts them. Regarding the state of the heart, or of its blood in the lungs, which corresponds to these things, there is no need to say more than has been said above in section 420; only that instead of the purification of the blood, its defilement occurs; and instead of the nourishment of the blood by fragrant scents, it is nourished by stenches, just as it is in heaven and in hell.

422. XVIII. ***That love purified by wisdom in the understanding becomes spiritual and heavenly <note>original: "celestial"</note>.*** A person is born at a basic natural level <note>original: "born natural" — referring to the initial state of human consciousness focused on the world and self</note>, but in proportion as their understanding is raised into the light of heaven, and their love is simultaneously raised into the heat of heaven, they become spiritual and heavenly. In this state, the person becomes like the Garden of Eden, which exists in both springtime light and springtime heat. It is not the understanding that is made spiritual and heavenly, but the love; and when the love is transformed, it also makes its "spouse"—the understanding—spiritual and heavenly. The love is made so by living according to the truths of wisdom that the understanding teaches and demonstrates. The love absorbs these through its understanding and not from itself; for the love cannot raise itself unless it knows truths, and it cannot know them except by an elevated and enlightened understanding. It is then raised in proportion to how much it loves truths by putting them into practice. It is one thing to understand and another to will; or one thing to say and another to do. There are some who understand and speak the truths of wisdom, but neither will nor do them. When the love performs the truths of light that it understands and speaks, then it is raised. A man...

It emphasizes that love only becomes spiritual and heavenly when it acts upon the truths provided by the understanding, rather than just acknowledging them.</summary>

A person may see this from his reason alone; for who is he that understands and speaks the truths of wisdom while he lives contrary to them—that is, while he wills and acts against them? Love purified by wisdom becomes spiritual and celestial because a man has three degrees of life: natural, spiritual, and celestial (as discussed in Part III). A person is capable of being elevated from one degree to another, but he is not elevated by wisdom alone, but by a life according to wisdom. For a person’s life is his love; therefore, as far as he lives according to wisdom, so far he loves it. He lives according to wisdom in proportion as he purifies himself from uncleannesses, which are sins; and he loves wisdom in proportion as he does this.

423. The fact that love purified by wisdom in the understanding becomes spiritual and celestial cannot be as easily seen through its correspondence <note>original: "correspondence" — the spiritual connection between physical things and internal states</note> with the heart and lungs. This is because no one can see the quality of the blood by which the lungs are kept in their state of breathing <note>original: "respiration"</note>. The blood may be full of impurities and yet not be distinguishable from pure blood; likewise, the breathing of a merely natural person appears similar to the breathing of a spiritual person. 

But the difference is well understood in heaven, for everyone there breathes according to the "marriage" <note>original: "marriage" — meaning a perfect union or integration</note> of love and wisdom. Because the angels are known by that union, they are also known by their breathing. Consequently, when anyone who is not in that union comes into heaven, they experience anguish in the chest and gasp for breath like someone in the agonies of death. Therefore, such persons throw themselves headlong down and never rest until they are with those who have a similar way of breathing; for then, by correspondence, they are in a similar affection <note>original: "affection" — a person's inner desire or emotional state</note>, and thus in similar thought. 

From this, it appears that in a spiritual person, the purer blood (which some call the "animal spirit" <note>original: "animal spirit" — a historical medical term for a subtle fluid believed to carry life and thought through the nerves</note>) is purified; and it is purified in proportion as the person is in the union of love and wisdom. This purer blood corresponds directly to that union; and as it flows into the blood of the physical body, it follows that the physical blood is also purified by it. The opposite happens with those whose love is defiled in the understanding. But, as we said, no one can examine this by any experiment on the blood, but only by the affections of love, because these correspond to the blood.

424. XIX. ***That love defiled in and by the understanding becomes natural, sensual, and corporeal.*** Natural love, when separated from spiritual love, is the opposite of spiritual love. This is because natural love is the love of self and the world, while spiritual love is the love of the Lord and the neighbor. The love of self and the world looks downward and outward, while the love of the Lord looks upward and inward. Therefore, when natural love is separated from spiritual love, it cannot be raised out of a person’s <term>proprium</term> <note>original: "proprium" — a Latin term used by Swedenborg to refer to the "ego" or the self-centered nature of a human being</note> but remains immersed in it. As much as the person loves this selfhood, they are glued to it. Then, even if the understanding ascends and sees the truths of wisdom by the light of heaven, the natural love pulls it...

<page-num>424, 425</page-num>
<header>ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART V.</header>

...down and joins it to itself in its own selfhood <term>original: "proprium" — Swedenborg’s term for a person's sense of self or ego, which can be either hellish or heavenly</term>. There, it either rejects the truths of wisdom, or distorts them, or wraps itself in them simply to speak them for the sake of reputation. Just as natural love can rise by degrees and become spiritual and heavenly <note>original: "celestial"</note>, it can also descend by degrees and become sensual and bodily <note>original: "corporeal"</note>. It descends in proportion as it loves to have power <note>original: "dominion"</note> from no love for being helpful <note>original: "use"</note>, but solely from the love of self; this love is what is called the devil. 

Those who are in this love can speak and act like those who are in spiritual love; however, they speak and act either from memory or from an understanding that has been elevated on its own into the light of heaven. Nevertheless, the things they say and do are like fruit that looks beautiful on the outside but is rotten within, or like almonds that appear sound but are worm-eaten inside. In the spiritual world, these appearances are called fantasies <note>original: "phantasies"</note>. Through these, harlots—who are called sirens <note>original: "syrens"</note> there—make themselves look beautiful and dress in elegant clothes; yet when the fantasy is removed, they appear as ghosts <note>original: "spectres"</note>. These are also like devils who disguise themselves as angels of light. 

When that bodily love pulls down its understanding—which happens when people are alone and thinking from their love—they think against God in favor of nature, against heaven in favor of the world, and against the goodness and truths of the church in favor of the evils and falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note> of hell. Thus, they think contrary to wisdom. This shows the nature of bodily <note>original: "corporeal"</note> people: they are not bodily in their understanding, but in their love. That is, they are not bodily in their understanding when they speak in the company of others, but when they speak with themselves in spirit. Because they are such in spirit, after death they become what are called bodily spirits in both their love and understanding. At that point, those who in the world had an extreme love of ruling for the sake of self, while also having a high level of intellectual development, appear like Egyptian mummies in their bodies and stupid and idiotic in their minds. 

Who in the world today knows that this love is actually like this? Nevertheless, there does exist a love of ruling based on a love of service <note>original: "use"</note>—a love of service not for one's own sake, but for the sake of the common good. People can hardly tell one from the other, but there is still a difference between them as great as the difference between heaven and hell. The differences between these two types of power-seeking may be seen in the work **ON HEAVEN AND HELL**, numbers 531 to 565.

### 425. XX. *That the capacity for understanding, called rationality, and the capacity for acting, called liberty, still remain.*

These two capacities <note>original: "faculties"</note> of a person were discussed above in sections 264 to 267. A person has these two capacities so that they may change from natural to spiritual—that is, be reborn <note>original: "regenerated"</note>. As we said before, it is a person's love that becomes spiritual and is reborn, and it cannot...

<summary>Swedenborg describes how self-centered love corrupts the understanding and leads to a "corporeal" spiritual state, while emphasizing that humans retain the core capacities of rationality and liberty necessary for spiritual transformation.</summary>

--- Page 180 ---
<meta>This page continues the explanation of how the human mind functions, specifically focusing on the two faculties of "rationality" (the ability to understand truth) and "liberty" (the freedom to act on that understanding) as the essential tools for spiritual growth.</meta>

<page-num>425, 426</page-num>
<header>PART V. THE DIVINE LOVE.</header>

...become spiritual, or be reborn <note>original: "regenerated"</note>, unless by its understanding it knows what is evil and what is good, and from that knowledge, what is true and what is false. When the mind knows these things, it may choose one or the other. If it chooses what is good, it can be informed by its understanding about the means by which it may reach that good. All the means by which people may reach what is good are provided. 

To know and understand these means is the role of **rationality**, and to will and do them is the role of **liberty**. Liberty also includes the will to know, understand, and think about these means. Those who believe—based on church doctrine—that spiritual or theological matters transcend human understanding and must therefore be believed without being understood, know nothing of these faculties of rationality and liberty. Such persons cannot help but deny the faculty of rationality. 

Furthermore, those who believe—again, based on church doctrine—that no one can do good by themselves, and therefore that good should not be done from one's own will for the sake of salvation, cannot help but religiously deny both of these human faculties. Consequently, those who have confirmed themselves in these persuasions are deprived of both after death, in accordance with their belief. Instead of the heavenly liberty they might have enjoyed, they are in "infernal liberty" <note>a state of being enslaved to one's own harmful impulses while perceiving it as freedom</note>. Instead of the angelic wisdom they might have possessed through rationality, they are in hellish insanity. 

What is remarkable is that these individuals acknowledge that both these faculties exist when it comes to doing evil and thinking false things. They do not realize that the liberty to do evil is actually slavery, and that the "rationality" of thinking false things is irrational. 

It must be kept in mind, however, that both liberty and rationality do not belong to man, but are the Lord’s within man. They cannot be claimed by a person as their own property, nor can they be given to a person to own independently; they are continually the Lord’s presence within him, and yet they are never taken away. Without them, a person cannot be saved, because without them he cannot be reborn, as was said above. For this reason, a person is taught by the church that he cannot think truth or do good from himself. 

But since a person does not perceive it any other way than that he *does* think truth and do good from himself, it is obvious that he ought to believe that he thinks truth **as if** from himself, and does good **as if** from himself. If he does not believe this, then he either fails to think truth and do good at all—and thus has no religion—or he thinks truth and does good from himself, and in that case, he attributes to himself what is actually Divine. That a person ought to think truth and do good as if from himself can be seen in the **DOCTRINE OF LIFE FOR THE NEW JERUSALEM**, from beginning to end.

426. XXI. *That spiritual and celestial love is love toward the neighbor and love to the Lord; and that natural and sensual love is love of the world and love of self.* By "love toward the neighbor," we mean the love of being useful <note>original: "uses" — Swedenborg's term for performing beneficial functions or services for others</note>, and by "love to the Lord," we mean the love of performing those useful services, as was shown before. These loves...

It also establishes that while these powers come from God, humans must exercise them "as if" they were their own to progress spiritually.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the relationship between human faculties (rationality and liberty) and the performance of "uses," or service to others, as a path to spiritual growth.</meta>

426. ...are spiritual and celestial, because to love <term>uses</term> <note>original: "uses" — In Swedenborg's writing, "uses" are useful services or good deeds performed for the benefit of others, society, or the Lord's kingdom.</note>, and to do them out of love for them, is separate from the love of a person’s <term>proprium</term> <note>original: "proprium" — A Latin term meaning "what is one's own." It refers to the human ego or selfhood, which naturally feels independent of God.</note>. He who spiritually loves uses has no regard for himself, but for others outside of himself, whose well-being affects him. The loves opposed to these are the love of self and the love of the world, for these have no regard for use for the sake of others, but only for the sake of self. Those who do this invert the divine order; they put themselves in the place of the Lord and the world in the place of heaven. Consequently, they look away from the Lord and from heaven, and to look away from them is to look toward hell. More concerning these loves may be seen above in section 424. 

A person, however, does not feel and perceive the love of doing uses for their own sake as clearly as he feels the love of doing uses for the sake of self. Because of this, he does not know, when he performs uses, whether he does them for the sake of use or for the sake of himself. But let him know that he performs uses for the sake of use as far as he avoids evils; for as far as he avoids evils, he performs uses not from himself, but from the Lord. Evil and good are opposites; therefore, to the degree that anyone is not in evil, he is in good. No one can be in evil and good at the same time, because no one can "serve two masters at the same time." 

These observations are intended to show that although a person does not consciously perceive whether the uses he performs are for the sake of use or for the sake of self—that is, whether they are spiritual or merely natural uses—he may still know it by this: whether he thinks evils are sins or not. If he thinks they are sins and therefore does not do them, then the uses he performs are spiritual. As he turns away from sins with aversion, he also begins to consciously feel the love of uses for the sake of use, and this comes from a spiritual delight in them.

427. XXII. **That it is the same with charity and faith and their conjunction, as with the will and understanding and their conjunction.** 

The heavens are distinguished according to two loves: celestial love and spiritual love. Celestial love is love for the Lord, and spiritual love is love toward the neighbor. These loves are distinguished by this: celestial love is the love of good, and spiritual love is the love of truth. This is because those who are in celestial love perform uses from a love of good, and those who are in spiritual love perform uses from a love of truth. 

The marriage of celestial love is with wisdom, and the marriage of spiritual love is with intelligence. For it is the nature of wisdom to do good from a foundation of good, and it is the nature of intelligence to do good from a foundation of truth; therefore, celestial love produces good, and spiritual love produces truth. The difference between these two loves can only be described in this way: those who are in celestial love have wisdom inscribed on their lives and not just on their memories; therefore, they do not talk about divine truths but simply do them. However, those who are in spiritual love have wisdom inscribed on their memories; therefore, they talk about divine truths and do them based on principles stored in the memory. Since those who are...

It also defines the difference between celestial love (acting from a love of good) and spiritual love (acting from a love of truth).</summary>

<header>PART V. THE DIVINE LOVE. 427—429</header>
<page-num>171</page-num>

427. Those in celestial <term>love</term> <note>original: "celestial love" — In Swedenborg's theology, the highest form of love, which is love for the Lord.</note> have wisdom written upon their lives; therefore, they immediately perceive whether what they hear is truth or not. When they are asked if something is truth, they only answer that it is, or that it is not. These are the people meant by the Lord's words, "Let your communication be *Yes, yes; No, no*" (Matthew 5:37). Because they are like this, they will not listen to anything regarding "faith," saying, *What is faith? Is it not wisdom? And what is charity? Is it not doing?* When they are told that faith consists in believing what is not understood, they turn away, saying, *This person is out of his senses.* These people are in the third heaven and are the wisest of all. In this world, people become like this by immediately applying the divine things they hear to their lives, turning away from evils as hellish <note>original: "infernal" — Having the qualities of or relating to hell.</note>, and worshiping the Lord alone. Because these people are in a state of innocence, they appear to others like infants; and since they do not argue about the truths of wisdom, and there is no pride in their speech, they also appear simple. Nevertheless, when they hear anyone speak, they perceive everything about that person's love from the sound of their voice, and everything about their intelligence from their speech. These are the people in the marriage of love and wisdom from the Lord, and who represent the cardiac <note>original: "cardiac" — Relating to the heart. Swedenborg describes heaven as a "Grand Man" where different groups of angels correspond to different organs; the 'cardiac' angels correspond to the heart and represent the highest love.</note> of heaven mentioned above.

428. But those who are in spiritual love, or love toward the neighbor, do not have wisdom written on their lives, but they do have intelligence. It is the role of wisdom to do good from an affection for what is good, but the role of intelligence to do good from an affection for what is truth, as we said above. These people also do not know what faith is: if it is named, they understand it as truth, and if charity is named, they understand it as doing truth. When they are told they must "believe," they say it is a meaningless way of speaking, and add, *Who does not believe truth?* They add this because they see truth in the light of their heaven; to believe what they do not see, they call either simplicity or foolishness. These constitute the pulmonic <note>original: "pulmonic" — Relating to the lungs. These angels correspond to the respiratory system, representing the intellectual or spiritual side of heaven.</note> of heaven, mentioned above.

429. But those who are in spiritual-natural love have neither wisdom nor intelligence written on their lives, but they have something of faith from the Word, so far as this is joined with charity. Not knowing what charity is, or whether faith is truth, they cannot be among those in the heavens who are in wisdom and intelligence, but only among those who are in knowledge. However, those who have avoided evils as sins are in the lowest <note>original: "ultimate heaven" — The third and lowest level of the angelic heavens.</note> heaven, in a light similar to that of the moon by night. But those who have not stubbornly believed in things they do not know, and at the same time have felt some affection for truth, are instructed by the angels. According to their reception of truths and the conformity of their life to them, they are raised to the societies of those who are in spiritual love and the resulting intelligence; these become spiritual, while the rest remain spiritual-natural. Those who have lived in faith separate from charity are removed and sent away into deserts, because they are not at all...

<header>429—432 ANGELIC WISDOM CONCERNING PART V.</header>

in goodness, and consequently not at all in the marriage of goodness and truth, unlike all those who are in the heavens.

430. Everything that has been said in this Part concerning love and wisdom may also be said of charity and faith, if we substitute "spiritual love" for "charity," and "truth" (from which intelligence is derived) for "faith." It is the same whether you say "the will and understanding" or "love and intelligence," since the will is the receptacle <note>A container or vessel meant to receive something.</note> of love, and the understanding is the receptacle of intelligence.

431. To what has been said, I will add this memorable fact. In heaven, all who perform <term>useful services</term> <note>original: "uses" — In Swedenborg’s writing, a "use" is any work or service performed for the benefit of others or society, motivated by love.</note> out of a love for being useful, receive from the community they belong to a state of being wiser and happier than others. To perform useful services there means to act with sincerity, integrity, justice, and faithfulness in the work of one’s calling. This they call charity; they call acts of divine worship "signs of charity," and other things "obligations and acts of kindness." They say that when anyone performs their particular duty with sincerity, integrity, justice, and faithfulness, the community thrives and continues in well-being. They also say that this is what it means to be "in the Lord," because everything that flows from the Lord is a useful service; it flows from the individual parts into the community, and from the community back to the parts. The "parts" there are the angels, and the "community" is their society.

432. **THE NATURE OF A PERSON'S BEGINNING AT CONCEPTION.** No one can know the nature of the <term>initial form</term> <note>original: "initiament" — The very first beginning or primitive state of an organism.</note> or embryonic start of a person in the womb after conception, because it cannot be seen. Furthermore, it is made of a spiritual substance which natural light cannot make visible. Now, because there are some people in the world who focus their minds on investigating the beginning of man, or the father's seed by which conception is achieved; and since many of them have fallen into the error of thinking that a person is fully formed from the very first moment—simply growing larger to reach perfection—the nature of that beginning or "first stage" in its actual form has been revealed to me. This was revealed to me by the angels, to whom it was shown by the Lord. Because the angels have made this a part of their wisdom, and because the delight of their wisdom is to share what they know with others, they were permitted to represent the initial form of a person in a visual model before my eyes in the light of heaven.

It was as follows: I saw what looked like a most minute image of a brain with a delicate outline of something like a face in front, without any limbs or body attached. This initial form, in its upper protruding part, was a structure of touching globules or <term>tiny spheres</term> <note>original: "spherules"</note>. Each tiny sphere was composed of others still more minute, and each of these in the same way was made of the most minute of all; thus, it consisted of three degrees <note>In Swedenborg's system, "degrees" refer to distinct levels of existence or complexity.</note>. In front, in the flat section, something appeared outlined for a face. The rounded part was covered with a very fine, transparent membrane or <term>meninx</term> <note>original: "meninx" — A delicate membrane, like those that envelope the brain.</note>. The protruding part, which was a miniature model of the brain, was—

<summary>Swedenborg explains that charity is defined by performing one's daily work with integrity and describes a vision of the initial spiritual form of a human embryo as a microscopic brain structure.</summary>
<keywords>charity, useful services (uses), community, conception, embryo, spiritual substance, brain structure, degrees, angels</keywords>

--- Page 184 ---
<meta>This page concludes the final part of the work, detailing a vision of the human embryo's brain development. It explains how the brain's structure is designed to receive divine love and wisdom, while noting the presence of inherited corruption in its outer layers.</meta>

<page-num>432</page-num>
<header>PART V. THE DIVINE LOVE.</header>

[The primitive brain was] also divided into two chambers, as it were, just as the full-grown brain is divided into two hemispheres. I was told that the right chamber was the receptacle of love, and the left was the receptacle of wisdom, and that through wonderful interweaving, they were like partners and companions. Moreover, it was shown in the light of heaven, which shone upon it, that internally, the <term>compages</term> <note>original: "compages" — the complex structure or framework of an organ or body.</note> of this little brain—regarding its position and <term>fluxion</term> <note>original: "fluxion" — the pattern of movement or flow.</note>—was in the order and form of heaven, and that its exterior structure was in opposition to that order and form. 

After these things were shown and seen, the angels said that the two interior levels, which were in the order and form of heaven, were receptacles of love and wisdom from the Lord. They said that the exterior level, which was in opposition to the order and form of heaven, was the receptacle of hellish <note>original: "infernal"</note> love and insanity. This is because a person, through inherited corruption <note>original: "hereditary degeneracy"</note>, is born into evils of all kinds, and these evils reside in the outermost parts there. This corruption is not removed unless the higher levels are opened, which, as was said, are the receptacles of love and wisdom from the Lord. And since love and wisdom are the very essence of humanity—for love and wisdom in their essence are the Lord—and since this initial form <note>original: "primitive" — the first biological beginning or embryo of a human being.</note> of a person is a receptacle, it follows that in this initial form there is a constant impulse toward the human form, which it gradually assumes.

->THE END <note>original: "FINIS"</note><-

173

<summary>This concluding page describes a vision of the embryonic human brain, explaining how its internal structure is designed for divine love and wisdom while its external parts harbor inherited evil. It asserts that the human form develops from an inherent impulse to receive the Divine.</summary>

**BRAIN.** Its organization, 366, 373, 432. Injury <note>original: "Lesion"</note> of the brain, 365. The two brains [the cerebrum and cerebellum] continue from the head into the spine, 366. **CAUSE.** There is no single cause without an end <note>A goal or purpose</note> from which it starts, and an effect in which it exists, 167. In the secondary tool <note>original: "instrumental cause"</note>, the primary cause is perceived only as being one with it, 4. Nothing about a cause can truly be known without a knowledge of both kinds of degrees <note>Vertical/discrete and horizontal/continuous degrees</note>, 188. All causes exist in the spiritual world, 119. In causes, nothing is essential except the end <note>purpose</note>, 197. Causes produce effects, not by a smooth transition <note>continuity</note>, but in distinct stages <note>discretely</note>, 185. Causes explain effects, 119. To know effects from causes is to be wise; on the contrary, to investigate causes starting only from effects is not to be wise, 119. Causes may be seen through reason, it is true, but not clearly, except by looking at their effects, 375. See *End and Effect*.

**CELLULAR SUBSTANCE OF THE LUNGS,** what it consists of, 413.

**CHANGES** of state cannot exist without a substantial form <note>A physical or spiritual structure</note> which is the subject of them; just as sight cannot exist without the eye, 273.

**CHARITY** is every duty of a person's office that he performs from the Lord, 253. It belongs to the affection, 214. Charity and faith are the essentials of the Church, 253. They are actual substance and form, not abstract ideas; they do not exist outside of their subjects (which are substances), but they are the states of those subjects, 42, 209. Charity, faith, and good works are

arranged according to discrete degrees <note>Distinct levels of reality</note>, 214. Charity, among the angels, consists in acting with sincerity, uprightness, justice, and faithfulness in the duties of their office, 431.

In the Word <note>The Bible</note>, times of the day and seasons of the year signify the states of the Church, 73.

**CIVIL.** All things that are called "civil" [such as laws and social order] are substances and not abstract ideas; they do not exist outside of their subjects (which are substances), but are the states of those subjects, 209.

**CLOUDS.** By "clouds" in the Word <note>The Bible</note>, spiritual clouds are meant, which are thoughts, 147. In the spiritual world, thoughts based on truth appear as white clouds, and thoughts based on evil appear as black clouds, 147.

**COLORS.** There are colors of all kinds in the spiritual world, 380. Red and white are the fundamental colors, and all others derive their varieties from these and their opposites, which are a dark fiery color and black, 380. See also 348.

**COMMUNICATION** between the three heavens is achieved through correspondences <note>The relationship between spiritual causes and natural effects</note>, 202. In the same way, communication between the natural and spiritual man occurs, 90, 252. Communication by correspondences is not felt, 238. In the understanding, it is perceived only as truths being seen in light; and in the will, it is perceived only as useful actions being performed from affection, 252.

**COMPOSITES.** All composite things consist of degrees of height, or discrete degrees, 184, 190.

**CONCEPTION** of a man from his father is not a conception of life, 6.

**CONCLUSION** is something derived from love and wisdom, 363.

**CONFIRM,** to. The natural man may confirm <note>Provide evidence or arguments for</note> anything he wishes, 267. Evils and falsehoods of all kinds can be confirmed, 267. When they are confirmed by a person, they remain and become part of his love and his life, 268.

**CONFIRMATIONS** in favor of the Divine through the wonders of nature, 351—356. Everyone should guard against confirmations in favor of nature [as the cause of all things], 357. Confirmations of evil and falsehood close heaven to a person, 268.

**CONJUNCTION** <note>Spiritual union or connection</note>. In order for there to be conjunction, there must be reciprocity, 48, 115, 410. Conjunction of the Lord with an angel, 115. Of the spirit with the body, 390. Of the will and understanding; of charity and faith; of love and wisdom, 371.

-431. The joining <note>original: "conjunction"</note> of love and wisdom can be seen reflected in the joining of the lungs and the heart, 415. Their connection through correspondence is such that when one acts, the other does as well, 405.

There is nothing in the universe which does not correspond to something in a human being—not only to their feelings <note>original: "affections"</note> and thoughts, but also to the organs and internal parts <note>original: "viscera"</note> of the body. This correspondence is not based on them as physical substances, but on their functions <note>original: "uses"</note>, 324. Things that correspond act in the same way, with the difference being that one is natural and the other is spiritual, 399. On the primary correspondences of the natural with the spiritual, 377.

The purpose of creation is for all things to return to the Creator so that there may be a union <note>original: "conjunction"</note>, 167—172. **DEAD.** Everything that originates from the sun of the physical <note>original: "natural"</note> world is lifeless <note>original: "dead"</note>, 157, 162. That which is lifeless does not act

<column-break/>

on its own but is acted upon, 157. A person whose mind is a hell is described as "dead," 276.

**DEATH** of the body; when it occurs, 390. What a person becomes after death, 90.

**DECREASE** of spiritual heat and light happens through horizontal degrees <note>original: "degrees of latitude"</note>, 94, 186. In heaven, and in every society of heaven, light decreases from the center toward the edges <note>original: "circumference"</note>, 253.

**DEGREES.** There are two kinds of degrees: vertical degrees <note>original: "degrees of altitude"</note>, also called discrete degrees; and horizontal degrees <note>original: "degrees of latitude"</note>, also called continuous degrees, 184—188. Gradual decreases from thicker to thinner—or rather, increases from thinner to thicker—are called continuous degrees. Discrete degrees are completely different, acting as purpose <note>original: "end"</note>, cause, and result <note>original: "effect"</note>, 184. See also 65—68.

**DELIGHTS** in human life come from the feelings <note>original: "affection"</note> of their love, and pleasant experiences <note>original: "pleasantnesses"</note> come from the thoughts rooted in that love, 33, 316, 363.

**DENIAL,** the, of God, and specifically in Christianity the denial of the Divinity of the Lord, creates hell, 13.

**DESIRES** are offshoots <note>original: "derivations"</note> of love, 363.

**DETERMINATION** to act, the, is an offshoot <note>original: "derivation"</note> of love and wisdom, 363.

**DEVIL.** The desire to rule <note>original: "love of dominion"</note> stemming from self-love is called the devil; the attraction to lies <note>original: "affections of the false"</note> and the thoughts that originate from that love are called his "crew," 273, 424. See *Satan*.

**DIAPHANOUS** <note>Transparent or translucent</note> forms of the mind present from birth, 245, 255. They let spiritual light through just as crystal lets natural light through, 245.

**DIAPHRAGM.** Its link to the lungs, 384, 402, 403, 408.

**DIASTOLE.** <note>The phase of the heartbeat when the heart muscle relaxes and allows the chambers to fill with blood.</note> See *Systole*.

**DIFFERENCE** between various things, 185; between heat and light in the spiritual world and heat and light in the physical world, 89; between angels and men, 112; between "in itself" and "from itself," 76; between spiritual and physical atmospheres, 175; in the three heavens, 202; between the life of a worldly <note>original: "natural"</note> man and the life of an animal <note>original: "beast"</note>, 255; between the physical and the spiritual, 294, 295; between the thoughts of angels and those of men, 294, 295; between celestial and spiritual love, 427; between spiritual and physical language, 70, 295.

**DISCRETE.** To act "discretely" is to act through correspondences, 219.

**DISTANCE.** External thought, which is united with what the eyes see, creates the sense of distance, 130. Distances in the spiritual world are appearances, 108—112, 113, 124; they are appearances based on the spiritual connections <note>original: "affinities"</note> related to love and wisdom, or to goodness and truth, 7, 10.

**DISTANT,** the sense of being, comes only from the mind's judgment about space based on the objects in between, 41.

**DISTINCTLY ONE.** In the God-Man <note>Swedenborg's term for the Lord</note>, or the

<page-num>178</page-num>

<language>English</language>

<columns>2</columns>

<header>ALPHABETICAL AND ANALYTICAL INDEX.</header>
<sig>N 2</sig>

**Lord,** the Being <note>original: "Esse"</note> and the Manifestation <note>original: "Existere"</note> are distinctly one, 14–16, 34; likewise infinite things, 17–22; so also end, cause, and effect, 169; this is why it is said they are distinctly one, 14.

**Diversity** in created things arises from there being infinite things in the God-Man, and indefinite things in the spiritual Sun, 155.

**DIVINE, the,** is one and indivisible, 4. It fills all space in the universe without occupying space, 69–72. It is in all time without being bound by time, 73–76. As it is in the greatest things, so it is in the least things, 77–82. It is actually present in each and every thing of the created universe, 59, 60. It is not different in one subject than it is in another, but one created subject is different from another, 54. It is invariable and unchanging; consequently, it is everywhere and always the same, 77. See *God*.

**DIVINE BODY.** By the Divine body of the God-Man, the Divine Manifestation <note>original: "Existere"</note> is meant, 14.

**DIVINE ESSE AND EXISTERE.** Love and Wisdom taken together are the Divine Being <note>original: "Esse"</note>, but taken separately, Love is called the Divine Being and Wisdom is called the Divine Manifestation <note>original: "Existere"</note>, 34.

**DIVINE ESSENCE, the,** from which all things were created, is the Divine Love and Wisdom, 33. It is one, 35.

**DIVINE HUMAN, the,** 11, 12, 233. In the Divine Trinity it is called the Son, 146.

**DIVINE LIFE, the,** is the Divine Essence. It is one, 35.

**DIVINE LOVE AND DIVINE WISDOM.** See *Contents, Parts 1 and 2*.

**DIVINE PROCEEDING.** In the Divine Trinity it is called the Holy Spirit, 146. What the Divine Proceeding or Holy Spirit is, 146–150.

**DIVINE SOUL.** By the Divine soul of the God-Man, the Divine Being <note>original: "Esse"</note> is meant, 14.

**DIVINE TRUTH.** The Lord became Divine Truth in the final results <note>original: "ultimates"</note> by fulfilling everything in the Word concerning Himself in the books of Moses and the Prophets, 221.

**DIVINUM A QUO.** The Divine Source <note>original: "Divinum a quo," literally "The Divine from which"</note>. In the Divine Trinity it is called the Father, 146.

**DRONES,** where they come from, 338, 339.

**DUST, damned,** what it is, 341.

**EAR.** It appears that the ear hears, but it is actually the understanding which hears through the ear, 363. Through the senses, man knows nothing of the countless parts within the ears, 22. The more deeply the ear is examined, the more wonderful are the things discovered there, showing perfections according to discrete degrees <note>discrete degrees: distinct levels of existence, like cause and effect, rather than a continuous scale</note>, 201. The right ear corresponds to the good of truth, and the left to the truth of good, 127, 384, 409. Ears of God, 18.

**EARTH, the,** in the spiritual world has neither rotation nor orbit, 101.

<column-break/>

**EARTHS, the,** are passive forces from which all effects exist, 178. In the earths there is an effort to produce useful functions in forms, or "forms of uses," 310–312. The first production from those earths, when they were still new, was the production of seeds, 312. The origin of the earths, 302–306. In the spiritual world there are earths, but they are spiritual, 173–178.

**EAST, the,** in the spiritual world, is where the Lord appears as a Sun, and the other directions are determined by it, 119–123. In whatever direction the angels turn their bodies, they face the east, 105. In the Bible <note>original: "the Word"</note>, the east signifies the Lord in the highest sense, and in a relative sense, love toward Him, 121, 122. In the spiritual world, those who are in a higher degree of love live in the east, 121.

**EDEN, the garden of,** represents man in terms of his wisdom and intelligence, 325, 422.

**EFFECT.** There is no effect on its own, or effect separate from a cause and a purpose <note>original: "end"</note>, 167. The effect is the complex container <note>original: "continent"</note> and basis of causes and purposes, 212. Every effect is the fullness of its causes, 217. Effects teach nothing but effects; considered alone, they do not explain a single cause, 119. Effects can only be seen as if in the dark if the causes of those effects are not seen at the same time, 107. To know effects from causes is to be wise; to seek causes from effects is not to be wise, 119. To see from effects alone is to see from fallacies, 187. All effects, which are called final purposes <note>original: "ultimate ends"</note>, become primary purposes once again in a continuous series, 172. See also 168, 256, 257.

**EFFLUVIA.** Streams of tiny particles <note>original: "effluvia"</note> flow unceasingly from every object in nature, 293. The effects they produce on the blood, 420.

**EFFORT** <note>original: "Endeavor"</note> does nothing by itself, but acts through forces corresponding to it, which produce motion. It is the essential element within the powers, and through those powers, it is within the motion, 218. In the earths, there is an effort to produce useful functions in forms, 310–312. In everything spiritual, there is an effort to clothe itself with a body, 343. Love, or the will, constantly tends toward the human form, 400. The power of effort in man is the will of man united to the understanding, 218. See *Power and Motion*.

**EGGS.** Reproduction by seed in eggs, 342, 347, 351.

**ELEVATION** of man into the heat and light of heaven, 138, 256, 258, 422.

**EMBRYO,** the state of the embryo or the child in the womb <note>original: "uterus"</note>, 399, 401, 402, 407, 410, 432.

**END, the,** or aim of this work is to discover causes so that effects may be understood through them, 188.

**END.** There is no end <note>purpose</note> alone without a cause and an effect, 167. The end produces the cause, and through the cause, the effect, 189, 241. The end is everything in the cause, and also in...

...the effect, 168, 197. There is a first end, a middle end, and an ultimate end—or end, cause, and effect, 167, 197. Ultimate ends become first ends again in a continuous series, 172. The purpose <note>original: "end"</note> of creation is that all things should return to their Creator, and that there should be a connection <note>original: "conjunction"</note>, 167–172, 329, 330. The goals <note>original: "ends"</note> of all creation have been uses, 314. The end defines <note>original: "qualifies"</note> the means, 261.
**ENTITY.** Imaginary entity, 43, 210.
**ENVELOPE OR COVERING.** Each discrete degree <note>discrete degrees: distinct levels of existence, like soul and body, that do not shade into one another</note> is distinguished from another by its own specific coverings, and all the degrees together by their common covering, which communicates with the inner and innermost levels, 194. The skin-like <note>original: "cutaneous"</note> covering of the spiritual body and what composes it, 257, 388.
**EPIGLOTTIS**, 382.
**EQUILIBRIUM.** The balance <note>original: "equilibrium"</note> of all things arises from action and reaction, 68, 263. Everything must be in equilibrium, 68. Equilibrium is destroyed when action overcomes reaction, and vice versa, 263.
**BEING** <note>original: "Esse"</note>. The **Being** is a substance, and the **Manifestation** <note>original: "Existere"</note> is a form of that substance, 43. A **Being** is not a **Being** unless it exists in a manifest form, 15.
**ESSENCE.** The essence of all love consists in connection <note>original: "conjunction"</note>, 47. The essence of spiritual love is to do good to others, not for one's own sake, but for the sake of others, 335.
**ETERNITY.** How the angels view eternity, 76. The eternity of every divine work arises from the union of love and wisdom within it, 36.
**ETHER**, 176, 183, 223, 374. See *Atmosphere.*
**EVIL.** The origin of evil comes from the abuse of a person's rationality and liberty, 264–270. Evils and falsehoods <note>original: "falses"</note> confirmed by a person remain and become part of their love and life, 268. All evils—and consequently all falsehoods, whether inherited from parents or added by oneself—reside in the natural mind, 270. Evils and falsehoods are entirely opposed to goods and truths, 271. See *Hereditary.*
**EXHALATIONS**, the action they produce in the blood, 420.
**EMPTYING** <note>original: "Exinanition"</note>. The state of emptying or humiliation of the Lord, 234.
**MANIFESTATION** <note>original: "Existere"</note>. The **Manifestation** is where the **Being** <note>original: "Esse"</note> is; one is not possible without the other, 14. Whatever exists from a **Being** makes one with that **Being**, 15.
**EXTERIORS.** The outer parts <note>original: "exteriors"</note> of the mind make one with the outer parts of the body, 136.
**EXTERNALS.** All the external things of the angels are correspondences of their internal states, but they are spiritual correspondences rather than natural ones, 87.
**EYE.** According to appearance, it is the eye that sees, but it is actually the understanding that sees through the eye, 363. See *Eyes.*
**EYES.** Through the senses, man knows nothing of the innumerable things within the eyes, 22. The more deeply the

eyes are considered, the greater wonders are discovered in them, including perfections according to discrete degrees, 201. The eyes of humans and the eyes of angels are both formed so that they may properly receive their own specific light, 91. The right eye corresponds to the good of truth, and the left to the truth of good, 384, 409, 127. Eyes of God, 18. See *Eye.*
**FACES.** Their infinite variety, 318. *Faces* of the angels are turned toward the East, 129. Face of God, 18.
**FACULTIES.** Humans have two faculties of life, which result in the will and the understanding, 30. Rationality and liberty are two faculties specific to humans, which distinguish them from animals <note>original: "beasts"</note>, 240, 264. The use and abuse of these two faculties, 267. These faculties are never taken away and exist in the wicked as well as in the good, 162, 240, 247, 266, 425.
**FAITH**, in its essence, is truth, 253, 429. It belongs to the thought, 214. See *Charity, Falsehood.* See *Evil.*
**FEET.** The right foot corresponds to the good of truth, and the left to the truth of good, 384, 409, 127. Feet of God, 18.
**FIBERS** <note>original: "Fibres"</note>. Where the origin of fibers is, there is the origin of life, 365. The action of fibers, 366. See also 207, 254, 367, 369, 370, 400, 410. Motor fibers <note>original: "Moving fibres"</note>, 190, 192, 207, 215, 254, 277. Nervous fibers, 190, 192.
**FIBRILS** <note>original: "Fibrillæ"</note>. Their great number compared to the multitude of rays proceeding from the stars, 366.
**FIBRILLARY** substance of the brain and its action, 366.
**FINITE.** The finite cannot exist except from the Infinite, 44.
**FIRE** is dead, and the fire of the sun is death itself, 89. Between spiritual fire, which is Divine Love, and natural fire, there is the same difference as between the living and the dead, 93. Fire corresponds to love, 87. How the fire of the Spiritual Sun becomes heat proportioned to the love of angels in heaven, and similarly, how the heat of the natural sun becomes heat suitable for humans, 174. In the Word <note>The Bible</note>, fire signifies love, 87; and even the Lord, in regards to Divine Love, 98.
**FLIES.** Where they originated from, 338, 339.
**FLOW IN**, to. Everything that flows in through the spiritual mind comes from heaven, and everything that flows in through the natural mind comes from the world, 261. Everything that flows in is perceived and felt according to the forms that receive it and according to their states, 275.

**GOD IS LOVE ITSELF**, because He is Life itself, 4—6. He is not in space, 7—10, 21. He is truly Human <note>original: "very Man"</note>, 11—13, 16, 97. Existing not from Himself, but in Himself, 16. All things in the created universe, when considered from the perspective of their uses, represent man as an image, and this testifies that God is Man, 319—326.
